# Decisions

Every design decision that still governs the code, by ADR number. Code cites
these numbers; link one as `docs/decisions.md#adr-NNNN`. A new decision gets the
next number and a short entry here: the rule as it holds, its
premise, and the owning files. History, alternatives and review rounds go in
the commit message. The original ADR texts are in git:
`git show docs-archive-2026-10-19:docs/adr/<file>`.

Retired numbers (their subsystems are gone; see the archive tag; ranges inclusive): 0001, 0004–0026, 0028–0109, 0112–0126, 0128–0131, 0133–0135, 0137, 0139, 0140, 0142–0152, 0154–0159, 0162–0165, 0167–0175, 0177–0189, 0191, 0197, 0198, 0207, 0208, 0223, 0224, 0226, 0227, 0229–0247, 0249–0265, 0267, 0271, 0272.

<a id="adr-0002"></a>
## ADR 0002 — Type Facts lives in this repository
The Type Facts producer (Rust, on tsrs; Go until ADR 0365) and the Rust process/session client live here behind their versioned process seam. One change adds, produces, consumes and measures a semantic fact. Reactive IR sees only wire facts. Owners: apps/solid-typefacts, rust/crates/typefacts, schema/.

<a id="adr-0003"></a>
## ADR 0003 — The Solid 2 compiler follows `solidjs/solid` next
Solid 2 compiler facts come from `solidjs/solid/packages/compiler` via the `yumemi-thomas/solid` fork at one exact commit. The fork adds only semantic-fact models, output-neutral recording hooks, validation, serialization and fact tests; lowering, output, diagnostics, runtime behaviour, features and performance are untouched. Compiler defects are fixed upstream separately; the affected fact stays open until the fork rebases. Owners: rust/Cargo.toml (`solidjs-compiler` rev), rust/dialects/solid-v2/compiler/src/lib.rs.

<a id="adr-0027"></a>
## ADR 0027 — The built-in Solid runtime comes from the dialect
The selected dialect's reviewed premises, with exact symbols, Type Facts and control flow, give the behaviour of `solid-js`, `@solidjs/signals` and `@solidjs/web` (its primitive-defining packages); no package document is read. Missing native behaviour is unknown, never "no effects". Similarly named packages are external. Owners: rust/crates/solid-dialect (`primitive_defining_packages`), rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/package_requirements.rs.

<a id="adr-0110"></a>
## ADR 0110 — The checker analyzes Solid 2 only
The resolved `node_modules/solid-js` major picks the dialect. A major this build has no dialect for (1.x, any future major) yields only a project-level `SC9013` refusal naming the deciding `package.json`; a missing or unparsable install defaults to 2.0. The 1.x-only rules `jsx-no-undef` (SC8005) and `prefer-classlist` (SC8013) are dropped, not replaced. The eslint-plugin-solid parity corpus is retired: `upstream_compat` is retained behaviour without upstream control, and a divergence worth keeping is pinned as a product-owned ownership case. `Version::V1` stays so the refusal recognises 1.x. Owners: rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/dialect.rs, rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/lib.rs.

<a id="adr-0111"></a>
## ADR 0111 — Dialect words belong to the dialect
Shared code asks the `Dialect` vocabulary, not hardcoded primitive lists: the dialect says whether a call returns a destructurable reactive tuple (`returns_reactive_tuple`) or a store (`returns_store`), so `createOptimistic` and `createOptimisticStore` need no shared list. A shared `match` on `Primitive` is a seam a new dialect re-enters through; variants no carried dialect names are not live arms. The contract-word half is retired. Owners: rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/lib.rs, rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/{indexes,local_access,source_discovery}.rs.

<a id="adr-0127"></a>
## ADR 0127 — Audited, defaulted and conservative vocabularies are separate
`Solid2::AUDITED` (the audited triple), `Solid2::DEFAULTED` (no resolved `solid-js`) and `Solid2::CONSERVATIVE` (an unresolved owner, answered by each field's `#[default]`) coincide today but stay separate, so moving the audited release changes only `AUDITED_INSTALLATION` and what `AUDITED` names. ADR 0194 assigns each release its answers and notice. Owners: rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/solid_2/releases.rs, rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/{dialect,release_scope}.rs.

<a id="adr-0132"></a>
## ADR 0132 — A relative edge inside one installed package is no package boundary
The package-requirement census skips a relative import whose importer and resolved file share an owning manifest (nearest `package.json` above each declaring a `name`). It stays a requirement if it climbs into another installation, reaches a nested copy of the package, or has an unresolved edge or unreadable manifest. Only part 1 of the original ADR is live. Owner: rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/package_requirements.rs (`is_package_local_edge`).

<a id="adr-0136"></a>
## ADR 0136 — `createComponent(C, props)` is a call edge to `C`
The dialect says which call renders a component argument (`Dialect::renders_component_argument`; Solid 2: argument 0 of `createComponent`). A call renders project function `F` only if the callee resolves into a primitive-defining package under a rendering name and argument 0 is a bare identifier with symbol `F`; else it is an escape. Only the attribution call graph reads the edge, not `function_call_sites` (component identity, execution roles). Owners: rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/solid_2.rs, rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/{indexes,attribution}.rs.

<a id="adr-0138"></a>
## ADR 0138 — `Dynamic` renders the component its `component` prop holds
The dialect lists `(export, prop)` renderers (`component_prop_renderers`; Solid 2: `Dynamic`/`component`) and says only `createMemo`'s accessor yields exactly its compute (`accessor_yields_only_its_compute`). `component_value_flows` follows a value into such a prop only if every reference is a proven read (parentheses, `?:`, `??`/`||`/`&&`, `const X = value` read as `X`, a one-argument parameterless arrow memo read as `X()`); parameters, member reads, `let` and other calls escape. The call graph walks the render as an edge. Owners: rust/crates/solid-facts/src/ast/component_value_flow.rs, rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/{indexes,attribution}.rs.

<a id="adr-0153"></a>
## ADR 0153 — Context roles are a dialect fact (live part)
`Dialect::context_role` names `createContext` (Create), `useContext` (Read) and `createComponent` (Render). `useContext(C)` yields `C`'s default or a value stored under its fresh id; a provider stores its `value` prop. Accessor provenance uses the roles to prove a parameter is read only as context. The package-context census chapters are retired. Owners: rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/{lib,solid_2}.rs, rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/accessor_provenance.rs.

<a id="adr-0161"></a>
## ADR 0161 — A call that runs on every normal completion
`unconditional_calls` returns the calls a function runs exactly once on every normal completion: each step from call to body is evaluated once, unconditionally, and no earlier statement of an enclosing block can leave by `return`/`break`/`continue`. In the caller-facing `synchronously_unconditional_calls` (ADR 0275, 0276) an earlier `throw` is an exit, an `async` function counts only calls before its first suspension, and a generator states nothing. A declared host's conditions are already folded (ADR 0166). Owners: rust/crates/solid-facts/src/ast/unconditional_calls.rs; read by solid-reactive-ir cleanup.rs and execution_role.rs. (The proposal-input use is dormant.)

<a id="adr-0166"></a>
## ADR 0166 — A host constant is folded per declared host
With exactly one declared host (`browser` or `node`), a named import of a bare package is a literal when every target its `exports` map selects exports the name as a module-level `const` bound to the same `true`/`false` literal (`@solidjs/web` `isServer`), read from the resolved bytes, never the name. A `let`, re-export, pattern or array target, disagreeing targets or two hosts fold nothing. The fold is a span-preserving rewrite blanking the dead arm; the fact file keeps the original `source_hash`. ADR 0295 adds Window-realm comparisons. Owners: rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/{host_constants,dependency_program}.rs, rust/crates/solid-facts/src/ast/host_constants.rs.

<a id="adr-0176"></a>
## ADR 0176 — An owner requirement after an `await` is proven unowned
A call in the producer's `calls_after_await` (after an `await` on every path of its async function's own body) runs from a promise continuation on a fresh stack, with the dialect's `fresh_stack_callback_owner` (Solid 2: none), so an effect, cleanup or owner-requiring call there is proven unowned (evidence: "runs after an `await`, from a promise continuation"). `onSettled`'s returned cleanup keeps the enclosing context; `runWithOwner` callbacks are nested closures, unaffected. Owner: rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/owners.rs (`AwaitContinuations`).

<a id="adr-0190"></a>
## ADR 0190 — A primitive receiver dispatches to its built-in prototype
A parameter-member invocation at a call site has no `SC9012` obligation when the argument is a primitive literal (not spread) or Type Facts resolves the call to one standard-library declaration of `String`, `Number`, `Boolean` or `BigInt`; object-typed built-ins (`Date`, `Array`, `Map`) are not covered. The demand plan thus asks for the resolved call of argumentless member calls rooted at an enclosing function's parameter. Premise: no program patches a primitive prototype to read reactive state. Owners: rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/interproc.rs, rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/demand_plan.rs (`parameter_rooted_member_call`).

<a id="adr-0192"></a>
## ADR 0192 — A built-in call in a leaf scope is resolved
The demand plan also asks for the resolved call of an argumentless method call in a leaf-owner callback (`callback_semantics_at(..).owner == Leaf`), settling a standard-library method there instead of raising an obligation. Demand stays limited to leaf callbacks and parameter-rooted calls: demanding every member call adds a second symbol candidate and loses proven violations. Optional calls stay open (the producer gives no resolved call). Owner: rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/demand_plan.rs.

<a id="adr-0193"></a>
## ADR 0193 — An application is the whole program
Without an explicit program boundary the nearest `package.json` decides: `"private": true` is an application (closed); otherwise publishing `exports`, `main`, `module`, `types`, `typings` or `bin` makes a library (open), publishing nothing is closed, and a missing or unreadable manifest is open. An explicit `--program-boundary`, the ESLint `programBoundary` setting or `.solid-checker/runtime.json` wins. The default is part of diagnostic identity. Closing drops only the unseen-caller assumption; callers must still be enumerated exactly. ADR 0444 reopens an application a workspace sibling depends on. Owner: rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/program_boundary.rs (`default_program_boundary`).

<a id="adr-0194"></a>
## ADR 0194 — The audited Solid 2 release is rc.21
`AUDITED_INSTALLATION` names `solid-js`, `@solidjs/signals` and `@solidjs/web` at `2.0.0-rc.21` (`Solid2::AUDITED` is `RC21`): rc.17's answers on rc.21's bytes (review in docs/release-audits/2026-11-04-solid-2-rc21-release-review.md), plus `TripleExports::Rc21` for what only the whole rc.21 triple answers. rc.17 answers as rc.13 but for `on`+uppercase spread listeners, an unguarded `createOptimistic` setter, `latest` absorbing a pending read, a deferrable `hydrate`, and `dynamic`'s static form from `solid-js`. `READ_RELEASES` is rc.0-rc.9, rc.13, rc.17, rc.21; unread releases get conservative answers under `SC9014`, older read ones the older-release notice. On the rc.17 triple `dynamicComponent` takes `dynamic`'s forms, `dispatchAsInteraction` runs `fn` inline in the caller's owner, `createDeferred` answers as `createMemo`, `lazyModule` as `lazy`'s loader, and `onHydrationEnd` runs later, owner conditional; `setLinkClaim` is an import-scoped gap closed only by declared CSR (an inferred host admits non-Vite hosts). Owner: rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/solid_2/releases.rs; review in docs/release-audits/.

<a id="adr-0195"></a>
## ADR 0195 — A source tracked before the await is a dependency
SC1002 (`reactive-read-after-await`) skips an after-`await` accessor call when the async function calls the same symbol in straight-line code before its first suspension of any kind (`await`, `for await`, `await using`). `straight_line_calls`, per innermost function, excludes `if`, ternary, `&&`/`||`/`??`, loops, `try`, `switch` and optional chains. Member reads (store paths, props) keep the rule. A mistake can only hide a finding. Owners: rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/static_rules.rs (`tracked_before_first_suspension`), rust/crates/solid-facts/src/ast/mod.rs (`straight_line_calls`).

<a id="adr-0196"></a>
## ADR 0196 — The compiler follows Solid rc.21
Fork commit, adapter revision literals, compiler-facts identity and identity document move together; the facts API is linear commits on `next`. Pin: distribution `ea3c7858`, upstream `2105ffb8` (rc.21), implementation `31d8fe49`, branch `solid-checker/compiler-facts-rc21`; trace 3, protocol 2. Against untouched rc.21, 3,000 outcomes over 8 profiles are byte-identical in code, maps, CSS and diagnostics. rc.21's lone dynamic SSR spread records the thunk it lowers to; `compile_program` refuses tracing. Only `on` + uppercase lowers as a listener (`Dialect::compiler_lowers_event_attribute`). Universal/Dynamic, TSRX and `sourceNames.locations` traces refuse. Owner: rust/dialects/solid-v2/compiler/src/lib.rs.

<a id="adr-0199"></a>
## ADR 0199 — A callback prop forwarded to an event runs on dispatch
A function literal passed as a project component's prop takes `ExecutionRole::EventCallback` when the component only hands it to DOM event dispatch: the tag resolves to one project function whose props parameter is one identifier, each reference a static member access that is an intrinsic event-handler value, inside such a handler, or a prop of another component meeting the same proof. `merge`/`omit` views, `const` aliases and un-namespaced `on...` spreads are followed; a props object escaping whole or a forwarding cycle proves nothing. Unproven stays uncertifiable. Owner: rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role.rs (`forwarded_event_prop_role`, `prop_reaches_only_events`).

<a id="adr-0200"></a>
## ADR 0200 — A literal a wrapper runs as a tracked compute is tracked
A function literal passed whole to an exactly resolved synchronous project wrapper takes `ExecutionRole::TrackedJsx` when every reference to the wrapper's parameter is a direct call inside one function literal that is the whole argument of a tracked primitive callback (`createMemo`/`createEffect` compute) in the wrapper's body. Wrappers of wrappers and calls from nested functions stay unproven. Owner: `solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role.rs` (`forwarded_tracked_compute_role`).

<a id="adr-0201"></a>
## ADR 0201 — A helper's read in its own body runs during the call
At a single-target call (a synchronous project function, outside JSX), an attributed read takes the call's role when written directly in the callee's body (not in a nested function, timer, listener, getter, `untrack` callback or default parameter) or when it is the accessor argument the body calls. Reads from deeper callees stay attributed; ADR 0204 extends the chain. Owner: `read_is_direct` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/interproc.rs`.

<a id="adr-0202"></a>
## ADR 0202 — Feedback reports what the user can act on
An unresolved member dispatch raises no obligation at a call whose role is event callback, deferred callback, tracked JSX or deleted code: no read rule reports there. The violations / "Needs review" / "Analysis coverage" tiers exist only behind `--internal` (ADR 0328). Owners: `interproc.rs` (dispatch roles), `solid-facts-backend/src/snapshot_emission.rs` (`render_default`).

<a id="adr-0203"></a>
## ADR 0203 — A closed program has no unseen caller of an exported helper
In a closed program an exported helper's `exported-parameter-member-dispatch` obligation is discharged when every reference to it or its aliases is its declaration, an import, an export specifier, or the callee of a call resolving to it. JSX renders, rendering props, values handed out, class members and unnamed functions keep it. Open programs are unchanged. Owner: `discharge_closed_program_export_dispatch`, `entered_only_through_call_expressions` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/attribution.rs`.

<a id="adr-0204"></a>
## ADR 0204 — Three reads that run while a component renders
(1) A read written directly in a function entered by a chain of plain identifier calls (no methods, at most eight hops; cycles prove nothing) is direct. (2) A callee's default-parameter accessor read runs at a call omitting that argument without spread. (3) A literal that is exactly a component prop runs during render when the child calls `props.<prop>` directly in its body. "Directly in a body" excludes nested parameter lists. Owners: `directly_entered_functions` (`solid-reactive-ir/src/interproc.rs`), `prop_literal_invoked_during_render` (`execution_role.rs`).
<a id="adr-0205"></a>
## ADR 0205 — Coverage gaps group by family and subject
Each analysis-coverage finding carries `coverageFamily` and `coverageSubject`, set at projection from the defect's fields; grouping never parses message text. Internal views only (ADR 0328). Owners: `coverage_group` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/projection.rs`; `render_coverage` in `solid-facts-backend/src/snapshot_emission.rs`.

<a id="adr-0206"></a>
## ADR 0206 — A component's owner carried to runWithOwner is not null
`runWithOwner`'s owner argument is proven non-null when it names a `const` initialized by a dialect-resolved `getOwner()` call directly in a proven non-async component body (not a nested function or default). Parameters, `let` bindings, properties and helpers stay conditional; disposal is a separate question. Owner: `component_owner_binding` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/owners.rs`.

<a id="adr-0209"></a>
## ADR 0209 — A method of an exact instance is followed in a leaf scope
A method call in a leaf-owner callback is followed when the receiver is a `const` bound to `new C(...)` (or `this` in such a method) and `C`, a single-declaration project class, itself declares the resolved method, unless an assignment writes a member of that name or replaces a prototype (ADR 0213/0314 narrow this). Owners: `exact_instance_method` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/cleanup.rs`; `member_name_may_be_reassigned` in `indexes.rs`.

<a id="adr-0210"></a>
## ADR 0210 — Host callbacks and member calls in a leaf scope
A standard-library call's arguments follow the audited timing table: inline callbacks (`forEach`, `map`, `call`/`apply` receiver, setter updaters, `Promise` executor) are walked like helper bodies; fresh-stack and deferred callbacks run after the scope; `PromiseLike.then` and unaudited callables leave SC9012 open, never a violation. Member calls are read from the resolved declaration, not the span's entity. ADR 0329 covers synchronous dispatch. Owners: `member_call_operations`, `standard_library_argument_operations` in `cleanup.rs`; `runtime_semantics.rs`.

<a id="adr-0211"></a>
## ADR 0211 — A value whose origin fixes its class fixes its members
A fresh built-in argument (array literal; `new` of Array/Date/Map/Set/WeakMap/WeakSet/RegExp/ArrayBuffer/DataView/typed array; `Array.from`/`of`, `Object.keys`/`values`/`entries`, `String.split`; species-built array results) runs prototype members that read no reactive state; an exact project class instance runs that class's own plain method. Followed through `const` only; void where the program may replace the member. Owners: `value_origin`, `class_method_symbol` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes.rs`.

<a id="adr-0212"></a>
## ADR 0212 — An accessor's value is proven from every write
Proven built-ins also include: a signal accessor whose init is proven and not a function, every setter reference a call and every write or updater return proven; a `createMemo` accessor with a synchronous literal; a plain call of an exact project function with every return proven; `??`/`||`/`&&`/`?:` of proven operands; array literals; members of a proven receiver (`sort` returns it; `map`/`filter`/`slice`/`split` build fresh values). Props, stores and cross-file accessors stay open. Owners: `accessor_origin`, `setter_writes_builtin`, `returns_builtin` in `indexes.rs`.
<a id="adr-0213"></a>
## ADR 0213 — A parameter holds what every caller passes
In a closed program a parameter-member obligation on a plain unwritten identifier parameter is discharged when the function is entered only through calls (ADR 0203) and every call's argument (or default), followed up to four hops, is a proven origin. A named prototype write (`C.prototype.m = f`) refuses only `m`; replacing a prototype or writing an unknown key refuses all. Owners: `CallGraph::parameter_holds_builtin` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/attribution.rs`; `member_name_may_be_reassigned`.

<a id="adr-0214"></a>
## ADR 0214 — A prop holds what every tag passes
In a closed program an obligation on `props.name` is discharged when `props` is the component's sole plain parameter, never assigned; the component renders only through resolved JSX tags (a call, `createComponent`, rendering prop or other escape fails); and every tag passes a built-in value (a spreading tag fails, a missing attribute is `undefined`, `children` is not followed). Owners: `prop_holds_builtin`, `rendered_only_through_jsx`, `value_holds_builtin` in `attribution.rs`.

<a id="adr-0215"></a>
## ADR 0215 — The value-flow proofs survive review
Computed writes record their literal or `const` key; other computed prototype writes refuse every member; a binding is written if any assignment target or loop head references it. An exact class instance has the class's own members only if no constructor returns a value and no field, parameter property or computed element on the whole `extends` chain can define the member (unresolved proves nothing). Function values must be current. Owners: `class_instance_is_exact`, `function_value_is_current`, `binding_written`.
<a id="adr-0216"></a>
## ADR 0216 — A forwarded prop is as reactive as its source
`<Inner value={props.value}/>` makes Inner's prop reactive, static or unresolved as the caller's prop is (least fixpoint over forwards); a prop is static only if no chain reaches a reactive or unresolved value. Props objects or stores passed whole, `merge`/destructured views, several children and `props.a.b` off a static head are unknown, never reactive. Owners: `classify_passed_expression`, `resolve_forwarded_props` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/source_discovery.rs`.

<a id="adr-0217"></a>
## ADR 0217 — Leaf scopes reach helpers, namespaces and more host callbacks
Argumentless calls in helpers a leaf callback reaches get a resolved-call fact. `ns.f()` on a namespace import binding follows the exactly resolved exported function declaration or `const` function; computed members and aliases keep the obligation. Host rows: `Promise` executor and `NodeList.forEach` inline; global `addEventListener` and `MediaQueryList.addListener` deferred; `MediaSession.setActionHandler` fresh-stack. Owners: `solid-reactive-ir/src/cleanup.rs`, `runtime_semantics.rs`; `solid-facts-backend/src/demand_plan.rs`.

<a id="adr-0218"></a>
## ADR 0218 — A callback every use of which is closed is not a strict read
A callback handed to a project function or component clears only the SC1001 finding, when every runtime reference of its subject (parameter, optionally one static property of an exact literal or attribute) is closed: a dialect tracked slot, exact unlabelled `untrack`, compiler-tracked JSX, a fresh-stack scheduler, or an exact forward. Storing, aliasing, unknown packages, DOM listeners and cycles are open; labelled `untrack` and `callback_may_open_strict_window` slots never inherit a window. Owners: `solid-reactive-ir/src/project_consumer.rs`, `projection.rs`; `Dialect::callback_may_open_strict_window`.
<a id="adr-0219"></a>
## ADR 0219 — A type query and a namespace call are accounted entries
ADR 0203's entry census also counts erased type queries (`typeof fn` in type position) and a namespace call whose property resolves to the call edge's declaration, only while no runtime code transitively reaches the module as a namespace object (escaping namespace import, `export * as`, dynamic `import()`/`require`). Specifiers resolve by the compiler's module graph; unresolved bare ones may name anything; files outside the program import no project module (ADR 0193). Owners: `module_namespace_escapes`, `specifier_reaches` in `attribution.rs`.

<a id="adr-0220"></a>
## ADR 0220 — The project's bundler answers where a load goes
Opt-in `--runtime-resolution required` runs a Node worker that loads the project's Vite config and asks a client plugin container (`resolveId`) about each load occurrence, joined by literal span. If present, the table alone answers `specifier_reaches`; unknown answers (error, virtual or query module, declaration target, `require`) may reach anything; any failure leaves an empty table; the run bypasses the daemon. Owners: `solid-facts-backend/src/runtime_resolution.rs`, `packages/cli/scripts/runtime-resolver.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0221"></a>
## ADR 0221 — A prop head and an async prefix run at the call
A props member chain's head (`props.snapshot?.x`) is a Get backed by the ADR 0216 fixpoint. A control-flow primitive's sole exact children literal runs while rendering. An exact plain call of an async project function runs its prefix to the first suspension (no suspending loops or `switch` tests). An omitted argument activates its default. New async/default paths need a runtime source witness; a store Get needs a key the dialect says warns. Owners: `local_access.rs`, `interproc::body_site_runs_during_call`, `Dialect::store_key_warns_strict_read` (exact rc.13).
<a id="adr-0222"></a>
## ADR 0222 — A returned source and a helper's prop read reach the caller
A project function (exact, current, synchronous, block-bodied, no loops/`try`/`switch`) whose every normal completion returns the same unwritten `const` bound to a dialect source primitive in that frame returns a fresh source per call. A component-local helper's prop read counts at each proven exact plain call, with the call's role and ADR 0216 backing. Owners: `solid-reactive-ir/src/source_discovery.rs`, `local_access.rs`; `completion_return_cover` in `solid-facts/src/ast/completion_call_cover.rs`.

<a id="adr-0225"></a>
## ADR 0225 — An exact receiver for getter instantiation
An argument's getter runs at the call copying it only when the receiver is a fresh closed object literal or one immutable local `const` initialized by one (no alias, export, descriptor change or earlier spread; no internal spread, computed or numeric key, duplicate, setter or `__proto__`). Anything else stays an explicit obligation. Only the receiver rule is live (used by ADR 0283); the contract protocol words are gone. Owner: `solid-reactive-ir/src/property_gets.rs`.

<a id="adr-0228"></a>
## ADR 0228 — The certified contract tier is retired
`bundledContracts`, `installedPackages` and `exportConditions` stay in the WASM `CheckRequest` and its typings for compatibility, accepted with no effect. Owners: `solid-checker-wasm/src/lib.rs`, `packages/wasm/index.d.ts`.

<a id="adr-0266"></a>
## ADR 0266 — Standard runtime configuration premise
Assumed unless analyzed source holds a positively identified exact runtime reference: a call of `enableExternalSource`, or a value reference to `DEV` or `OBSERVE` (a named import used only in truthiness tests does not veto). Identity is the exact declaration name-node span the dialect lists, never spelling; type-only uses and unresolved references never veto. A veto makes every violation project-wide uncertifiable; there is no third state. Owners: `solid-reactive-ir/src/runtime_configuration.rs`, `solid-dialect/src/solid_2.rs`.

<a id="adr-0268"></a>
## ADR 0268 — Standard hydration host
Fourth clause of the standard runtime configuration premise: hydration runs on Solid's standard serializer runtime, with no custom `sharedConfig.load`/`has` or other host callback. A positively identified runtime reference to `sharedConfig` (via `solid-js/internal` or the server declarations) vetoes, except direct reads of `hydrating`/`done` and truthiness tests; standard `enableHydration`/`hydrate`/`render`/`renderToString` never veto, and the main-entry `sharedConfig`, lacking declaration identity, stays Assumed. Owner: `solid-reactive-ir/src/runtime_configuration.rs` (`HydrationHost`).
<a id="adr-0269"></a>
## ADR 0269 — A browser-target config for client applications
The ESLint adapter ships an opt-in `browser-<dialect>` config (`browser-v2`): the dialect config plus `settings.solidChecker.runtime.target: "browser"`, which a later `runtime` setting overrides. For client apps the user declares the target; nothing infers it. Owner: `packages/cli/eslint.cjs`.

<a id="adr-0270"></a>
## ADR 0270 — Browser host from feasible execution reachability
Without an explicit target, discovery infers a browser host from closed, non-executing config (one conventional `vite.config.*`, scripts naming Vite only in plain forms, no custom resolver) and client roots, or refuses on an unproven premise with a `solid-checker: note: browser host not inferred` line. Premises: conventional config, successful build, app starts. A callback registered from browser-executed code with a reviewed trigger can run in the browser unless proven not to. Inference is monotone and never certifies; an explicit target wins. A config plugin counts only as an exact installed closure (each package's file digests and dependency edges, runtime peers included) in `host-plugin-pins.json`; `host_plugins::tests::installed_closures` prints a closure's identity and checks an install against the pins. A pinned closure vouches for the plugin under the admitted options, not its runtime peers' semantics (SC9014 still applies). `@solidjs/vite-plugin` next.45–51, read against next.44, keep under admitted options the client resolve conditions, non-hydratable dom client output, entry probe order and no resource claims. Dev-only label passes (`sourceNames`), the performance-tracks module and build-time root-reachable core dedupe (next.51) leave the host unchanged; the root-install requirement for core imports covers the dedupe. Pinned: the rc.17 corpus's next.51 installs (darwin-arm64; compiler rc.16 or rc.17) and the demo app's install via `vite-plugin-solid` next.27. Owners: `solid-facts-backend/src/inferred_host.rs`, `host_config.rs`, `host_plugins.rs`; `solid-reactive-ir/src/hosts.rs`.
<a id="adr-0273"></a>
## ADR 0273 — Transported member stability premise
Members of an object from a factory or context are assumed never reassigned, deleted or redefined. Only a visible write vetoes: a static member write by name on any receiver, or `Object.defineProperty`/`assign`/`Reflect.set` by exact library identity, where unknown keys or spread arguments veto every member. Each member is Assumed or Vetoed. Over-veto only withholds. Owner: `MemberStability` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/accessor_provenance.rs`.

<a id="adr-0274"></a>
## ADR 0274 — Option getters through a forwarding helper
A local helper's call copies its parameter when the helper (exact, current, synchronous, directive-free module, never naming `arguments`/`eval`) passes the plain unwritten parameter to the copying call as a `const`'s whole value or an unconditional `return`, outside classes, JSX and fragments. Getters of a hazard-free prefix (none mentioning `this`, `super` or `eval`) are guaranteed. Owner: `solid-reactive-ir/src/property_gets.rs`.

<a id="adr-0275"></a>
## ADR 0275 — A helper's operation counts only on every completion
In the leaf-scope walk a called body's operation (or call to the next helper) counts for the call only if it runs on every normal completion the caller can continue from (an earlier `throw` is a way out; an async helper counts only its prefix before the first suspension); otherwise it is an uncertifiable `leaf-owner-forbidden-call`. The leaf callback's own body keeps the statically-present-code premise. Owner: `reached_on_every_call` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/cleanup.rs`.

<a id="adr-0276"></a>
## ADR 0276 — A read through a helper counts only on every completion
A completion site is a call, `new` or member expression in a function's straight-line flow (no conditional, logical right operand, logical assignment, loop, switch or try; nothing before it can `return`/`break`/`continue`/`throw` out; in an optional chain only what precedes the first `?.`). A summary row is direct only if proven on every completion at every hop; others stay uncertifiable "only on some of its paths". Owners: `AstFacts::completion_sites` in `solid-facts/src/ast/mod.rs`; `site_on_every_completion`, `read_is_direct` in `interproc.rs`.

<a id="adr-0277"></a>
## ADR 0277 — A callback runs only when its helper calls it on every completion
A helper invokes its parameter during the call only if a call of the parameter is a completion site of its synchronous body; this governs inline-arrow roles, stored-literal proofs and accessor-by-reference reads. A call on some paths is an uncertifiable obligation (SC2001 stays silent); `cb?.()` is no completion site. Owners: `invokes_parameter_during_call` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role.rs`; `parameter_called_on_every_completion` in `indexes.rs`.

<a id="adr-0278"></a>
## ADR 0278 — Exact callback mapping and never-returning guards
A call TypeScript resolves to a `never`-returning signature is an exit; sites after it are no completion sites. A written parameter is not the caller's callback. An argument reaches its parameter only through an exact mapping: no `f.call`/`apply`/`bind`, a current function value, no earlier spread. Owners: `project_callee_at_parameter` in `execution_role.rs`; `never_returning_call_before`, `parameter_completion_calls` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes.rs`.

<a id="adr-0279"></a>
## ADR 0279 — Assertion and never signatures, and exact method callees
The Type Facts producer states per resolved call whether the selected signature carries an `asserts` predicate or returns `never`; either makes the call a possible exit (a throw in an undeclared helper is outside the model). A method callee runs its declared body only for a module namespace member, an unwritten member of a `const` object literal, or a member of a `const` bound to `new C()` of the declaring class. Owners: `Call.AssertionSignature`/`NeverReturns` in the producer and `typefacts`; `member_runs_declared_method` in `execution_role.rs`.

<a id="adr-0280"></a>
## ADR 0280 — A call's arguments decide its helper's branches
A call proves parameter values (literals, callable types, omitted arguments) only over an exact mapping with no spread. For the leaf walk's first hop, a called helper's own reads and by-reference accessors, the walk decides `if`/`?:` tests and `&&`/`||`/`??` right operands over `typeof p`, truthiness and null/undefined comparisons, making sites guaranteed, possible or unreachable; results record a `FunctionSource` dependency. When ADR 0222 checks a returned store root, a reference inside a call argument escapes only if the member chain is the bare root, is itself an argument or callee, or is returned by a function; a chain consumed where written (statement, initializer, operand in a callback) does not escape. Owner: `member_chain_escapes` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/source_discovery.rs`.

<a id="adr-0282"></a>
## ADR 0282 — Tuple slot closures through destructuring
`const [a, start] = make(); start()` names the factory's closure when the pattern is a `const` array pattern of plain identifiers over one direct call, and `make`, an exact synchronous project function, has one unconditional return of a hole-free array literal whose slot is an inline arrow or an unwritten `const` arrow it declares. A project function Gets `p.key` on every call when it is exact, current, synchronous, directive-free and never names `arguments`/`eval`, `p` is a plain unwritten identifier parameter whose every reference is a static non-callee member read, and the member is a completion site. <a id="adr-0284"></a>
## ADR 0284 — Dependency source mode
`--dependency-source <specifier>` analyzes an installed package's runtime source as project code. Entries are selected by reading `exports` as Vite with the Solid plugin does for the declared target and build (first of `solid`, `import`, `module`, `default` and host conditions; else `mainFields`); patterns, arrays and `browser` maps refuse. <a id="adr-0285"></a>

`f` calls its parameter `p` on every completion when a completion site `g(…, p, …)` of `f` passes the identifier `p` itself (unwritten, exact position, no earlier spread) to a function declaration `g` that calls that position on every completion, to a fixpoint. `const` callees refuse (temporal dead zone); a module with direct `eval` adds nothing; no destructured parameter may precede `p`. Before the typed-source fallback admits an object literal's property as a source, every read of that property symbol must be Closed: a `const` receiver, all references unwritten static member reads, initialized by an object literal (or an exact factory returning a fresh one) with that data property. Children get only ADR 0204's attribute proof, not the body-call proof. Owner: `component_child_function` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role.rs`.

<a id="adr-0327"></a>
## ADR 0327 — A callback only an event runs has no owner
A plain function literal is seeded unowned when every invocation ADR 0319's walk finds in owner mode (any primitive slot refuses; a call's role is event only when its carrying function is a compiler event handler) is an event dispatch, so an owner primitive directly in it is an SC4001 violation. Premise: an event handler runs unowned (synchronous dispatch from an owned scope breaks it; in the backlog). Owners: `unowned_event_literals` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role.rs` and `indexes.rs`; `owner_node` in `owners.rs`.

<a id="adr-0328"></a>
## ADR 0328 — Uncertifiable results are internal
Users see a finding that is not uncertifiable or is a project-level notice (`subjectKind: "project"`: SC9013, SC9014). Human formats show only those and `--certify` fails on a violation or notice; `--format json` and WASM keep every finding and the certification status; `--internal` restores the ADR 0202 tiers and strict certification; ESLint reports only user-facing findings. Owners: `user_facing`, `certify_fails`, `render_user` in `solid-facts-backend/src/snapshot_emission.rs`; `userFacing` in `packages/cli/eslint.cjs`.

<a id="adr-0329"></a>
## ADR 0329 — A DOM call that dispatches synchronously is not trusted
The leaf-scope walk refuses a call, optional or not, whose exact `lib.dom` declaration is `focus`, `blur`, `click`, `dispatchEvent`, `checkValidity` or `reportValidity` on any interface (and window globals), or `HTMLFormElement.reset`/`requestSubmit`, `HTMLElement.showPopover`/`hidePopover`/`togglePopover`, `HTMLDialogElement.show`/`showModal`/`close`/`requestClose`, `AbortController.abort`, `XMLHttpRequest.open`/`abort`, `Document.execCommand`. Tree mutation and attribute writes stay trusted (ADR 0642 corrects this for `remove`). Owner: `synchronously_dispatching_dom_member` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/cleanup.rs`.

<a id="adr-0330"></a>
## ADR 0330 — A ref creation is SC6001 only in the unowned apply phase
A creation inside a `ref` expression is an SC6001 violation only if it runs when the applied callback runs and nothing between gives it an owner. Eager arguments, IIFEs and default parameters run while rendering (owned); a `createRoot` callback owns it, directly or through inline synchronous slots. Any other owner-supplying slot (`runWithOwner`), a project helper, an unread callee, a discarded factory result or a generator makes it uncertifiable. Premise: a per-element `createRoot` is never disposed. Owners: `ref_phase`, `nested_owner`, `discover_directive_creations` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/directives.rs`.

<a id="adr-0331"></a>
## ADR 0331 — A function is a component only where `createComponent` calls it
A JSX-returning function is a proven component when every reference is its declaration, module surface, a dialect callback or renderer prop (ADR 0138) or a proven `lazy` default (ADR 0292). Any other reference (alias, storage, argument, method, anonymous default export, unread router) leaves it a component whose body, except callbacks a primitive places and functions a tag renders, is an `UndecidedRegion` (non-style violations uncertifiable). A tag naming an exact alias (`const`, unwritten `let`) of an unwritten function binding renders it as its own. A call proves no component only if every other reference is one above, a `typeof` or an exact alias only called or tagged, and no importer is unseen (open program, namespace hand-out); else its calls' findings stand and a body read or write they clear is uncertifiable (a host component proves nothing). A callback position is one entry; it clears no render. Owners: `component_statuses` (`indexes/components.rs`), `write_roles.rs`, `named_callbacks.rs`, `site_in_undecided_component` (`lib.rs`), `diagnostics.rs`.

<a id="adr-0332"></a>
## ADR 0332 — A site no fact places is an obligation
A read, write or action with execution role `Unknown` (no fact) is projected as an uncertifiable finding of its rule (SC1001, SC2001, SC2002), never dropped; `is_uncertifiable` per seed sets kind and wording. `Unreported`, the proven no-breach answer without a single role (ADR 0323's merge of non-reporting roles, code past the strict-read window), is `Unknown` to every consumer and projects nothing. The same holds for a read in a callee called after an `await` in a tracked async computation (SC1002, via the reads attributed to that call), `resolve`/`until` in code no role places (SC2004/SC2005), options the syntax cannot read beside an async computation (SC7002), and a dialect primitive referenced as a value (SC9012, `PRIMITIVE_VALUE`). A coverage rule (`RuleMetadata::uncertifiable`) disabled by options hides its findings but still blocks `certified`. Two identity rules guard the primitive lookup: calling a primitive's method (`createEffect.call`/`.bind`) is not calling the primitive, and an import binding whose symbol declares nothing (unresolved imports share the one unknown symbol) names no primitive. Owners: `projection.rs`; `retain_enabled` in `solid-facts-backend/src/diagnostics.rs`.

<a id="adr-0333"></a>
## ADR 0333 — A write past a dominating await runs with no owner
A write past a dominating await is legal and silent: the await is at statement level of its innermost block, unskippable from the block's start, and the write comes later in that block, in the same async non-generator function, outside instance fields, JSX and compiler tracked regions (facts schema 65, `dominating_awaits`). A `try` block counts; its `catch` and `finally` only when the block's first statement awaits an operand that cannot throw synchronously (a call of an identifier naming an async non-generator function declaration, with only literals and enclosing-function parameters as arguments: a hoisted declaration is initialized, calling an async function never throws, and its native promise is adopted without project code), facts schema 66 `try_head_awaits`. A read there is past the strict-read window, also silent. Premises: code after an await runs on a later task with no owner, and no program redefines `Promise.prototype.constructor` or `then`, which awaiting a native promise reads synchronously (as ADR 0190 for primitive prototypes) (runtime-oracle `precision-write-after-try-await`, `precision-write-in-catch-after-async-call`). No event-only write proof: a synchronous dispatch from an owned scope runs the handler under that owner. Owners: `follows_dominating_await`, `follows_try_head_await`, `semantic_write_execution_role_within`, the solid-facts AST visitor.

<a id="adr-0334"></a>
## ADR 0334 — An audited TanStack router renders its route components
A function given as the `component`, `pendingComponent`, `errorComponent` or `notFoundComponent` of the options literal of an exact `@tanstack/solid-router` route call (`createFileRoute(path)(…)`, `createLazyFileRoute(id)(…)`, `createLazyRoute(id)(…)`, `createRootRouteWithContext()(…)`, `createRoute(…)` or `createRootRoute(…)`) is rendered there (ADR 0331's census). So are `createRouter`'s `defaultComponent`, `defaultPendingComponent`, `defaultErrorComponent` and `defaultNotFoundComponent`; an anonymous inline function at such a key is a component. The audited releases, `2.0.0-beta.29`, `rc.0`, `rc.4`, `rc.6`, `rc.8` and `rc.9`, each with the `@tanstack/router-core` it loads, render these values, read at most a member such as `preload`, and call none. Premises: every runtime import of the router resolves, as the bundler agrees, to an audited release whose bytes and router-core's bytes match the recorded digests; no manifest, lockfile or patch directory replaces either; and the project does not reach route options by computed key or reflection (`Object.values(route.options)`). Uncertifiable: a project file reading one of the four keys back (member, literal key, destructured key), which could call the value, and (a trade-off) an unaudited release's route components. Owners: `solid-reactive-ir/src/route_components.rs`; `AUDITED_ROUTE_RELEASES` in `solid-dialect/src/solid_2/releases.rs`; `route_replacement_recorded` in `solid-facts-backend/src/installed_bytes.rs`; audit evidence in rust/ARCHITECTURE.md.

<a id="adr-0335"></a>
## ADR 0335 — An audited @solidjs/router renders its route definitions
The `component` of an object-literal route definition in the `routes` array of `createRouter({ routes: [...] })` (an exact named import of `@solidjs/router`), or recursively in a definition's `children` array, is rendered there (ADR 0331's census). The audited releases (`2.0.0-next.16`, `next.18`, `next.26`, `next.41`; `dist/`, both export conditions) copy `component` into the route record, render it only by `createComponent` in the outlet, and otherwise read only its `preload` and the unexported `SERVER_ROUTE` brand. A config naming `links` (called with the router context) withholds it, as does a `useAction` call (`action.apply({ r }, args)`) given neither an arrow nor the router's `action`/`formAction` wrapper, inline or a `const` every reference to which, program-wide (Type Facts, binder; direct named imports, no re-export, namespace or `eval`), is `useAction`, `useSubmissions`, a call or an intrinsic attribute (unseen code: ADR 0300), or beside a prototype spelling or `apply` write (`router_context_as_this`). As ADR 0324 and 0334: every runtime import resolves (attested, bundler agreeing) to an audited release with digest-matching bytes, no record replaces the package, and no analyzed file outside the audited installs obtains a definition. The router hands definitions out only through `useRouteMatches`, `RouterContext` and the instance's `routes`/`config` (no route prop, `preload` argument or other hook), so the router is imported only by name (never those two, a namespace, default, deep path, dynamic load or re-export), and every `createRouter` result is a `const` whose every compiler reference is its declaration, module surface or a JSX tag, in a module no file reaches as a namespace; any non-literal load, module hazard or `import.meta.glob` withholds it. As the router calls `route.preload(...)` on the record and `config.transformUrl(...)` on the config, the config and each definition count only without getters or methods, every key but `routes`, `component` and `children` being a non-callable literal or an arrow. Premise (owner decision, as ADR 0190 and 0333): no program installs callables on `Object.prototype`, which the router would call with the config or a record as `this`; any `prototype`, `__proto__` or `setPrototypeOf` spelling, and ADR 0292's veto, withhold it as a tripwire. A project `component` read then belongs to no definition. Residual premise: no reflection over router internals. Also followed: `defineRoute(...)`/`defineRoutes(...)` (identity helpers), a stock-history call at the config's `history` (ADR 0324), an anonymous inline component value, and a constant route table whose every compiler reference is its declaration, module surface or the `routes` of such a config, in a module no file reaches as a namespace. Not followed: definitions built by a project call, `children` thunks, `fileRoutes()` output. Owners: `router_config_values`, `router_directories`, `definitions_exposed`; `AUDITED_ROUTE_CONFIG_DIGESTS`.

<a id="adr-0336"></a>
## ADR 0336 — A shorthand property is a reference of its value
`{ value }` declares a property and reads the binding `value`, so the Type Facts reference index also files a shorthand's name under `GetShorthandAssignmentValueSymbol`. Without it, censuses needing all references of a binding (ADR 0331's component identity, ADR 0335's router instance and route tables) miss an object holding it: `const holder = { Chip }; holder.Chip()` (false SC1001, fixture `component-identity-escapes`, `Stored.tsx`). Owner: reference scan `shorthand_property_is_a_reference_of_its_value` in `apps/solid-typefacts/src/references.rs`.

<a id="adr-0337"></a>
## ADR 0337 — A view, a const alias or a DOM spread does not invalidate the props root
A props read keeps the callers' classification (ADR 0216) when the props object's other uses are all inert: an argument of a view-building call, a `const` root alias, a spread onto an intrinsic element. The same test gates spreading the parent's props to a child. `omit(props, …)` always builds a view; `merge(...)` only without spread arguments and with an argument syntactically never falsy; the props may be any source. Premises: `Proxy` exists (else `merge` can return its last source); no caller function invoked as `props.onX()` deletes or redefines a getter of its `this` or the props (as ADR 0324 and 0335; root member calls count as inert, and no caller value is checked for `this`). Uncertifiable: reads through the view or alias (`rest.x`, `local.x`), destructuring, a view bound to a non-`const`, a `merge` that may return its only source, a spread onto a component. Trade-off: props reads behind a view-building call are proven signal-backed and reported, safe on these premises. Owners: `call_builds_view`, `props_root_attachments_are_views`, `props_root_unaccounted_uses`; fixture `omit-view-props-root`.

<a id="adr-0338"></a>
## ADR 0338 — A Solid getter has a known `typeof` and `length`
`createSignal` and `createOptimistic` item 0 and `createMemo`'s result are `read.bind(null, node)` over `function read(el)` in `@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.13`: `length` 0 in every build (`Dialect::accessor_getter_length`; unaudited dialects state nothing). So an exact, unwritten, unexported binding of one is a proven truthy function of `length` 0, which `@solid-primitives/utils` `access(v)` always calls: reading through it in a setup body is a violation. Unknown arity (alias, function literal, props value, store, setter, import) decides nothing. A callee calling a parameter reads through its argument unless that is proven not a getter; an unproven argument, or a member call on a getter-holding primitive result (`createSignal(1).slice(0)`), stays an SC9012 obligation. Premises: nobody redefines a received getter's `length`; no analysed file may replace `Function.prototype.bind`, which builds every getter (a write to a member named `bind`, a prototype write of unproven key or owner, or ADR 0292's reflective-write veto withholds the fact); no builtin is patched beyond that tripwire's sight (owner decision, as ADR 0335). Uncertifiable: reads through another helper hop, accessor arrays, reads after an `await`, bindings in a file with direct `eval`, exported bindings (others can redefine them). Trade-off: `--certify` fails on an unproven argument (SC9012). Owners: `argument_values.rs` (`solid_getter_length`), `callback_standing.rs`; fixtures `package-access-helper`, `dependency-source-owner`.

<a id="adr-0339"></a>
## ADR 0339 — A mention of a function-valued binding runs after its declarator only where that is proven
A `const` or `let` function-valued binding is not hoisted: a mention before its declarator throws (`var` reads `undefined`). A call, JSX render, primitive argument or callback role naming one yields a call site and reachability edge only if the mention provably follows the declarator; otherwise findings resting on it are uncertifiable or absent, never violations. Within a module it follows if in the declarator's body after it, inside the function, in a function or arrow created later, in an earlier arrow or nested declaration whose every mention follows, or in a hoisted declaration nothing running before the declarator can call. Across modules, only if the caller statically imports the declaring module and neither it nor its imports sit on an import cycle. Credited: never-written hoisted function declarations; `const` or `let` by source position (not in a `switch` case clause); module evaluation order of imports (static import or re-export, `import x = require()`). Never credited: `var`, `require()` calls, dynamic `import()`, `createRequire`, anything else. Premises: ES module semantics; the analysed files are the whole program, loaded only by static import, recorded `require` or lazy glob; installed dependencies import no project module (ADR 0300); module-level code before a declarator runs project code only via functions written there. Uncertifiable: mentions across unresolved or ambient modules, nonliteral or unrecorded `require`, possibly eager `import.meta.glob`, JavaScript outside `allowJs`, direct `eval` in either file, non-literal parameter defaults (they run before the body). Trade-off: no edge for a withheld mention, so the checker stays silent where a call site is only unproven. Owner: `solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes/initialization.rs`.

<a id="adr-0340"></a>
## ADR 0340 — SC1002 reaches what the continuation runs
A read is after the await wherever the continuation runs it, not only where written. Four proven shapes extend the region, with the existing proofs and the ADR 0195 exemption: (1) after a dominating await, an IIFE or a function literal in a slot an exact standard-library declaration invokes before returning; (2) a `.then`, `.catch` or `.finally` callback on a proven native `Promise`, the call awaited or returned by a synchronous computation; (3) an exactly resolved `async` project function awaited or returned, directly or via `Promise.all` or `allSettled`, reading after its own first await; (4) an exactly resolved synchronous project function called after the await (on the continuation's stack), for accessor calls on every normal completion (ADR 0276). Premises: `@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.13` attributes every read below the returned promise's async frame, so a handler's read is in the computation's frame when the chain is awaited or returned synchronously; a handler on a possibly settled receiver is queued before adoption with no frame below, where the runtime is silent, so only a receiver proven pending counts. Uncertifiable: rejection handlers; fulfillment handlers on receivers not proven to fulfill; `async` helpers that may resume before adoption; possibly tracked calls before the first await; a computation whose owner a `createRoot` `dispose` may dispose; store keys another path may alias; reads after `for await`; contents-dependent calls of a `sort` comparator, a reducer without initial value or a `replace` replacer. Trade-off: a mistake in any shape only adds reports, so each has a runtime-oracle case (docs/open-work.md). Owners: `solid-reactive-ir/src/static_rules/after_await.rs`, `static_rules/pre_await.rs`; `is_inline_standard_callback` and `standard_call_name` in `runtime_semantics.rs`.

<a id="adr-0341"></a>
## ADR 0341 — `flush` on a `createEffect` apply's stack is SC2008
`flush()` running only on a `createEffect` apply's stack is SC2008, a warning. **The claim is universal and is the no-op, not the warning:** it always runs in the effect phase, where the running flush drains nothing, so it is pointless everywhere, dead code included. The dev build usually warns too (`FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK`); nothing throws and no published type expresses it, so the finding is the checker's own claim. Premises: `@solidjs/signals` rc.1 and later reviewed releases mark the effect phase around an apply, and `flush` returns undrained during a flush (read from published bytes and probed; rc.0 has neither marker nor report); the flush finds the apply's queue running, so both load one installed `solid-js` copy; no server build is selected. The rc.3 dialect release names it `flush-in-effect-callback` (the vocabulary has no name). Unclaimed: helpers non-effect code may call (export, alias, argument, timer, microtask or event handler, other caller), applies passed by name, `flush` imported from `@solidjs/signals` itself or via another package. Trade-off: reporting dead calls gives up reachability, so a helper with any non-effect caller goes unreported. Owner: `solid-reactive-ir/src/effect_phase.rs`; rule page `docs/rules/flush-in-effect-callback.md`.

<a id="adr-0342"></a>
## ADR 0342 — A member of a compound component that only JSX member tags reach is a component
A function stored in a compound component (`export const Split = { Root, Panel }`, `Object.assign(Root, { List })`) and reached only through member tags (`<Split.Panel>`) is a component, like a tagged function (ADR 0331): a tag is `createComponent(Split.Panel, props)`, so storing is no escape if tags are the only way to the member. The holder is a module-top-level `const` closed object literal or an `Object.assign` target; its Type Facts references are only its declaration, module surface, or the leftmost name of a JSX tag ending at a value; no computed member write or reflective call (`Reflect.set`, ...) reaches it or `Object`. Premises: ADR 0331's (analysed files are the whole program; nothing enumerates exports reflectively); `Object.assign` unpatched (no write to a member named `assign`, no prototype write of unproven owner, none to `Object`); no direct `eval`; the holder's module has no import cycle or unresolved load, so no tag renders during its initialization. Uncertifiable (ADR 0331): a member nothing renders, read as a value, called plainly or destructured; a holder in a type, passed anywhere or reached via a namespace import; a definition built by a project call. Trade-off: a proven member's body is a component's: its calls are no retained callables, and reads via helpers it calls are violations, not obligations. Owners: `solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes/compound.rs` (read by `indexes/components.rs`, `interproc/retained_literal.rs`); fixtures `compound-components*`; census detail: rust/ARCHITECTURE.md.

<a id="adr-0343"></a>
## ADR 0343 — `innerText` is a content source on Solid 2.0
`@solidjs/web` rc.13 lists `innerText` as a property, so writing it replaces the element's child nodes, as `textContent` does. `jsx-no-duplicate-props` (SC8003) counts an exact `innerText` beside children, `innerHTML` or `textContent`. eslint-plugin-solid 0.14.5 lacks this arm at 6d3bc311: a kept divergence, pinned as `innertext/solid-v2/content-positive/001` in `fixtures/ownership-cases/cases.json` (product-owned, ADR 0110). Premise: the published typings accept the prop, so tsc is silent and the claim is the checker's own. Trade-off: it reports a combination upstream does not. Owner: `has_inner_text` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/upstream_compat/syntax.rs`.

<a id="adr-0344"></a>
## ADR 0344 — Behavioural evidence classifies a static-only finding; a refutation is a candidate, not a failure
A static violation no dev diagnostic covers is not by default a false positive. A runtime-oracle case can observe the page via `behaviour` entries in `oracle.json` (DOM text, window counter, node identity, thrown error, or write-reflected, testing a two-source element text cannot). A page showing the defect confirms the finding; correct behaviour makes it a false-positive candidate, listed before covered verdicts but not failing the gate (the fix belongs in the checker). A shown defect without a finding is an unproven clean claim, as for dev diagnostics. Premises: observations are the page's real dev-runtime behaviour, each write's probe bound to its source; a write whose probe stays unchanged, or whose outcome differs from its declared `expect`, is a harness error, not evidence. Static-only: a static source no write reaches (static `textContent`). Trade-off: a candidate costs visibility, not a failed gate. Owner: `behaviour` and `staticOnly` in `scripts/runtime-oracle.mjs`; format in `fixtures/runtime-oracle/README.md`.

<a id="adr-0345"></a>
## ADR 0345 — A ref expression's own functions run where they are written
A `ref` expression runs in two places. The function it yields to the ref application (the expression, or an element, conditional branch or logical operand of it) runs unowned: a violation. A function it evaluates without yielding (getter body, tagged template substitution, `void` operand) is undecided. Code a call in it evaluates runs during render under the surrounding owner: an IIFE, a called factory's body and a callback a dialect slot runs during its call (`untrack`) are owned; at module initialization, unowned (a violation); in a component's children, undecided (it runs in that component's memo). A generator never runs when called; a callback no dialect slot describes may run owned or not: both uncertifiable. Only a function literal, bare identifier or element 0 of an event handler's tuple names a callback. Premise: an owner edge reaches the enclosing function; with none (a method no resolved call names, an export a closed program never calls) the owner is unknown, so uncertifiable. Trade-off: an effect from a factory a ref calls is owned, so SC6001 only, not SC4001 (open item in docs/open-work.md). Owners: `ref_value_marks` and `yielded_value_contains` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/owners/owner_context.rs`; fixtures `ref-expression-owner-walk`, `directive-owned-effects`, `callback-containment`.

<a id="adr-0346"></a>
## ADR 0346 — A callback's identity is the entity whose symbol covers its whole span
A typefacts entity names the symbol at its demanded span's first byte: the value's only if it covers the whole span (`symbol_covers_span`, Type Facts wire schema v21, handshake protocol 80), else a token inside, like the callee of `f(x)` or the object of `o.p`. Every callback identity reads this exact answer: owner discovery and the owner callback index, event handler and ref roles, named callbacks, reachability roots. TypeScript wrappers (`h as X`, `h!`, `h satisfies X`) keep the identity of `h`. A call-valued callback names a literal only via an identity wrapper: a project function, declared or bound by an immutable `const` to a function literal, returning one of its parameters on every completion and nothing else, neither async nor a generator, never otherwise referencing that parameter, its name, `arguments` or `eval`; one declared later in its module (`wrap = function …`) names nothing. A callee names only the identifier it is; a member read of a server function with a resolved declaration is no server call. Premises: the covering answer is exact; the wrapper read is the one that runs (a wrapper changed in another file invalidates its callers via the cross-file owner cache). Uncertifiable: a call-valued callee's returned callable (SC2001, SC1001). Trade-off: reactive reads keep their leading token (`a.b.c`, `sig().x`, `props.x ?? []`), staying attributed to the source they go through; making them exact removed six correct SC1001 violations in a corpus trial, so they were not changed. Owners: `indexes.rs` (`EntitySymbols::exact`), `indexes/identity_wrappers.rs`; fixtures `callback-ts-sugar-identity`, `identity-wrapper-callback`, `identity-wrapper-rejections`, `call-valued-callee-identity`, `server-member-transport`.

<a id="adr-0347"></a>
## ADR 0347 — An invoked literal runs under its call's owner; a namespace hand-out is reachable
A direct call of a function literal runs its body inside the call, so it is an owner edge under the enclosing function: unowned at module scope, owned in a component body; withheld for instance field initializers (run at construction) and JSX in the owner's own body (children run in the component's memo, ADR 0345). In a closed program an export a namespace import hands out is seeded with the open-world unowned bit: a callback unowned whatever the caller does is a violation; a requirement in the export's own body is uncertifiable; an export only an unnamed escape reaches is never proven. A dynamic import hands out only the names its result reads (`const { a } = await import(x)`, `lazy(() => import(x), options)`). A leaf body is not region-owned, its `onSettled` is queued unowned (rc.13), and a `createTrackedEffect` body always runs under the tracked node. Premise: a namespace or dynamic import whose specifier does not resolve to the module does not count; else ADR 0203's "may name anything" premise would seed every export of a closed program. Mechanics: `rust/ARCHITECTURE.md`, `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`, `docs/rules/leaf-owner-forbidden-call.md`. Fixtures `owner-invoked-literal`, `owner-invoked-literal-forms`, `owner-guard-iife`, `owner-namespace-reachable`, `owner-namespace-named-reads`, `owner-tracked-body-forbidden`, `owner-leaf-*`.

<a id="adr-0348"></a>
## ADR 0348 — An inert standard call is inert for its runtime arguments; an async prefix runs on the caller's stack
An audited standard call is inert only for proven runtime arguments: built-in receiver, no spread, primitive coercing/iterating slots, function callback slots; annotations prove none. An async function's prefix up to its first own suspension runs on the caller's stack; the flight walk owns the tail. Catch/finally joins lose dominance unless operand proofs exclude a synchronous throw. Premise: intrinsic built-ins (ADR 0338). Objects, opaque arguments and unproven catch joins stay uncertifiable. Owners: `static_rules/{pre_await,after_await}.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/reactive-read-after-await.md`.

<a id="adr-0349"></a>
## ADR 0349 — The Promise and array-iteration intrinsics hold until a visible write
Native promises come from intrinsic constructors/statics, fetch, async functions, native chains, or project functions returning these on every path; types alone prove nothing. Native handlers are queued; aggregate elements settle the result. Premise: Promise, its handlers/constructor, Symbol.species and array iterators are intrinsic; visible named or reflective writes to them or their prototypes, or a Promise subclass, withdraw it, turning claims into obligations, never clean; alias writes stay a residual premise. Opaque collections, fetch outcomes and typed thenables stay unproven. Owners: `indexes/intrinsics.rs`, `static_rules/{native_promise,pre_await,after_await}.rs`; fixtures `read-after-await-premise` and tripwire variants.

<a id="adr-0350"></a>
## ADR 0350 — A call is primitive by its result, and a numeric signal's accessor is a number
A standard call's result can be primitive whatever its evaluation. A signal accessor is numeric only with a compiler domain of numbers alone, an immutable pair initialized by a decimal literal, and setters referenced only in direct calls with decimal literals. Types alone, functional or escaped setters and unknown domains stay unproven. Premise: exact compiler domains, no binding reassignment. Owners: `indexes/primitive_proof.rs`, `static_rules/pre_await.rs`; fixtures `read-after-await-{reach,premise}`.

<a id="adr-0351"></a>
## ADR 0351 — An object literal's function is the callee of its member read when nothing else reaches the literal
A member call on an unexported `const` object literal names its function if the literal has no spread, computed key, accessor, duplicate, `this` or `__proto__`, and every reference is a plain non-optional member read, never written or deleted. Async members join the flight; synchronous ones carry straight-line completion reads. Method producer and function spans share their end, differing only by method-header text. Premise: analysed files are the whole program. Escapes, classes and unproven members stay silent. Owners: `indexes/closed_members.rs`, `static_rules/{native_promise,after_await}.rs`; fixture `read-after-await-reach/variants/member-helpers`.

<a id="adr-0352"></a>
## ADR 0352 — Context reads require an owner and a provided value
Unowned, rc.13 `useContext` throws `NoOwnerError` even with a default (SC4001). Under a proven owner, SC4002 needs a default-less project const and a closed census with no provider or escape; namespace or module publication withholds the census; package and open contexts stay unproven. Owners: `owners/{context_census,missing_owners,owner_discovery}.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/{missing-owner,missing-context-provider}.md`.

<a id="adr-0353"></a>
## ADR 0353 — Audited render callbacks run under a root
`@solidjs/web` rc.13 and rc.17 run `render`'s code in `createRoot`, which `hydrate` reaches on every path. rc.17 may defer it: execution is not stated but the root is (`callback_runs_in_created_root` is timing-free), so setters in that code and helpers it calls violate SC2001 unless past an await. Other web releases' placement is uncertifiable. Owners: `solid_2.rs`, `execution_role/write_roles.rs`; rule page; fixtures `setter-in-render-root` (+ rc.9 twin); oracle `render-root-write`.

<a id="adr-0354"></a>
## ADR 0354 — Possible suspension withholds strict-read placement
A read possibly after its own async function's await is uncertifiable: the skipped-await path cannot prove a strict-read-window violation. Calls and writes lose placement too; unconditional awaits still close the window. Exclusive arms and nested closures are separate; loop back edges and destructuring evaluation order are kept via AST facts schema 73. First-iteration reads may lose recall. Owners: `execution_role/body_roles.rs`, `solid-facts/src/ast/`; mechanics: `docs/rules/strict-read-untracked.md`; fixtures `strict-read-maybe-suspended`, `await-continuation-reads`; runtime oracle `strict-read-possible-await`.

<a id="adr-0355"></a>
## ADR 0355 — Root allocation does not prove a directive leak
A `createRoot` in ref apply owns its work, may be empty, and may be disposed, so SC6001 withholds its leak claim as an uncertifiable lifetime obligation in direct and returned callbacks; direct unowned computations keep their proof. The dialect identifies root-creating calls. Owners: `directives.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/primitive-in-directive-application.md`.

<a id="adr-0356"></a>
## ADR 0356 — Synchronous dispatch withholds event-owner proofs
Standard-library `EventTarget.dispatchEvent`/`HTMLElement.click` calls withhold event owner and execution proofs, as delivery may inherit an owned caller stack. Until targets are joined, the veto covers all event callbacks, forwarded literals included. Owners: `indexes/event_delivery.rs`, `owners/`, `execution_role/`; mechanics: `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`.

<a id="adr-0357"></a>
## ADR 0357 — Possible suspension retains both owner contexts
Possible suspension keeps both original and unowned contexts, so SC4001 is an obligation. It follows ordinary calls and owner-preserving callbacks, invalidates lexical-root shortcuts, never seeds dormant functions. Definite continuations keep their proof; fresh roots own their callbacks. Owners: `owners/{owner_context,missing_owners,owner_discovery}.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`.

<a id="adr-0358"></a>
## ADR 0358 — Exact handler holders enter the owner graph
Event members, tuples and intrinsic spreads resolve through the immutable receiver census. Web rc.13 enables non-namespaced `on` spread keys, lowercase included; rc.17 only `on` plus uppercase. Getters, generators and unknown holders keep obligations; synchronous dispatch withholds unowned proof. Owners: `object_members/handlers.rs`, `owners/`; mechanics: `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`.

<a id="adr-0359"></a>
## ADR 0359 — Alternative owner creations are proven jointly
A package function whose same-operation creation calls cover every exit (`if (c) createEffect(…); else createRenderEffect(…);`) requires an owner. Early returns, try, loops, async, `never` and member callees stay uncertifiable. Owner: `owners/missing_owners.rs`.

<a id="adr-0360"></a>
## ADR 0360 — A refused file has no compiler facts
A compile error (`CompilerProviderError::Refused`) is no failure: the file keeps AST and Type Facts, `ExecutionMap::refused` places no site, violations located or evidenced in it are uncertifiable, and SC9015 names it (ADR 0441 makes a proven refusal an error; this notice covers the rest). A refused JSX closure file keeps a `*` package off source (SC9005); a named one fails. A missing or untrusted trace is fatal. Owners: `file_execution_map` (`lib.rs`), `walk` (`dependency_sources.rs`), `diagnostics.rs`.

<a id="adr-0361"></a>
## ADR 0361 — The tsc oracle is TypeScript 7.1, the producer's compiler
Native `tsc` 7.1 (current pin: ADR 0374) via `typescript/sync` (Node), pinned apart from `packages/cli`'s 5.9.3. The CLI has no diagnostic length. Owner: `scripts/lib/tsc-oracle-api.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0362"></a>
## ADR 0362 — The producer's TypeScript follows the pinned tsrs rev (ADRs 0365, 0374)
The Go producer's pin (b85298b6a81f) is history.

<a id="adr-0363"></a>
## ADR 0363 — Go producer fixes from the Rust port (the Go producer is archived, ADR 0365)
Fixed in the Go producer and ported: one wire symbol name encoder; the reference index never answers a retained or pruned symbol as empty; a file entering the program invalidates importers with an unresolved import.

<a id="adr-0364"></a>
## ADR 0364 — No retained analysis across updates until instantiation-stable ids and recorded dependencies
Superseded by ADR 0398: derived state returned with instantiation-stable ids and recorded dependencies, as per-file passes reused by read set.

<a id="adr-0365"></a>
## ADR 0365 — The Type Facts producer is Rust on tsrs; the Go producer is deleted
`apps/solid-typefacts`, a Rust workspace (own `Cargo.lock` and target) over the tsrs checker pinned to the fork `yumemi-thomas/tsrs@49f5348` (upstream plus one commit exporting two checker functions), is the only producer: native (`bin/solid-typefacts`, release, fat LTO) and WASM (the `reactor` cdylib in `packages/wasm`). It supersedes the producer part of ADR 0002 and ADR 0362 (TypeScript is what the tsrs rev ports, b85298b6a81f); ADR 0361 stands (same release line). Archive: `../sc-worktrees.noindex/tsgo-archive`; corpus sweep 26.8 s against Go's 52.4 s. The differential harness (`docs/typefacts/differential.md`) compares any two producer binaries, by default against the archived Go one.

Accepted trades: an unpublished pinned fork of a single-vendor compiler; clean native build 76 s vs 27 s, incremental 60 s vs 1 s (fat LTO); CMake for mimalloc; an `unsafe` boundary module for tsrs's arena frees. Owners: `apps/solid-typefacts`, `scripts/build-typefacts.sh`, `scripts/typefacts-source-identity.mjs`, `packages/wasm/scripts/build-owned.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0366"></a>
## ADR 0366 — A binder-level symbol's id is a pure function of the program
A proven binder-level symbol's id is `H(domain, key)`, keeping the `symbol:h:<24 hex>` spelling and wire. Key: `Intrinsic` (checker singleton, by pointer), else `Global(name)` (in the checker's `globals` table), else `Export` (the declaration's reference is a module export), else `Decl` over the minimum declaration by `(path bytes, name start)`, not merge order. A pre-merge binder symbol maps first to its merged symbol (one `JSON`, one `Set`). Private and unique-symbol names hash without checker counters (`#<classId>@x`, `@sym@<id>`). Ask order decides nothing. Binder-ness: not `Transient`, or a merge clone (a declaration's binder symbol recorded as merged into exactly it) without check flag or derivation link. Anything else (instantiations, `this` clones, mapped and spread properties) gets its exact derived key or no id (ADR 0398), never a binder id; so do colliding keys and symbols not owning every declaration they list (each declaration's binder symbol merges into it, or it is that declaration's local half): a parameter property's parameter shares the property's node, so only the property keeps the hash. Derived symbols: ADR 0372; multi-origin properties: ADR 0371. Properties read through an instantiation or a class instance's `this` type have derived ids. Owners: `apps/solid-typefacts/src/binder_key.rs`, `src/identity.rs`, `tests/binder_ids.rs`.

<a id="adr-0367"></a>
## ADR 0367 — A table's facts do not depend on demand order, batch order or checker warm-up
Superseded by ADR 0398 for check-first: each file gets its own checker, not one checker for every demanded file in path order. Still in force: a type descriptor is its location's type (no batch-wide sharing by symbol id); tsrs runs its canonical history; no checker counter reaches a fact (runtime identities, wire symbol names and library type names drop `#<id>@` and `@<id>`); type text is display only (`descriptor_text_gate`). Owners: `apps/solid-typefacts/src/passes.rs`, `src/durable.rs`, `src/tests/history_order.rs`.

<a id="adr-0368"></a>
## ADR 0368 — A dependency-source program lists the project's roots first, in tsc's order
TypeScript orders a program's files by its roots, and its answers can depend on that order. The generated sibling tsconfig (ADR 0284) listed roots by path, so closure files under `node_modules` preceded `src`: on probus-hk (TanStack Router), TypeScript 7.1 reported 188 router call errors there that project `tsc` does not, and 21 calls taken as TypeScript-owned (`recovery`) hid 5 SC9012 findings. Root order, not check order, caused it. Now configured roots come first in `ParsedCommandLine.fileNames` order (the producer's `ModuleFact.root`), then other project files by path, then the closure, so TypeScript holds them in `tsc`'s order (measured on probus-hk, unproven for every closure). Owners: `dependency_program.rs` (`program_modules`), `dependency_sources.rs` (`program_document`), `apps/solid-typefacts/src/module_graph.rs`.
Review: roots are placed as the loader places them (an extensionless name's first existing extension, a project-reference redirect); an unplaceable root (`unmappedRoots`, protocol 83) refuses the sources (`ProgramPreparationUnavailable`), never path order. Roots under `node_modules` stay listed. A project call's validity is its own program's (kept open): `Recovery` there is TypeScript's (silent); `Recovery` only in the generated program is SC9012 `dependency-source-program-call-error`. A `Recovery` call in selected package source to a non-callable callee refuses that package (SC9005). A call as callee (`get()(x)`): the outer call's fact is demanded at the whole call, and the producer marks it on a callee-spanning error.

<a id="adr-0369"></a>
## ADR 0369 — Binder-level answers are reused across updates under recorded dependencies
Superseded by ADR 0398 and deleted: whole per-file passes are reused under their read sets, leaving the binder-level halves (reference spans, entity symbols, symbol declarations kept with `Text`, `Exports`, `Ambient` and `Options` digests) nothing to save.

<a id="adr-0370"></a>
