## ADR 0370 — A parameter that may reach an undescribed callee is an obligation at its callers
A proven source passed to an analyzed function (project or selected package source, also via `.call`/`.apply`) is SC9012 `forwarded-to-undescribed-callee:<callee>` at the argument when its position may reach an argument SC9012 `package-callee` would report: the parameter, a binding it initializes, a rest element or spread, or `arguments`, anywhere in the body, or a reached position of a further analyzed function (past a spread, any later one). Never a violation: no link (current callee, unkilled value, call running, final callee) is proven. Owner: `ForwardedUncaptured` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`.

<a id="adr-0371"></a>
## ADR 0371 — A multi-origin property's id is its combination and origins, when they fix its type
A union, intersection or spread property is `H("synth", combination, name, origins)`: the binder keys (ADR 0366) of its declarations' own properties (not a late-bound `__computed`), whole, sorted for a union, in written order for an intersection (overloads) and a spread. The container is unkeyed, so the key must fix the type (id-keyed consumers read one type): no `Partial` flag; each constituent's own property (resolved member or a generic target's own member) and spread side a declared property or its instantiation, never mapped, inherited from a mapped type or spread-merged; each origin a TypeScript interface, type-literal or class member (or parameter property) with a written type (a method: return and parameters), outside any function, mapped or conditional type, without `this`, `super` or an identifier spelling an enclosing type parameter. Else a counter. A `Set` id is never re-resolved; a call keeps its own target; declarations are sorted. Owner: `apps/solid-typefacts/src/binder_key.rs` (`set_key`).

<a id="adr-0372"></a>
## ADR 0372 — A derived symbol keeps its own id
Policy E (STABLE-IDS) rejected. A derived symbol (instantiation, clone, mapped property) may differ in type from its source, and the client joins by id: typed-source tables and the descriptor and callability fallbacks apply one entity's type to the whole id; component identity and write roles treat an id's resolved call sites as all its entries. With erasure, methods reached through a cloned or instantiated receiver went silently clean (`MethodAlias.tsx`; rc17: 3 SC2001). Retention gains nothing (ADR 0369). Revisit once those consumers read types per entity and need a census.

<a id="adr-0373"></a>
## ADR 0373 — The stable-id scheme as built (ADRs 0366-0372)
A symbol id is a hash (`symbol:h:`) naming one symbol in every checker of the program: binder keys (0366), set keys (0371), exact derived keys or no id (0398); no erasure of derived symbols, which id-keyed consumers would conflate (0372); roots first (0368). `retainedFiles` stays on the wire at zero. Type-derived facts are reused only as whole per-file passes under their read sets (0398).

<a id="adr-0374"></a>
## ADR 0374 — tsrs 3bbcc754, TypeScript 7.1.0-beta, and the oracle moved with it
The producer pins `yumemi-thomas/tsrs@db57178` (upstream `3bbcc754`, v0.19.0, plus the export commit). Its TypeScript is `3e9236361fa9`, so the tsc oracle moved with it (ADR 0361): typescript 7.1.0-beta. Lib offsets and `symbol:h:` ids moved, so differential tests take only a reference on the same tsrs rev. Supersedes the pins in ADRs 0361, 0362, 0365.

<a id="adr-0378"></a>
## ADR 0378 — A write's legal role needs all of its function's entries
An event-only proof (ADR 0327) decides first. Otherwise an unplaced write takes the role of its function's call sites and naming positions (a nested closure its own, unless each layer between runs inline or on a fresh stack). An owned-scope entry is a violation if it is the binding's only one. Uncertifiable: an unplaced entry, a recursive JSX render, or any other reference to the binding or a named expression's outer one (prop, store, alias, method, open or escaping export). Owner: `execution_role/write_roles.rs`.

<a id="adr-0379"></a>
## ADR 0379 — `make verify` skips WASM parity for analysis-only changes
When every path the branch changes against origin/main is in `solid-facts`, `solid-reactive-ir`, the v2 rules crate or prose (`verify-delta.mjs --wasm-scope`), `make verify` skips the WASM package and parity gate; CI's `wasm-parity` job (macOS 27, image `xcode-27`; path rows follow the host OS) runs the gate on every pull request over `wasm-package`'s built files. Parity compares two builds of one analysis whose only WASM-only behaviour is threads (`Scope::spawn` panics on wasm32-wasip1), so those crates' `clippy.toml` disallows the thread APIs and a new site needs an allow naming its sequential arm. Accepted trade: a 32-bit or WASI-only analysis difference surfaces in CI, not locally. `SOLID_CHECKER_VERIFY_WASM=1` forces the gate. Owners: `scripts/verify.sh`, `scripts/verify-delta.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0380"></a>
## ADR 0380 — The runtime oracle reuses a case's browser half by content
`feedback run --execution-cache` stores a run's browser half (Vite, Chromium, scenario, frame attribution: raw records only) keyed on all it reads: sources and manifest, the native development models (`feedbackFacts`, which instrument the sources), TypeScript error count, scenario and supplied responses, runtime options, browser binary, tooling manifests, the CLI's scripts and Node. A hit re-hashes every recorded runtime input; any changed or missing one misses. Findings, observations and automatic warnings are recomputed from the current analysis each run. Failed scenarios are never stored. `scripts/runtime-oracle.mjs` uses it by default (`--cache off` disables). Owners: `packages/cli/scripts/feedback-execution-cache.mjs`, `feedback-browser.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0381"></a>
## ADR 0381 — Catalog names follow eslint-plugin-solid where accurate; scope is a superset
A rule mapping onto an eslint-plugin-solid rule (0.18.1, its `v2` config) takes the upstream name unless that no longer describes what the checker proves; the former name stays a `RULE_ALIASES` entry. A shape upstream reports and we do not is added, subject to invariants 1 and 2: one `tsc` reports or the runtime refutes is not adopted, the refusal pinned in `fixtures/tsc-oracle` or `fixtures/ownership-cases`. What we prove beyond upstream stays. This covers semantic rules only; the policy rules ADR 0110 retired stay retired. Owners: `rust/dialects/solid-v2/rules/src/rules.rs`, `solid-facts-backend/src/dialect.rs` (`RULE_ALIASES`), `docs/rules/README.md`.

<a id="adr-0382"></a>
## ADR 0382 — A retained daemon keeps its session across an edit
The resolution witness observes only program files outside the tracked sources (a tracked edit is an overlay update; observing it made every edit a rebuild). It reads the `Modules` demand `distinctBareImports` (protocol 84): non-relative imports, one row per importer (text and resolved, included and symlink path), so a `.d.ts` repeating `import("pkg")` no longer overflows the 64 MiB frame and drops the daemon to one-shot. An update keeping the program's files keeps the witness plus the edited files' imports, a superset of a fresh one. A dangling symlink's cache digest is its link text (inference still refuses on it). Owners: `daemon.rs` `answer`, `daemon_resolution.rs`, `inferred_host_input_digest`, `module_graph.rs`.

<a id="adr-0383"></a>
## ADR 0383 — A retained IR build is proven equal to a fresh one
`SOLID_CHECKER_IR_SHADOW` (`solid-reactive-ir/src/shadow.rs`) rebuilds fresh after every incremental build and compares `Program` table by table. `panic` names the first differing table and row (process tests, `.cargo/config.toml`); any other value logs, drops the caches and serves the fresh program (daemon, opt-in: one fresh build per edit). The cache domain holds `RuleOptions` and `runtime_symbol_redirects`, and one predicate decides "TypeScript unchanged" (`cache::typescript_unchanged`); both stages' reuse had ignored them (`incremental_staleness_process.rs`). `scripts/ir-edit-fuzz.mjs` (random edits, daemon against one-shot) and `scripts/ir-read-lint.mjs` (a ratchet on raw cross-file reads) gate `make verify`.

<a id="adr-0384"></a>
## ADR 0384 — The IR's project-wide facts are folds of per-file contributions
`solid-reactive-ir/src/base`: a path is a `FileId`; a base index is `key -> [(FileId, values)]`, folded from `fn(&FileView, &BuildConstants)` contributions that by type read one file. An update replaces changed files' contributions and returns the exact Δ of keys. Indexes: references, callees, module edges, members, hazards, definer keys, items; read by the eval, dispatch, reflective-definition and prototype-write vetoes, assigned member names, `MemberStability`, `ModuleCensus` and the namespace census. A build reads a fresh fold. Shadow mode updates the previous build's indexes and compares them with the refold: any difference is a shadow mismatch.

<a id="adr-0385"></a>
## ADR 0385 — Derived answers are memos with recorded reads
`solid-reactive-ir/src/base/memo`: a memo table maps a key to a value computed through a `Ctx`, its code's only way to read the build: a file section (source, compiler map, entity rows; the key's own file is free), an L1 index key, a file's slots, a build constant, another memo. Reads are kept as `QueryId`s with the answer's fingerprint (a memo's: the revision it last changed in) plus a reverse map. Changed inputs (section fingerprints, the store's index Δ) mark readers dirty and theirs maybe-dirty; an answer is reused, verified through its memo reads in recorded order, or recomputed, and an equal recomputed value stops propagation. Keys and values are `Canonical` (no hash containers). Only context-free answers are memos, so reads form a DAG; a cycle answers the table's fail-closed value, is reported and empties the store. `SOLID_CHECKER_IR_VERIFY_REUSED` recomputes each reused answer and names the differing table and key.

<a id="adr-0386"></a>
## ADR 0386 — Member resolution is memoized; an unported read is a probe
`object_members/memo.rs`, `assigned.rs`: object-member targets, spread handlers and literal receivers are one memo per file slot, `ReceiverClosed` one per receiver (owner, literal, declaration, symbol), `PrototypeOwnerAt` one per `R.prototype` write, the assigned-member census one project memo. The lookup's `Targets`, `SpreadTargets`, literal-receiver set and `AssignedMembers` assemble them in file order (a path's later slot still overwrites an earlier one). A receiver's uses come from the base `symbol_uses` index plus TypeScript's references of its symbols, the host-prototype proof from the `spellings` index, never a scan of every file. Unported tables are read through a **probe** (`base/memo/probes.rs`): a keyed question the lookup answers from its table, recorded as an input with the answer's fingerprint; the store re-asks every recorded probe in `begin`, so a moved answer dirties exactly its readers. Probes may not read memos.

<a id="adr-0387"></a>
## ADR 0387 — Project-wide answers are memos; what is not yet narrow reads the whole program
`solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes/project_memos.rs`: the effect-apply tables, exported primitive consts, withheld initialization mentions, parameter-completion calls, never-returning calls, the exempt store-key census and the any-file vetoes (intrinsics, string code, tuple iteration, the `bind` patch, objects assigned into, router parameters) are memo tables (ids 32+). Never-returning calls are per file, reading only that file's source and `Signatures` section (each entity's location and whether its resolved call never returns or asserts). The rest read the lookup via `Ctx::project`, recorded as `Input::Project`, which moves on any file change, any TypeScript table not reported unchanged (`cache::typescript_unchanged`), or a closed-program flip; such answers are reused only across builds where nothing moved, narrowing as their tables port. Fixed points (parameter completion) and the recursive initialization proof stay one whole-program answer each (cone recomputation is S7). Answers are sorted vectors; the lookup keeps per-build hash views. Premise: module-resolution attestations change only with a source edit or a cache-domain change.

<a id="adr-0388"></a>
## ADR 0388 — The component census is memos
`solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes/census.rs` (ids 64+): memos (ADR 0385) for component identity (per file, and per function whose direct JSX return needs its references), call-graph entries per function, and per file tag renders, prop render sites, compound holders' values, lazy `default` candidates and witnesses, and result-access forwarding. An identity reads its own file, the reference index's key of its binding symbol, and the files holding those references, calls or tags with their per-file answers, so an edit elsewhere leaves it unrun. Calls and tags come from the L1 callee index, keyed by the symbol a call's callee resolves to (a build with runtime redirects corrects it via the redirected calls). Initialization reads ADR 0387's memo, the module census and `eval` ADR 0386's probes; other unported tables (functions by symbol, primitives, TypeScript symbols and descriptors, route values, namespace hand-outs, the `Object.assign` and prototype-write vetoes) are adapter memos over `Ctx::project`. The entity section also covers resolved-call declarations and references. A path's slots are one source (the join checks each), so memos read its first slot.

<a id="adr-0389"></a>
## ADR 0389 — The client patches its symbol closure from exact per-file changes
The per-file passes (ADR 0398) answer `referenceChangesExact`: every id whose `symbols` answer may differ from the previous table's (a moved merged row, a moved reference bucket's holders, every scratch-answered id); inexact for a first table, a restarted merge, a rebuilt reference index, the sequential run. The client patches its closure (`typefacts/src/session/closure.rs`), asking changed and newly reached ids and references of tier ids that changed or entered; a `ClosureIndex` of counts (seeds, alias in-edges, roots per path, one-hop aliases) keeps the cost to the changed paths. The client asks every retained analysis for them. `TYPEFACTS_VERIFY_SYMBOL_PATCH=1` compares each patch with a full closure (the IR edit fuzz sets it). The TypeScript index patch takes an added or removed import alias; the balanced idle policy keeps those indexes.

<a id="adr-0390"></a>
## ADR 0390 — Returned-callback uses are a memo; the digest keeps its source hashes
`solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes/returned_uses.rs` (table 128): the uses of the function a factory call (`createReaction`, `lazy`, `lazyModule`, `mapArray`, `repeat`) returns are one memo per call, as `Call`, `Argument(i)`, `Preload` and `Jsx` uses. It reads the call's file, the exact symbols holding the result (bindings it initializes, then bindings initialized from those, in any file) and, per symbol, two L1 keys: TypeFacts' references and the new `use_spans` index (spans of callees, arguments, tags, `preload` objects and alias initializers whose entity is the symbol). Only files holding such a span are read. What a use inherits (the callback primitive receiving it, that callback's owner) depends on non-memo tables, so the consumer assembles it (`callback_owners.rs`) into a per-build cache.

<a id="adr-0391"></a>
## ADR 0391 — The invocation-flow answers are memos
`solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role/flow_memos.rs` (ids 96+): each file's ADR 0319 answers and event-only census are memos, each literal computed from an empty context. Per file, not per literal: on solid-groove 29k per-literal entries cost 150 ms of bookkeeping and lock contention, the walks 25 ms. The walk runs over a world trait: the lookup's for a query nested in another computation, else the memo context, where each file the walk holds is a recorded read, component identity is the census's per-file memo, the primitive at a call the census adapter, `eval` the hazard probe, a tag's component its entity row and the `FunctionForSymbol` probe, a primitive slot the call's signature section. Call-site roles, project callees at a parameter, window-inheriting callbacks, member tags' declared methods and absent-member vetoes read `Ctx::project`. A budget-bound walk records what it read. The lookup assembles them.

<a id="adr-0392"></a>
## ADR 0392 — The returned-callback and returned-source digest sections bind answers
`solid-reactive-ir/src/indexes/returned_invocations.rs` (tables 160, 161). The binding-chain walk (`returned_primitive_invocation`) is a memo per file reading the resolved binding through a probe (`BindingNameAt`, asked only where the file has an entity row or reference at the span), that binding's file and `CallPrimitives`. Returned sources (ADR 0222, 0287) are a memo per file through `Ctx::project`. `returned-callbacks` digests each positive answer a consumer can read (an execution or owner for an argument at the call's count) and each factory call's sites; `returned-source-inputs` each positive answer and what a fragment reads of the creating call (`returned_source_facts`). An inline factory callback with a site in another file is classified in that file's context, so it brings back every participating file's source hash.

<a id="adr-0393"></a>
## ADR 0393 — Project passes solve on kept state and publish per key
`solid-reactive-ir/src/base/fixpoint`: three solvers keep nodes, ordered definitions and values between builds under stable keys (`NodeKey`, `DenseIndex`; slot order never reaches an answer): a keyed fold of per-file rows in path rank (K1), a monotone least fixed point re-solved on the changed definitions' forward cone (K2), a Jacobi loop replayed from per-round trajectories (K3, lemma 4′). An ordered fold (K4), unused in production, was removed in the 2026-10-25 audit. A cone over a quarter of the nodes, reordered surviving files, a K3 stopped at its cap and a poisoned store solve fresh. Output is published per key (`Input::Derived`, `memo/derived.rs`): an equal value keeps its revision, a moved key marks its memo readers after `begin`; reading before the pass published fails closed. Shadow and verify-reused modes compare each kept solve with a fresh one, naming the first differing key. Proof of use: the resolver's handoff join (`Resolver::join_handoffs`, derived table and solver 224).

<a id="adr-0394"></a>
## ADR 0394 — The retained daemon verifies its answers while idle
`daemon/idle_shadow.rs`. After a materialized answer and 300 ms idle, a background-priority thread opens a fresh state from the same request, answers the same check, and compares findings JSON, status and notice byte for byte and the IR `Program` table by table (ADR 0383's `first_difference`). Builds that saw different inputs (tracked source and config hashes, package inputs) are inconclusive, never a mismatch. A request cancels the check (a flag read between the fresh answer's phases); it restarts after the next debounce. It runs only while available memory (Linux `MemAvailable`; macOS free, inactive and speculative pages, purgeable included) is at least the daemon's resident size plus `SOLID_CHECKER_IDLE_SHADOW_MIN_FREE_MB` (default 1024), whatever the daemon's own limit; meanwhile the watchdog exempts the check from that limit, and once room runs out abandons it first. With one connection per request and no push channel, the next request delivers a mismatch: logged (stderr, `<socket>.shadow.log`, first differing table and row), the whole state dropped (memos, indexes, per-file caches, producer) and the answer opened from scratch, response header `recovered`. Accepted: the stale answer was already served. Off by default, like oxlint's language server (each check costs about a cold check's CPU); `SOLID_CHECKER_IDLE_SHADOW=1` enables it, `panic` ends the daemon (tests, CI); off while the inline shadow is on. Debug builds have `_FAULT` and `_DELAY_MS` hooks. Cost: a transient second build (+0.8 GB on solid-groove, +1.4 GB on app-game since ADR 0399), none on the next edit once it ends; an edit arriving mid-check measured +10% on app-game.

<a id="adr-0395"></a>
## ADR 0395 — Interprocedural summaries are canonical sets
`SummaryReads` (`interproc.rs`) kept or dropped rows by arrival: a later dominating row sat beside the weaker earlier one, so the rows, and the `origin`, `origin_context`, `declaration`, `kind` and structured-return label ties picked, depended on worklist order. Now each key `(display, origin, declaration, transported)` holds the rows no other dominates, under the product order on `local`, `conditional` and a rank (hops from the seed, origin, declaration, display, symbol, kind, context, owner, undecided). A product order, not "flags, then a tie-break": every propagation transfer is monotone per component, so a dropped row only yields rows the survivor's yield dominates, and the fixed point is order-free. Rows sit in canonical order, so emission's stable sort and structured returns' first row read the canonical first row; delta propagation reads an arrival log.

<a id="adr-0396"></a>
## ADR 0396 — The accessor-provenance resolver is built from per-file memos on kept folds
`solid-reactive-ir/src/accessor_provenance/resolver_memos.rs` (tables 240–246, derived tables 240–247, state 240). The resolver's walks run over a world trait (`resolver_world.rs`): the lookup, or the memo context, which records each answer. Its stages are memos per file (bindings, parameter inputs, receiver census, origins with blocked contexts) and per context (object contexts); their rows fold in path order on kept K1 folds, whose output the next stage reads per key: binding locations, direct uses, providers, the handoff join's `HandoffUses` (K2, ADR 0393). The pass publishes the accessors, source kinds and each file's lookup answers (binding resolutions, call primitives, context targets, iteration receivers), so a reader moves with its keys; a parameter channel's Type Facts half and a vetoed dependency member are adapters. A duplicated path or foreign symbol names builds fresh; shadow and verify-reused modes compare both builds.

<a id="adr-0397"></a>
## ADR 0397 — A stage chunk records what it read; the cross-file digests are gone
`solid-reactive-ir/src/base/reads.rs`: a per-file stage chunk (discovery, reachability, local accesses, graph, results, owner fragments) records its cross-file reads while computing: memo answers read outside any memo (table, query, revision it last changed in) and whole-table lookup views (`Section`: components, member and spread targets, literal receivers, flow answers, event literals, never-returning calls, returned sources and callback sites, dispatch and reflective hazards, reflective prototype writes, imported primitive consts), fingerprinted from the value once per build. A per-build lookup cache (`Recorded`) and a parallel helper's workers hand their reads to the result's reader, which records what its filler did. A chunk is reused while its file is unchanged (`unchanged_paths`) and every read answers as recorded. Deleted: `cross_file_proof_digest`, `member_resolution_digest`, the exempt-store census memo, `transport_identity_matches`, `retained_source_paths`, `changed_source_symbols`; result chunks record transported-call, source-origin and accessor reads (`interproc/transport_reads.rs`), graph chunks the accessor entries they embed (closing ADR 0383's latent key), and local accesses diff source discovery's tables by symbol. Kept: `call_role_syntax` (result chunks read other files' syntax outside the lookup), the same reason the leaf-owner and static-API passes stay unchunked. Removed in the 2026-10-25 audit: the late aggregates' whole-stage reuse (saved under 5 ms) and discovery's chunk reuse (saved under 9 ms; its unchanged-file identity stays). Reachability survives the balanced idle policy. `SOLID_CHECKER_IR_READ_MISSES=1` names each rerun's first moved read.

<a id="adr-0398"></a>
## ADR 0398 — Per-file checkers with exact ids are the producer's only mode
A file's facts are a pass: its own fresh checker checks the file first, then answers its demands, async facts and reference scan, and computes the rows of every id they name; the program's other non-declaration files get a reference pass. Answers reading every file (reference spaces, the default-library write census, structural-accessor descriptor suppression) are deferred and completed from the merged passes. Ids: binder (0366) and set (0371) keys, else the exact derived key (root and canonical type context, `derived_key.rs`); no key, no id (an absent fact), never a counter. A pass is reused while its read set (the forward closure over recorded resolutions of the file and every global contributor) is unchanged path by path in `Text`, `Exports` and `Resolutions` (every resolution record of the file and the files its edges reach), with `Options`, `Ambient` (program order), its census answers and its demands; the rest run on a pool (`SOLID_TYPEFACTS_PER_FILE_WORKERS`). This holds across a rebuilt chain (an import edit, a deleted or new file, a rebase): a kept read set's members kept their edges, so it covers the same paths (recomputed and compared); a newly imported file joins only read sets reaching the edited file. If two passes mint one id for two keys, the table is the sequential shared-identity run's. Supersedes ADR 0367's check-first and ADR 0369; the selection flags are gone (no escape hatch). Owners: `apps/solid-typefacts/src/per_file.rs`, `passes.rs`, `derived_key.rs`, `identity.rs`, `typefacts/src/session.rs`.

<a id="adr-0399"></a>
## ADR 0399 — The idle check's fresh build ends its producer after the facts
The fresh state (ADR 0394) closes its producer once the facts and resolution witness are answered: package inputs, analysis and emission read only those, and a closed session fails rather than relaunching, so a later exchange is inconclusive, never a verdict. The fresh state is dropped before the comparison, which serializes one row pair at a time. Daemon RSS peak, with the slice's sharing commits: app-game 4.5 to 3.6 GB, solid-groove 2.3 to 1.9 GB. Owners: `daemon.rs` (`AfterFacts`), `daemon/idle_shadow.rs`, `shadow.rs` (`compare_tables`).

<a id="adr-0400"></a>
## ADR 0400 — The daemon's residue per edit
Outside the reactive IR and producer, an app-game body edit cost resync 19 ms, package inputs 23, solve 17, emit+cache 12, and a 5.5 MB answer per linted file; now 10, 13, 6, 10 (release, interleaved against the parent, loaded machine). Each fix is exact: (1) `snapshot_of` scanned a location's file to place it; it now computes each file's line starts once per answer, O(log n) (keeping line starts per source `Arc` was removed: saved under 3 ms in the 2026-10-25 audit, groove 7 ms faster without). (2) The package boundary read each import's manifest and scanned every file per import; now one read per manifest and a set of held files. (3) The resolution witness drops absent entries under an absent ancestor (whose entry fails first) and resolves a directory entry's canonical path as its parent's, once per check; tracked sources' and directories' stamps are read on six workers. (4) The inference closure's digests and imported manifests' hashes were kept by file stamp; removed (saved under 6 ms in the audit), kept again by ADR 0600. (5) `--only-file <abs path>` (`--format json`) returns only findings that can matter to that file: project-scoped, pathless, primary or related location equal after lexical normalization, or a path it cannot settle (relative). Positions are recorded as the report is written (`FindingIndex`); all else, kept findings' bytes included, is the full report's. The ESLint adapter sends it for the native checker only and projects per file as before.
Accepted: resync keeps a floor of about 10 ms on app-game (6,000 witness stats per request, cache hits included) until an event watcher can prove what did not change; the boundary still re-derives per request (about 6 ms).

<a id="adr-0401"></a>
## ADR 0401 — The invocation-flow walks read what they ask
`execution_role/flow_memos.rs`, `indexes/callee_world.rs`. Of ADR 0391's whole-program questions, a project callee at a parameter and a member tag's declared method resolve over a `CalleeWorld` (the lookup, or the memo context: the call file's member targets, literal receivers and entity rows, the `FunctionForSymbol`, `BindingAt`, `ClassFor` and `PathFile` probes, the files they name, the assigned-member memo; the lookup under runtime redirects). An absent member reads that memo and a `DefinedReflectively` probe; a window-inheriting callback the dialect and the effect-apply memo. A site role is a memo per file, call and the walk's sorted `classifying` context (table 256), the last on `Ctx::project`. A body edit now recomputes 1 flow and 1 event answer (app-game: was 879 and 715; groove: 466, 419) and reruns every site role (5,278; 2,199): IR 1,373 to 1,275 ms on app-game, groove unchanged, one-shot +1-3%. ADR 0387's premise holds: the daemon reopens its state when the resolution witness fails or a tracked directory changes, and an import edit (ADR 0398) is a source edit.

<a id="adr-0402"></a>
## ADR 0402 — Transport and the member-invocation refresh are kept between builds
`solid-reactive-ir/src/accessor_provenance/transport_memos.rs` (table 384), `interproc/refresh.rs` (state 384). Transport: each file's call answers are a memo over the resolver's memo world (ADR 0396), which serves post-construction queries with stage 4's blocked contexts (derived 384). A row keeps targets needing a summary node; the graph's function set picks them at the fold, so the memo reads nothing the stage computes. Refresh (ADR 0320): each file's invocation contexts, unmodeled uses and forwarding edges are kept with its recorded reads and every callee answer they read, re-asked before reuse (the function table and a callee's syntax are not recorded reads). Asked every build: a consumer's tracked-or-fresh proof (never cached) and each edge's callee among the nodes. The 8-round forwarding loop runs on the K3 solver (ADR 0393), truncation replayed. The node index's in-place patch was removed (saved under 5 ms in the 2026-10-25 audit). A path listed twice runs fresh; shadow and verify-reused modes compare with a fresh build, poisoning on a difference. Import edit: refresh 246 to 9 ms (app-game), transport 90 to 3.

<a id="adr-0403"></a>
## ADR 0403 — Source discovery's post-passes keep their rows
`solid-reactive-ir/src/source_discovery/post_passes.rs`, `post_rows.rs`, `trace.rs` (solver slot 320). Each post-pass is rows plus a fold. A file's rows (per TypeScript path for typed sources, per file for the rest, components included) are kept while the file is in the build's `unchanged_paths`, its memo and section reads hold, and every other answer it recorded still holds (`trace::Dep`: another file's facts, references, declarations, entity sites, the tuple veto, an earlier pass's table at a key; a symbol's function and a binding resolution are the lookup's hand-out reads, ADR 0408). Each distinct answer is asked once per build; only its readers recompute. Folds run in file order under each pass's rule; groupings are kept K1 folds (ADR 0393). Alias rounds are solved from their sources over kept candidate indexes: in a Jacobi round only rows holding a key the previous round added can add, and pass 8's Gauss-Seidel sweeps fire each row at its earliest (sweep, position). Pass 10's forwarded-props loop replays, its unresolved sets depending on round order; no K2 cone is used there. Pass 4's fallback for an identifier return without entity takes the least-located same-name declaration, not a `HashMap` hit; no fixture or corpus project reaches it. Duplicated paths, dependency-source builds and a moved domain (closed program, caller proof, redirects) recompute every row. Shadow and verify-reused modes compare with a run on empty state.

<a id="adr-0404"></a>
## ADR 0404 — The owner graph is patched per file
`solid-reactive-ir/src/owners/owner_state.rs`, `owner_emission.rs`: the owner stage keeps fragments, resolved edges, symbol winners, final contexts and each file's emission between builds, keyed by `(interned path, span)`. A recomputed fragment equal to the kept one changes nothing downstream; else its own edges are re-resolved, and every file's only if its shape (node spans, symbols, generator flags) moved. Context bits are solved by the plain worklist in file order on the patched graph (the cone solve on two kept `Monotone` solvers was removed: saved under 5 ms in the 2026-10-25 audit). A node whose context moved dirties its file; a file's emission also re-runs when the closed provider census or an `onSettled` callback's file moved, its lookup reads folding into the fragment's proof. Fragment reads now merge once (`Reads::extend_all`): per-file `extend` re-sorted the whole set each time (9 of 14 ms on app-game).

<a id="adr-0405"></a>
## ADR 0405 — Final ordering is cheap because its two whole-program answers are chunked and the memo sweep is incremental
The gate recomputed the two whole-program apply memos (`effect_apply_calls`, `effect_apply_regions`) every build. `effect_execution.rs` now keeps in the retained store (`SemanticLookup::with_kept_state`, solver ids 448 and 449) a per-file chunk with its recorded reads (ADR 0397), its own file's facts included (another file can re-point its rows): the file's calls carrying a defer option and the callbacks it supplies and may escape to. The memo folds them (a callback's region needs a supplying site anywhere; an escape only adds `Unknown`). Defer calls' proofs stay computed fresh (they read other files' bodies, which a chunk would not see). A one-shot build, a probe and a path held by two slots keep nothing. The memo store's `end` scanned all inputs (250k) to release unread ones and walked its live set in ordered maps; it now visits only inputs that lost a reader or were allocated since the last end (`Graph::unread`), with hash containers for graph and live set. Rows keep production order and the stable sort by location (`into_program`); per-path run assembly was not built (it would save under 1 ms).

<a id="adr-0406"></a>
## ADR 0406 — The memo begin and the read sets cost what changed
`base/memo/build.rs`, `base/reads.rs`. (1) A file's TypeFacts fingerprint parts are kept with the identity of the rows each digests: entities by address and length (sound since the store keeps the last build's table, so every named allocation stays alive and immutable), entity-symbol rows by `Arc` (`PathSymbols`, changed only via `make_mut`, so the store's clone forces a copy). Same rows reuse the digest; anything else, full table replaces included, digests again. The producer's change list is not read, so a path it omits is still seen (`bookkeeping_process`). References are still digested every build, being assembled per build from the whole symbol table. Shadow and verify-reused modes re-digest each reused part and compare. (2) A read set is sorted runs shared by `Arc` plus a tail of at most 64; adding a set adds its runs by pointer, so the stage reads every result chunk carries are neither copied nor sorted per chunk; past 16 runs the smaller half merge. A check may ask a twice-held read twice. (3) `SymbolUses`, `Spellings` and `GlobalWritten` are no longer probes: the store keeps their L1 indexes, whose Δ marks exactly the moved keys. Other probes are re-asked: their answers depend on other files' rows, and their cost is mostly building tables the build needs anyway. `SOLID_CHECKER_IR_BOOKKEEPING_LOG` logs these costs (begin phases then run on one thread, so times are CPU).

<a id="adr-0407"></a>
## ADR 0407 — The interprocedural fixed points stay whole; graph chunks re-ask the function table
`interproc/reads.rs`, `structured_returns.rs`, `graph.rs`. The structured-return Jacobi loop (up to nodes+1 rounds over every node) lost its reader when contract emission went: `StructuredReturnUnresolved` reads only the property's file, the import's symbol chain and the declaring files' syntax. It is deleted, so no K3 replay is needed. The parameter-member obligation asks its pure conjuncts cheapest first. `fixedPointsMs` times each pass: on an app-game import edit, forwarding, calls, dispatch, factory and returned propagation take 2.3 ms together (seeds: returned 9, transported 3.6), so their cone solve (S7 design §5) is not built. A body edit keeping the late inputs skips the stage. A graph chunk records which function a callee symbol names and each ADR 0285 forward decision (`GraphCalleeRead`, removed by ADR 0408), reused only while each answers so: a callee binding written in another file had left a forward stale.

<a id="adr-0408"></a>
## ADR 0408 — What the lookup hands a chunk of another file is a recorded read
`indexes/hand_outs.rs` (tables 640-643), `base/reads.rs`, `cache.rs`. Lookup readers such as the role machine reached other files via `function_for_symbol`, `binding_at_reference`, `class_for_symbol`, `file_by_path`, `symbol_references`, callers, or a returned use's or binding's file, and read their syntax, compiler map and entity rows unrecorded. Each hand-out now records its answer (a symbol's `functions_by_symbol` bucket, the asker's binding rows, else a probe memo; absences too) and the handed file's four section fingerprints, deduplicated per frame, asked once per build. `call_role_syntax` (ADR 0397) is deleted. Two aggregations drop these reads: stage reads a result chunk inherits (one proof per build, checked once), and the namespace census and resolver builds. `same_reachability_ast` compares all AST tables, not twelve: a same-length `&&` to `||` in a callee kept a stale role. Route component values are a section. Removed: `GraphCalleeRead`, the refresh rows' callee re-asks. Re-parses (completion covers, `synchronously_unconditional_calls`) touch only a chunk's own or handed-out file; the rest run in memos or unchunked passes.

<a id="adr-0409"></a>
## ADR 0409 — The memo store's hot path is sharded and records inputs without fingerprints
`base/memo.rs`, `base/memo/build.rs`, `base/memo/locks.rs`. Input reads record no fingerprint (`begin` and `invalidate` push input changes; the value was never compared), once per entry. Reverse edges apply in a batch (`Graph::flush`) before `mark` and `end`, their only readers, skipped by `mark` when no changed input is interned. Tables and polled keys are 16 key-hash shards (the query id picks the shard), on the heap: the idle check's small stack overflowed. Locks are parking_lot's. A first build of the generated 1,000-file corpus waited on locks longer than it worked (14 cores); first IR 1.68 to 1.36 of the main merge base (CI gate 1.25). The rest of the cold gap is per-entry memo cost and kept solvers, not locks.

<a id="adr-0410"></a>
## ADR 0410 — The producer's per-edit work follows the edit
`apps/solid-typefacts`: `identity.rs` (`ExportScan`), `completion.rs`, `passes.rs`, `per_file.rs`, `inputs.rs`. (1) Exported identities are kept per module with each root's lasting stand-in (`binder_key::lasting_root`: a binder symbol owning its declarations, or the least binder symbol merging into a merge clone; one symbol per stand-in in every checker of a chain). A parse-reusing update rescans only the edited module and modules reaching it; otherwise, and after a rebuilt chain, the pass workers scan as before. (2) A pass prepares its entity rows and records which rows deferred answers (spaces, library writes, structural suppression) change; these are re-asked only if the run's output changed, a changed output references an id it asks, or a symbol it reads changed structural standing. (3) Parse-reusing updates keep the read-set graph, global contributors and census, comparing only replaced files and paths with moved export digests. (4) A compact demand group keeps its one expansion; kept passes hold it, compared by identity. (5) After a rebuilt chain, workers compute text digests; read sets compare as bitsets. `SOLID_TYPEFACTS_PER_FILE_VERIFY_REUSE` checks kept answers against recomputation. App-game: body edit update 35 to 3 ms, analyze 104 to 18; import edit update stays about 200 ms (re-parse: tsrs reuses a file in place only with the same imports, and handing parsed files to a new chain breaks per-chain file regions).

<a id="adr-0411"></a>
## ADR 0411 — A cold build's memo bookkeeping is accepted for incremental edits
`base/memo.rs`, `base/memo/derived.rs`, `base/memo/build.rs`, `base/memo/serial_digest.rs`, `base/index.rs`, `base/reads.rs`, `pipeline.rs`. Cuts: installs intern inputs in 16 hash shards and queue reads per table shard without the graph lock; the graph's 16 shards take the queues and apply reverse edges (sorted runs, readers a sorted `Vec`) on analysis workers at `mark` and `end`; `end` skips the live walk when every entry was asked, walking memo reads only (an input read's fingerprint is `u64::MAX`); derived tables are 16 key shards; index folds group per file on workers, and a first build folds its four eager indexes side by side; the Signatures section digests a tagged binary serialization (field names, variant indices, lengths), not JSON; read-set merges sort adaptively. `SOLID_CHECKER_IR_WORKERS=<n>` caps analysis workers to mimic a smaller runner. Accepted: on a generated 1,000-file corpus first IR stays 1.19x (14 cores) to 1.35x (four workers) of a memo-free build (per-entry bookkeeping, kept solvers); on real projects edits run 3-9x faster and first IR faster (app-game body edit 3,930 to 457 ms, first IR 1,636 to 837 ms).

<a id="adr-0412"></a>
## ADR 0412 — An edit reruns the chunks whose reads moved; the balanced policy keeps them
`interproc/reads.rs`, `transport_reads.rs`, `indexes/components.rs`, `census.rs` (`component_spans`, 91), `hand_outs.rs`, `runtime_configuration.rs`, `cache.rs`. (1) A result chunk's proof is its own reads, the result view's dependencies, and each stage-table entry it asks: transport, the resolver's origins and transported reads, discovery's accessors and source kinds (only via `AccessorsRead`, `SourceKindsRead`). It no longer includes what building the resolver, transport, discovery and refresh read, or a recomputed graph chunk's reads: one binding moved in the edited file reran all 1,611. Narrowing exposed unrecorded reads: `hazard_present` (nested member dispatch's eval hazard) is now a probe answer; `callee_symbols`' alias-set filter reads the callee symbol's declarations and files. Instrumented over coverage, process tests, app-game and groove, no result chunk reaches another whole-program lookup table. (2) One file's component identities read its `component_spans`, not the census (an import edit reran 1,406 local-access and 1,444 owner chunks). (3) The exported-primitive-consts section and memo are deleted: the imported constant's file is a `binding_at_reference` hand-out. (4) The runtime-configuration scan keeps each asked symbol's identity and alias chain, re-asking only chains that read a moved row (`FactTable::symbols_with_moved_declarations`, from index leaves two tables do not share): 42 to 3 ms. (5) Timings: `namespaceHandOuts` held the memo begin (census 5 ms), `reusePlan` the warm-up (`memoWarm`, 33 ms; plan under 1 ms). (6) `Balanced` keeps graph and result chunks (about 95 ms per app-game edit for about 50 MB; under about 19 GB of RAM the daemon exceeds the 15% limit anyway). Open: a prefix or import edit renumbers local symbol ids, moving their `hand_out_function_bucket` reads (110 graph chunks on app-game).

<a id="adr-0413"></a>
## ADR 0413 — The static prepass's file rules are per-file chunks, and their reads are per-file
`static_rules.rs`, `late_chunks.rs` (new), `pipeline.rs`, `cache.rs`, `timings.rs`, and the per-file questions of `base/memo/build.rs`, `base/reads.rs`, `execution_role/flow_memos.rs`, `indexes.rs`, `indexes/{completion,hand_outs,project_memos}.rs`, `interproc/reads.rs`, `object_members.rs`. The prepass's three file rules (props destructure, primitive value references, primitive result methods) walked every file each build. Each file's output is now a chunk (`late_chunks::FileChunks`), reused while the file is unchanged (`unchanged_paths`, source hash, compiler semantics) and its props-discovery answers (`DiscoveryRead`) and lookup reads (`ReadProof`) hold; kept chunks replay reads, moved ones rerun. Member targets, literal receivers and never-returning calls are asked of their file's memo; flow site roles and parameter-completion slots are per-file memos (`project_file_recorded`), so an edit moves no whole-table section for every chunk. The after-await rule and the leaf, owner, API and server passes stay whole-program. `reactiveIrReuse.ownerMs` now emits the owner stage's four sub-timers, kept but unemitted before.
Static prepass: app-game 106 to 39 ms, groove 37 to 15.

<a id="adr-0414"></a>
## ADR 0414 — The memo begin's index builds and the warm-up's component lane use the analysis pool
`indexes.rs` (`declaration_symbols`, the warm-up lanes), `indexes/callees.rs` (`bindings_by_reference`), `indexes/components.rs` (`functions_by_symbol`). (1) Whole-program indexes the first probe or warm-up lane touches build on the analysis pool with the serial builds' answers: bindings by reference per file, then per path shard (one path per shard keeps each key's first writer), then completed per path; declaration symbols per path run, a path's second run merged in table order; functions by symbol per file, merged in file order. (2) The warm-up's components lane, last to finish, takes the whole pool once others end. (3) Not built then: partial re-attestation of import identities. An edited file's ambient module or reference directive can change another file's resolution, so an unchanged importer is exact only with the producer's word on its resolution inputs (open-work section 2); ADR 0419 takes that word for in-place updates.

<a id="adr-0415"></a>
## ADR 0415 — A source file's local ids survive an insert above them
`apps/solid-typefacts/src/binder_key.rs` (`LocalChains`, the `Local` key), `identity.rs`, `derived_key.rs`. An unexported binder symbol whose minimum declaration is in a non-`.d.ts` file is keyed `Local(path, chain, name)`, not by `Decl`'s name span. A chain hashes the declaration's position (enclosing named scope's position, kind, name text, rank among that scope's declarations of that kind and text) and its source text. Positions are unique per file (induction on nesting), so chains are; only `Local` hash input starts with a NUL. A key survives an edit only while its declaration's text and position hold: an earlier inserted declaration of the same kind and text shifts later ranks, and the text stops a displaced id naming another declaration (`an_inserted_same_named_declaration_hands_out_no_id`, `identical_declarations_in_one_scope_get_distinct_ids`). Re-resolution walks the file; `symbol_for` still requires the resolved symbol's key to be the requested one. A `.d.ts`, a declaration no scope scan reaches (a JSDoc tag), a synthesized position, or declarations sharing a name span keep `Decl`. An edit inside a declaration moves its own and enclosing ids, not nested ones (`an_edit_inside_a_declaration_moves_only_its_own_id`). The IR is unchanged: chunks reading unmoved ids hold.
The cost is in the IR: a moved id switches `function_for_symbol` bucket (`hand_outs.rs`: 1024 buckets by a hash of the id string), so a body edit in an unexported declaration reruns both buckets' readers (app-game: 28 to 77 graph chunks). 16384 buckets restore them (3.5 chunks) but add 13 to 20 ms to mid-file and import edits; not taken (open-work, section 4). Not kept: a per-path slice of `initialization_rejected` cut groove's reachability reruns from 518 to 3 without changing graph, result or wall time, and added as many slice memos.
App-game mid-file edit 616 to 557 ms, import 899 to 796; IR equals an append's; the remaining gap over an append is the TypeFacts exchange (65 against 16 ms: every moved span's answer changes), open.

<a id="adr-0416"></a>
## ADR 0416 — The changed-reference scan answers from a reverse index of the served canonical ids
`apps/solid-typefacts/src/passes.rs` (`Merged::canonical_index`, `ids_with_canonical`, `reindex`), `per_file.rs` (`per_file_symbol_changes`). `Merged` keeps a reverse map (canonical id to served ids naming it), built by the first query after a from-scratch merge (`None` until then; a full merge pays nothing) and moved by `Merged::update` for exactly the ids whose served row changed: the changed-rows report's candidates (old and new rows per changed position). Other served rows keep providers and output, so their canonical id. Under `SOLID_TYPEFACTS_PER_FILE_VERIFY_REUSE` and in every test build each query is checked against the scan (`ids_with_canonical_scan`); a mismatch is a reuse mismatch and serves the scan. A unit test drives random incremental and from-scratch merges with queries between.
The type-facts request (`daemonPhases.facts.typeFactsMs`): 110 to 63 ms on import, 78 to 30 on mid-file, 83 to 33 on body.

<a id="adr-0417"></a>
## ADR 0417 — Verification reads clean memos by stamp; the graph is an RwLock
`base/memo.rs`. `reads_unchanged` answers a clean dependency from its stamp, cloning neither key nor value (not in verify-reused mode). Verifications read-lock the graph; `synced_graph` and `clear` write-lock it. Saves 5 to 10 ms on a code edit.

<a id="adr-0418"></a>
## ADR 0418 — A program build asks the disk each question once
`apps/solid-typefacts/src/overlay.rs` (`OverlayFs::build_scope`, `Lookups`), `project.rs` (`build_program`, `update_inner`, `rebase_if_due`). Program builds repeat disk questions: importers of `./util` probe the same candidates, bare specifiers walk the same `node_modules` ancestors (an app-game import edit's chain rebuild: 66,400 lookups, 23,500 distinct). Within a build the overlay now keeps its base's answers (`file_exists`, `directory_exists`, `stat`, `realpath`, `get_accessible_entries`, misses too) in sharded maps owned by a `BuildScope`, opened around a configuration parse plus program build, an update candidate (`prepare_update` or `build_program`) or a rebase, and dropped when the build returns. Overlay state is read first, so edited or deleted files never reach them; a duplicate starts empty; queries outside a build (module graph root positions) hit disk as before. TypeScript assumes one file system snapshot per build; the next build asks again, so a file created or package installed between builds is seen (`a_file_created_after_a_failed_probe_is_found_by_the_next_build`, `answers_last_one_build`). Contents are not kept (a rebuild reads each of its 4,465 files once). `typefacts:update` reports `fsHits`, `fsMisses`, `fsReads` in the timings trace.
App-game import edit: program rebuild 173.5 to 149.8 ms, from the 42,900 repeats; the 23,500 distinct lookups remain (stat and lstat, about 150 ms thread time per rebuild).
Symbol ids hash the file path and `function_for_symbol` buckets by that hash, so body-edit graph reruns depend on the project path: one binary and edit reran 21 to 27 chunks on one app-game copy, 91 to 131 on another (IR 335 against 385 ms; an A/A of 9 body edits differed by 44 ms). Arms are compared on one copy, where an A/A is within 5 ms for every kind.
Not built: a cache outliving a build (open-work section 2); one `stat` answering `file_exists`, `directory_exists` and `stat` (19,000 distinct paths behind the 23,500 misses: about 1 ms); a directory listing answering probes beneath it (on a case-insensitive volume a differently-cased probe finds the file, the listing does not).

<a id="adr-0419"></a>
## ADR 0419 — An update the compiler made in place keeps the attested imports
`apps/solid-typefacts` (`UpdateOutcome::graph_kept`, `module_graph.rs` `omit_modules`), `typefacts` (protocol 85: `Response.graph_kept`, `ModuleGraphRequest.omit_modules`, `Session::last_update_graph`), `solid-facts-backend` (`lib.rs` `kept_attestation`, `patch_import_identities`; `daemon.rs` `answer_with`). The compiler replaces an edited file in place only when the new text keeps the old imports (specifier, mode, order), module-augmentation and ambient module names, reference, type, lib and check-js directives, and synthetic JSX-runtime and import-helper imports, and no package redirect group holds the file (`can_replace_file_in_program`, `can_keep_synthetic_imports`); the new program then shares the old processed files and module resolutions. The producer reports this (a `Reused` update, one changed file, no rebase after) as `graphKept` on the update's answer; other updates, a producer restart and an update re-sent after one report false.
Then the session keeps the previous generation's attested imports and asks only for the edited file's rows, without module inventory (`omitModules`): only its import positions can differ; unedited rows are those held. This requires the previous attestation (full or kept) to be from the still-running producer process and the immediately preceding generation, over the same files; otherwise every file is asked. The daemon likewise skips the `program_modules` comparison (same file set) and records the witness's edited-file imports without the inventory it holds. This is the producer's word on resolution inputs that open-work section 2 asked for; nothing is re-derived from edit text. A package manifest an unedited row names is a witnessed path, so its change rebuilds the program before a kept row is served. Shadow mode (`SOLID_CHECKER_IR_SHADOW`, the fuzz's) also asks the full attestation; a difference is reported (`kept attestation mismatch`) and the full one served.

Not built: partial attestation after an update not made in place (an import edit rebuilds the chain and asks every file).

<a id="adr-0420"></a>
## ADR 0420 — Entity rows are indexed per path, in the build caches
`solid-reactive-ir` (`indexes/entity_locations.rs`: `EntityLocations`, `PathLocations`; `EntitySymbols::locations`; `symbols.rs`; `cache.rs`), `solid-facts` and `typefacts` (`entities_shared_for_path`). Two questions find entity rows by position: the entity at an exact `(path, start, end)` (first in table order) and the smallest entity inside a span. Each was a whole-project map rebuilt each build (414,000 rows on app-game: both maps and their drop, about 40 ms of an edit, in the memo begin's world). A path's rows change only where the table delta names it (`TypeScriptChanges::entity_paths`, the premise `EntitySymbols` is patched under), so each path keeps, beside the symbol maps, its shared row slice (the table's own `Arc`, not a copy), the first row's ordinal per exact span, and ordinals by start byte. The symbol-patching loop replaces named paths; a first build, a full or inexact transition, or an alias graph the symbol patch refuses rebuilds every path. Lookups answer as the maps did (same first row; smallest contained entity, ties by start byte then row) and record no read, like the maps' initializers. Shadow mode compares the patched index with one built from the whole table, serving the fresh one on a difference. `first_entity_by_location` is gone.

<a id="adr-0421"></a>
## ADR 0421 — A retained edit's disk checks run beside the producer's update; its bookkeeping runs after the answer
`solid-facts-backend` (`daemon.rs`: `State::edit_overlapped`, `After`, `module_lines_kept`; `lib.rs`: `NativeIncrementalSession::edit_during`, `Edited`), `solid-reactive-ir` (`MemoStore::finish_deferred_end`, `IncrementalBuilder::defer_memo_end`). If every edited file keeps its module lines (lines naming `import`, `from`, `require`, `<reference` or `declare`), the witness check and closure read run during the producer's update; a stale witness swaps in a fresh session once the update lands, before analysis. Otherwise the witness is checked first: beside a program rebuild those reads slowed file-system lookups more than they saved (import edit +19 ms). Only order differs; both are exact. The memo sweep runs after the answer is written or its write fails: the daemon serves one request at a time, and a build first runs any due sweep. A sweep failure discards the state, like a failed request. Extending the witness with edited files' imports stays inline after the facts: deferred, it saw the disk after analysis and emission, not when the producer resolved; it costs about 0 ms. Its prune drops only absent entries under an absent one, never one an earlier request recorded present. The cached answer is still built before replying (the idle check records it with the answer); the idle check's fresh build defers nothing.

<a id="adr-0422"></a>
## ADR 0422 — The synthetic base-versus-head race is removed
`.github/workflows/performance.yml`, `benchmarks/compare-performance.mjs`, `benchmarks/accepted-regressions.json`, `codspeed.yml`, `benchmarks/run-codspeed-case.mjs` and `scripts/performance-workflow.test.mjs` are deleted. The workflow raced merge base against head on the generated 1,000-file chain corpus (each module imports the previous; one same-span body edit at the root, in-process) and gated on the ratio. That corpus hid main's real cost (about 150 ms per edit, while app-game took 6.9 s), missing the instant-edits stack's gains, and its worst-case dependency shape made reuse bookkeeping the whole signal: 1.75x on a four-core runner, 1.13x locally, while on real projects the stack is 1.4x to 18x faster. A gate blind to what users wait on is removed, not retuned. `make verify-performance` keeps structural invariants and ceilings where the machine is controlled.

<a id="adr-0423"></a>
## ADR 0423 — CI gates edit latency on real projects, base against head
`.github/workflows/real-projects.yml` times what users wait on: one CLI check with the retained daemon, full report, after a code, mid-file, body (where the manifest names an anchor) or import edit, on app-game (`apps/web`, 1,611 program sources), sensor-sim (`packages/frontend`) and solid-groove. `benchmarks/real-projects/manifest.json` pins each repository commit, the Solid 2 release-candidate patch, install command and lockfile digest; `benchmarks/real-projects-prepare.mjs` fetches, patches, installs, and refuses a tree whose lockfile or diff moved. Merge base and head run interleaved on one runner and project copy, each with its daemon; the gate (`benchmarks/lib/real-projects.mjs`) fails if the median paired head/base ratio exceeds 1.25 for any project and edit kind, or a head answer bypassed its retained daemon. Differing answers get a looser ratio (ADR 0451). Absolute head ceilings (app-game code 1,000 ms, every edit 2,500 ms) only report until calibrated on runners. app-game's daemon and producer peak at 3.2 GiB, so CI sets `SOLID_CHECKER_DAEMON_MAX_RSS_MB=8192`. Owners: the workflow, `benchmarks/real-projects*.mjs`, `scripts/real-projects.test.mjs`.

A setter whose source's options argument is no readable object literal may carry `ownedWrite: true`, so its write is uncertifiable, not a violation (upstream is silent; a setter found by type alone has no source in view and is uncertifiable too). <a id="adr-0428"></a>
## ADR 0428 — `reactivity` is a group key over SC1001, SC1002, SC1005 and SC1007
`rule-options.json`, the ESLint plugin and inline directives (ADR 0429) accept `reactivity` as one key toggling `strict-read-untracked`, `reactive-read-after-await`, `uncalled-accessor` and `reactive-handler-frozen` (`RULE_GROUPS`): the four defects upstream's `reactivity` reports. Expansion is two-phase (`RuleOptions::parse_with_groups`): the group sets its members, then each member the document names wins, whatever the key order. Findings name the member, never the group. ESLint cannot report a member under the group's id, so `solid-checker/reactivity` is a registered no-op rule, valid in migrated configs and disable comments; severities stay per member. SC4001 is not a member (upstream has no owner model); SC9005 and SC9012 are obligations, not rules (ADR 0440). Nor is SC1003, though upstream's `reactivity` also files destructured props as `untrackedReactive` (its test rows 5 and 63): SC1003 is a different claim (a setup-time props snapshot, decided by callers) under its own upstream key `no-destructure`, so disabling `reactivity` leaves it on. This reverses the README's sentence that one key never suppresses unrelated defect classes: upstream treats the four as one class, and a migrating project needs the key. Owners: `solid-reactive-ir/src/upstream_compat/rule_options.rs`, `solid-facts-backend/src/dialect.rs`, `packages/cli/eslint.cjs`.

<a id="adr-0429"></a>
## ADR 0429 — Inline directives are honored by the checker, under both plugin prefixes
The native checker reads `disable`, `enable`, `disable-line` and `disable-next-line` comments in two families: `eslint-*` naming `solid-checker/<name>` or upstream's `solid/<name>`, and `solid-checker-*`. ESLint's grammar (solid-facts `SuppressionCommentFact`, facts schema 76) and matching (`suppression.rs`) apply, so a comment means the same in both tools; no names means every rule; the families scan independently. Names resolve through the catalog, `RULE_ALIASES`, the group key (ADR 0428) and, in `solid-checker-*` comments, SC codes. A comment removes only site violations, after every reclassification: never an uncertifiable result, notice or project finding. An unresolved `solid-checker/*` name is project notice SC9016 (`--certify` fails on it, like SC9014); silently accepted: the pinned v0.18.1 `solid/*` names (`UPSTREAM_RULE_NAMES`; `no-single-arg-create-effect` has no alias, ADR 0432), adapter ids, retired ids (including obligations' names and codes: SC9005 and SC9012 are never suppressed, ADR 0440) and other plugins' names; other `solid/*` names are not read. ESLint applies `eslint-disable solid-checker/<key>` to the reporting key, so the adapter forwards the keys it enables for the file (`--host-suppresses`) and native skips them; a member reported through `certification`, and the no-op `reactivity` rule, are native's, and ESLint's unused-directive warning on them is accepted. Owners: `solid-facts` (`ast/suppression.rs`), `solid-facts-backend/src/suppression.rs`, `packages/cli/eslint.cjs`.

<a id="adr-0435"></a>
## ADR 0435 — The dispatch veto excludes sites that cannot deliver a handler under an owner
ADR 0356's veto is the set of its dispatch sites; a site leaves it on one of two facts. Receiver: the global `window` (its events reach only `window`'s listeners, never a JSX handler), or the global `document` where the dialect says so (`document_dispatch_reaches_no_element_handler`, read on the rc.17 bytes: `eventHandler` walks `composedPath()` from the event's target calling only the `node[$$type]` handlers `addEvent` sets on elements, and the document has none; other releases keep the veto). The receiver resolves through its `lib.dom.d.ts` `var` declaration with no project binding, never by spelling: a parameter or constant named `window` keeps the veto. Caller: a callback handed directly to a reviewed fresh-stack scheduler, or module top level outside any function, class and JSX element, has no owner and no strict-read window; a `runWithOwner(null, …)` callback has no owner but keeps its enclosing window open, so it excludes only owner and write proofs. A site in a function nothing places keeps the veto. An event-handler caller is excluded only by ADR 0437, a test runner callback by ADR 0438. Every site and its classification enter the report's `premises`. Owners: `indexes/event_delivery.rs`, `solid-dialect` (`document_dispatch_reaches_no_element_handler`), `solid-facts-backend/src/diagnostics.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`; fixtures `event-dispatch-*`.

<a id="adr-0436"></a>
## ADR 0436 — A spread with a complete declared key set and no listener key is not a handler site
A spread onto an intrinsic element no exact object resolves (ADR 0358) holds no handler and raises no SC4001 obligation when Type Facts reports the spread argument's declared-type key set complete and the dialect lowers none of its keys as a listener (`spread_key_is_event == Some(false)`; an unaudited release answers `None` and keeps the obligation; rc.13's lowercase `onclick` stays a listener). Complete: every value-carrying constituent is an object type with no index signature of any key type and a fully resolved property list; unions and intersections add members' keys, nullish members none. No set: `any`, `unknown`, primitives, type parameters and other instantiable types, reverse-mapped types, a mapped type whose constraint is not a union of literal keys, an `as`-clause mapped type unless it instantiates a top-level alias with type-variable-free arguments (`@solidjs/signals`' `Omit<T, K>`, `[P in keyof T as Exclude<P, K[number]>]`, rc.17 `store/utils.d.ts:226`; lib's `Omit` is `Pick<T, Exclude<keyof T, K>>`, no `as` clause, judged by its constraint), an object type with no property or signature (`{}`, an empty interface: admits any non-nullish value, declares nothing; `object` is not an object type here), and an all-nullish type. The proof reads the declared type, not the value (a value typed `{ class?: string }` may carry more keys at run time: structural subtyping, excess keys), as every checker-resolved type does; direct `eval` withholds it. The producer answers `declaredKeys` as sorted names, a pure function of the type (ADR 0367), demanded only at intrinsic-element spread arguments in a dialect setting `spread_declared_keys`; the wire carries it from handshake protocol 84 (Wire table schema v23, row flag 16, compact demand bit 19). Owners: `apps/solid-typefacts/src/type_facts.rs` (`declared_keys_at`), `typefacts`, `demand_plan.rs`, `object_members/handlers.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`; fixtures `spread-declared-keys-*`.

<a id="adr-0437"></a>
## ADR 0437 — A dispatch written in an event-only function leaves the veto in one step
A synchronous dispatch written directly in a function literal that ADR 0327's census (`event_invoked_literals`: closed program, compiler facts not withheld) finds invoked only as event deliveries, outside a class element and compiler-wrapped JSX, is classed `event handler` and leaves ADR 0356's veto for owner and read proofs. A handler also called from a component body keeps it (mixed invocation); one under a component's children leaves it, since ADR 0327 proves such a handler ownerless wherever written (the owner expected the veto to stay there; not built). ADR 0435's rounds (remove a site once proven ownerless under the larger set, repeat) cannot start, since the proof they reuse is withheld while any site stays; the census reads no veto, so one induction replaces them. Soundness: while a non-excluded site remains, every event proof is withheld. With none, each delivery comes from a host event (no owner or window; ADR 0327's premise), a window or document dispatch (no element handler), or a site in a scheduled, module-level, test-runner or event-only function; an event-only function's site runs only inside a delivery, so by induction on time no site runs on an owned stack, and removing sites only strengthens ownerless proofs. The answer is one `OnceLock`, independent of demand order (ADR 0367), entering the digest as the owner and read bits; a debug build fails if the census reads the veto it computes. Owners: `indexes/event_delivery.rs`, `execution_role/event_literals.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`; fixtures `event-dispatch-handler-*`.

<a id="adr-0438"></a>
## ADR 0438 — A callback a reviewed test runner registers runs with no owner and no strict-read window
A function literal handed directly to a test runner's registration (`test`, `it`, `describe`, `suite`, hooks, and members like `only`, `skip`, `serial`, `each(rows)(…)`) runs from the runner's scheduler without a Solid owner or strict-read window, for tests, hooks and suite bodies only where the runner's bytes show it (below). A suite body its runner calls inside the registration call (jest-circus, mocha, `node:test`, playwright) takes the role of the call's position; `bun:test`'s is not placed. A dispatch site in such a callback is classed `test runner callback` and leaves the veto; its body takes a scheduled callback's role for every rule (owner, write and read; fixtures `test-runner-vitest/variants/role-*`). The registration's root identifier must resolve through imports to declarations all in the installed declaration files of `vitest`, `bun-types` (`bun:test`), `@types/node` (`node:test`), `@playwright/test`/`playwright`, `@jest/globals`, `@types/jest` or `@types/mocha` (`TEST_RUNNER_DECLARATIONS` in `runtime_semantics.rs`), never by spelling (a project `it` or a wrapper, `it("x", wrap(fn))`, does not count); only a curried registration's outermost call registers (`describe.each(rows)` and `it.skipIf(cond)` take data, not callbacks). Reviewed premise, from the published bytes the corpus installs (vitest 5.0.0's runtime; the fixture pins 5.0.5 declarations only): vitest 5.0.0 calls a test as `fn()` in `runTest` (`dist/chunks/run.CQOUYP-x.js:3876`), a hook in `callSuiteHook` (`:3584`), a suite body in `runWithSuite` (`:2763`, `:3248`); playwright 1.63.0 calls `fn(testFunctionParams, testInfo)` (`lib/worker/workerProcessEntry.js:1655`), a hook through `resolveParametersAndRunFunction(hook.fn, …)` (`:1775`); no Solid API is on those stacks. Suite bodies are per runner (`SuiteBodyRun` in `runtime_semantics.rs`). vitest records the suite and runs the body in `collect` (5.0.0 `run.CQOUYP-x.js:2763`), called from `collectTests` after `importFile` (`:3446`-`:3454`), so later wherever registered. playwright 1.63.0 calls `body()` in `_describe` (`lib/common/index.js:2308`), jest-circus 30.5.2 `blockFn()` in `_dispatchDescribe` (`build/index.js:1792`), mocha 11.8.0 `opts.fn.call(suite)` in `suite.create` (`lib/interfaces/common.js:153`, from `bdd.js:46`), and `node:test` (Node 24.22.1, `internal/test_runner/test.js`) builds the suite in its constructor (`:1804`; `createBuild` runs the body at `:1840`, from `createSubtest` `:1052`): the body runs inside the registration call and takes that position's role (owned under `createRoot` or a component body, ownerless at module level; a dispatch in it is classed as the call's position, the owner graph gives it a `Preserve` edge, and an `async` body is not placed, as its resumptions run elsewhere). `bun:test` is native: its suite body is not placed. Every runner records tests and hooks for later: jest-circus `add_test` (`:1867`) then `_callCircusTest` (`:965`), mocha `suite.addTest` (`bdd.js:93`) then `Runnable.run` (`lib/runner.js:806`), `node:test` from `Test.start` (`:1262`) once the root has concurrency and the build phase (`harness.js:53`, `:1238`) is over, which a registration made during file load never finds. Fixtures `test-runner-suites`, `test-runner-node/variants/suite-inline*`, `test-runner-vitest/variants/suite-scheduled`, `curried-argument`, `test-runner-bun/variants/suite-unproven`. Async callbacks: a registered `async` callback is wholly in the premise: its prefix to the first await runs on the runner's stack and every resumption (after `await`, `for await`, `await using`, in a loop) runs with no owner (ADR 0333) and no strict-read window, so any call in it is a satisfied entry, unlike the event census, which refuses one a suspension may precede. vitest 4.1.11's `@vitest/runner` reads the same. Test-only helpers: when every invocation of a project function literal, followed as ADR 0327's census follows it (`test_invoked_literals`: the same walk in owner mode, closed program, compiler facts not withheld, same budget, the one entry a call written directly in a registered callback), is a call in a registered callback or another such helper, it runs on the runner's stack or its continuation, and a dispatch written directly in it is classed `test-only helper` and leaves the veto. Soundness: by induction on the call chain every invocation starts in a registered callback, whose owner and window are the runner's. Refused: an export of an open program (ADR 0442 follows one in a closed program), a rebinding, alias or stored value the walk cannot follow, an invocation from a component body, an event handler (a mix of the two entries), a primitive's callback or an unplaced caller, and a program not declared closed (a call past a suspension in an async helper is a satisfied entry since ADR 0442); a helper handed to a project function only tests call stays test-only (the walk follows the parameter). The census reads no veto (ADR 0437); a helper's own reads and writes take their role from ADR 0319's flow. Residual: a runner wrapping tests in a Solid root or component, a patched runner, a `node:test` test registered after its build phase (it may run inside the call), a helper also called from an event handler. Sites and class are report `premises`. Mechanics: `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`; fixtures `test-runner-*`.

<a id="adr-0439"></a>
## ADR 0439 — An optional DOM call in a leaf scope is read like the non-optional call
In the leaf-scope walk an optional call `el?.m()` (an optional chain, or a `let` typed `T | undefined`) whose exact declaration is a `lib.dom` member is judged by that declaration alone, like the plain call: only ADR 0329's synchronous dispatchers refuse, in either form, so `dialog?.focus()` stays an obligation and `el?.scrollIntoView()`, `setAttribute`, `append`, `select` and `observe` are certified. `getContext` of `HTMLCanvasElement` and `OffscreenCanvas` keeps the optional refusal (a failed WebGL context fires `webglcontextcreationerror` at the canvas synchronously); the plain call keeps its trust, an approximation. Premise: ADR 0329's (a member outside its list dispatches no event; custom-element reactions are not modeled). Demanding resolved calls in every file moved other rules (ADR 0192) for 7 fewer obligations; not built. Owners: `member_call_operations`, `refused_when_optional` in `solid-reactive-ir/src/cleanup.rs`; fixtures `leaf-scope-optional-dom`, `leaf-scope-project-callees`.

<a id="adr-0440"></a>
## ADR 0440 — SC9005 and SC9012 are obligations; SC9011 is SC9012's context `package-callee`
An uncertifiable result is internal (ADR 0328): unseen by users, ignored by `--certify`, kept only by `--internal` and the JSON. A rule key for one promised a switch useless to users that, in the native checker, hid the gap but not the `uncertifiable` status (`withheld_coverage`). So SC9005 `package-behavior-unavailable` and SC9012 `reactive-dispatch-unresolved` become obligations: same code, name and metadata (`RuleMetadata::configurable` is false), never filtered by enablement, with no rule-options key, plugin-manifest entry or ESLint rule. SC9011 `reactive-source-uncaptured`, a source handed to a package callee, merges into SC9012 with `analysisContext: package-callee` (`PACKAGE_CALLEE`); its internal defect kind is unchanged. Every former name still parses as a no-op: `RETIRED_RULES` gains the three names and SC9005's four former names (once `RULE_ALIASES`, whose target is no longer a rule), and the ESLint plugin keeps four deprecated no-op keys (`RETIRED_RULE_KEYS`), so no configuration breaks. This supersedes ADR 0248; SC9011's page content is SC9012's. Owners: `Rule::metadata`, `static_defect_wording`, `retain_enabled`, `RETIRED_RULES`, `rule_manifest_json`, `RETIRED_RULE_KEYS`; `docs/rules/README.md`.

<a id="adr-0441"></a>
## ADR 0441 — SC9015 is an error for a removed 1.x attribute `tsc` accepts
The pinned compiler errors on `on:`, `oncapture:`, `attr:`, `bool:`, `use:` and `classList` on a native element in the dom and ssr generates, whatever the options (`packages/compiler/src/shared/removed.rs:19-52`, `dom/element.rs:226-285`, `ssr/transform.rs:1634, 2238` at the pinned fork; solidjs/solid #4199, merged before rc.17). The file does not build, so SC9015 is a violation (error), one per file, judged against the pinned compiler, not the installed one: a project on an older compiler (corpus `app-game`, rc.4, which accepts `attr:`) still gets it. Invariant 1 narrows it: TypeScript exempts hyphenated names from the excess-property check, so only `attr:data-z`, `on:custom-event` and their kind are errors; `attr:title`, `bool:hidden`, `on:click`, `use:tip` and `classList` are TS2322 on the rc.17 typings and keep ADR 0360's notice (a different claim: the file has no compiler facts). Other refusals keep the notice too: malformed markup errors only while `validate` is on, which the checker does not model, and a fragment under an element, an unimplemented form and a namespaced component callee belong to a generate or limitation not claimed for the project. Classification uses the pinned compiler's own messages (`classify_refusal`, pinned against the real compiler by a test); an unrecognized message is unproven. Premise: the project's generate is dom or ssr, the only two the checker compiles. Owners: `classify_refusal` in `rust/dialects/solid-v2/compiler/src/lib.rs`, `compiler_refused_finding` in `solid-facts-backend/src/diagnostics.rs`; oracle cases in `fixtures/tsc-oracle/rule-cases.json`; fixture `compiler-refused-file`.

<a id="adr-0442"></a>
## ADR 0442 — A function handed to project code takes the role of each place that code invokes it
A function literal, or a same-file named function the file hands on (a whole argument, a non-intrinsic JSX attribute value, a `const` alias), takes for a write the join of the roles of all invocations ADR 0319's forward walk finds: through the callee's parameter (every use a call or a hand-on to another exact project callee); through `props.<name>` of an exactly resolved project component (an intrinsic `on…` attribute or a call in the child's handler is a delivery, a call in the child's body or a tracked computation is owned, a leaf-scope call is exempt, a prop forwarded to another component recurses within the walk's budget); and through a reviewed scheduler's callback slot (`setTimeout(fn)`, a fresh stack). A call is placed by what its own position proves, with no census of its function's other entries; in a function entered through a parameter it takes the role of the call that handed the value over, not of other callers. The join follows ADR 0378: an owned invocation (the body, a tracked computation) makes the write a violation regardless; otherwise every invocation must be placed (several non-reporting roles give `Unreported`), and the obligation stays for a census gap, an unplaced invocation, a destructured, `let`, stored or escaped use, a `merge`/`omit` view, a spread or repeated attribute on the element, a package component known only by declarations, a primitive's callback slot, or an unresolved child. A prop literal needs no closed program (a child's use of a prop ignores other callers); ADR 0450 makes the answers one memo per function. In a closed program the walk also follows an exported function declaration through other modules' references when each is a call the graph accounts for (`export *` is no hand-out; an alias, re-export specifier, value use, escaping namespace or open program refuses). So ADRs 0437 and 0438's censuses place a dispatch in `fireAndFlush(() => el.dispatchEvent(…))` called only from test callbacks (class `test-only helper`, leaving the veto) and in a handler-only `withTx(() => el.click())`, and flows through exported helpers (`makeNavigation`'s returned closures) are placed.
