Supporting steps: a `const` object pattern over an object the value is a member of binds the member by its plain-identifier key (`const { add } = renderPanel()`; a rest element or a quoted, computed or nested key refuses); in the test census only, a call past a possible suspension in an async helper is a satisfied entry, as in a registered async callback (the resumption has no owner or window, ADR 0333; the part before the first await runs on the caller's stack, which must still be a runner's). A write in a layer the walk runs in place (an immediately invoked function, a callback the standard library runs inline) takes the enclosing function's answer only if no layer's position follows a suspension: an `await` that may precede the write in its own or any enclosing function up to the answered one puts it in the ownerless continuation (ADR 0333), which no invocation places, so it stays unplaced (`write_flow_role`; fixture `prop-callback-roles/variants/after-await`). Under a synchronous dispatch (ADR 0356) host event deliveries are gated alike on both routes: the invocation found through `onClick={props.onPress}` and a call in `onClick={() => props.onPress()}` (placed by ADR 0327's census, which then refuses) both stay unplaced (`variants/sync-dispatch`). Premises: ADR 0319, 0327, 0333, 0378 and 0438's. Not built: reads for a forwarded or aliased owned call (SC1001 stays uncertifiable; a direct body call is ADR 0204), `merge`/`omit` views for an owned role, a registered test callback named by identifier, and unmodeled positions the walk refuses. Owners: `execution_role/event_literals.rs`, `invocation_flow.rs`, `write_roles.rs`; fixtures `prop-callback-roles`, `helper-literal-roles`, `test-runner-vitest/variants/helper-literal-*` and `helper-exported-*`; `solid-facts-backend/tests/invocation_flow_process.rs`.

<a id="adr-0443"></a>
## ADR 0443 — Three untracked reads rc.17 leaves silent are SC1001: a map function, a provider's `value`, a `runWithOwner` callback in a tracked scope
Probed on rc.17 browser dev builds (`scripts/probes/rc17-untracked-reads`): (1) `mapArray` and `repeat` run the map function under `runWithOwner`, which sets `tracking = false` (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev-shared.js:5779`), inside the map computation whose recompute clears `strictRead` (`:4196-4200`); a read there subscribes nothing, and only a call passing `name` opens a window (`dist/dev.js:3093-3102`, `3623-3633`), so unnamed it is stale and silent. The vocabulary had said map callbacks "run tracked". (2) Solid 2's context element reads `value` once under `<provider>`'s window (`solid-js@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/solid.dev.js:9-16`, `48-49`, `65`) and stores a snapshot (`dist/dev.js:1834-1844`); a child's `useContext` subscribes to nothing; the dev build warns. (3) `runWithOwner` clears tracking but keeps the call's window, so in an effect or memo compute, or a JSX expression, a read in its callback is untracked without warning. All three are SC1001 violations via new `Dialect` questions (`callback_runs_untracked_without_window`, `callback_clears_tracking_in_calling_window`) and a role, `UntrackedUnwindowed`, whose hint promises no dev warning unless one is certain; a provider's value is placed through its tag's exact `createContext` binding. Not reported: JSX the callback writes (it tracks itself), `untrack`, a stored function, a read after an await the call follows (also through an immediately invoked function; that is SC1002's), a call `tsc` rejects (upstream case 32's one-argument `createEffect` is TS2554), and `createRoot`, whose code at `dist/dev-shared.js:3478-3481` was not probed in a computation. Legal on rc.17 and kept as divergences from eslint-plugin-solid 0.18.1's `badUnnamedDerivedSignal` (ownership cases `strict-read-untracked/unnamed-derived/*`): an effect compute or memo returning a function, and a function a component returns that a tracked consumer calls. Case 34's condition `props.big` is a defect, its arrow is not; case 35 is undecided (uncertifiable). Owners: `reports_untracked_reads_at`, `callback_clears_tracking_in_calling_window` (`solid-dialect/src/solid_2.rs`); `semantic_role_before_awaits`, `unwindowed_role_at_call`, `context_provider_value_role` (`solid-reactive-ir/src/execution_role`); fixtures `mapper-untracked-reads`, `provider-value-untracked`, `run-with-owner-in-computation`; oracle and ownership cases.

<a id="adr-0444"></a>
## ADR 0444 — A workspace package a sibling depends on is an open program
ADR 0193's default made every `"private": true` package, and every manifest publishing nothing, a closed program. But an unseen workspace sibling may import it, so closed-program proofs (ADR 0203, 0213, 0214, 0442) falsely certified "no other caller" (kui's `kernel`, imported by every `feature-*`). An application is now closed only if no package of a workspace holding it depends on it. Workspaces read: `package.json` `workspaces` (array or `{ packages }`; npm, yarn, bun), `pnpm-workspace.yaml` `packages` (no key: pnpm's default, every subdirectory) and `lerna.json` `packages` (default `packages/*`), in the package's directory or any ancestor; patterns are `*`, `?`, `**` and `!` negation over directories holding a `package.json`. It is a member if a pattern lists it or it is the root; its dependents are the other members and the root manifest. A dependency is a name in `dependencies`, `devDependencies`, `peerDependencies` or `optionalDependencies`, a `workspace:` or `npm:` spec naming it, or a `file:`, `link:`, `portal:` or `workspace:` path resolving to its directory. Open instead: a workspace not read exactly (brace, class or other pattern syntax, an anchored or multi-line YAML list, another `workspaces` shape, an invalid or unreadable sibling or ancestor manifest, over 20,000 directories listed), and `rush.json` or `nx.json` without workspace patterns (a project graph not package-based). An explicit boundary still wins. An open boundary only withdraws proofs: results move to uncertifiable, never to violations. Premises: a package imports a sibling only through a declared dependency, as managers require (not via a relative path out of its directory, a `tsconfig` `paths` alias or an undeclared hoisted install); Deno and Bazel workspaces are not read. The daemon keys its answer on one digest of every file and member listing read (`program_boundary_digest`), so a new dependent reopens the package at once. Owner: `solid-facts-backend/src/program_boundary.rs`; fixtures `program-boundary-workspace`, `program-boundary-pnpm`, `program-boundary-standalone`.

<a id="adr-0445"></a>
## ADR 0445 — A census with no entry proves no component
ADR 0331's census is vacuously complete for a function referenced only by its declaration and module surface, so it needs a reference that renders the function: a tag, a dialect renderer prop, a proven `lazy` default, an audited route option, or a compound member a tag reaches. A JSX-returning function with none is a component only as an exported capitalized binding, the host's by module surface (a file route's default export, a library component). Otherwise (dead, unexported, lowercase, or bound to a call's result, `const rows = items.map((x) => <li/>)`) its body is an undecided region, as for an escaped reference: non-style violations stay uncertifiable. An anonymous function literal called where written has that call as its only entry and is no component: it takes its caller's role, and module scope has no owner. Premise left: an exported capitalized function nothing renders, called plainly by unseen code, is judged a component. ADR 0203, 0213 and 0214 discharge toward silence for a function with no caller and never report; their closure is ADR 0444's. Owner: `compute_function_component_status` (`indexes/components.rs`); fixture `component-census-no-entry`.

<a id="adr-0446"></a>
## ADR 0446 — Precision is measured on a reproduction set, refreshed by one pre-release run
`fixtures/uncertifiable-repro` stands in for the corpus: a slice iterates on it in seconds anywhere, and each case is a test the slice must move. A case is one cause or shape, never one finding, of a `kind`: `uncertifiable` (a cause holding results, ADR 0328), `violation` (a triaged real defect) or `clean` (a certified shape a runtime probe or mutation confirmed, which no later change may make uncertifiable or a violation). A local case is one short file in its main rule's shared project; a whole-project cause gets a project: the dispatch veto (ADR 0356), one per site kind since one unexcluded site holds every handler proof, and the open program boundary (ADR 0444), which also defeats the test-only-helper exclusion (ADR 0438). The component census (ADR 0445) moved no corpus result and gets none. The one `node_modules` holds only published bytes (files `tsc` and the browser/development conditions read, each with its sha256 under the package's version and integrity, `packages.json`); every case type-checks against them and runs under the corpus configuration. `cases.json` is the one table: kind, cause, files, source (app, file:line, commit), weight (ADR 0448's for uncertifiable cases), expected results, status; snapshots pin findings. A cause is what `scripts/uncertifiable-causes.mjs` reads around a site with TypeScript's symbol resolution, from the premises and a second sweep with `--program-boundary closed`: a grouping label, never evidence. A case is faithful when its result matches its source's rule, analysis context and message family and reads as the same cause; `make repro-causes` checks the set side, `--report` the corpus side. No continuous intake: issues with minimal repros carry what users see; once before each release a pinned, versioned manifest of public Solid 2 repos is restored, swept, reproduced and discarded (CONTRIBUTING.md): a known cause updates its weight, a new one becomes a case unless not faithfully reproducible (then `notReproduced`, with why), and a false positive blocks the release. Budgets, with separate lines for violation and clean cases: `scripts/repro-budget.mjs` in `make verify`. Owners: `fixtures/uncertifiable-repro`, `scripts/{uncertifiable-causes,repro-reduce,repro-budget,corpus-sweep}.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0447"></a>
## ADR 0447 — Veto weight is what a veto-off sweep clears
A result is the dispatch veto's (ADR 0356) only if a sweep by a scratch build whose sites withhold nothing (`withholds_owner_proofs`/`withholds_read_proofs` answer false; never committed) clears it; one the sweep keeps takes its other cause (`--noveto-report`). On the rc.17 corpus the shape reading gave the veto 787 results, 108 of which the veto-off sweep keeps, and missed 279 it clears; the sweep gives it 958. app-game has event-target and helper sites together, so its case's project holds both kinds; excluding one moves nothing. Owner: `scripts/uncertifiable-causes.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0448"></a>
## ADR 0448 — Coverage counts sites whose mechanism a source read verified
A label is a grouping: the rc.17 corpus gave 95.7% of results a case's label, yet sampled sites read as their case's mechanism in 315 of 551 reads. So an uncertifiable case records `labelWeight` (results its label takes), `sites` (distinct app, file, line and rule; a file several projects analyze counts once) and `verification.samples`, a seeded draw of 5 sites (all if fewer), each read from source against the case's file (`same`, `different`, `unsure`, with the mechanism). `verifiedWeight` is `sites` if every sample reads `same`, else 0; `unsure` counts against. The classifier splits a mixed label until each case is one mechanism; a mechanism under 10 distinct sites gets no case and joins `notReproduced` with its reason. `--report` checks counts and samples against the sweep; `--draw` redraws. Only verified coverage is quoted: 2,350 of 5,183 sites (45.3%) at 187 cases; label coverage 4,834 of 6,734 results (71.8%); 1,494 sites sit under 611 labels of fewer than 10. A source read can miss a second blocker the label hides, so every verified case was also tested by experiment: in a corpus clone, remove the case's blocker at a recorded or drawn site and see whether the site moves (859 site runs). A stay sets the case's weight to 0 (74 cases, 1,427 sites refuted: writes unplaced before the await, other obligations on the line, a project-wide veto or census). 2,247 verified sites are experiment-confirmed; 103 (25 cases) had no faithful edit and rest on reading. Owners: `scripts/{uncertifiable-causes,repro-budget}.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0449"></a>
## ADR 0449 — The classified dispatch veto and the write-flow table are read sections
ADR 0356's veto classifies the dispatch hazard's sites by receiver, caller and the two censuses (ADRs 0435, 0437, 0438) over the whole lookup, so `Section::SynchronousDispatch` binds its value (raw hazard, owner answer, read answer); a memo reading it reads the whole program while the hazard is present, else only the hazard's probe. The test-runner census runs over the lookup, read only through the veto. A walk following an exported function declaration into other modules asks the census's memo-world answers (`exported_call_sites_of`: hand-outs, the symbol's references, the call graph), so unrelated edits leave it unrun. The `declaredKeys` demand (ADR 0436) takes protocol 86 on Wire table schema v23, after protocol 85; its keys join a file's Entities fingerprint. Owners: `indexes/event_delivery.rs`, `execution_role/{invocation_flow,flow_memos,event_literals}.rs`, `indexes/census.rs`, `base/{reads.rs,memo/build.rs}`.

<a id="adr-0450"></a>
## ADR 0450 — Edits on a project with dispatch sites cost what they cost on one without
Only a write's enclosing functions ask the write-flow table, the veto's real cost, so it is one memo per function and guard, `WriteFlowAnswer` (table 47, key `(path, span, exempts root)`, computed through the lookup by `write_flow_answer`, walk cache cleared per answer), asked by `write_flow_role`; a build computes only what its chunks ask. Veto (classification 12 ms on app-game): (1) the two accessors note `Section::DispatchVeto`; `Section::SynchronousDispatch` is again the raw hazards index, the hazard's probe alone; (2) `DispatchVetoAnswer` (table 46) holds `(owner proofs, read proofs)`, read by `project_file_recorded` while the hazard is present, so readers rerun only when the hazard flips or either bit moves (equal reruns keep their revision, ADR 0385; sound as readers see the veto only through the bits); (3) the test-runner census is asked only of a dispatch's own function (literals answer independently; union over a path's slots), with placed sites from the hazard index's `SynchronousDispatch` entries. The default program boundary (ADR 0444) is decided once per request (the analysis takes the boundary, the answer's inputs the digest, of that read); the daemon keeps it (`BoundaryCache`) while its observations hold: each file's or listed directory's device, inode, length, modification and change times (the daemon's file stamp, ADR 0400), a path's absence, the project's canonical real path. A decision that listed a symbolic link is never kept; a file is read once per decision. Test: `dispatch_veto_reads_process.rs` (retained against fresh, every reused memo answer recomputed, `IncrementalBuilder::verify_reused_memos`; a reader recorded without the veto fails it by name). Owners: `indexes/{event_delivery,project_memos}.rs`, `execution_role/{event_literals,invocation_flow}.rs`, `base/{reads.rs,memo/build.rs}`, `cache.rs`, `solid-facts-backend/src/{program_boundary,daemon}.rs`.

<a id="adr-0451"></a>
## ADR 0451 — A change that moves findings is gated on edit latency at a looser ratio
The ratio is gated whenever the base answered from its retained daemon: 1.25 for equal answers, 1.5 (`DIFFERING_RATIO_LIMIT`) for differing ones; a head answer outside its retained daemon still fails outright. A ratio, because ceilings are uncalibrated absolute times of one runner image (ADR 0423), while the paired median needs no calibration and is already measured. 1.5 because moved findings can add work (a new rule, more results); a real 2.0 to 2.7x regression still exceeds it. Ceilings stay reported. Owners: `benchmarks/lib/real-projects.mjs`, `scripts/real-projects.test.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0452"></a>
## ADR 0452 — A closure a tracked scope builds runs where it is called
A tracked scope (a memo or effect compute, a tracked JSX expression) gave every write and read in it its role, but building a closure runs nothing. `enclosed_by_stored_function` skipped named functions ("the call sites' business"; anonymous stored literals went to their invocations), and the lexical role returned before call sites were consulted (`write_roles.rs`, `direct`): `SC2001` reported `const openFn = () => setOpen(true); return openFn` (legal: rc.17 runs it from a click), and a read in a returned or stored closure was certified tracked though rc.17 warns `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED` when the body calls it. Writes: the named closure's calls decide (`named_closure_in_tracked_scope`): in the scope's own pass, directly or via in-place layers (an IIFE, an inline standard-library callback), an alias or an outer closure, a violation; in a leaf scope or handler, legal; only returned or stored, uncertifiable. Names decide nothing (a capitalized closure is judged by its calls; one returning JSX is a possible component). Reads (`unrun_closure_within`, both tracked arms of `semantic_role_before_awaits`): a stored function, or a named closure not entered only by tracked calls, takes no role (uncertifiable); an IIFE, or a name whose every reference is a tracked call, keeps `Tracked`. A default (`(x = set(1)) => x`, `({ a = count() }) => a`) runs only when its function is called, so closure questions (`functions_around`: the stored-literal, JSX-attribute, region and named-closure guards) take the functions whose body or parameter list (by whole span) holds the site: a function inside a default (an IIFE, an inline callback) belongs to the closure, and a write there is unplaced whatever its lexical role (`write_flow_role` and the walk's entries had used the body-based function, placing it by a returning helper's calls). For summary rows (`interproc/graph.rs`, `callback_chain.rs`) a call in the owner's own list is no body call: it runs only when the caller omits the argument. The `containing_ast_function` lookups (about 150) still put nested list sites in the surrounding function: ADR 0454. The same query serves the component-child, provider-value, `merge` argument, named-callback, retained-literal and summary-owner questions. A named closure with a default is unplaced for writes and reads: its list is not inert, so the call graph withholds its call sites (ADR 0339), costing the violation for a body write of a closure with a non-literal default called in the scope. Also uncertifiable: an object method run by coercion (`valueOf`), a recursive closure (its calls are in progress). The corpus gains 17 uncertifiable results, no violation. Unchanged: `project_consumer` and `runs_outside_owner_call` read `attribute_function_within` alone. Fixtures `write-named-closure-in-tracked-scope`, `read-closure-in-tracked-scope`; rule page `no-write-in-pure-computation`.

<a id="adr-0453"></a>
## ADR 0453 — The runtime oracle runs in its own pinned Solid rc.17 install
The oracle borrowed corpus apps' `node_modules`, with non-rc.17 compilers: `@solidjs/vite-plugin` 3.0.0-next.51 asks for `@solidjs/compiler` and `@solidjs/babel-plugin` `^2.0.0-rc.16`, resolving to rc.18 (helge-dev) or, in an older install, rc.4 (probus-hk). Invariant 7 forbids a newer prerelease, and the compile is evidence. `scripts/runtime-oracle-install` (manifest and lockfile; `make runtime-oracle-install` runs `npm ci`) pins every Solid package (compiler and babel plugin by `overrides`) and what the tooling and packages cases import, at rc.17 or exact versions. A run refuses a compiler not at `solid-js`'s version. `oracle.json` `install` is removed; `--install` still names another directory to compare, unasserted. Owner: `scripts/runtime-oracle.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0454"></a>
## ADR 0454 — A site in a parameter list belongs to the function whose list it is
A default value (`(x = cb()) => x`, `({ a = count() }) => a`), and a function written in one, runs only when its function is called without that argument. `containing_ast_function` (innermost function whose *body* holds a site) put it in the surrounding function, so `function runNested(cb) { const g = (x = cb()) => x; return g; }` as `createMemo(() => { runNested(() => setV(1)); return 1; })` was an `SC2001` for a write that never runs (`calls_parameter_in_body` and `write_site_role` placed it in `runNested`'s body and activation). rc.17 throws for none of its variants (forwarded `(x = runBody(cb)) => x`, branched, returned directly, destructured, declared, an IIFE in the default, argument supplied; `oracle-behaviour-parameter-list-owner`: counters stay 0 where the checker said violation). The owner primitive, `AstFacts::innermost_function_owning` / `functions_owning`, is the innermost function whose body or parameter list (first parameter to body; name and type parameters are bound around it) holds the site. Over it, `owners.rs`: `owning_function`; `containing_body_function` (the owner if its body holds the site, else none); `owned_by_body` (`containing_ast_function(span) == function` for a call, member, JSX element or binding; true only in the body); `in_parameter_list`; `functions_around` is `functions_owning`. `containing_ast_function` stays the role pipelines' lexical lookup: a list site takes the role around its function, then is withheld (`parameter_default_owner`, now the owner of a site directly in a list, `unrun_closure_within`, the write guard); switching it globally made `prop-head-get`'s dormant default a violation instead of uncertifiable. A call in the owner's own list (`run(cb, x = cb())`) runs when a caller omits `x`: no body call, kept unplaced; no proof that every caller omits it is built. A parameter called in a list may run: `calls_parameter_in_parameter_list` keeps the summary row uncertifiable. The write guard covers every function's list, not only a named closure's: an anonymous callback's default is uncertifiable (`forEach((item, i = setV(1)) => …)`, index supplied), as is a true one (an IIFE omitting the argument, `createMemo((prev = setV(1)) => …)`). Costs: those true violations; a forbidden call in an IIFE's default in a leaf callback is one obligation, at the IIFE, not also at the call (`DefaultImmediate`). The owner graph (SC4001, `owner_discovery.rs`) makes a list call an edge from no source that may be unowned (`MayBeUnowned`): uncertifiable, held by the owning function's context (zero, never called, counts as unowned), not by a providing region around it. A component's own list under its owned body stays silent unless the call is an invoked function literal (`m = (() => onCleanup(…))()`: legal, uncertifiable, the in-place invocation has no source). SC3001's leaf walk treats a call in the callback's own list as a body call, uncertifiable: rc.17 calls an `onSettled` callback with no arguments (`@solidjs/signals` dist/dev.js `onSettled`, `untrack(callback)`), so its default runs in the leaf scope (`oracle-parameter-list-owner-leaf`; was silent), but no dialect fact states a primitive's arguments, so no violation is proven. A component's own list belongs to a possible component (`in_component_parameter_list`), not module initialization: a read there is uncertifiable, not silent. `reachability.rs` and the summary graph (`interproc/graph.rs`) place a list call by `owning_function`: reached with its function by a non-guaranteed edge, so uncertifiable (also, a cost, for a legal tracked or handler call and one supplying the argument). Unchanged: `async_context.rs` `inside_non_component_function` (no probe differed); `upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`, retained behaviour. Fixtures `parameter-list-owner`, `write-default-in-helper-closure`; rule page `no-write-in-pure-computation`.

<a id="adr-0455"></a>
## ADR 0455 — A write's owner is the one its callback's call gives it, through the layers that run in place
A callback's lexical hold no longer places a write (`write_region_adjustment`, `write_roles.rs`). The root, call-site, first-run and unaudited-root arms use `direct_callback_runs`, so a list site reaches the write guard (uncertifiable) in all their scopes (`createRoot`, `render`, `hydrate`, `untrack`, `dynamic` static, `flush`, `dispatchAsInteraction`, `createRenderEffect`'s first-run apply, an unaudited root; `createRoot` and `untrack` pinned). New arm `detached_in_place` climbs from the site through layers on the callback's stack (`layer_run`, shared with `runs_in_place_within`: an IIFE, an argument a standard-library call runs inline) and owner-keeping calls that run it during the call (`untrack`) to the primitive callback. Owner `None` (`runWithOwner(null, …)`) is `Detached`, legal anywhere. Owner `Conditional` (maybe null, or a bound `createOwner()` result, which `run_with_owner_callback_owner` does not prove non-null) is `OwnerUnknown`, uncertifiable directly and through layers (there it was an unproven violation). A proven owner (`createOwner()`, a component's `getOwner()`) is a root whenever the callback runs: the write is `CreatedRoot`'s, a violation directly and through layers, even in a handler (rc.17 throws; not through a parameter-list layer, whose omission is unproven). A write in a layer under `untrack` takes the role at the `untrack` call (`CallSite`), like a direct one. The climb ends at a layer that stores the function, hands it to an unknown call or runs it on a fresh stack. A named closure called in an `untrack` callback (rc.17 throws) gets ADR 0452's walk (`named_closure_in_owner_keeping_callback`, beside `named_closure_in_tracked_scope`): a call in the callback's pass takes the role at the `untrack` call (a violation under an owned scope; legal in a handler, under a detached owner, on a fresh stack); only returned or handed by name, uncertifiable; a list site, unplaced. An anonymous literal the callback returns, stores or hands to a call not run in place is unplaced (`unrun_layer_in_owner_keeping_callback`); handed to a project helper that calls it, a violation by the helper's summary (rc.17 throws). Costs, uncertifiable instead of violations: a closure with a default called in a root or `untrack` body with the argument omitted (rc.17 throws; the omission is unproven, as in a memo), and a write under a bound `createOwner()` owner, directly or in a layer. Fixtures `write-detached-owner-layers`, `write-default-in-root-closure`. In the oracle's harness (a dispatching entry) every handler write, and an `untrack` IIFE in a handler, is uncertifiable.
Disposal (`fixtures/reactive-ir/write-owner-in-cleanup`): rc.17's write guards (`setSignal`, `refresh`, store setters) test `!disposalDepth`, which `runDisposal` raises around each `onCleanup` callback (`dev-shared.js:5620-5626`, `:1314`; rc.13 throws), so a cleanup's write under a proven owner lands. `Dialect::callback_runs_only_in_disposal` (`onCleanup` only) and `write_guard_in_disposal` place it (by release: `Rejects` through rc.13, `StandsDown` on rc.17, `Unknown` for an unread release and the conservative vocabulary; tokens `disposal-rejects-writes`, `disposal-stands-down`, none, so an older id decodes `Unknown`). Legal only when the climb through inline layers ends at an `onCleanup` call with a discarded result (rc.17 returns the callback), no await before the write, and a standing-down release; `Unknown` there is uncertifiable. A violation needs proof the write is outside a disposal (a component body, a fresh stack, a scheduled callback, a past-await continuation, an effect's returned cleanup, a function only events call); a named, stored or handed-on layer is uncertifiable. An action call stays a violation (its guard does not test `disposalDepth`). Cost: a named root helper called from a body is no longer reported. A function passed by name to `createRoot` asks the same at its call; an effect created in a cleanup is unplaced, since a flush there applies it in the disposal. An effect's returned cleanup also runs in a disposal when its owner is disposed (a rerunning memo, a `Show` turned off, a removed row, a disposed root), unordered against a rerun, so on a standing-down release it is unplaced whatever the effect tracks (`effect_provably_reruns` proves no violation there; rc.13 reports it, and an unread release keeps the proof). The rerun that finds the owner alive throws unreported (`docs/open-work.md`).

<a id="adr-0456"></a>
## ADR 0456 — A called `onCleanup` handle runs its callback where it is called
rc.17's `onCleanup(fn)` returns `fn` (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev.js:2377-2405`, prod `signals.js:65-67`), so in a memo `const h = onCleanup(() => setV(1)); h();` runs the write at `h()` with `disposalDepth` 0 and throws `REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE`. The write took the callback's lexical `Deferred` role and the handle was never read: 60 of 75 disposal fuzz items (Chrome-confirmed, 7 `ACTION_CALLED_IN_OWNED_SCOPE`) were silent. `Dialect::disposal_callback_returned` (`Returned`, `NotReturned`, `Unknown`) answers what the call returns: `Returned` for `onCleanup`'s callback when the read signals release is rc.17, else `Unknown`, keyed on `write_guard_in_disposal`'s `StandsDown`, exactly `Read(RC17)` (a digit of its own would double the variant table). `cleanup_handle.rs` takes the census by exact resolution. The callback runs only in the disposal when the result is discarded or bound to an unread `const`; it also runs at the handle's calls when the result is a `const` whose every reference is an in-file call (not exported, no `eval`, no other-file reference) or the call is on the result (`onCleanup(fn)()`); anything else (stored, passed, returned, aliased, an unread release) is unplaced. The write walk climbs to the callback through in-place layers (an IIFE, an inline standard-library callback, `untrack`) and judges the write at each handle call: one rejecting it is the violation, an unplaced one leaves it uncertifiable, all legal keep the disposal's role. A closure the callback builds, or a write under an owner a call in it gives (`runWithOwner`, `createRoot`), is left to its own entries: a named closure is judged at its calls, taking the handle's role; `runs_in_disposal` does the same for an owner-given write. A handle call proven outside a disposal is `NotIn`, a violation. Costs: a passed, aliased or object-member handle, and any called handle off rc.17, is uncertifiable. Not this mechanism (`docs/open-work.md`): an action in a closure a cleanup hands to `createRoot` by name; `flush()` in an effect's cleanup. A handle call in an instance field, a possible or undecided component, or a refused file is unplaced by the backend's own predicates (it withdraws by the write's location, never the call's). A callback registered by name is judged as inline when `fn` is exactly resolved, else uncertifiable; a write that may follow an await is placed at no handle call. A handle call in a dead branch (`if (false)`, after a `return`) or a function proven not to run (every call and render unreached, or unreferenced) is unplaced too. Fixture `write-cleanup-handle-call`; rule page `no-write-in-pure-computation`.
<a id="adr-0457"></a>
## ADR 0457 — A helper that calls a callback in its own body is a layer that runs it in place
A Chrome-confirmed fuzzer `SC2001` false positive (rc.17 throws nothing; the write lands): `runWithOwner(null, () => { inline1(() => setW(17)); })`, `inline1` a project function or a package export read from source (`export function inline1(cb) { return cb(); }`). ADR 0455's climb (`detached_in_place`) crossed only an IIFE or inline standard-library callback (`layer_run`), so it stopped at the helper and the write took the lexical role, which `nested_literal_runs_during_body` proved through the helper: the component body's, a violation, also for a maybe-null owner. `layer_run` now also takes a direct argument whose exact non-primitive callee calls that parameter in its own synchronous body on every completion (`invokes_parameter_during_call`, the proof `callee_callback_timing` takes for reads; ADR 0266, 0300 for dependency source): the call is on the callback's stack, under its owner. Every walk `layer_run` serves gains it: the owner climb (null legal, proven owner a violation, maybe-null uncertifiable), the disposal climb (`runs_in_disposal`: on rc.17 a cleanup running a helper that runs `runWithOwner(o, …)` is legal), the `untrack` layers and `runs_in_place_within`. A helper that keeps, schedules or hands on the callback, or calls it in a closure (`createRoot(() => cb())`), proves nothing and ends the climb: the write's invocations decide, else uncertifiable. Review amendment: the proof is "called at least once", not "every run is that call". `parameter_uses_during_call` separates a callee whose every use of the parameter is a direct body call (`InPlace`) from one that also passes, stores or closes over it (`InPlaceAmongOthers`); reassigning it or reading `arguments` proves nothing. Among others, only a violation reads the in-place run (a proven owner, `NotIn`); every legal arm (null owner, `Only`, `untrack`, `runs_in_place_within`) leaves the write unplaced. Fixture `write-detached-owner-helper`;

<a id="adr-0458"></a>
## ADR 0458 — A violation at a site no execution reaches is uncertifiable
Five Chrome-confirmed fuzzer false positives (on rc.17 each flagged site had 0 hits and no diagnostic; rendering the function or removing the dead guard raises it) had one cause, unpruned unreachable code: `false && setW(1)` in a called handle's callback (SC2001), `return; class K { static { onCleanup(…) } }` and `return; onCleanup(…)` in a timer (SC4001), `f1()` only in a `Child1` nothing renders or names (SC2001), and an async `Child1` returned from a discarded IIFE, or never rendered (SC4001). One query, generalizing ADR 0456's handle-call reachability, answers it (`SiteReach`, `execution_role/site_reach.rs`): `Unreached` proves the site never runs; `MayRun` proves nothing. Syntax gives dead regions (`AstFacts::unreachable_regions`, facts schema 77, `solid-facts` `ast/unreachable.rs`): statements after one in the same list that never completes normally (`return`, `throw`, `break`, `continue`, a block or `if` whose arms all do; a hoisted `function` declaration is not dead), and arms a literal test excludes (`if`, `?:`, `while`, `for`, the right of `&&`, `||`, `??`; literals: booleans, `null`, numbers, strings, `void`, function, object and array literals, and `!`/`&&`/`||` over them; no identifier, `undefined` included). Whether the site's function runs is a least fixed point; it never does if: written in dead code or a never-run function; a constructor, method or accessor of an unexported class with only unreached references (no static block, static initializer or `@` in scope); bound to an unexported name, no module calls `eval`, and every resolved call or render and every reference is unreached, in a type query or returned by a discarded IIFE; or an anonymous function a discarded IIFE returns. A module-scope function may run (modules and hosts are not followed; ADR 0445 keeps the module surface), as may any other unnamed value once created. The pipeline asks it of every reportable row's location after all producers and gates (`unreached_report_sites`, `Program::unreached_sites`, compared by the shadow build); the backend makes a violation at an exact unreached location uncertifiable for any rule, beside the undecided-component downgrade (`diagnostics.rs`), except style rules (SC80xx) and SC2008 (it claims a no-op wherever it runs). The handle census (ADR 0456) uses the same query, and a named function with only unreached calls that is also handed on as a value no longer counts as unreached. Not covered: a site after an always-throwing call (SC3001's dead tracked-effect body), a `try` that cannot complete, `undefined` or a constant binding as a test, an unreferenced module-scope function, a site reached only through an unreached call of a function also called elsewhere (the finding's location is reached), an evidence step in dead code. Fixture `dead-code-sites`.
<a id="adr-0459"></a>
## ADR 0459 — A `const` destructuring of a namespace import binds the export it names
`const { onCleanup: cleanup } = S` (`import * as S from "solid-js"`) holds exactly the export `S.onCleanup` reads, so `cleanup(…)` names the primitive wherever `S.onCleanup(…)` does: a module namespace object is immutable, a `const` slot is never reassigned, and the copy equals the export while the export binding is never reassigned, true of Solid's function exports (the dialect's primitive list gates it). solid-facts records the slot (`AstFacts::namespace_member_bindings`, facts schema 77) only for a non-ambient `const` declarator whose initializer the binder resolves (an identifier under TypeScript wrappers) to a value namespace import binding, with an identifier or (cooked) string-literal key and a bare binding value; computed keys, defaults, rest elements, nested patterns, `let`/`var`, a shadowing `S` and exported slots are excluded, so every mention is in the declaring file. The IR names the slot's symbol after the export (`add_solid_import_names`, `referenced_primitive`), `effect_phase::declaring_file` follows it to the namespace's import, and the backend's demand plan asks for it (`PrimitiveImports`). A mention that may run before the declarator throws `ReferenceError`: ADR 0339's proof decides each (`namespace_slot_mention_withheld`); a withheld call names no primitive, leaving an uncertifiable `SC9012` obligation. Only claims about the call itself (`missing-owner`, `flush-in-effect-callback`) need the guard, and they read the guarded path (`call_primitive_name_cached`, the primitives memo); JSX control-flow, post-row and async-context sites read names unguarded, since what they claim runs only after the tag or call succeeded. A new call-site claim must read the guarded path. Not this: a namespace of a project module re-exporting Solid (unresolved in namespace form too), and a local value read through a literal (`const { useIt } = { useIt: r1 }`). Fixture `namespace-destructured-api`; `rc13-flush-in-effect-callback` gains the destructured `flush`.
<a id="adr-0460"></a>
## ADR 0460 — A write through in-place layers is placed where the layers run, in a render-effect apply and a control-flow row
The misuse fuzzer found writes (signal and store setters, `refresh`) silent in two places, for every binding. (1) A direct write in a `createRenderEffect` apply was already uncertifiable (its first run is synchronous under the component's owner only with no `defer`/`schedule`, no pending compute and, on rc.17, no lane seating it), but one layer down (an IIFE, a helper calling its callback, a named closure the apply calls) it took the apply's legal lexical deferred role. `detached_in_place` now treats a climb through in-place layers to a callback sharing its call's owner on its first run (`CallbackOwner::InheritsFirstRun`) as a write at the call, so uncertifiable; a parameter-list site, or a callback a helper also hands on, gets no placement. `callback_takes_call_owner` extends ADR 0455's named-closure and unrun-layer rules to that apply. (2) `control_flow_execution_role` gives every function nested in a control-flow children callback (`For`'s row) the deferred role, even an IIFE in the row's JSX child, which runs in place in a render computation. `write_role_walk` now follows the layers between write and row (`control_flow_nested_layers`): all in place, the write takes the outermost call's role (in a JSX child a violation, as a direct write there was); one named, stored or handed to an unknown call, uncertifiable; a primitive's callback, a JSX attribute value (a handler) or a scheduler's callback keeps its own role, so handlers, timers and effect applies in a row stay legal. Fixture `reactive-ir/write-render-apply-row-layers` (4 uncertifiable, 7 violations, negatives), every case checked on the rc.17 fast path.
<a id="adr-0461"></a>
## ADR 0461 — `delete` of an optional store property is a store write
rc.17's `deleteProperty` trap drops a delete outside a setter behind the `set` trap's gate (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev.js:5761-5767`, `inDraft` `:4955-4957`), so `delete store.path` is reported where `store.path = x` is, with the same store proof (bare root identifier, static member path) and exemptions (own setter, compute function, rc.0-rc.6 readonly root). Upstream reports every delete; tsc reports a required (TS2790) or readonly (TS2704) operand, so the operand must carry the new Type Fact `writableOptionalProperty`: its resolved property symbol is `Property | Optional` and not readonly, which type-checks under every compiler option for a bare or parenthesized operand (tsc sees only through parentheses: `delete store.v!` and `delete (store.v as T)` are TS2703, silent). A chain past the root (`store.a.b`) is silent when a link's primitive value domain may hold `null`/`undefined` (TS18047/TS18048), else uncertifiable, never a violation: without `strictNullChecks`, which the facts do not record, a link's type proves nothing about its runtime value, and an `undefined` link throws before any write (the tsc oracle's loose project). A store delete whose facts were not served is uncertifiable. A non-optional property whose type includes `undefined` is TS2790 under `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`, silent. Wire table schema v24 (row bit 17, no payload), demand bit 20, protocol 87. Refused, silent: element access, optional chain, cast root, a `const` alias (the assignment form follows none). Owners: `writable_optional_property_at` (`apps/solid-typefacts/src/type_facts.rs`), `no_store_delete` (`upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`), `collect_delete_spans` (`demand_plan.rs`); fixture `store-delete`.
<a id="adr-0462"></a>
## ADR 0462 — `Loading`/`Errored` called as functions need an owner; an async read in a rendered local function is uncertifiable
The misuse matrix found two codes with no recall. (1) `NO_OWNER_BOUNDARY`: rc.17's `Loading(props)` and `Errored(props)` (`solid-js` `dist/solid.dev.js`) call `createLoadingBoundary`/`createErrorBoundary`, whose `createBoundary` (`@solidjs/signals` `dist/dev.js`) warns when `getOwner()` is null; the hydrated wrappers pass straight through with no owner (`noHydrationId`). Only JSX `<Loading>` was asked about. The dialect now answers `OwnerRequirementRole::Boundary` for `Primitive::Loading | Primitive::Errored`, and owner discovery records the call as operation `boundary-call` (reported as `boundary`), placed like an effect: unowned contexts, `runWithOwner(null)`, after an `await` (`calls_after_await`). JSX `<Loading>` keeps its placement: rc.17 dev's `createComponent` runs a component in a transparent root, so a tag in a handler does not warn and is not claimed; the module-scope tag claim stays (prod creates the boundary with no owner, and the dev root is never disposed either). `createLoadingBoundary`/`createErrorBoundary` are not in the published typings, which accept `Loading({ fallback: 0, children: 1 })`. (2) `ASYNC_OUTSIDE_LOADING_BOUNDARY` was claimed only for an accessor call sitting in rendered JSX. A read written directly in a named local function (`const wrapUse = () => { a1(); }`), not a generator or async, with a never-written binding, is now followed through direct calls (up to four named layers) to a call in rendered JSX and recorded there as an `AsyncRead` with `call_attributed`: nothing proves the read runs pending while the call renders, so SC5003/SC5005 are uncertifiable, never violations (`push_call_attributed_async_reads`, `local_access.rs`). Silent here, resolved later in the batch: an alias (`const useIt = a1`) or object-literal destructuring (`const { useIt } = { useIt: a1 }`) of the accessor (ADR 0463), and a callback handed to a project helper for the boundary call (ADR 0474), as for every owner requirement (rule page). The fuzzer's render loop (`Object.entries(mod)` in `main.tsx`) is whole-program flow the mount census does not follow, so even the direct read stays uncertifiable there. Fixtures `boundary-call-owner`, `async-boundary-call-attributed`.
<a id="adr-0463"></a>
## ADR 0463 — An exact `const` alias of a local reactive binding is its source
The misuse matrix found a local value read through an alias silent for every read and store rule (`const useIt = r1; useIt()`: `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED`, `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ`, `UNTRACKED_READ_AFTER_AWAIT`, `STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER` 0% agree), and its object-literal form (`const { useIt } = { useIt: r1 }`, left out by ADR 0459) silent for every rule. Source discovery's pass 5 (`resolve_setter_action_aliases`) followed `const set = setW1` for setters and actions only, and no literal slot had a row. solid-facts now records `AstFacts::local_alias_bindings` (facts schema 79): a non-ambient, unexported `const` holding exactly a binding the binder resolves to a same-module `const` variable, as `const a = b` (under parentheses and TypeScript wrappers) or a static slot `const { k: a } = { k: b }` of an object literal of only static data properties (no spread, computed or numeric key, getter, setter, method or `__proto__`), the key present once with an identifier value. Pass 5 reads one row per fact and, for those rows only, also gives the alias the source's accessor entry and every per-source fact (kind, primitive, phase, async provenance and options, origin, runtime standing, summary and return eligibility), even where the typed pass (ADR 0294) already proved the alias holds the source; setters and actions follow literal slots too, so every rule sees the source. A mention that may run before the alias's declarator throws `ReferenceError`: ADR 0339's proof now covers aliases (`names_local_alias`), and a withheld mention (`local_alias_mention_withheld`) is unplaced in local access, so its read, write or action is uncertifiable. SC1002's pre-await tracking compares sources by accessor declaration (`source_identity`) and store paths after the root's name, so a key read before the await through source or alias tracks it for both (the `after-await-tracked` runtime case). The store mutation rule (`upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`, product-owned proven-source logic, code unchanged) now sees an alias store, so ADR 0461's refused `const` alias delete is reported. Refused: a `let`/`var`, conditional, parameter, imported or exported alias, a `let` source, a default, a nested pattern. Not fixed: a direct read before its source's own declarator is still reported (open-work). Fixture `local-alias-bindings`; `store-delete` and `store-strict-read-keys` gain an alias violation each (both confirmed on the rc.17 fast path).
<a id="adr-0464"></a>
## ADR 0464 — A site that names a binding before its declarator is proven to have run is uncertifiable
`[1].forEach(() => r()); const [r] = createSignal(0)` was SC1001 (Chrome on rc.17: "Cannot access 'r' before initialization", no Solid diagnostic; `tsc` is silent inside a closure). ADR 0339 ordered only mentions of function-valued bindings, only for call edges; reads, writes and store accesses of destructured bindings, and every other rule, never asked. The proof (`indexes/initialization.rs`) now also decides every binding an array or object pattern declares with an initializer and answers two sets per path: mentions the call graph withholds (ADR 0339's order for a function-valued binding; its non-literal-default withholding, which says nothing of the mention's own order, stays there alone), and mentions unordered under the full order, which also places an object or class method at its creation, like an arrow, and credits a project module's mention of an installed dependency's binding (ADR 0339's premise: the package imports no project module, so the load making the name usable finished evaluating). The pipeline asks the second set of every reportable row's location and a propagated read's origin (`early_mention_report_sites`, `Program::early_mention_sites`, compared by the shadow build); the backend makes a violation there uncertifiable for any rule but style rules (SC80xx), beside ADR 0458's downgrade. A mention anywhere in the site counts (`setX(r())` throws before the write). Not covered: an identifier bound to anything but a function or call (`const r2 = r`, `const r = pair[0]`); a site after an unordered mention in the same run (a throw ends it; ADR 0458 does not prune it either); a `for (const [k, v] of …)` binding (no initializer, bound before each iteration). Fixture `destructured-binding-tdz`. Review: a mention in a function written within the reported site does not count (it runs when called; the frozen rc.17 corpus lost its one SC1001 to such a callback); a decided binding with no compiler reference list has its same-file binder mentions withheld and unordered; under the full order a `var` initialized by a call in its function's straight-line flow is ordered like a `const`.

<a id="adr-0465"></a>
## ADR 0465 — A store write past the root is TypeScript's where a link may be nullish, and proven only under strictNullChecks
SC2003 reported `store.a.b = 1` with `createStore<{ a?: { b?: number } }>({})`, which strict `tsc` reports (TS18048), likewise `store.a.push(1)` and a nullable link (TS18047). ADR 0461's `delete` rule now covers assignment and the in-place array call: each link the member chain reads past the root (`AstFacts::member_chain_links`: every object below the written member that is itself a member, as written, through transparent wrappers) is asked for its primitive value domain (demand plan, capability `store_write_chain_links`), and Type Facts states the program's effective `strictNullChecks` (protocol 87: the analyze answer's `strictNullChecks`, kept with the retained table, `TypeScriptTable::strict_null_checks`; the producer uses the checker's rule, `get_strict_option_value`: the option, else `strict`, which TypeScript 7 turns on unless `false`, so a tsconfig without a `strict` key is strict). `chain_links_verdict` (`upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`): no link (`store.v = 1`, `list.push(1)` on an array store, a cast root) keeps the violation. Under strictNullChecks a link whose domain may hold `null` or `undefined` makes the write TypeScript's (silent); one excluding both keeps the violation (Chrome on rc.17: `STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER`); a missing or unknown domain, an element-access link (no `undefined` without `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`) or a chain under a type assertion (`store.n!.b`, `(store.o as T).b`; Chrome: they throw `TypeError`) is uncertifiable. Without strictNullChecks, or with no answer, every write through a link is uncertifiable: a type then proves nothing about a link's runtime value. Moved: `rows[props.index].done` (solid2-precision) to uncertifiable. Residual: a project without strictNullChecks gets an internal uncertifiable result where strict `tsc` would report; the tsc oracle requires silence in both passes, so no `removed-because-redundant` case pins TS18048/TS18047 (fixtures `store-write-chain-links`, `store-write-chain-links-loose`). Review: a nullish link is TypeScript's only in a chain without optional access (`store.list?.push(2)` short-circuits: uncertifiable) and in a file `tsc` reports in (a JavaScript file, whose `checkJs` the facts lack, or one with `@ts-nocheck`, `@ts-ignore` or `@ts-expect-error`, is uncertifiable). The effective option follows TypeScript 7 (`strict` on unless `false`), the oracle's authority (typescript 7.1.0-beta); a TypeScript 5.x project without a `strict` key is a residual (docs/open-work.md). Merged with ADR 0461: `delete` keeps its parenthesis-only operand and `writableOptionalProperty`, and its links past the root take this verdict, so a nested delete through non-nullish links under strictNullChecks is a violation, no longer uncertifiable (Chrome on rc.17: `STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER` at `store.a.b` in a body and a handler and at a narrowed `store.o.b`). Amendment (recall batch): the directive veto reads the parser's comments (`AstFacts::typescript_directive_comment`, facts schema 80), not the source text, so a string literal naming a directive is none; it is computed once per file at extraction.

<a id="adr-0466"></a>
## ADR 0466 — A callback passed by name is placed as the inline callback in its position
The containers fuzzer found silent a write in an IIFE or `forEach` inside a memo compute passed by name (`const cb1 = () => { (() => setW(1))(); return 1; }; createMemo(cb1)`; rc.17 throws `REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE`), though the inline compute and a direct write in `cb1` were reported. The cause, across every write host and layer (none, IIFE, `forEach`, a named closure, a helper calling its callback): ADR 0455/0457's climbs (`detached_in_place`, `runs_in_disposal`) found a callback's primitive only for a literal argument, and `write_role_walk`'s memo arm dropped writes nested in a named compute. By name, where rc.17 throws: silent for `untrack(cb1)` with a layer and a render-effect apply (inline: uncertifiable, ADR 0460); uncertifiable where inline is a violation for `createRoot(cb1)` with a layer and an effect or render-effect compute. Legal `runWithOwner(null, cb1)` was uncertifiable. `missing-owner` had no by-name gap. `by_name_position` now resolves the one primitive position whose bare identifier argument is exactly the function's current binding (a declaration, or a `const` arrow passed after its declarator in the same function; not a method, generator, named function expression, or two positions), which both climbs treat as the inline argument. If not the function's only entry (other references, hand-out, export) it is one run among others (ADR 0457): a rejecting run is a violation (`CreatedRootAmongOthers`, new `CallSiteAmongOthers` for `untrack`, whose legal answer is a final `Unknown`); a legal one proves nothing (after `runWithOwner(null)` the function's entries decide, their census not counting the position). The memo arm takes writes a named compute runs in place (`runs_in_place_within`); `NamedCallbackRole::effect_compute` (an effect's tracked compute named as the whole argument; reads still not admitted) answers `TrackedJsx` for them. `onCleanup(f)` as `f`'s only entry runs `f` only in the disposal, so `write-owner-in-cleanup`'s `CleanupNamedOwnerHelper` and `CleanupNamedRootBody`, and rc.3's exempt store setter in a by-name root body, went from uncertifiable to legal (fast path: writes ran, no diagnostic). Costs, uncertifiable where rc.17 throws: an alias, a named function expression, a `const` named in a nested function. Fixture `write-callback-by-name`, every case checked on the rc.17 fast path.

<a id="adr-0467"></a>
## ADR 0467 — An effect apply's read of an async source stays a strict read
The misuse matrix counts `const a1 = createMemo(async () => 1); createEffect(() => 1, () => { a1(); })` (and the render-effect form) as wrong-rule: rc.17 throws `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ`; the checker reports `strict-read-untracked` (SC1001). rc.17's `read()` (`@solidjs/signals` `dist/dev-shared.js:5320-5325`) throws for a pending source in a strict-read window before it would warn (`:5441-5446`), so a run raises one code, never both, and which is timing. On the fast path the apply throws when first run with the source pending (plain, module-scope source, render-effect apply even with the source rendered) and warns when the mount waits on a rendered async read, under a `Loading`, when the compute reads this or another async source, or on a guarded later run. Nothing local orders the first run against the source's settling (a held mount anywhere above, a waiting compute, ADR 0460's unproven first-run premises), so `pending-async-unsuspendable-read` claims no apply read and SC1001 stays the violation: the read is untracked in the apply's strict-read window either way. Its message adds that a pending run throws `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ` instead, when the projection holds an async read row of proven async provenance at the site (`FindingSeed::StrictRead::apply_reads_async_source`) and the compute does not read the source. Not built: a first-run pendingness proof (it needs every async read that can hold the mount). Fixture `strict-read-async-apply` (rc.17 fast path for every case).
<a id="adr-0468"></a>
## ADR 0468 — A callee spelled in a runtime-equivalent way is the function it names; an unnamed callee is an obligation, never nothing
Equivalence mode and the containers fuzzer found every rule silent on `(void 0, setW)(1)`, `(0, f)()` and, for missing-owner, `(f1)()` over a helper registering `onCleanup`: unresolved callees were dropped (no local-access row, no owner edge; a function without owner context counted as never running), and `call_edge_symbol` and reachability took a callee's first token as its target. (1) `AstFacts::composite_callee_operands`/`composite_callee_references` record what a comma, conditional or logical callee may evaluate to (not `&&`'s left). An operand that may be a local setter, action or accessor makes the call an SC9012 obligation; a new owner edge `MayRun` passes the caller's owner context minus "proven unowned" to every function it may run, also for a callee binding written anywhere, a mention before its declarator (ADR 0339/0464) and an exact object member tail whose method does not read `this` (a comma loses the receiver; one that reads it throws). The first-token guard covers owner edges and reachability. (2) solid-facts records `(void 0, f)`, `(0, f)` and nested inert prefixes as a direct call of `f` (facts schema 81), never a member tail, effectful prefix or `(0, eval)`; the demand plan asks the symbol of an identifier under a TypeScript wrapper, so `setX!(1)` and `(setX as T)(1)` resolve. Parentheses and an optional call of a proven non-nullish binding already resolved. (1) removes three missing-owner false positives on main (rc.17's fast path reports none): `let k = () => onCleanup(…); k = () => {}; k()`, its `k?.()` form after `k = undefined`, and `(f = h)()`. Moved, silent to uncertifiable: two TypeScript-wrapped callees in `async-call-prefix` and `own-setter-wrapper-identity`. Not covered: a returned callable (`(void 0, q.bind(null))()`, RC1), `const fn = o.m; fn()`, a conditional alias. Fixture `callee-spelling` (39 cases).
<a id="adr-0469"></a>
## ADR 0469 — A read through layers that run in place is placed where they run
The containers fuzzer (RC7) and equivalence mode found SC1001 silent for a read in an IIFE, `forEach` callback or helper's callback inside a `createRoot` or `runWithOwner` body or an effect apply, and for `setK(r())` in an IIFE in an apply, though direct reads are reported: role checks matched only reads written directly in the callback, so a layered read took the callback's deferred (silent) role. `climb_in_place_layers` (`execution_role/write_roles.rs`) walks out through the layers the write rule trusts (`layer_run`: an IIFE, an inline standard-library callback, a helper calling its callback, even one that keeps it), stopping at a primitive's callback, a stored or handed-on function, a fresh stack, a generator or a layer called inside JSX, and records a parameter-list site and an await (always or possibly) before the site. `callee_roles.rs` (reads only; writes untouched) places such a read as if written at the outermost layer's call before falling back to the deferred role: a reporting place stays a violation, an await on every path makes it legal, a possible await or a parameter-list site makes it uncertifiable. Unchanged: TDZ (ADR 0464) and the render-effect apply's first run (ADR 0460). Not this: an action in a named closure called in place inside a callback `createRoot` receives by name (the write engine's climb does not cross a named closure; equivalence item 2); a callable returned through a primitive and called later (RC1a) (`createRoot(() => <div>{r()}</div>)`, a pre-existing false positive, is ADR 0470). Fixture `read-in-place-layers` (13 violations, 8 legal, 3 uncertifiable; each on the rc.17 fast path).
<a id="adr-0470"></a>
## ADR 0470 — A read in JSX a callback writes takes the compiler's tracked role
A Chrome-confirmed false positive on main: `createRoot(() => <div>{r()}</div>)` was SC1001; rc.17 is silent, since the pinned compiler compiles the child to `insert(_el$, r)` (an attribute to `effect(() => …)`, a component prop or child to a getter), which tracks the read. `semantic_role_before_awaits` placed the callback in its effect-apply and `createRoot`/`runWithOwner` branches before the compiler's tracked-region check, so a read in JSX the callback writes took the call's window; the existing escape covered only a callback that clears tracking (`runWithOwner`). `own_jsx_compiler_role` (`execution_role/callee_roles.rs`, reads only) now runs first: when the compiler's narrowest placement of the read is in the read's own function, a tracked region is `TrackedJsx` and a prop or child getter `DeferredCallback`; an untracked once-evaluated value or a handler factory falls through unchanged. A read in the callback's body beside the JSX stays a violation. Not this: JSX in an effect apply, where rc.17 throws `PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK`, a code no rule claims (open-work). Fixture `read-jsx-in-callbacks`.

<a id="adr-0471"></a>
## ADR 0471 — A closure in a root's body runs under the root where it is called, whatever region holds the root call
The disposal fuzzer found SC2002 silent for `onCleanup(() => createRoot(() => { const g = () => a1(); g(); }))`, its by-name, function-declaration and effect-cleanup forms, and an IIFE in `f1` with `(() => { createRoot(f1); })()`; rc.17 throws `ACTION_CALLED_IN_OWNED_SCOPE` at disposal. `write_allowed_spans` drops a root body from the regions a write may treat as imperative, but the cleanup, effect apply or handler around the root call stays one, so its lexical role (`DeferredCallback`) covered the closure and returned before its calls were walked. A root body in a component body was right (its rendering role proves nothing for a nested literal). For a guard that rejects in a root (actions; setters, whose disposal exemption the walk still reads at the call), `named_closure_in_root_callback` takes the innermost named closure whose innermost primitive callback, literal or named by its binding, is a root body, and its calls decide, as ADR 0455 does for `untrack`. A site run in place by a function a primitive names as a root's body, in a position the by-name climb does not admit, is unplaced instead of legal (`in_place_in_root_named_callback`). `by_name_position` admits an argument in in-place layers (IIFE, inline standard-library callback, helper) whose outermost call follows the declarator in the binding's scope, with no await on the way and not in JSX. A closure stored, returned or kept and run elsewhere stays uncertifiable; one called in place and also kept is a violation (one run suffices). Not this: `createRoot` written directly in an effect apply throws `PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK` before its body runs, yet a write or action in the body is reported, inline (before) and now through a named closure (open-work). Fixture `write-named-closure-in-root`.

<a id="adr-0472"></a>
## ADR 0472 — An owner operation in a returned function no owner edge reaches is uncertifiable
`const mk = () => () => onCleanup(f); const h = mk(); setTimeout(() => h())` was certified, as were the same returned through `runWithOwner(null, …)`, `createRoot`, `untrack` or a memo; rc.17 reports `NO_OWNER_CLEANUP`. The owner graph gives a call an owner edge only for an exact function symbol, an invoked literal or a modeled callback slot, not a binding holding a call's result, so the returned function kept context zero, which emission reads as "no syntax runs it" (no report bit), and a lexical providing region around it discharged it too. Context zero is proof only for a function nothing can call. `emit_file` now treats an operation whose function (or an enclosing context-zero function) is a `return` argument or a concise arrow body as unowned and runtime-uncertain, which the providing region cannot discharge: uncertifiable, never a violation. A sound violation needs the returned value's exact transport to its calls (every return the same function, a `const` result, every use a call, each primitive's result contract behind `Dialect`), not built (open-work `review-r1-owner-mix`). Cost: a result called under an owner, in a root or never is uncertifiable. Fixture `owner-returned-callable` (14 uncertifiable, 1 violation, 2 clean; each on the rc.17 fast path); three existing findings were added, all uncertifiable SC4001 at returned functions (`callback-ts-sugar-identity` `makeHandler`, `directive-phases` `innerComputation` beside its SC6001, `object-member-import` the getter's returned closure).
<a id="adr-0474"></a>
## ADR 0474 — A helper call runs the callback the helper calls, for the owner graph
Containers RC2: `NO_OWNER_CLEANUP` was silent for an `onCleanup`-registering callback handed to a project helper that calls it (`function h1(cb) { cb(); }`) in a timer, promise callback, effect apply, handler or `runWithOwner(null)`: the owner graph linked the caller to `h1` but not the call to the function literal it receives, so the callback kept owner context zero, which emission reads as never entered. ADR 0457's census (`helper_argument_run`, now shared by the write and read role walks and owner discovery, `execution_role/callback_standing.rs`) proves whether the exact helper calls the parameter on every completion or on some paths, and whether it also stores or hands it on. For a non-primitive call with no other callback edge, `owner_discovery.rs` adds an edge to each function-literal argument the helper calls: the call's own kind if always called, `MayRun` (ADR 0468) if on some paths, plus a `MayBeUnowned` root edge if also kept or handed on; none if never called, the parameter is reassigned or `arguments` is read. Moved: `uncertifiable-repro/sc4001`'s clean `withOwner(fn)` (`getOwner() ? fn() : createRoot(fn)`) gains an internal uncertifiable (the census proves neither the guard nor the hand-on). Fixture `owner-helper-callback` (7 violations, 6 silent, 2 uncertifiable; each on the rc.17 fast path). Amendment: a call behind an exact `getOwner()` test (the guard `push_owner_requirement` reads) adds only the source's owned part; one behind `!getOwner()` is unrecognized (the facts record no unary expression; source text is not read) and keeps the "some paths" edge; a hand-on to a `CallbackOwner::Creates` position (`createRoot(fn)`) adds an `Owned` edge, not `MayBeUnowned` (`helper_argument_owner_runs`).

<a id="adr-0475"></a>
## ADR 0475 — A creation the runtime refuses enters nothing
Chrome-confirmed false positives: a write or action in a `createRoot` body written in an effect apply (inline, through a named closure, by name), and a write in a component rendered only in an owner-backed `onSettled`, a `createTrackedEffect` callback or an effect apply, were SC2001/SC2002 violations, while rc.17 throws `PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK` or `PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE` at the creation (rendering creates a transparent root, `solid.dev.js:44-69`, `dev-shared.js:1455-1495`) and the body never runs. ADR 0458's `SiteReach` now asks whether an entry is refused (`refused_entry`): a component tag, or a callback argument (literal or reference) of a primitive SC3001's `cleanup_rule` forbids under a leaf (`creates_forbidden_primitive`, not `onCleanup`/`flush`), written in the own extent of an effect apply or a `CallbackOwner::Leaf` callback. Such a callback is never entered, nor is a named function, even at module scope, whose calls and renders are all refused or unreached and whose references are all refused, unreached or erased. The backend makes every rule's violation there uncertifiable with its own wording (`UnreachedSite::refused_creation`). A render elsewhere keeps the violation. Lexical: an `onSettled` called out of band is no leaf at runtime, so a write in a root there is uncertifiable where it may be a violation (a cost, never a new report); an effect cleanup, helper or IIFE between the apply and the creation is not followed (unchanged). Fixture `write-in-refused-creation` (8 uncertifiable, 4 violations; each on the rc.17 fast path). Review amendment: the refusal also holds through layers the apply or leaf callback runs in place (`creation_refused_at` climbs `layer_run`'s in-place layers: an IIFE, a helper calling its callback), since rc.17's guard covers the whole callback run; a layer that also runs elsewhere, or a site past an await or possible suspension, keeps its answer. Before this, `(() => createRoot(f1))()` in an apply was a violation on main, and ADR 0471 extended it to a named `f1`.

<a id="adr-0476"></a>
## ADR 0476 — The store setter's window is a dynamic extent, and a write past the root is unproven
The fuzzer's Chrome probes (witnessed: the raw value read back) found SC2003 wrong both ways around the own-setter exemption (ADR 0425), which was lexical. A write the setter's callback hands to `queueMicrotask`, `setTimeout` or `.then` runs after `storeSetter` closed the window (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev.js:5847-5866`, `inDraft` `:4955-4957`) and is dropped with STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER, yet the checker was silent; a closure defined outside `setStore(…)` and called inside it commits, yet was reported. `store_write_standing` (`upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`) now answers exempt, dropped or unproven. The write is climbed out through in-place layers (`layer_run`: an IIFE, an inline standard-library callback, a helper calling its callback), crossing JSX, to the setter's own callback or the store's compute function. A fresh-stack scheduler, or an await on every path before the write, drops it; a possible await leaves it unproven. A named function is in the window when every reference is a call in one, by name or as a method, or the setter's own argument (`setStore(bump)`); exact `const` aliases of the setter open the same window, and a write through an exact `const` alias of the store is the store's (legal.mjs found it reported, Chrome-confirmed). One call outside drops the write on that run. A hand-out, an export, a binding that may change, a method an unresolved call in the window may reach, a caller chain deeper than three, an IIFE in JSX inside the callback, and an await in an async compute function leave it unproven, never dropped. Separately, a write past the root (`store.a.b = 1`, `store.items.push(1)`, `delete store.a.b`) is uncertifiable unless a nullish link makes it TypeScript's: the proxy serves a child only through `isWrappable` (`:2272-2283`), which refuses frozen and non-plain values, and no type states plainness, so a frozen intermediate was reported though the write lands. The root stays a violation, because `createStore` wraps its initial value unconditionally (`:5904-5915`). Owners: `shared_reactivity.rs`, `docs/rules/no-store-mutation-outside-setter.md`; fixture `store-setter-extent`; ownership cases `…/own-setter-deferred-write/001`, `…/own-setter-outside-closure/001`.

<a id="adr-0477"></a>
## ADR 0477 — A dispatch on an await continuation leaves the dispatch veto
A synchronous dispatch (ADR 0356) in an async, non-generator function after a dominating `await` of that same function (`follows_dominating_await`, ADR 0320's continuation: outside JSX, a compiler tracked region and an instance field) leaves ADR 0435's veto as caller class `await continuation`, for owner and read proofs: the continuation resumes from the microtask queue on an empty stack, so its delivery has no owner and no strict-read window (the premise ADR 0320 already rests on). A dispatch before the await, after an await under a condition, or in a closure written after the await (it runs on its caller's stack) keeps it. Found by the fuzzer: its entry (`scripts/fuzz/checker.mjs` `MAIN_SOURCE`, the oracle's `mainSource`) drives handlers from `async function exercise() { await pause(200); … element.dispatchEvent(…) }`, so every fuzzed and oracle program had its handler proofs withheld (an inline `onClick` write was SC2001 uncertifiable); the fuzzer now records such a premise per run (`entryVeto`). Owner: `indexes/event_delivery.rs`; mechanics: `docs/rules/missing-owner.md`; fixtures `event-dispatch-await-{continuation,before,conditional,closure}`.

<a id="adr-0478"></a>
## ADR 0478 — flush-in-action climbs the layers that run in place
SC2006's position proof read the call itself, so `flush` in an IIFE, a `[x].forEach` callback or a helper's callback in an action step was silent, though the rule page's probes show a synchronous helper throws `FLUSH_IN_ACTION`. The call now climbs ADR 0469's in-place layers (`climb_in_place_layers`: an IIFE, an inline standard-library callback, a helper calling its callback in its own body, in any module), and the step proof reads the outermost layer's call. A layer in a parameter list (ADR 0454) and a site after an await in its layer are not claimed; a helper that also hands its callback on still is, since one in-place run throws. The fuzzer's helper layers (cross-file included) had been certified false cleans. Owner: `static_api.rs` (`action_step_body`); fixture `rc9-flush-in-action-layers`.

<a id="adr-0479"></a>
## ADR 0479 — A named function with one call runs in place there
`layer_run` (ADR 0469) answers `InPlace(call)` for a named function when its binding holds it at every call (`function_value_is_current`, ADR 0221), it has exactly one call site (ADR 0448 credits only calls after the declarator), in its file, and every other reference is its declaration: nothing aliases, passes, stores or exports it, so it runs only on that call's stack. Excluded: methods, generators, a named function expression (also entered by its own name), an exported function or one a namespace hands out (a consumer outside the analysed files leaves no reference), and a symbol whose references are not known. Every in-place climb reads it, for violations and legal proofs alike; `layers_run_inline` now steps to the function owning the layer's call, not its lexical parent, since the one call may sit in a nested function (review found a write placed in the component body when the closure's only call was in an effect). The fuzzer's closure layer (`const f = () => …; f()`) had `flush-in-action` cases certified false clean. Moved: SC2006 on a named closure (`rc9-flush-in-action-layers`); clean where the write is legal at its one call (`store-setter-extent` DeepChain, `write-owner-in-cleanup` CleanupCalls*); violations where rc.17 throws (`write-detached-owner-helper` NullHelperOwnRoot, `write-named-closure-in-root` CleanupRootByNameInNestedFunction). Owner: `execution_role/write_roles.rs` (`sole_call_of_named_function`); fixture `write-named-function-one-call`.

<a id="adr-0480"></a>
## ADR 0480 — Literal slots and object-literal arrows are exact local aliases
ADR 0463's `local_alias_bindings` (solid-facts schema 82) also records a slot of a destructured `const` array literal (`const [a] = [r1]`; a spread ends the slots), a slot of a `const` array-literal holder whose every reference is a `const` array pattern's initializer (`const pair = [0, r1] as const; const [, a] = pair`: nothing writes, passes or exports it), and a slot of a destructured call of a `const` arrow whose expression body is an object literal of static data properties (`const useThing = () => ({ a: r1 }); const { a } = useThing()`: each call builds the literal fresh, its values `const` variables, never the arrow's parameters). Pass 5 gives each the source's facts, as for 0463's aliases. The store mutation rule's own-setter exemption (ADR 0476) closes its store set over these aliases both ways and reads a setter reached through one (`literal_alias_is_own_setter`): in the first cut the legal-context matrix found 36 Chrome-confirmed false positives there (a write through the alias inside its own setter). Not followed: a member read of the call's result (`thing.useIt()`), a block-bodied or imported hook, a holder read any other way. Owners: `solid-facts` `ast/visitor.rs` (`record_local_alias_candidates`), `ast/extract.rs` (`finish`); `upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`; fixture `local-alias-literal-slots`.

<a id="adr-0481"></a>
## ADR 0481 — A member read of an exclusive arrow result is the const it names
solid-facts (schema 83) records `local_alias_members`: a static member read `thing.key` is exactly the `const` variable the literal's `key` names when `thing` is a non-ambient, unexported `const` bound to a non-optional call of an ADR 0480 object-literal arrow, and every resolved reference to `thing` is the object of a static, non-optional member read neither written nor deleted (so nothing can replace the property). `callee_symbol` and `callee_symbols` answer such a callee with the source's symbol, so setter and action calls through it are judged as direct ones. Not yet followed: a store written through such a member (the store rule's ported root walk wants a bare identifier). Owners: `solid-facts` `ast/{visitor,extract,types}.rs`; `indexes/callees.rs`; fixture `local-alias-member-reads`.

<a id="adr-0482"></a>
## ADR 0482 — A member alias is a root for the ported rules
In `upstream_compat`, `member_root` stops at a member read that is exactly a `const` (ADR 0481) and returns it as the chain's root, and `source_symbol_at` resolves that span to the `const`'s symbol, so `holder.state.v = 1` writes the store's own property, as `state.v = 1` does. The store rule accepts such a root wherever it required a bare identifier (`bare_root`: the rc.0-rc.6 readonly-root exemption, the mutator and delete paths); `chain_links_verdict` counts no link at or inside it; the `delete` path ends its link walk at that root (a follow-up: it walked down to `holder` and skipped every delete through a member, 78 grid false cleans); the own-setter exemption reads an alias-member callee as the setter slot it names (`holder.setState(() => { holder.state.v = 1; })` commits). Upstream eslint-plugin-solid roots the write at `holder` and stays silent: a kept divergence, pinned by ownership cases `no-store-mutation-outside-setter/hook-member-write*`. Owners: `upstream_compat/mod.rs`, `upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`; fixture `local-alias-member-reads`.

<a id="adr-0483"></a>
## ADR 0483 — A computation created with no owner in a createEffect apply is SC6002
`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` throws `PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK` in dev from `assertNotInEffectCallback` (`dist/dev-shared.js:3947-3957`: `inEffectCallback && context === null`), which `createOwner` (`createRoot`) and `setupComputedNode` (every computation) call at creation. New rule `primitive-in-effect-callback` (SC6002, error) reports `createMemo`, `createEffect`, `createRenderEffect`, `createRoot`, `createProjection` and function-form `createSignal`/`createStore` (`Dialect::throws_creating_in_effect_callback`, read on rc.17 only; it rests on the answer that is `StandsDown` exactly for rc.17 signals, tested beside it) when the call runs on a `createEffect` apply's stack with no owner set between: SC2008's effect-phase walk in an owner-preserving mode (`ownerless_effect_phase_origin`), crossing `untrack`, IIFEs, inline standard-library callbacks and helpers but no other primitive callback.
