The marker is one global a nested synchronous effect run lowers, so the throw is proven only when no call runs earlier in the apply (a tagged template before the creation counts as one); otherwise, and through a helper, it is uncertifiable. A `createRenderEffect` apply (its first run is owned) is not claimed. An effect created there is SC4001 too (a different claim: the leak). Premise, shared with SC4001 and recorded in open-work: the apply runs from an ownerless drain (a synchronous `flush()` under an owner would run it under that owner; the review found this, and no census of such calls exists). Owners: `solid-dialect` (`dialect.rs`, `solid_2.rs`), `effect_phase.rs`, `static_api.rs`; rule page `primitive-in-effect-callback`; fixture `primitive-in-effect-callback`.

<a id="adr-0484"></a>
## ADR 0484 — The effect-phase walk crosses nested closures and in-place helpers
The walk SC2008 and SC6002 share (`effect_phase.rs`) refused every function nested in an exported one, since its `exported` test read the export statement's span, which holds the whole exported function, so a closure written in an exported component and called in place was never followed; it now asks `module_exports`, which excludes nested functions. A literal handed to a project helper that calls it in its own body on every completion and never hands it on or keeps it (`layer_run`'s `InPlace` at that call) is crossed to the helper's call, one helper layer. This fixes certified false cleans for `FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK` and `PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK`. Owner: `effect_phase.rs`; fixture `effect-phase-layers`.

<a id="adr-0485"></a>
## ADR 0485 — A helper's in-place callback after an await carries the continuation's reads
The read-after-await walk (`static_rules/after_await.rs` `visit_call`) followed IIFEs, `runWithOwner` and standard-library inline callbacks but no project helper's, so `run(() => count())` after the `await` was silent. A literal a project callee calls in its own synchronous body on every completion (`layer_run`'s in-place answers, ADR 0457: the exact callee, from project or dependency source) runs on the continuation's stack, so its synchronous prefix is reported as the continuation's (SC1002); one in-place run suffices. A helper that keeps or hands on its callback is not claimed. Moved: `solid2-precision` `ShadowedFilterAfterAwait` (a project `filter` method calling the callback) is SC1002 by that proof, not the name. Certified false cleans: 46 -> 10. Owner: `static_rules/after_await.rs`; fixture `after-await-helper-callback`.

Read summaries carried only tuple-returning primitives' accessors (`go_returned_source`, a Go-producer parity marker no ADR explains), so `const wrap = () => { m1(); }; wrap();` in a component body was silent while the direct `m1()` is SC1001 (rc.17 warns `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED`). An accessor-kind source bound to a plain identifier (`const m = createMemo(...)`) now enters `summary_source_symbols` (`discover_file.rs`); return eligibility is unchanged. Moved: `uncertifiable-repro/sc9012` (`useQuery`'s body reads its memo, called from a component body: SC1001; the escaped `Overview` stays uncertifiable). Cost: 57 more corpus SC1001 uncertifiable (memo reads through functions placed nowhere proven). Accepted: an async memo read through a helper is SC1001 where the direct read is SC5001 (ADR 0467); rc.17 throws or warns either way. Owner: `source_discovery/discover_file.rs`.

<a id="adr-0487"></a>
## ADR 0487 — The read-after-await rule reads member aliases
Both read-after-await paths (`after_await.rs` `report`'s dominance loop, `static_rules.rs` `call_accessor_symbol`) resolved a member callee to TypeScript's property symbol, so `thing.useIt()` on an ADR 0481 member alias was a typed obligation directly after the await and silent in a closure or helper. Both now ask `local_alias_member` first and read the `const` it names; they change together, since one alone made them disagree and turned two recall gaps into false cleans. ## ADR 0488 — A settled cleanup after an await is unowned
Owner emission applied ADR 0320's await continuation to effects, cleanups, context reads and boundary calls by operation span, which for `onSettled`'s returned cleanup is the callback, not a call. An `onSettled` call after an await of its async function on every path (its callee in the producer's `calls_after_await`) is now on the continuation: no owner is current, so a callback returning a cleanup throws `SETTLED_CLEANUP_UNOWNED` (rc.17), as anywhere unowned (SC4001). A possible continuation does not count. Owner: `owners/owner_emission.rs`; fixture `settled-cleanup-after-await`.

<a id="adr-0489"></a>
## ADR 0489 — The stored-literal walk follows a named function's one call
`runs_in_unproven_stored_literal` (`interproc/retained_literal.rs`) decides whether a call in a function literal in a component may be attributed through summaries. It continued outward from an IIFE's call but treated every named closure as stored, so a read in a component-level closure called only from a closure in an effect apply (`const relay = () => { peek(); }; relay();`) was claimed neither at its call nor at `relay()`. A name-bound function whose only run is its one call (ADR 0479, `layer_run`'s `InPlace`) now continues the walk from that call, like an IIFE. Owner: `interproc/retained_literal.rs`; fixture `write-named-function-one-call`.

<a id="adr-0490"></a>
## ADR 0490 — An async read in a named local function is unsuspendable where its call is
ADR 0462 followed a read written directly in a named local function (not a generator, binding never written) through direct calls only to a call in rendered JSX, dropping leaf-owner calls. So a call in a component body's strict-read window (`f1();`, also through a further named layer), at module scope, or in a leaf owner (`createTrackedEffect(() => { wrapUse(); })`, `onSettled`) was silent, though rc.17 throws `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ` or `PENDING_ASYNC_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE` (Chrome). `push_call_attributed_async_reads` now records the read at such a call with a direct read's role and leaf owner there: a body call counts only where `nested_literal_runs_during_body` holds; other calls keep walking outward. It is an SC5001 violation on a direct read's premises; `call_attributed` still makes only SC5003/SC5005 uncertifiable. Owner: `local_access.rs`; fixture `async-unsuspendable-call-attributed`.

<a id="adr-0491"></a>
## ADR 0491 — A store link the store's own initializer wrote, and nothing can reach raw, is wrapped
ADR 0476 left every store write past the root (`store.a.v = 1`, `store.items.push(1)`) uncertifiable: the proxy wraps a child only when `isWrappable` accepts its raw value (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev.js:2272-2283`), frozen or non-plain values are no type's, and a handed-out setter or store can install any value later. `links_proven_wrappable` (`upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs`) now proves wrapping where nothing can: the write's root resolves, through exact `const` aliases, to slot 0 of a function-local `const` array pattern of at most two slots and no rest, initialized by `createStore` (exact primitive) with one argument, a closed object literal with no getter, the setter slot absent or never referenced; every reference to the store or an alias is an alias initializer or the root of a static, non-optional member path that is written, deleted, called as an in-place array method other than `sort` on an array literal, or read where the literal holds a primitive; and each link of the write is a data property holding an object or array literal. The raw value is then a fresh literal no code can reach (no proxy leaves as a value, so no `$TARGET` and no freeze) and no write lands outside the setter, so the link is wrapped and the write dropped (`STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER`, Chrome). Applied after the ADR 0465 checks, only under strictNullChecks in a file `tsc` checks, so a nullish link stays TypeScript's. Moved: `solid2-precision` `profile.user.name` to violation. A store reached through a hook, object or tuple leaves as a value and stays uncertifiable. Review: a link must hold an object or array literal (a regular expression, which the facts class as primitive, and a cast primitive are served raw); a direct `eval` in the file refuses the proof (it reaches the setter unreferenced); a reported array call must write `length` (`push`, `pop`, `shift`, `unshift`, `splice`; `reverse`, `fill`, `copyWithin` may write nothing). Fixture `store-write-literal-links`.

<a id="adr-0492"></a>
## ADR 0492 — A leaf owner's callback runs the callbacks project helpers call in place
`leaf-owner-forbidden-call` (SC3001, `cleanup.rs`) judged a call in a leaf callback (`onSettled`, `createTrackedEffect`) only in the callback's own synchronous extent and followed non-primitive calls into the helper's body, so it missed a primitive in a callback the helper receives (`run(() => { createMemo(() => 1); })`, `function run(cb) { cb(); }`): the helper calls a parameter, naming no operation. rc.17 throws `PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE` (Chrome). The call now climbs ADR 0469's in-place layers (`climb_in_place_layers`) when every crossed layer is a callback handed to a project function (no standard-library or dependency declaration) and the outermost helper call is in the callback's own extent, not in a parameter list or after an `await`; the operation is reported where written. An IIFE, an inline standard-library callback (`forEach`, a `Promise` executor) and a named function keep their paths (`helper_forbidden_operations` with its argument-sensitive verdicts, the host-callback census), so nothing is reported twice and an IIFE whose argument disables the operation stays silent. A helper that hands its callback to a timer, stores it, or calls it after an `await` is not crossed. Fixture `leaf-scope-helper-callback`.

<a id="adr-0493"></a>
## ADR 0493 — An owner requirement in layers run after an await is on the continuation
Owner emission (`owners/owner_emission.rs`) put an effect, cleanup, context read or boundary call on an await's continuation (ADR 0320) only when written after the `await` itself; in an IIFE, a project helper's callback or a name-bound function called once after it, the owner was conditional (uncertifiable), though rc.17 warns `NO_OWNER_EFFECT` / `NO_OWNER_CLEANUP` / `NO_OWNER_CONTEXT` (Chrome). The operation now climbs ADR 0469's in-place layers (`climb_in_place_layers`) and is after the await when the climb follows an `await` on every path (`suspended == Some(true)`) and crosses no parameter list; an await on one path stays conditional. Review: no climb through an inline standard-library or dependency callback, which may run zero times (`[1].reduce(fn)`, an empty `forEach`; `climb_crosses_library_callback`, also for ADR 0494), or ending in a function that calls `getOwner()` anywhere (the push-time guard test follows only IIFEs). Amendment: ADR 0488's settled cleanup climbs likewise from its `onSettled` call. Fixture `owner-after-await-layers` (a helper handing its callback to `setTimeout` through a parameter stays a known miss).

<a id="adr-0494"></a>
## ADR 0494 — `resolve`/`until` in layers run in place inside a tracked callback
SC2004/SC2005 (`static_api.rs`, `resolve_tracked_scope`) asked only whether the call sat directly in a tracked callback, so in an IIFE, a helper's callback or a name-bound function called once in a memo compute it was uncertifiable, though the observer is active and rc.17 throws. ## ADR 0495 — SC6002's earlier-call test runs per layer
SC6002 (ADR 0483) proves `PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK` only where no call runs in the apply before the creating call (a synchronous effect run lowers the marker), so any helper on the walk made it uncertifiable. The effect-phase walk (`effect_phase.rs`) now records an earlier call per crossed layer (calls and tagged templates written in it before the path, outside its nested functions) and, for a helper, whether its body calls anything before its parameter (`parameter_called_first`, `callback_standing.rs`: exact project callee, not async or a generator, an identifier parameter, no `arguments`). With no helper crossed, the apply's positional test stands. Review: what may run first is anything in the layer before the path, parameter list and nested functions included (a getter or JSX component runs with no call written), except the inner layer the walk came from, plus what the path call's arguments evaluate outside the functions they create; in a helper, anything before its first call of the parameter, that call's arguments included; JSX counts as a call. ADR 0497 followed a store handed through an object-literal arrow only into destructuring, so the misuse matrix's `hook-member` binding (`const thing = useThing(); thing.useIt.a.v = 7`, 156 programs, Chrome: `STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER`) stayed uncertifiable. `links_proven_wrappable` now starts the root walk at the `const` an ADR 0481 member alias names, and `held_by_member_holder` accepts an arrow call initializing a function-local `const` holder whose every reference is the object of a static, non-optional member read not written, deleted or iterated, each read at the store's key being an exact member alias that is itself a contained store use: the holder never leaves, and the store reaches code only through that read. <a id="adr-0501"></a>
## ADR 0501 — The fuzzer's entry renders each component by name
The runtime oracle's entry (`scripts/runtime-oracle.mjs` `mainSource`) mounted every export through `Object.entries(mod)`, a namespace escape (ADR 0347) that leaves no placeable mount, so no proof needing a component's mount (ADR 0460's first run, ADR 0467's pending apply) could hold in a fuzzer case. A case's `oracle.json` may now list `mount`, its exported components: the entry imports them by name and renders each by its own top-level `render(() => <C />, host)` in the namespace's sorted order; the namespace loop after skips them, so other exports render as before and the hand-out stays. Without `mount` the entry is byte-identical. The generator lists every `export function` component (`scripts/fuzz/emit.mjs`); the checker materializes the same entry (`scripts/fuzz/checker.mjs` `mainSourceFor`).

<a id="adr-0502"></a>
## ADR 0502 — `schedule` withholds a render effect's first run as `defer` does
rc.17's `effect()` runs a render effect's first apply on the call only when neither `options.defer` nor `options.schedule` is set (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev.js:1865-1868`); with `schedule` it is enqueued and runs from the flush, not under the caller's owner. The checker read only `defer`. The dialect now answers `effect_apply_schedule_options` (`createRenderEffect`'s apply); a `schedule` set or unreadable makes the apply's owner conditional (`callback_owner_at_call`), never none, since which releases honour the key is not modelled per release, and a first-run read with it is not the caller's (`read_escapes_synchronous_extent`). This is a precondition for proving a first run (ADR 0503).

<a id="adr-0503"></a>
## ADR 0503 — A render effect's first run is on the call under a module-scope render
ADR 0460 left a write in a body's `createRenderEffect` apply uncertifiable. The first run is on the call (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev.js:1851-1869`, `runEffect` keeping the caller's `context`) unless `defer` or `schedule` is set, the first pass is born staged (`dist/dev-shared.js:4438-4462`) or lane-seated (`:4388-4396`), the compute errors or goes pending (`:4227-4240`), or a boundary above shows its fallback (`dist/dev.js:1957`); and nothing placed the mount. `first_run_proven_at_call` (`execution_role/write_roles.rs`) excludes each: no options argument; a function-literal compute, not async or a generator, that calls, reads, references, awaits, renders and throws nothing; the call on every completion of the body; and a synchronous mount (`Dialect::mount_runs_component_synchronously`: `render`, not `hydrate`, `@solidjs/web` `dist/web.dev.js:542-568`) at module top level whose first argument is an arrow whose whole body is the component's self-closing tag (`mounted_by_module_render`, `owners/async_context.rs`). Module scope is outside every flush, lane, transition and boundary, so a write in that first run throws `REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE` (an action, `ACTION_CALLED_IN_OWNED_SCOPE`). One such mount suffices. A `schedule` that may keep the first run off the call (ADR 0502) leaves a write directly in the apply unplaced (it was silent). Fuzzer cases carry such a mount (ADR 0501). Fixture `render-apply-first-run` (violations `FirstRunWrite`, `FirstRunWriteLayered`; uncertifiable `Unmounted`, `Scheduled`, `OptionsVariable`, `ComputeReads`, `ConditionalEffect`, `Hydrated`, `RenderedLater`). Review: the compute must be one primitive literal, returned or the whole body, with no parameter or one plain one (a dynamic `import()`, a literal thenable or a destructuring parameter keeps the first run off the call); the rendered binding must hold its function (`function_value_is_current`: a `let` reassigned before the render); and the `render` must run on every module evaluation (outside a class, in straight-line module flow, a completion site; solid-facts schema 85 gives the module its own frame for `try` blocks and exits, so a top-level `try { render(…) }` is straight-line).

<a id="adr-0504"></a>
## ADR 0504 — A render-effect apply's first read of a source pending since the body is SC5001
ADR 0467 kept an apply's read of an async source SC1001: rc.17's `read()` throws `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ` for a pending source before it would warn (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev-shared.js:5320-5325`, `:5441-5446`), and nothing ordered the first run against the settling. A render effect's first run is on the call (ADR 0503, `dist/dev.js:1869`), and a halt does not stop it (only `schedule` checks one, `dist/dev-shared.js:2038-2042`). `pending_apply_first_read` (`execution_role/write_roles.rs`) proves the read is in that run: it sits in a function-literal `createRenderEffect` apply, directly or through in-place layers crossing no library callback, list, await or callback-handing helper, after no written apply call (an earlier throw ends the run); ADR 0503 places the call on the mount; the source is a same-body `const` declared on every completion before the call, `createMemo` of one async function literal, pending from creation until a microtask (`:4626`, `:3642-3660`), with no `loadingValue`; and nothing between source and call runs code (each call there creates a signal or memo or calls a `const`-bound local function that calls nothing). The row (`AsyncRead::apply_first_run_pending`; at a closure's call, `pending_origin`/`pending_apply`) is an SC5001 violation, and the strict read at the site or with that origin in that apply is withdrawn, since the run throws first. Not built (review): a `createEffect` apply, whose first run in `render`'s tail flush any pending render effect in the mount (a fragment, a helper's JSX or effect, a module-level effect) holds until the source settles. Fixture `strict-read-async-apply` gains `main.tsx`: `RenderApplyReadsAsync`, `RenderApplyReadsAsyncRendered` move SC1001 -> SC5001 (their rendered reads' SC5003 a violation, the mount resolved); `ApplyReadsAsync`, `ApplyReadsAsyncRendered`, `ApplyComputeReadsAsync`, `ApplyComputeWaitsOnOtherAsync`, the review's `SharedClosure`, `ReadAfterCall`, `BodyReadsFirst` and the unmounted `UnmountedApplyReadsAsync` keep SC1001 for apply reads.

<a id="adr-0505"></a>
## ADR 0505 — A user effect's first apply under a static first mount meets its source pending
ADR 0504 left a `createEffect` apply SC1001: its first run, queued at the call, runs in `render`'s tail `flush()` (`@solidjs/web@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/web.dev.js:542-568`), which a pending render effect in that flush or a transaction an earlier pass left holds (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev-shared.js:1690-1733`, `:2180`). `user_apply_runs_in_mount_flush` (`execution_role/write_roles.rs`) adds premises so nothing can create such a node:
- the component renders a static intrinsic tree: lowercase tags only, no attribute, spread, expression child, fragment or component;
- the body runs only the effect, the source, signals or memos of primitive literals and calls of inert local `const` functions; it reads no member, has no `throw`, class or tagged template, and destructures only local literals;
- the source's async compute is one primitive literal;
- the mounting `render` is the first code that can create a node. The memo `ModuleScopeRuns` lists project files whose module scope may run code (a call, member, reference, JSX, class, `await`, `throw`, method or hazard) and whether any file loads a module other than Solid's own (whose evaluation only installs hooks) or an analyzed project file. Only the entry may run code; before the call, only standard-library code and local `const` functions running only that, referencing no import but `render`.

solid-facts schema 86 records `throw_statements`. Fixture `effect-apply-first-run`: two positives, one variant per premise; `strict-read-async-apply`'s `ApplyReadsAsync` (the first render) moves SC1001 -> SC5001. Mounts by a child, router, loop or `hydrate`, and later renders, stay SC1001. Review (also ADR 0504): the read must be on every completion of the apply and each in-place layer up to it (`read_on_every_completion_of_apply`), and a closure-carried read on every completion of each function up to the call (`push_call_attributed_async_reads`); `if (v === 1) return;` or a loop that may not run, before the read in the apply or a closure it calls, was SC5001 though the first run skips the read. Fixture rows `RenderApplyGuarded` and the `guarded` variant. Fuzzer: legal contexts `{effect,render}-apply-guarded` and `-guarded-closure` (container `guard-return`); the legal judge counts an SC5001 violation the runtime never matches, at an unexecuted site or anywhere else, as a false positive; probes `pending-async/apply-guarded-first-run`, `apply-guarded-closure`.
<a id="adr-0506"></a>
## ADR 0506 — Reachability follows function literals a call runs or receives; a call-result binding's calls run nothing
The fuzzer's mount axis renders each misuse component as a parent's child, not exported, and found two defects:
- **False cleans (364 per mount, confirmed in Chrome).** A write in an IIFE or helper callback in such a component was dropped and the program certified: reachability (`reachability.rs`) reached functions only as roots, which only functions nested in exported declarations are. Now a function literal a call invokes (an IIFE's callee) or receives (a project helper's callback, `[1].forEach(fn)`) is reached when the call is, and (review) so is every function created in a reached function's body or parameter list; before, an arrow a private component only returned or stored (`(() => () => set(1))()`, `[() => set(1)]`) was certified. Reaching says nothing about when a function runs; the write and read roles decide.
- **A false positive, already on #141.** `const wrapped = wrap(() => set(1)); wrapped()` was a violation even when `wrap` never runs the arrow: `functions_by_symbol` names the function nested in a call initializer for the binding, and the call graph took each call of the binding as a call of it, though the binding holds the call's result. The mapping stays as identity evidence (server functions a `query` wraps, `lazy` loaders' tags). `SymbolFunction::Resolved` records `through_call`, and `CallSitesOf` drops such a symbol's call and render sites (census table `FileCallResultSymbols`). `lazy(loader)`'s result is not such a binding: its render runs the loader (ADR 0292).

Moved: `use-site-role`'s `UsesWrite`, violation -> uncertifiable (its write in `writeOf`'s memo was proved only by the unsound call path; the use-site arm places no write). `Tdz.tsx`'s module-scope `wrap` arrow, `unplaced-site-obligations`, `write-scope-roots-rc3/rc9` and `uncertifiable-repro/sc1001` gain uncertifiable rows for newly reached code. Fixtures `private-component-layers`, `call-result-binding`. Fuzzer: `--mounts root,child,show,for` on both grids; probes `write/private-child-iife`, `write/call-result-binding`, `write/private-returned-closure`, `write/private-array-element`. Unchanged: a write a helper runs only on a branch its arguments rule out (`maybe(cb, false)`), in `[].forEach`, or in a component JSX renders in a dead branch is claimed wherever it may run (the `SiteReach` convention, ADR 0457), as in exported components.

<a id="adr-0507"></a>
## ADR 0507 — A child an inert parent renders is mounted as its parent is
ADRs 0503-0505 prove a first run under a module-scope `render` of the component itself. `module_render_sites` (`owners/async_context.rs`) now also returns a mount through up to four parents (`MountSite::parents`). The child's tag must be:
- self-closing, with no attribute, spread or child;
- in the parent's own body, in the argument of its one (unconditional, straight-line) return or its expression body;
- reached from there through intrinsic elements only, each a child of the next (`static_child_tag`).

The compiler evaluates such a tag (`createComponent`) while the parent builds its tree, so the child's body runs inside the parent's, outside any computation. The parent must run nothing else (`inert_parent`): its body is one `return <tree>` (review: an earlier statement may coerce, iterate or read a throwing getter, ending the mount), with plain parameters, no call, member read, tagged template, class, `throw`, nested function or fragment, and no JSX but static intrinsic elements and the tag. A pending earlier pass of the mount stages later ones (`@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev-shared.js:1690-1708`), and a throw ends it. ADR 0505's other premises (`render_runs_first`, the static tree) apply to the root render. Not built: `<Show>`, `<For>` and other components, which run children in a computation or later. Residual, as in ADRs 0503-0505, which assume the body completes normally: an implicit throw in the component's body before the site (a coercion through a throwing `toString`), and a loop that never ends. Fixture `child-mount-first-run` (violations `PlainParent` SC2001, `Grand` SC5001 through two parents, variant `effect-child` SC5001 through ADR 0505; uncertifiable `CallingParent`, `ShowParent`, `PropParent`, `ConditionalParent`). Probe `write/child-mount-first-run`.

<a id="adr-0508"></a>
## ADR 0508 — `<Show when={true}>` and a one-row `<For>` render their child during the mount
ADR 0507's climb from a child's tag to the parent's returned tree may now pass two holders besides intrinsic elements:
- The dialect's `<Show when={true}>`: one attribute, `when`, the literal `true`, no spread, its one child the climbed node (`shown_child`). Its value memo computes when `Show` is created (`computed` runs a non-`lazy` node then, `@solidjs/signals@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/dev-shared.js:4913-4932`) and reads `props.children` when the condition holds (`solid-js@2.0.0-rc.17` `dist/solid.dev.js:1571-1600`), rendering the child inside that compute.
- The dialect's `<For each={[…]}>`: one attribute, `each`, a non-empty array literal of primitive literals, no spread, its one child a parameterless arrow whose expression body is the tag (`for_row`). `For` builds its `mapArray` on the first read of its result (`dist/solid.dev.js:1548-1562`), which the parent's insert makes on its first pass.

The child's body then runs during the parent's tree under an owner (the memo or the row), and a render effect created there runs its first apply on the call (`dist/dev.js:1851-1869`). `inert_parent` admits the holders and the row arrow. Not built: a non-literal `when` or `each`, `keyed`, `fallback`, a row taking the item, `<Switch>`/`<Match>`, `<Index>`. Fixture `child-mount-first-run`: `ShowParent` (uncertifiable under ADR 0507) and new `ForParent` are violations; new negatives `ShowBindingParent`, `ForEmptyParent`, `ForItemParent`. Probes `write/show-mount-first-run`, `pending-async/for-mount-first-run`. Review (Chrome-checked): whitespace text without a line break is a child the compiler keeps (`<For> {() => <C />} </For>` hands `For` an array, and the row never runs); and ADR 0503's first-run write, like ADR 0504's read, now needs no call written before it in the apply (`apply_runs_nothing_before`), since a pending read there throws first (fixture row `render-apply-first-run` `ReadThenWrite`; probes `write/for-inline-whitespace`, `write/apply-write-after-pending-read`).

<a id="adr-0509"></a>
## ADR 0509 — A value a caller hands over is followed, or its use stays open
The fuzzer's source axes (PR #149) hand the misuse component its signal, store, action or async memo through a prop, a context, a hook's return or a module export. Causes and fixes:
- **Setters and stores found by type alone were silent.** On rc.17 a setter not proven `createSignal`'s took the role that reports nothing (an optimistic setter meets no guard); it is now unplaced unless proven `createOptimistic`'s. The three store rules read only proven stores; a typed store is now an obligation. The demand plan types names destructured from a non-primitive call (`const [, set] = useCount()`).
- **Accessor transport carried reads only.** A hook's returned object or tuple slot and a context value now also resolve to `Callable::Setter`/`Callable::Action` (in `write_call_targets`, out of every read consumer's table); a post-pass writes or acts with the source's row, replacing a typed one, and gives a transported async read its async row.
- **A call of a prop's value was dropped.** Pass 10 records per prop the exact sources visible sites pass (`PassedSources`) and whether a getter calls an async source; `props.name(…)` on the exact parameter, directly in the component or a primitive's callback, reads, writes or acts with each (one witness suffices); deeper, or with any other value passed, it is an obligation (`closed-parameter-member-dispatch`).
- **One fail-closed net for the rest (`handed-member-dispatch`).** A call whose callee has no row and is, through members, aliases, destructuring or local literals, a caller-supplied value (props or a view, `useContext`, a project function's result, an alias of an imported source) is an obligation where it would report a write, action or untracked read, or may run in a leaf owner; for a callee typed as a Solid accessor (declared in an installed Solid package, ADR 0509's `descriptor_type_role`), only for a read. It also covers store writes past such a root, `refresh` of an unresolved target, reads after an `await` through helpers, argument closures and IIFEs, and an unproven helper call of a prop read. Pushed last, it yields to any other cause at its site. Correction: transport drops it only where the target's summary is complete (ADR 0686), and a hook's result used unbound or iterated is such a value too (ADR 0689).
- **A strict read of an async source that may meet it pending is unproven** (rc.17 throws first): an obligation, fixing the module/context async wrong-rule class.
 Cost: two real-app clean repro cases (`clean-context-provider`, `clean-store-draft`) are uncertifiable. Not built: context proofs in an open program, props through views, the producer's empty origin module for published `.d.ts` files (typed rules elsewhere still need a stub's `declare module`).

<a id="adr-0510"></a>
## ADR 0510 — A module-scope or parent-handed async memo meets the first run pending; nothing in a layer runs before the read
ADR 0509 left an apply's read of an unseen async source an obligation. Three sources now meet ADR 0504/0505's first run pending (`pending_apply_first_read`, `execution_role/pending_sources.rs`), each `createMemo` of one async function literal with no options, pending until a microtask:
- **A module-scope memo** with a literal compute, in a project module whose scope only creates (`module_scope_only_creates`: each call a `const`'s whole initializer, `createMemo` of a literal compute, `createSignal` of at most one literal, `createContext`; nothing else runs), read under a mount whose `render` passes `render_runs_first` (no TLA, every module seen) from an entry no project module imports (the `ModuleScopeRuns` census now also lists creation-only modules and imported files; the entry is then a root of evaluation, so every module it loads has evaluated, ECMAScript `InnerModuleEvaluation`), the component with plain parameters and nothing in its body before the effect (`body_runs_nothing_between` from the body's start). Unread, such a memo joins no transaction (`joined` false, `:4520`), and a parking seam skips a loading source (`:2194-2204`), so `render_runs_first` admits such modules for ADR 0505 too.
- **A parent's memo** in a mount site's last parent, as an exact prop (`<C data={data} />`, `props.data()` on the one plain, never-written parameter) or through one provider of a module-scope `createContext` (`<Ctx value={{ k: data }}>`, `useContext(Ctx).k()`; the provider sets its value on a root before `children` builds the memos the parent's insert reads). ADR 0507's climb admits such parents: `const` creations before the one return, tag attributes naming them (`handed_attribute`), the provider as a holder (`provided_child`).
- **A static tree** (ADR 0505) may hold string attributes, primitive-literal children (`insert` of a non-function places it with no effect) and reads of the body's own signal or synchronous memo of a literal (`settled_expression_child`); an async memo's read still holds the flush.

Correction (Chrome-confirmed false positives of ADRs 0503-0505): only the apply's own earlier calls were checked, so `(() => { JSON.parse("{"); a1(); })()` was SC5001 (a write there SC2001). `runs_nothing_before_in_layers` now walks each layer up from the site: no call in its body before the position or among the layer call's arguments before the function, a project helper calling the argument first (`parameter_called_first`, ADR 0495), and a wrapper's body too (`push_call_attributed_async_reads`). Wrong rule: a read through a local function whose call got no local-access row (`wrapUse()` after an `action` creation) kept SC1001; it gets ADR 0509's opening row. Review (Chrome-confirmed false positives): `const v = props.cfg.x;` (an `any`, undefined) before the effect, or `(v as any).a.b;` before the read, throw first but were claimed; `throws_nothing_before` now also refuses, in each layer and in the body before the effect, a member read, computed member, spread or iteration, operator, `delete`, destructuring (except a creation's tuple or ADR 0505's local literal), tagged template, class, `throw` or loop, and a layer with a pattern parameter. A second root reaching the source's module (a project file none imports, `warm.ts`: `import "./source"`) refuses: a page loading it first settles the memo in the checkpoint between the two scripts (the census now records import edges); premise: no script outside the project's files imports the source. The body's name for an imported source is the read's own resolved callee, not an import matched by name. Fixture `pending-source-first-run`.

<a id="adr-0511"></a>
## ADR 0511 — Handed and hook-created async memos meet the first run pending through layers and closed aliases
Shapes ADR 0510 left uncertifiable SC5001 although rc.17 throws `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ`:
- **Props read through layers.** ADR 0509 placed `props.k()` only directly in the component or a primitive's callback (`props_call_placed`), deeper an obligation. `props_call_pending_in_layers` (`local_access.rs`) places it where every source a visible site passes is an async accessor `pending_apply_first_read` proves from the call (its climb walks an IIFE, a closure called once, a helper calling its callback first; fixture `pending-source-layers`). A body-defined wrapper the apply calls once (`const wrapUse = () => { props.a1(); }`) is read at `wrapUse()`, as for an own source (`discover_props_call`, `pending_apply_first_read_from`); a second call keeps it.
- **A memo a hook of another module creates** (`const { a1 } = useVals()`, `const vals = useVals(); vals.a1()`; the transport already carried the read to the memo's declaration). `hook_source_pending` (`pending_sources.rs`) proves it where the hook is inert (`inert_hook`: a sync, non-generator, parameterless project function running only inert creations, throwing nothing, its one straight-line `return` a literal of data properties naming its bindings), creates the memo with a literal compute on every completion, and the body calls it once before the effect (`hook_call_in_body`: no argument, the exact unwritten hook, every completion, a `const` name or object pattern). `BodyAdmits::hook` lets ADR 0505/0510's body premises admit that call and an object pattern of its result (`hook_result_destructuring`). The read must go through that call's own result (`read_through_hook_call`); another instance's read (`shared.a1()` of a module-scope `useVals()`, settled before mount) was SC5001 where rc.17 warns SC1001 (`hook-shared-instance`).
- **Closed aliases of handed values** (`const d = props.k`, `const d = ctx.k`, `const { d } = { d: props.k }`) hit SC9012 before the proof. Two exact steps: the resolver's escape census (`census_rows`, `accessor_provenance/resolver_index.rs`) no longer counts as an escape a Get of a named member held whole by a one-name `const`, a data property or an array element (`gets`): it copies the value (a named write of the member keeps its veto); and an object pattern of an inline object literal follows a slot whose own value is transported (`resolver_values.rs`; a local source keeps ADR 0463's alias facts). For props, `handed_member_alias` (`pending_sources.rs`) follows both forms to `props.k`, and `discover_call` treats the alias as the prop's call (ADR 0509's `discover_props_call`). The pending proof takes the alias declared in the body before the effect, `only_called_members` admits it, and `BodyAdmits::handed` lets the body read named members of the handed object (a data property of the object the proven mount passes). A passed function is followed through the alias; a write in it stays claimed at the passed arrow.
- **`<Loading>` holders (researched, then built).** rc.17's `Loading` returns `createLoadingBoundary(() => props.children, …)`, which builds the children in `computed(fn, { name: "children" })` at creation. Chrome: a child's render effect meets the parent's memo pending, as does a user effect under a static tree; a render-effect write throws; a user effect whose tree reads the memo waits for the reveal and warns `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED`. Built: `loading_child` (`owners/async_context.rs`) lets ADR 0507's climb pass `<Loading fallback={<primitive literal>}>` with no `on`, no spread and one child; ADR 0505's static tree limits the user-effect case to boundaries with nothing pending below. `suppress_findings_owned_by_enabled_rules` (`projection.rs`) drops an SC1001 at an SC5001's span only for a proven pending read; if both are uncertifiable both stay (rc.17 warns `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED`).
- **Aliases of an imported source** (`const d = a1`, `const { d } = { d: a1 }`, `a1` a named runtime import) hit SC9012, since ADR 0463's exact alias is a `const` of the file. File rows now record one-name module-scope `const`s (`FileRows::module_consts`) and mark such aliases (`AliasRow::import`, syntactic); the alias pass treats one as exact where the accessor it names is declared by one of those `const`s (an import is a live binding of it). `local_alias_mention_withheld` and the initialization census (`names_local_alias`) withhold a mention that may precede the alias's declarator, as for a local alias: an alias declared after the effect is in its temporal dead zone at the first run. Tuples of an imported source stay open with the handed ones.
- **Tuples: not built** (`const [, d] = [0, props.k] as const`, or of a context member or imported source). ADR 0282's census follows a slot only where no code may change the array iterator and the value is proven no code constructor (`string_code_reference`). Building tuples needs that proof for an import of a primitive-created `const`, a provider's value or callers' values; not built. The fuzzer's harness entry calls `make(type)`, an unresolved call of a non-literal argument, which alone sets ADR 0282's veto in every grid program.

<a id="adr-0512"></a>
## ADR 0512 — The fuzzer's fast path is Chrome, driven by a Rust executor
The differential fuzzer's fast path runs in headless Chromium, not Happy DOM: one Vite server with the Solid plugin serving cases from memory, one reused page per worker, a fresh document per program, on Chrome's virtual time. Projects, `tsrs -b` batches, checker runs and pages (over CDP, with Playwright's switches) run in a Rust executor (`scripts/fuzz/exec`, its own workspace); verdicts stay in JS. Trust is measured: a stratified runtime differential against `feedback run` gives records identical to the host's (both keep 41 frames; the oracle runs `--source-instrumentation off`, whose `withOperation` wrapper frames moved 8 of 4,340 sampled records), and hosts are compared record for record after each host change. Loops: `make fuzz-focus CODES=…`; `make fuzz-smoke` (ADR 0916); full grids before a PR. `--fast-host happy-dom` remains. Owners: `scripts/fuzz/chrome-host.mjs`, `exec/`, `focus.mjs`, `smoke.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0513"></a>
## ADR 0513 — The fuzzer measures recall on axes the base grids do not draw
`--recall` grids (`mx-recall`, `lg-recall`) cross each new binding, callee form, layer, callback form, mount and file source with every cell's base grid, adding recall-only contexts and a `LOADING_ON_OUTSIDE_HOLD` template; mutation mode pairs each misuse shape with its legal twin, checker only. They are opt-in, so base grids, seeds and baselines stay byte-identical, and out of `fuzz-focus` by default. Owners: `scripts/fuzz/misuse.mjs`, `mounts.mjs`, `matrix.mjs` (`recallAxes`), `mutation.mjs`.

<a id="adr-0514"></a>
## ADR 0514 — The fuzzer's browser is the pinned Playwright's; a Linux VM where process starts are gated
Without `--browser`, the oracle and the Chrome host run the headless shell the pinned Playwright names (`browsers.json` revision, the platform's cache and layout; `scripts/playwright-browser.mjs`) and stop when it is absent, never taking the cache's newest build (invariant 7: a cache held Chrome 156 where Playwright 1.60.0 pins 148). `scripts/fuzz/vm.sh` runs the fuzzer in a Colima VM on a Mac whose security agents gate every process start: equal outcomes, 3.7x faster than the best macOS setting.

<a id="adr-0515"></a>
## ADR 0515 — Exact copies, named callbacks and assigned callees the fuzzer's recall grid found silent
Each is the same value or call by construction, so every rule follows it (fail-closed facts, no name matching). In `solid-facts`: a `const` initialized by a conditional whose arms are both one `const` (`c ? r : r`) or by an inline identity arrow on it (`((x) => x)(r)`) aliases that variable (ADR 0463's fact); a slot of `const { k } = N` over a `const N = S` holding a namespace import is `S.k` (ADR 0459's fact); `(slot = f)()`, a plain assignment to a binding as callee, invokes `f` (ADR 0468's peel; not in an `async` function, whose after-await owner placement reads the operands and went silent on the peel). In the IR, a primitive's callback position names a function through an exact `const` copy only when every reference to the copy follows its declarator in the declarator's function (a copy reached from a nested function may run first and throw), and through a named function expression's binding whose own name is unreferenced (reads and `by_name_position` alike); a read in a function nested in a named callback climbs the in-place layers to its call, as in an inline callback.

<a id="adr-0516"></a>
## ADR 0516 — The recall grid's remaining false cleans fail closed, and four spellings are exact
Fable's read of the round-2 recall grid named ten causes. Without a sound proof the site is now an obligation, never silent: a function handed whole through a copy or object-literal slot no callback edge follows has unplaced reads and an owner seed both owned and unowned (`function_handed_unfollowed`); a named write callback at several positions or behind an adjusted inline role is unknown (`write_role_walk`); `f.call`, `f.apply` and `f.bind(…)(…)` of a local setter, action or accessor are `reactive-dispatch-unresolved`, except in an event handler, where every rule they stand for is legal; a read in a loop that awaits is after a suspension from the loop's start (`loop_suspension_start`); a hook value handed on after a suspension is an SC1002 obligation. A sync generator called in place whose iterator is used (`generator_iterator_used`: the result is not a discarded statement) runs its body where stepped, which nothing follows: an owner seed both ways, an SC2008/SC6002 origin marked mixed, an SC2006 step and SC1002 continuation marked uncertain, an open leaf-scope obligation; a discarded iterator stays silent. A named helper an action body calls, a named apply and a helper with callers outside the effect are reached (SC2006, SC2008) as uncertain unless every run is proven. Exact, so violations: a slot of a `let` destructuring of a namespace import nothing writes or redeclares (not a hoisted `var`), an exported slot (it holds the export wherever read; an importer's mention is its own import binding), a pattern nested over a literal holding the namespace once, a getter whose body is one `return` and a default the literal leaves unset (solid-facts schema 87), and `(x ||= h)()`'s operands. Not built: a namespace of a project module that `export *`s Solid, `LOADING_ON_OUTSIDE_HOLD` (no rule). Costs, uncertifiable where silent: an exported generator iterator, a copy handed on before its declarator, `runWithOwner(null, named)` after an await, a read after a loop that may await. Gate review: callback targets resolve a `const` copy, a `const` function expression and an object literal's member handed whole (`exact_function_argument`, ADR 0358's receiver closure), replacing an owned-and-unowned seed; a stepped generator gets a `MayRun` edge (after a suspension, `MayBeUnowned`), passing its call's role through for reads and, for writes, only from a handler; `f.call`/`f.bind()()`/array `f.apply` and a total composite callee are judged as `f()` at the written call (`discover_call_as`), a non-total one clean only where every operand's call is; an unwritten `let` function is current. Fable review: a default for a key `Object.prototype` supplies never applies, and a generator a scheduler calls runs none of its body (`MayBeUnowned`); fixture `dispatch-and-copies`. Settled cleanup (ADR 0488): an `onSettled` call that may follow an await (a loop's later iteration, a skippable await) takes the possible-continuation placement at the call, not its callback: uncertifiable (fixture `settled-cleanup-after-await`, `LoopAwaitLater`). Effect phase by value (`effect_phase::named_layer`): an apply named by a named function expression's binding (ADR 0515's `named_expression_binding`), its `const` copies (alias facts, transitively) or an object literal's data-property slot (only through ADR 0358's receiver closure, never the property's symbol, which another object of the type may share) is followed like the binding; any reference that is neither an effect-phase position nor a call the walk proves makes the origin mixed. SC6002 and SC2008 move from silent or `missing-owner` alone to their own rule (fixture `rc13-flush-in-effect-callback`: `applyCopy`, `expressionApply`, `applyBox`, uncertifiable `sharedCopy`, `sharedBox`). Leaf owner by value (`containing_leaf_owner`): a function whose every value position (`owners::exact_value_positions`: its binding, copies, a receiver-closed slot) is a leaf slot of one owner, with no other use, runs under that owner, its reads judged as in the leaf (fixture `dispatch-and-copies`, `LeafByCopy`).

<a id="adr-0517"></a>
## ADR 0517 — The recall grid's certified false cleans: exact names, climbed layers, and obligations where no proof exists
The baseline of `0acd56c72` (all eight grids, every code) had 3,044 false cleans on `mx-recall`, most certified, none elsewhere; Chrome confirmed three false positives before a declarator. Each fix is exact or an obligation; none matches a name. Before a declarator: a callback position whose mention may precede the function's declarator (`const early = () => createMemo(write); early(); const write = …`) gives the function an unknown role, and a violation after an unordered mention written before its declarator in the same function (`[1].forEach(() => { r(); count(); })`) is uncertifiable. Namespace barrels: a static member of a namespace import of a project module (`import * as S from "./solid"` over `export *` or a named re-export) is the primitive its property's symbol resolves to; SC2008 and SC6002 take the package from the callee's exact symbol's declarations when Type Facts relocates no signature (one package, else nothing). After an await: the async computation is found through ADR 0516's `exact_function_argument` (a named function expression's binding, an object-literal slot), and a composite callee the producer never lists (`(slot = r)()`) in the function's own body (total, one accessor, dominated: a read; else an obligation). A read summary carries `r.call(…)`, `r.bind(…)()` (decided) and composite callees (undecided). Named callbacks: one named at a `createRoot`/`runWithOwner` position is placed at the call when that is its sole entry (`by_name_position`), JSX it writes keeping the compiler's role, else unknown (not async callbacks); a read in its in-place layers climbs to the layer's call, past the callback's own awaits; `named_callback_write_role` climbs layers before scanning positions, and several owner-keeping positions with no rejection are unknown; `resolve`/`until` climbed into a function a tracked position names is SC2004/SC2005. `LOADING_ON_OUTSIDE_HOLD`: rule `loading-on-outside-hold` (SC5006, warning) is an obligation only, at a `<Loading on>` (or spread) whose children render a component, hold an unproven read, or read an async source referenced outside it; a constant `on` never re-arms; the fuzzer claims the code, placeless. Open programs: the provider census runs, and a read with no provider found is SC4002 uncertifiable (amends ADR 0352). Probes: `scripts/fuzz/probes/{tdz,barrel,named-callback}/`, `read-after-await/exact-*`, `strict-read/dispatch-*`; fixtures `named-callback-tdz`, `namespace-solid-barrel`, `after-await-exact-callbacks`, `dispatch-read-through-closure`, `loading-on-outside-hold`, `named-inheriting-callback`, `rc13-flush-in-effect-callback/Barrels.ts`.

<a id="adr-0518"></a>
## ADR 0518 — A top-level `await` leaves pending-read findings unproven
SC5003 (`async-outside-loading-boundary`) claimed every rendered read of an async source outside a `Loading` boundary, assuming the source pending at the read's first run. A top-level `await` suspends module evaluation (microtasks run, timers too if it waits on one), so a node created before it (a module-scope memo, or a module-scope hook instance a root already read) can settle by the mount, and a settled read needs no boundary (rc.17 warns nothing). The fuzzer's `module-hook-shared` source found 50 such legal programs reported (`const shared = useVals(); createRoot(…shared.a1()…); await …; export function C1() { createRenderEffect(() => { shared.a1(); … }) }`), false positives confirmed in Chrome. Fix: `ModuleCensus::may_settle_before_mount` (a project module with a top-level `await`, or a load the census cannot see) sets `AsyncRead::settle_unproven`, and projection then makes SC5003 uncertifiable; its `ssrSource: "client"` wording stays, since that throws on the server whatever the client's timing. Premise: no dependency module awaits at its top level (the census does not scan installed packages; SC5001's apply proofs refuse any unseen load). Trade: any top-level `await` in a project makes every SC5003 uncertifiable, even for a memo the reading body creates; which nodes the checkpoint settles is not modeled. Fixture variants `pending-source-first-run/{module-render-read,module-render-await}` (violation, uncertifiable); `scripts/fuzz/rediscovery.json` records the rediscovery. Review (Chrome-confirmed false positive): SC5001 on a component-body or root read had the same gap (`settled-body`: rc.17 warns `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED`, the checker proved SC5001), so the flag makes SC5001 uncertifiable too, except an apply's first-run row (ADR 0510's own proof). Those proofs (`render_runs_first`, `module_scope_only_creates`, `module_scope_may_run`) now also count `for await` and `await using`. Precision cost: an unread module memo stays pending through an `await` (`module-body-await` throws in Chrome) yet is uncertifiable. The generator's `settled` variant also takes `body` and `root`. Variants `module-body-settled`, `module-body-await`, `module-for-await` (uncertifiable).

<a id="adr-0519"></a>
## ADR 0519 — The fuzzer's runs end with an answer: a supervised browser, deadlines, and disks that hold a full run
A night of full-grid runs hung or failed silently five ways: a dead browser stayed the executor's zombie, the grid waiting with no error; a disk out of inodes failed late with ENOSPC and harness rows; mx-recall in one process exhausted Node's heap; a VM paused by a sleeping host left a grid step waiting on no child; editing the oracle's claim table re-ran every cached runtime record. The executor (`scripts/fuzz/exec`) now supervises its children: each is reaped on exit, with the exit and the browser's last stderr lines logged; the DevTools connection fails at once (every waiting and later command, a page's event wait within a second); startup and the Vite helper's calls have deadlines; a batch whose browser died reruns its failed programs on a new browser, at most three restarts per executor (each logged), then they are harness rows, never verdicts; shutdown kills and waits on both children. `exec.mjs` bounds the start, every request and close, killing the executor on a missed deadline or malformed output, so a grid fails instead of hanging. `focus.mjs` streams each grid's log live, kills a grid process after `--grid-timeout` (120 minutes), gives recall grids a 12 GB heap, runs one process per code with `--split-codes`, deletes `cases/` with `--prune-cases`, and reports a grid that judged nothing. `vm.sh` keeps runs and caches on the VM's data disk (a local block device, never the host's virtiofs share), refuses to sync below 10 GiB or 10% of inodes, and `detach` runs a command that outlives the SSH session. The runtime cache key hashes `runtime-oracle.mjs` without its `codeRules` table (`oracleRecordHash`), which only judges a record, and takes the spawned Node's version. Caches and focus outputs live under `$TMPDIR/*.noindex` (Spotlight indexing slowed timed tests). The runtimes run on Node, never the caller's `process.execPath` (`scripts/lib/node-runtime.mjs`): under the bun running the repository's tests the fast path ran on bun, where rc.17 reported no `UNTRACKED_READ_AFTER_AWAIT` for a read after an `await` through a wrapper, failing `misuse-fire.test.mjs` on every machine. Outcomes are unchanged:

## ADR 0520 — An action body is placed by its callers, and a yielded promise detaches the next step
The fuzzer's `action` recall context (NO_OWNER_CLEANUP, 588 certified false cleans of 624): with `const a1 = action(function* () { onCleanup(() => {}); })` called as `a1()` from a button handler, the checker certified `onCleanup` clean, since the generator, a callback with no owner edge, took the component body's providing region by position. rc.17 steps the generator on the stack of the action's caller (`@solidjs/signals` `dist/dev.js:1573-1607`), so the body's owner is the caller's (none in a handler), as also for `createEffect`, `useContext`, `Loading(props)` and an `onSettled` cleanup there (probed in Node, dev build). Fix: the stepped generator's owner node takes the symbol of the `const` holding the action (`action_binding_name`); a call or callback argument naming that binding (through `const` copies, not a written binding) targets the generator (`named_action_generator`); no providing region answers for a stepped generator, so its operations are judged on the graph: every caller unowned is a violation, a caller's owner is inherited (a leaf caller's leaf, where `onCleanup` is SC3001's claim; a children-capable caller throws `ACTION_CALLED_IN_OWNED_SCOPE` first and the body's context is owned), and a generator no binding holds, or whose binding reaches a component prop, a slot or an assignment (`action_binding_escapes`), is seeded open-world unowned: an obligation. Steps: a yielded native promise (`Promise.resolve()`, `new Promise(…)`, `Promise.all` and siblings, resolved by the standard library's declaration, which the demand plan now asks for a `yield` operand) is assimilated, and the next step runs from its continuation with no owner (`:1618-1636`), as does every step of an async generator; a plain value steps again synchronously (`:1637`). solid-facts records every `yield` with its operand and dominated block region (schema 88, `AstFacts::yields`); `action_step_resumption` makes an operation in the region proven unowned (`after_yield`, a new `OwnerRequirement` flag with its own evidence line), and uncertain where a possible-thenable `yield` may precede it; a call after one carries the new `ResumesUnowned` edge. Legal twins stay silent: an action called only from a root or body (the action-call rule reports), never called, from a tracked body, or after `yield 1`; writes in an action body are unchanged (execution roles untouched). Residual: an action handed to a component prop is an obligation, not the violation the ADR 0319 census gives a plain helper (the census refuses generators); a stored prop called from a timer is certified clean for a helper and an action alike (pre-existing).

<a id="adr-0521"></a>
## ADR 0521 — A generator stepped at its own call places its write at the call
Recall cells `generator` in `pos-handler-root` and `pos-handler-owner`: 38 certified false cleans of REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE, such as `createRoot(() => { (function* () { setW1(7); yield 1; })().next(); })` in a button handler. ADR 0516's stepped-generator arm in the read-role walk (`callee_roles.rs`) gave the write the creating call's read role (the handler's legal `EventCallback`), skipping the root. rc.17 runs a fresh generator's first `next()` to its first `yield` on the creating call's stack, where `createRoot` made the root owner (`@solidjs/signals` `dist/dev-shared.js:1537-1543`; `runWithOwner` `:5758` sets `context`), and `setSignal` (`:5619-5636`) and the action guard (`dist/dev.js:1545-1556`) throw under a children-capable owner. Fix (`stepped_generator_in_place`, `write_roles.rs`): a write in the site's own non-async generator, not a primitive's callback, whose iterator is the call expression itself, stepped there (`generator_iterator_used`, `iterator_stepped_at`), takes the creating call's *write* role, as a closure run in place would (a root, bound owner, `untrack`, `runWithOwner(null)` or the handler). It rejects only if the one step is a `next` and no own `yield` may precede the site (none before it outside an exclusive arm or in a loop repeating it, per ADR 0520's `action_step_resumption`); a site a straight-line `yield` dominates (`YieldFact::region`), or a body stepped only by `return()`/`throw()`, never runs (legal, `Unreported`); any other `yield` leaves it unplaced. The read arm keeps ADR 0516's answer, `Unknown` for a write. Action calls share the path (`push_action_invocation`); ADR 0520's action bodies, never stepped at a call, are untouched. Fixture `write-stepped-generator-in-root`. Residual: the read arm, stopping at the generator, still places a write in a closure the first step runs in place (`(function* () { [1].forEach(() => setW1(7)); yield 1; })().next()`).

<a id="adr-0522"></a>
## ADR 0522 — A read in layers that run in place is the enclosing function's, and a class member's calls are obligations
Recall context `ref-factory-body` (1,030 certified false cleans of STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED; layers `helper`, `iife`, `closure+helper`, `module-helper`, every binding): `const dir1 = () => { h1(() => { r1(); }); return () => {}; }; <p ref={dir1()} />` was clean; rc.17 warns at element creation, as JSX evaluates `dir1()` eagerly in the body's strict-read window (`@solidjs/web` dist/web.dev.js:1016-1022 applies the ref under `runWithOwner(null)`, keeping the window). The role was right (`UntrackedRendering`, ADR 0469's climb), but `direct_reference_contributions` credited the anonymous callback's node, whose summary nothing consumes, so `dir1` had no read, and `local_access` declined a site in a named non-component function; a body statement `dir1();` too. Fix: a read in layers run in place (ADR 0469's `layer_run`: an IIFE, a helper's callback it calls in its own body on every completion, a named closure called once, any module; generators stepped at their call) is also a row, at the call, of each node whose own body holds a call the climb passes (`in_place_run_calls`, `in_place_layer_owners` in `interproc/reads.rs`): `local` if the call is not on every completion; undecided past a possible await, a parameter list or a stepped generator; none past an await on every path; with a direct read's escapes (a primitive's callback, a fresh stack, a retained literal). The direct-read proof accepts it when the outermost layer call is written directly in the callee's body (`body_site_runs_during_call_through_layers`): a violation, as is a hook body calling a helper layer. Also fail closed: a summary consumer's call in a class member's body (`wrapUse()` in a constructor or method, a hook's returned accessor; 264 false cleans per class context), silenced at `call_result_reads`'s non-component gate, is an obligation (ADR 0516's answer for a direct read); a read in a generator stepped at its call, in a function an intrinsic `ref` applies, kept the deferred role (`ref_applied_read_role` climbed layers only, `climb_in_place_layers`) and now takes `in_place_run_site`, unplaced through the generator (14 and 7 false cleans per ref context, SC1001 and SC5001). Writes were unaffected: their walk places layers itself (ADR 0469, 0521). Fixture `read-in-place-layers-summary`. Costs and residuals: a class member's helper call is an obligation even when every construction is in a handler or tracked attribute; a layered read after an await in an async hook is an attributed obligation, like the direct read; only direct-call and `Function.prototype` rows take the layer climb (a store head Get in a layer, or a read reaching it through a non-layer edge such as a helper called twice in the callback, stays unclaimed at the enclosing function); a factory in a JSX attribute stays tracked and silent.

## ADR 0526 — A standard-library write of a store's proxy and a destructuring leaf are store writes
Store recall variants (3,246 certified false cleans of STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER, 210 wrong-rule): `Object.assign(s, { v: 7 })`, `Reflect.set(s, "v", 7)`, `Object.defineProperty(s, "v", { value: 7 })` were clean; `({ v: s.v } = { v: 7 })` was an SC1001 read in a body, else nothing. rc.17 warns STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER for each outside the setter and stores nothing (the `set` and `defineProperty` traps return `true` after warning: `@solidjs/signals` `dist/dev.js:5420-5451`, `:5691-5697`, `:5743-5749`; 700 runs). Fix: `AstFacts::is_plain_write_target` and `destructuring_assignment_leaves` make each leaf of a plain assignment's pattern a written member for the write arm and a non-read for `local_access` and pre-suspension store reads. `no_store_standard_library_write` reports a call Type Facts resolves to the standard library's `ObjectConstructor.assign`, `Reflect.set` or `ObjectConstructor.defineProperty` with the store's bare identifier as first argument root, with an assignment's exemptions (the setter window, the draft) and ADR 0509's handed container. Silent: a shadowed `Object`, a project `assign`, a spread first argument, the store in another argument (a copy), a copy target. A past-root target is uncertifiable, silent where a link is nullish under strictNullChecks (ADR 0465); with a `Readonly` store root the arm is silent (unmeasured). Fixture `store-standard-library-writes`. Residuals (unmeasured): `Object.defineProperties`, a `for` head's destructuring pattern; no ADR 0491 literal-link proof past the root.

## ADR 0527 — Refs, setup positions, flow bodies, store operations and action bodies enter the recall grids, with the placements they proved wrong
Recall grids (`--recall` only; base grids and random profiles unchanged) gain catalogue slice A's top shapes, each cell fire-checked first on rc.17's fast runtime (misuse fires its code, legal is silent): seven intrinsic `ref` forms (`REF_CONTEXTS`: inline, named, array, conditional, a factory's apply or body, a `For` row); eleven setup positions (`POS_CONTEXTS`: a JSX attribute, a class static block, field, constructor and method, a body `forEach`, a handler expression, `flush(fn)`, a root, bound owner or `untrack` in a handler); `For`, `Repeat`, `Show` and `Match` function-child bodies (`FLOW_CONTEXTS`; rc.17 runs a function child only when `child.length > 0`, so `Show`/`Match` children take one parameter); owner-code action bodies; recall-only legal cells (`recallLegal`: a write or action in `onSettled`, a tracked effect, `runWithOwner(null)`, an action body); 21 store operations and 5 legal copy and draft forms (`recallVariants`; a `{ v, items: [1, 2] }` store for order-changing mutators). Placements per code: tables in `misuse.mjs` (`REF_`, `POS_`, `FLOW_PLACEMENT`). On the build before each fix they found certified false cleans in every family and one false positive, now placed: an intrinsic ref callback's read had the non-reporting `DirectiveApply` role (11,398 programs), now its element's creation role when the ref yields the literal, else unplaced; a ref factory's returned literal is applied with no owner; only a function an event attribute's expression yields is its handler, a sequence yielding its last operand (an IIFE there runs at setup); a read nested in a flow body is placed at its in-place run site; a class member's read is an obligation (ADR 0516); a named leaf-owner callback keeps the write exemption (684 legal false positives; ADRs 0520-0522 and 0526 record the action, generator, layered-read and store-write fixes). `rediscovery.json` names old-build programs for each; its test reads the recall grids. Hand-off layers (`HANDOFF_FORMS`: the site's function handed by name to an array callback, `.call`/`.apply`/`Reflect.apply`, a tagged template, a wrapper or a member) are built but held: their owner-region, read-hand-off and climb-bound false cleans exist on main too (`docs/open-work.md`). Harness: `focus.mjs` stops when a grid cannot start (no executor, under 5 GiB or 5% of inodes free) rather than judge every code at zero; the executor's Vite helper exits with its parent. Callback props (`CBP_CONTEXTS`: a same-file child calls `props.run()` in its body, JSX, a handler, an effect apply, `onSettled` or never; `run` inline, named, forwarded, destructured or spread) take their base grid only (`CBP_PLACEMENT`).

Saturation ladder d11/d9/d2c/d2b/d2 writes inside a handler-created root at 0/8/15/16/17 IIFE layers; rc.17 throws `REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE` at all five, but `detached_in_place` exhausted at 16 and fell back to the handler's legal compiler role. ## ADR 0533 — The role log: which classifier arm answered, folded per grid into saturation numbers
`SOLID_CHECKER_IR_ROLE_LOG=<file|dir>` (`solid-reactive-ir/src/role_log.rs`) writes a JSON line per outermost role decision (`read_execution_role`, `semantic_execution_role`, `semantic_write_execution_role`, an action call's role): site, final role, every tagged arm passed, and the last that returned the role. Arms: `role_arm!("<file stem>.<name>", role)` at each `return ExecutionRole::…` of `execution_role/` (named by meaning in the read and write walks), `arm_id!` at each owner-emission answer (`root-owned`, `context-0`, `after-await`, …); `default` marks a non-reporting answer for want of proof (an assigned member's function, an allowed region's unplaced nested function, a tracked root, incompatible returned invocations, owner context zero). Write-only, and hooks return their input: no answer moves (`make coverage`: 0 moved); unset, a hook is one atomic load. `scripts/fuzz/roles.mjs` (and `focus.mjs --role-log`) folds a grid's log and rows into `roles.json`: per-arm decisions, arms never hit (inventory from source), and per slice findings per 1,000 programs, arms first hit against `--roles-against`, and decisions silent by default; a slice whose new programs raise none of the three is saturated for its axis. Region coverage (`cargo llvm-cov`), once installed, stays the stronger per-PR measure; the arm log substitutes only for the classifiers.

## ADR 0535 — Hand-offs inside a primitive's callback
`inside_non_component_function` answers for a primitive's callback, so a named function nested in a root body or effect apply skipped ADR 0531 and took the silent nested role (full-6: 2,094 rc.17 SC1001 warnings certified clean). Such reads now go to their inline carrier; an unplaced hand-off is an obligation; a member's function value (`o.m = …`, `this.m = …`) is unplaced. Writes, actions and `refresh` likewise; a hand-off to project code there is unplaced; DOM `addEventListener` keeps its listener, `removeEventListener` only names it. full-6 writes: 704 of 740 obligations. Fixture `read-handed-in-callback`. Open: summary-only hand-offs.

*Amended (batch 11, later assignment).* "A member's function value is unplaced" was a member's only: a function written as a variable's value after its declaration (`let h = () => {}; h = () => flush(); h();`) has no binding name, and the call of `h` resolves to the declaration's initializer (or to nothing), never to this value, so every walk that asks who calls a function read "nobody" and placed a site in it nowhere (`runs_unplaced_once`'s `function_binding_name(..)?`, the effect-phase origin, the read nets). Chrome (rc.21) reports FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK, FLUSH_IN_ACTION and PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK at the site while the program was certified with no row: 15 false cleans in the fuzz cells `la-*` (`let` with an initializer, with none, and assigned in another function; the effect-apply and action hosts; 1a87feae8), the real oracle's `unproven-clean` in 6 programs, found probing the research briefs' unprobed flush and `resolve` leads. `assigned_function` (local_access.rs, was `member_assigned`) answers a function written as the value of any assignment, so each of those walks treats it as handed where nothing places it: an uncertifiable row (`function-run-unplaced`) of SC2006, SC2008, SC6002 and the read rules, never a violation (nothing here proves the call runs it). A `let` called before its reassignment (`let h = () => flush(); h(); h = () => {};`) and the member and element forms were rowed before. Cost (34 projects, release): standard U +3 (121,994 to 121,997; 6 rows added, 3 replaced at the same start by another row of the same program), with package sources +13 (142,463 to 142,476; 10 `package-finding-at-entry`), V unchanged. The legal twins (a function assigned and never called) go from clean to uncertifiable in 4 to 8 of the 12 programs a code has: nothing proves a function assigned to a variable is never called. Not done: a precise answer for a `let` assigned once before its only call (a violation where the single assignment dominates the call).

A call in a project component's prop literal is no unproven store: the consumer's role and ADR 0204's timing decide, as for a direct read. `body_site_runs_during_call` withholds only JSX sites in the function's own body, so a prop-literal closure proves its read. (2) Leaf-owner discovery walks a tagged template as a call of its tag, in the callback and a helper's extent (possible only there); an unresolved tag leaves the scope unproven. (3) A transported async read takes ADR 0535's placement: unplaced, escaped or member-assigned functions are obligations, not the control-flow child's render role (which proved SC5003 where rc.17 raises PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ). (4) SC1002's stack walk follows 16 in-place layers past the chain depth (exhaustion an obligation), `f.call`/`f.apply`, tags and named inline callbacks of exact project functions. The effect-phase walk (SC2008, SC6002) now treats an inline carrier (ADR 0531's `HandOff::Inline`) as a layer walked from the carrier, and a project component's callback prop as the calls the component makes of it, when the props parameter (one identifier read as static members, or an object pattern binding the prop to one identifier) reaches nothing else; another prop's `this` and spreads refuse, a forwarded prop is not followed.
