## ADR 0543 — The fuzzer's warm checker: a retained daemon answers `--feedback-facts`
A cheap fuzz program's one-shot check took ~30 ms, half spent starting the producer and opening the program; the retained daemon refused `--feedback-facts` (`carried`) and answered `feedback_facts: false` at both enablement sites. The request now carries the mode (`CheckRequest.feedback_facts`), both sites read it, cached answers key on it, and each mode is its own daemon (socket identity) refusing the other. Retained feedback models equal fresh ones (same facts and rule options; `RuleOptions.development_feedback` is in the retained builder's identity); session and daemon process tests compare whole answers with fresh ones across A→B→A, repeats, permutations, mode switches, fresh and reused slots, abandoned checks and restarts. Feedback checks are retained only when `SOLID_CHECKER_DAEMON=1` asks; release `feedback run` stays one-shot by default. A directory stamp moved over unchanged entries (name, type, inode; an untracked entry's own stamp) no longer rebuilds (the executor's batch type check creates and removes `tsconfig.tsbuildinfo` in its slot, which rebuilt 20 of 20 programs at generation 1); a created, removed, renamed or replaced entry still does, and a tracked file's fingerprint stays its bytes. `--stop-daemon` ends a daemon once it has answered what it accepted, or after `SOLID_CHECKER_DAEMON_STOP_SECS` (60) via the pid beside its socket. A daemon closes inherited descriptors above stderr at start, and the executor starts one process at a time: macOS `std` sets close-on-exec only after creating a pipe, so a client started meanwhile gave its daemon another command's stdout pipe, whose reader then waited for the daemon to end (three grid passes stalled on one `prepare`; the process test rediscovers it on the old build). Fuzzer `--warm-checker` (executor `warmChecker`): one daemon per slot directory; `--warm-verify` fails a program whose warm answer differs from a one-shot run at the same path; a killed or failed warm check stops its daemon before slot reuse; the executor stops its daemons at exit; not with the role log (ADR 0533). Import lines changed from the slot's previous program cost a re-resolving producer update (~25 ms against ~12 ms alone). Default was off until a full cached grid agreed under `--warm-verify`; on since warm-verify-1 (three grids, 48,973 programs with Chrome, 0 differences); `--no-warm-checker` opts out.

<a id="adr-0544"></a>
## ADR 0544 — Role work and reachability stay polynomial in nested layers
A read's role asks of each named closure around it (`unrun_closure_within`, ADR 0452) whether all its calls are tracked, and placing each call asks the same of the closures around it: one call per layer costs 2^depth placements per read (debug, 189 s at depth 20). `closure_unplaced` (`execution_role/body_roles.rs`) keeps the outermost query's placement, reused only where recomputing answers the same: its computation met no cut on a closure in progress before it began, no closure it entered is in progress now, and the context is equal (the read-recording frame `base::reads::current_frame`; an open role-log decision; `flow_cache_state`: whether a flow or write-flow answer computes and how often their per-answer caches were cleared; the callbacks `inherits_callback_role` places). Not kept under a probe or a proof's deferred check; a reuse replays its role-log record. Placements grow as depth·(depth+3); `BuildTimings::closure_placements` counts them, and the complexity ladder gates them under 2·depth²·placements(1) to depth 20. Reachability from a root into a call-graph cycle no longer walks every simple path (factorial when dense): `path_counts` counts on the condensation (`condensation_counts`, Tarjan), equal to the path count when acyclic and nonzero exactly where a path reaches the call. `ReactiveWrite::multiplicity` is read only as zero or not (projection ignores it), so no decision moves; on a cyclic graph it is no longer the simple-path count.

<a id="adr-0545"></a>
## ADR 0545 — Warm checks: parsed files outlive a chain, the producer's build inputs are witnessed, the role log is each answer's
Imports moving defeats in-place replacement (`reuse_program`), and the fresh chain reparsed every file (bundled libraries, installed declarations: most of a ~25 ms warm check) and redigested every text (~1.9 ms). `file_regions::ParseCache` gives a new chain each file with equal parse options and text byte-equal to what the host reads now; the chain adopts the file's region, alive while any chain reads it; only a committed head's files stay cached. A parse depends only on options and text, a bound file is frozen (`unsafe_boundary` invariant 8), and tsrs's watch mode and project system share files likewise. A cached file's text digest stays in its entry (its address is unique while held). The randomized incremental oracle runs without the cache.

The daemon's resolution witness modelled only bare and `#` imports; relative and `paths` resolutions relied on tracked sources and directory stamps, so a `package.json` rewritten in place in an imported directory kept the old resolution, unlike one-shot (`warm_resolution_process` fails on the old build). The producer now records every question its builds ask below the overlay (build-scope lookups and read paths; a new chain replaces them, an in-place update adds its own) and answers them on `Modules` with `buildInputs`. The witness observes each at least as finely as tsrs reads it: kind following links, a stat's kind and file size (all tsrs reads of it), the canonical path, a listing with each entry's followed kind, a read's bytes; a tracked source's bytes stay the overlay's. Absent probes under an unchanged directory `lstat` stamp stay absent. `graph_kept` also reports an in-place update that asked nothing new; a kept update fetches no inputs. Observation trails the build by a few milliseconds, like every witness entry.

A warm slot keeps one directory, `slots/<n>/program/`: per-program slot names (e34cb6e75) gave each program a socket and so a daemon (`warm-identity.mjs` sent no ids, hiding it). Each check names its program in `SOLID_CHECKER_IR_ROLE_LOG_PROGRAM`; path lines carry it as `g`, which `roles.mjs` prefers to the path. The client sends role log and program with the check; the daemon points the process-wide log at them for that answer only (`role_log::configure`), with a fresh diagnostic session and no cached answer, and waits out a cancelled idle check, recording none. Appends are whole buffers under an exclusive advisory lock; path ids never repeat in a process. Owners: apps/solid-typefacts/src/{file_regions,overlay,project,inputs}.rs, rust/crates/solid-facts-backend/src/{daemon,daemon_resolution}.rs, rust/crates/solid-reactive-ir/src/role_log.rs, rust/crates/typefacts (`BuildInputs`), scripts/fuzz/{exec/src/main.rs,roles.mjs,warm-identity.mjs}.

<a id="adr-0546"></a>
## ADR 0546 — Walks over cyclic callers are computed once per function, not once per path
k mutually calling local functions took 2.1-3.7 s at k = 8, 38-52 s at k = 9, over five minutes at k = 10 (release, f31337fa8); now 30-34 ms at k = 8-9, 50-54 ms at k = 12, 42 MB. Four walks enumerated every simple path through the callers; each now uses a strategy with the paths' answer, and follows the paths where that is not shown and always under the role log (its arms are every path's), a probe, a proof's deferred check, or test hook `solid_reactive_ir::follow_every_path`.
- Write-walk caller fold (`execution_role/write_components.rs`, `region_fold`). Path-dependent in general (a function whose callers are all on the path folds to nothing: a Hamiltonian-path question), but in a region of plain-call edges into plainly folding loops a rejecting entry ends every fold above it and a revisit reaches no function or entry the first visit did not, so the paths' first rejection is a depth-first search's with one visited set; without one, `Unknown` is absorbing, so each fold stops at its first `Unknown`, and where none can be `Unknown` the region folds to the role its first entries lead to. Each call site is placed once (root walk state plus its own function), recording every `visiting` query (`visit_contains`, `visit_insert`); the walk follows paths if a placement asked about another region function, an answered entry is written in one, an edge's loop is not plain or the fallback would place its `Unknown`, or after 2^20 steps.
- Site reach (ADR 0458, `site_reach.rs`) iterates its least fixed point from all `false` when the program has fewer functions than the depth limit (64), which depth-first derivation then never hits; at 64 or more it derives as before.
- ADR 0537's helper climb keeps each call's own answer (`handed_tail`, its only rejection); if no call within its depth rejects, a fold stops at its first unplaced entry, and if none is unplaced either and at most `depth` helpers have a call (no path runs out of calls), the climb answers none at once.
- The ordering proof (ADR 0339, `initialization.rs`) is a conjunction counting a function in progress as proved: it fails exactly when a failing mention is reachable, so a function proved once in a root's proof stays proved (`Proving`).
A component's two named functions that are each other's one call (ADR 0479) hung `layers_run_inline` (found by the differential test below); a layer met twice now ends that climb unproven.
A rebuilt chain (imports moved, ADR 0545) rescanned every module's exports. Shared files come from the parse cache as the same parsed and bound files (same binder symbol addresses), and a module's exported identities depend only on its file and the files it reaches over resolution edges, with their resolutions. So (`passes::Graph::rebuilt_export_rescan`) only modules reaching, over the new program's module and type-reference edges, a file that is not the same or whose resolution records differ are rescanned, as an in-place update rescans what reaches its file; all are rescanned if membership, compiler options or configuration changed, or a differing file joins the global scope or augments a module in either program. The crate's tests fail if an update's kept identities differ from a fresh scan (`identity::after_update`); the randomized incremental oracle (120 seeds × 30 steps × 2 tests) kept 111,183 module scans, all equal; `warm_resolution_process` adds re-export chains, a re-export retargeted by an import edit, a global augmentation and a deep dependency edited under an import edit, each warm answer equal to one-shot. The rest is the fresh chain's build (resolutions and reads, ~2.6 ms), which only an in-place update avoids.
Counters `BuildTimings::write_walk_steps` and `reach_evaluations`; the complexity ladder builds k = 2..12 dense components (three hosts, both declaration forms) and gates their sum under 2·(k/2)³·work(2) (k = 12: 3,082 steps in the body, 6,183 in the handler). `write_walk_strategies_process` (own binary: the switches are process-wide) requires equal IR for 500 randomized mutually recursive programs with the hook on and off (every eighth also with the role log).

<a id="adr-0560"></a>
## ADR 0560 — Path-dependent walks end at a bound; the helper climb and site reach answer exactly
ADR 0546 left walks exponential where its folds do not apply (ed30c5738): `untrack(() => fj(n - 1))` edges among k local functions (38.7 s at k = 8), alias closures `const gj = () => fj(n - 1); gj();`, site reach with 64 or more functions (over 40 s at k = 8), and a helper climb whose one rejecting call is its helper's last.
- Helper climb (`local_access.rs`, `first_rejection`). Where a reached call rejects (ADR 0546's ball), a helper's climb rejects exactly when a path within its remaining calls, over helpers not in progress, reaches a call whose own answer (`handed_tail`) rejects; a shortest such path repeats no helper, so breadth-first search decides it (`rejection_within`). The climb descends in fold order to each helper's first call whose helper's climb, else own answer, rejects; finding none, it follows the paths. k = 10: 679 → 35 ms.
- Site reach (`site_reach.rs`). A derivation holds in progress only functions the answer may read: the one written around it and those holding a call, render or reference of its name or its class's name. Where those are under 64 and all the program's own (`reads_few`), no derivation reaches the depth limit, so the least fixed point answers at any program size. Elsewhere the fixed point answers only functions it proves run (the limit only adds `true` to a monotone system); the rest derive depth first.
- The bound (`WALK_STEP_BOUND`, 2^16) caps path-dependent answers: the write walk outside its region fold (which callers a fold reaches cut is a Hamiltonian-path question), the climb when it follows paths (role log, probe, a proof's deferred check, or no rejection), and depth-first site-reach derivations. A write walk with its own `visiting` set counts region and path steps (`bounded_walk`; a nested walk with a fresh set counts its own, so an answer is a function of where the walk starts); past the bound it answers `Unknown`, which no flow fallback replaces; a climb answers `Unknown`, which no hand-off tail replaces; a site-reach query stopping at the bound or depth limit answers every later question undecided (`Reach::Undecided`, `function_runs` true): a violation there is uncertifiable with its own message (`UnreachedSite::undecided`), a cleanup-handle call unplaced, an owner operation kept. untrack k = 8 38.7 → 3.3 s, each write U.
Counters `BuildTimings::bounded_walk_peak` and `bounded_walks_exhausted` (also in `SOLID_CHECKER_TIMINGS` output). The complexity ladder builds these shapes at k = 2..12 within the cubic envelope, no walk at its bound; `write_walk_strategies_process` lowers the bound to 2^10 (test hook `bound_walks`) and requires each walk to end within 2·bound, work under 8·k²·bound, no write to come out clean, and an undecided query to leave its sites undecided.
Mutation testing (`write_walk_strategies_process`): two ADR 0546 climb short-circuits had survived (no `called <= depth`; a reachable unplaced call). One generated program in six is now a chain of 9 to 12 functions with member-stored, carrier-handed and apply-handed functions and second entries; the first mutant now fails. The second is equivalent in every shape built (argued, not proven): an unplaced `handed_tail` call sits in a member-stored function the write walk reaches through the same call sites, so the write is already `Unknown`; `an_unplaced_call_the_climb_reaches_leaves_the_write_unplaced` pins that premise. The quick fold's break and off-by-one descent mutants are equivalent too.

## ADR 0561 — Inline carriers and template tags are owner edges
mx-recall CONTEXT_NOT_FOUND (644bd1a81): `[1].forEach(hf)`, `map`, `Array.from`, `sort`, `Reflect.apply` and ``hf`x` `` left `hf` with no owner edge (context zero, ADR 0534), so SC4002's owner-gated provider proof never ran: 120 SC4001 obligations where rc.17 throws ContextNotFoundError. The owner pass now gives a function handed whole to an `InlineCallback` row of `runtime_semantics` (ADR 0531's `HandOff::Inline` table; the compiler-selected standard-library declaration) and a template tag the edge its direct call has: the caller's owner, `call_owner_edge`'s kind, ADR 0468's may-run edge for a written or early-mentioned binding. No dialect question: a synchronous call keeps the current owner, the fact `.call`/`.apply` edges rest on. No edge for a generator, a primitive's call, a spread, or a carrier in an instance field or the owner's own JSX (`invocation_runs_under_owner`). A carrier that may run it zero times (`[].forEach(hf)`) gives no proof (ADR 0563). Files: `owners/owner_context.rs` (`inline_carrier_arguments`), `owners/owner_discovery.rs`. Fixture `owner-handed-inline`; moved: `owner-region-no-edge` f18 (obligation → violation, rc.17 warns), and two leaf fixtures lose an SC4001 obligation beside their SC3001 violation.

## ADR 0562 — A callback prop runs where its child calls it
mx-recall CONTEXT_NOT_FOUND (644bd1a81), shape B, 120 programs: in `<Kid run={() => useContext(Ctx)} />` with `props.run()` (or `({ run }) => run()`) in `Kid`'s body or JSX, the arrow had no owner edge, so SC4002 became SC4001's obligation where rc.17 throws ContextNotFoundError. The owner pass reuses ADR 0538's proof (`component_prop_invocations`: an arrow, no spread on the element, the props parameter one identifier read as static members or an object pattern binding the prop to one identifier, every use a direct call or a test) and gives the arrow an edge from each call written directly in the component's own body or JSX (`owner_at` is the component), of that call's `call_owner_edge` kind. Same-file only: the edge rests on this file's facts. Not followed: a call in a nested function of the child (a handler, an `onSettled` callback: another owner); a forwarded, spread, defaulted or copied prop; a child in another file; a prop never called (no run: obligation). File: `owners/owner_discovery.rs`. Fixture `owner-callback-prop`; moved: `fp-owner-component-ref`'s component-`ref` callback, called in `Box`'s body, from two SC4001 obligations to silent.

<a id="adr-0563"></a>
## ADR 0563 — A carrier proves a run only where it provably runs the callback
Owner decision (zero false positives): `InlineCallback` (ADR 0531's `HandOff::Inline`) claims timing, not a count: `[].forEach(hf)` never runs `hf`. `carrier_runs::carrier_provably_runs` decides from the compiler-selected standard-library declaration only. Always run: `.call`/`.apply`, `Reflect.apply`, the `Promise` executor, a tagged template. Run only with an array literal receiver or source (through parentheses and assertions) holding an element, with no spread or hole: an `Array`/`ReadonlyArray` iteration method; `reduce`/`reduceRight` with two elements, or one and an initial value; `sort` with two elements that cannot be `undefined` (number, string, boolean, `null`, array or function literals: `CompareArrayElements` orders `undefined` uncalled); `Array.from` and the typed `from`s. May run zero times: `Set`/`Map`/`NodeList` `forEach`, `String.replace`, `JSON.parse`'s reviver, any variable, property or call receiver. ECMAScript semantics, no dialect question. Consumers: ADR 0561's owner edges (else the possible-unowned edge: SC4001/SC4002 obligations), SC3001's leaf walk (`guaranteed` only through a running carrier), every async-read row (`AsyncRead::carrier_run_unproven`: SC5001/SC5002/SC5003/SC5005 uncertifiable, with its own message; `handed_async_read_role` prefers a running carrier). SC1001/SC2001 keep their recorded trade; SC1002 kept its feasible-path premise until ADR 0566. Fixtures `owner-carrier-provably-runs`, `async-carrier-provably-runs`, `leaf-carrier-provably-runs`; moved: SC3001 violations to obligations in `leaf-scope-host-callbacks` (3, signal lists) and `leaf-scope-host-timing` (2, `NodeList`), with SC4001's obligation back at each callback.

<a id="adr-0591"></a>
## ADR 0591 — The Linux checker identity is the image's GNU build id
A warm Linux VM fuzz check spent 26.8 of 46.8 ms in the client (600 programs of mx-recall and lg-recall, shard 0/200, one slot), 96% reading and hashing the 40 MB executable per invocation for the daemon identity (`loaded_implementation_identity`, executing-inode SHA-256 of 04a890520). Now, as LC_UUID does for Mach-O, the one `NT_GNU_BUILD_ID` note in `/proc/self/exe`'s `PT_NOTE` segments names it (`gnu-build-id:`, `elf_build_id`): a few header reads. The linker computes it over the whole output, so a changed binary changes it; an image without exactly one build id of 16 to 64 bytes (8-byte `--build-id=fast`, big-endian or 32-bit, a malformed note) keeps the digest. Premise, as on macOS: nothing rewrites a linked checker's code and keeps its build id.

<a id="adr-0592"></a>
## ADR 0592 — The fuzz executor hands a slot the program its daemon can update
A warm slot's daemon (ADR 0543) updates its program in place when the next has the same file names and tsconfig.json, else reopens it (new producer, full reparse): 52.9 ms of daemon and producer CPU against 26.1. In grid order a third of programs differ from the previous (78% `App.tsx` alone, the rest add `helpers.ts`, `lib.ts` or `solid-api.ts`), and with as many slots as step-1 workers each worker got back the slot it had freed. Now a worker takes the oldest of the first 64 queued requests whose shape (`project_shape`: names and tsconfig) a free slot last held, else the oldest; a slot prefers a request of its shape; no request is passed over more than four times the workers. Only preparation order moves, never an answer.

<a id="adr-0593"></a>
## ADR 0593 — Memo-graph shard work runs on the caller below 4,096 entries
A warm fuzz check started about 100 daemon threads (11 checks traced: 1,138 clones), 77 in `base::memo::each_shard`, which gave the 16 graph shards to min(cores, 16) fresh threads at every flush and graph sync however small, each re-reading the cgroup quota (`available_parallelism`, uncached in `analysis_parallelism`). Now `each_shard` stays on the caller's thread below `SHARD_THREADS_MIN_WORK` (4,096) edges or nodes, as `parallel_slice_results` and `parallel_balanced_results` do below 256 and 64 items, and the core count is read once per process. The inline arm is the existing sequential (wasm32-wasip1) one; results keep shard order and each item touches its own shard. A random 6,000-node graph keeps the worker arm tested against a fresh store.

<a id="adr-0594"></a>
## ADR 0594 — The fuzz executor runs a check's own fan-outs on one worker
At a check per core (24 slots on 12 vCPUs warm), each check still spread its reactive IR, producer per-file passes and tsrs pool over every core: thread starts, futex wake-ups and `sched_yield` from 24 pools of 12. The executor now sets `SOLID_CHECKER_IR_WORKERS`, `SOLID_TYPEFACTS_PER_FILE_WORKERS` and `RAYON_NUM_THREADS` to 1 (`CHECK_WORKERS`) unless its caller set them; the checker's own `tsrs` type checks keep their threads. Answers do not depend on worker counts (`context_census_workers_process`; full JSON of 600 programs equal).

<a id="adr-0597"></a>
## ADR 0597 — A grid prepares two chunks ahead
`matrix.mjs` and `legal.mjs` prepared chunk k + 1 (150 programs) only after chunk k's preparation answered, so each chunk's end drained the executor's queue until the slowest check answered (VM about 70% busy, caches warm), and ADR 0592's slots took whatever came last. `preparedAhead` (pipeline.mjs) keeps chunks k + 1 and k + 2 preparing while chunk k finishes; stages and row order are unchanged.

<a id="adr-0598"></a>
## ADR 0598 — The recall grids are lean by default
`--recall` omits `LEAN_DEMOTED` (matrix.mjs: mounts such as `show-keyed`, `for-signal`, `loading`, `portal`, callback forms `function`, `let`); `--recall-full` restores them. Evidence: scripts/fuzz/README.md ("Demoting saturated recall values"). Nightly audits are lean; Sunday and manual `recall: full` runs are full; a report is compared only with an earlier audit of its mode. Trade: a defect only a demoted value shows is found weekly, not nightly; `rediscovery.json` `fullIds` pins such programs.

<a id="adr-0599"></a>
## ADR 0599 — Editor latency: what a result chunk's stage proof holds, and per-declaration indexes
The editor path is the `solid-checker/eslint` plugin under oxlint's or ESLint's language server: per lint pass a `--format json --only-file` request to the retained daemon (`--only-file` takes as long as the full report). Three causes, none moving a finding:
- `SiteReach` (ADR 0458) found a declaration's references by binary-searching every identifier of the file, per function per query: 230 ms of final ordering. `AstFacts::reference_identifiers_to` answers exactly from the lazy `references_by_declaration` index, re-checking candidates with `reference_declaration`, in `identifiers` order. Three more lazy indexes (`span_index.rs`) give, in table order, the calls, returns and bindings whose peeled callee, argument or initializer is a span; 37 linear `calls` scans use the first.
- The result chunks' stage proof (ADR 0397) held `function_call_sites` of every function, read by the direct-reference contributions and ADR 0522's in-place layer climb, so moving a function's span reran all 1,611 result chunks (200 ms). Both decide only summary rows, which result chunks read through the result view and compare by value (`dependency_matches`), like graph chunks and member-invocation refresh; their reads now join `chunk_reads` (replayed into the stage after its proof). Body, mid and import edits rerun 1 to 18 result chunks.
- Stale result chunks run on the workers, not one at a time (`parallel_balanced_results_from`, from two items).
`compute_uninitialized_references`' unlisted-binding fallback, which scanned the reference table per binding, reads `references_to` per name. A second, equally exact pass: local-access chunk proofs on the workers (49 to 28 ms); the in-place climb per node on the workers (propagation 31 to 18 ms); initialization order warmed with the other memos, each binding decided once, not per mention, over a borrowed view of TypeScript references (`SymbolReferenceList`); hash sets for the memo store's dirty marking (15 to 9 ms); probes claimed one at a time. 494 to 502 ms for an end edit. A census inside a whole-program memo must not run on the workers: a worker asking the lookup waits on the lock its caller holds (it hung). What remains is `docs/open-work.md`'s memo begin residue and late-stage passes.

<a id="adr-0600"></a>
## ADR 0600 — The daemon keeps a content digest while its file stamp holds
ADR 0400 (4) is reversed: a content digest is kept per path while the file's stamp (device, inode, length, modification and change times) equals the one read before and after hashing, the trust given other disk inputs. The 2026-10-25 audit measured app-game (under 6 ms); on sensor-sim's pnpm workspace digests were 30 of a 47 ms cache hit, now 17 ms.

<a id="adr-0610"></a>
## ADR 0610 — A write that may follow an await takes a permissive entry role
A write its async function may or may not reach past an await (a `catch` or `finally` whose `try` head is no proven inert async call, code after an await in a branch or a loop's second pass, a `for await`) was Unknown before the walk read its function's entries (`write_roles.walk-unknown-3`). It runs on the entry stack in the entries' role, or on a later task, where rc.17's guard reads an ambient owner every entry point restored (`dev-shared.js` `setSignal`, `devGuardStoreSetterWrite`): legal. The walk now continues to the entries (`LoopPlan::suspendable`, never folded as a plain edge) and keeps a permissive role (`past_possible_suspension`); a rejecting or unplaced one, a named effect compute and a tracked position stay Unknown. Measured first on rc.17: no write past an await reports in an async function, its loop, `for await` or `finally`, an async memo or effect compute, or a handler's async function (45 programs), now SC2001 legal cells (`async` base, the rest recall, `handler-async-*` new). Fixture `write-past-possible-await` (the corpus repro `write-after-await`, 256 sites, has this shape: a submit handler's `catch` and `finally`).

<a id="adr-0611"></a>
## ADR 0611 — A write past an await of a function it runs in place inside is legal
ADR 0610's SC2001 legal cells found 16,528 false positives on main (`lg-recall`, every `memo-async*` and `effect-async*` cell): `await p; (() => set(1))()` in an async memo or effect compute, the write in an IIFE, a helper's callback (same file or imported), a named closure's one call, or through module and depth layers, was `no-write-in-pure-computation` by the compute's lexical role (`callee_roles.tracked-callback`), as suspension checks read only the write's own function. rc.17 runs it on the later task with no owner, silently. `suspension_through_in_place_layers` (write_roles.rs) climbs the layers `layer_run` proves run in place on their caller's stack and owner, asking at each layer's call whether it follows an await of its own async function: on every path, the write is `Unreported`; on some, a rejecting role is no claim. A Solid primitive's callback is never an in-place layer, so a `createRoot` body past the await keeps its owner and stays a violation (rc.17 throws). Fixture `write-past-layered-await`; `rediscovery.json` lists 16 of the grid's ids.

<a id="adr-0612"></a>
## ADR 0612 — Past an await an async compute has no observer and no owner
The fuzzer's async family (`ASYNC_FAMILY_PLACEMENT`, 476 probes on rc.17, then the recall grids) found three defects of one premise, taking the compute's synchronous run for all of it. SC2004/SC2005: `resolve`/`until` past a compute's await were violations (12,176 legal programs), but the guard reads `getObserver()`, which no later task has; `past_suspension` (own function, or a layer's call, ADR 0611) clears a proven await and makes a possible one an obligation. SC4001: `createEffect`, `onCleanup`, a boundary and a settled cleanup past a compute's await were silent (2,376 misuse programs, 1,140 certified false cleans, 190 more as a second code), as the providing region answered `root_owned`; it now owns only the synchronous run. This exposed `calls_after_await` counting a `catch` or `finally` past a `try` head that may throw before suspending (it runs under the function's owner): it is a possible continuation unless `follows_try_head_await` proves the head (a plain async function's too). SC1002: the object of a written member past the await (`s.a` in `s.a.v = 1`) was skipped as part of the longer path (4,124 certified misses, second codes of SC2003), a skip ADR 0498 removed from the strict-read window; `read_as_part_of_longer_path` is now the one test, and a member read in a function run in place past the boundary counts as read there, like an accessor call (not via a library callback, which may run zero times: ADR 0566). A thrown code in an async compute only rejects its promise, so a cell there needs a `try` and a marker (README, Not built); three first placements failed this; the new legal cells pass. Fixture `past-await-owner-observer`; `eslint-plugin-corpus` gains two proven SC4001s (`nestedScope`, `nestedReactiveScope`).

<a id="adr-0613"></a>
## ADR 0613 — A synchronous flush under an owner withholds the drained callbacks' ownerless proofs
A `createEffect` apply, a `createRenderEffect` apply after its first run, a `createReaction` callback and an effect bundle's `error` member run from the queue's drain, and rc.17's `flush()` drains on its caller's stack without setting an owner (`dist/dev-shared.js:2646-2721`), so a flush under an owner runs them owned. On the fast runtime, after a `createEffect`, a `flush()` in a component body, memo compute or root body let a `createEffect` in the apply raise nothing (an SC4001 false positive) and made a write there throw `REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE` (a certified false clean); in a handler or `runWithOwner(null, …)` the throws stay. The dialect names those callbacks (`callback_runs_drained_by_flush`); a census (`indexes/flush_census.rs`) lists every reference to the flush primitive. A call does not veto where its position proves no owner (the dispatch census's classes, ADRs 0356, 0435, 0437, 0438, and a compiler-reported inline element handler: the owner graph's unowned edge; handler classes only without the dispatch veto) or where the effect-phase walk proves it runs only on a `createEffect` apply of the same `solid-js` copy (the running queue drains nothing). Any other call, or non-call reference (`run(flush)`), vetoes program-wide (`FlushVetoAnswer` memo, `FlushVeto` section, kept as ADR 0450 keeps the dispatch veto; a debug tripwire refuses reads while the census computes). Under the veto the drained callback's unowned edge is `DrainedByFlush` (possibly owned, never proven, and leaving any region written around the call; a plain conditional edge would let a `createRoot` around the effect answer for the apply, a false clean); SC6002 is uncertifiable; a write in such a callback, a layer it runs in place, a render effect's later run, an apply's returned cleanup (a rerun runs it from the drain), a bundle member or with an apply role is unplaced (SC2001 uncertifiable). Not built: narrowing to effects created before an owned flush can run (cross-component order is not modelled). Not vetoed: a flush in dependency code, packages analyzed as source included (premise; `@solidjs/router`'s `preload` calls one), via a computed member or an escaped namespace object. Fuzzer: `FLUSH_DRAIN_CONTEXTS`. Fixtures `flush-veto-*`; eight older snapshots moved to uncertifiable, none to clean. Review added: `onSettled` called without an owner takes the same queue, so under the veto its callback is possibly owned (`QueuedLeafDrained`) and its writes unplaced (probed: a `missing-owner` false positive, a write false clean); a `flush()` in a parameter default runs at its function's call, not at module evaluation (in the dispatch census too). An `onSettled` under an owner is a true leaf, but the write role cannot tell call sites apart, so under the veto it is unplaced too, a recall cost (`leaf-owner`, `setter-in-root-*`, `write-scope-roots-*`, `eslint-plugin-corpus` move to uncertifiable).

<a id="adr-0614"></a>
## ADR 0614 — One read answers one way on every path
Certified second-code misses of `mx-recall` (focus-final, 2026-11-04: 7,110 rows, all on main's build too) and the census's first runs. Mostly, a read had parallel paths and fixes reached one. `discover_member` and the spread walk kept `semantic_execution_role` after ADR 0520 moved accessor calls to `read_execution_role`, so a store or prop read in an intrinsic `ref` took the silent `DirectiveApply`; `gate_refuses` returned where ADR 0531's accessor path asks `handed_read_role`, dropping a store read in a named function a carrier runs (and an escape with no follow-up, ADR 0516); `reads.rs`'s store row lacked ADR 0522 layer owners. All three now match the accessor's. SC1002's helper walk read only accessor calls; it now reads a store in `wrapUse()` past the await and through a receiver holding a `const` (ADR 0481), keys compared after the root. An object's callable holding a carrier takes the hand-off (obligation), like a named function.
Runtime premises measured wrong: `Loading`/`Errored` as functions and `resolve`/`until` create a computation, so rc.17 refuses them in an effect callback (SC6002) and a leaf owner (SC3001); SC2004's hint said to call `resolve` in an apply. The observer guard needs its own answer: an action's first step answers to its callers (a tracked caller reports no read, so ADR 0547's role proves nothing), a flush-drained apply to the veto (ADR 0613), and a deferred role under tracked JSX proves nothing (all SC2004/SC2005 obligations). A pending read in an action step takes the leaf owner all its calls have (SC5001). SC1002 of an async source is an obligation: settled it warns, pending it throws first (both measured). An apply or its cleanup in a flow child is no render (SC5003 false positive). Fixtures `store-read-placement`, `after-await-store-paths`, `after-await-async-source`, `boundary-in-effect-callback`, `observer-callers`, `observer-flush-drained`, `pending-read-action-step`, `async-read-under-jsx`.

<a id="adr-0615"></a>
## ADR 0615 — A defect fails where it is created
Owner's question: find an issue when it is created. Of the ledger's 72 rows and this stack, 7,264 flagged rows all fail on main (only `flush-*` shapes new), found hours to three months after the cause (ADR 0585: `named_callback_execution_role`, 2026-07-31, found 11-03). Causes: (1) cells are hand tables per code, so a context reached a code only by a slice's choice: 2,334 of 4,100 code × context pairs had none (`memo-async`, from 10-27, reached SC2001 on 11-04: 16,528 false positives). (2) Second codes (tallied since ADR 0498), `not-fired` rows (judged no finding, no second code computed) and another rule's violation at a site (never judged) were measured but failed nothing. (3) No generated program ran per pull request: CI and `make verify` ran the fuzzer's unit tests only, `make fuzz-smoke` ran by hand, and `existsSync` skipped 60 of 64 ledger rows, which named a grid with no manifest; ADR 0586's regression (5820be453) lived five hours to a hand run. (4) A fix reached one of parallel paths (ADR 0614). (5) A premise written down, not exercised: the flush premise, ADR 0483 to ADR 0613.
Built: **the census** (`mx-census`, `matrix.mjs censusCells`), a default grid of every pair no cell lists, in plain shapes, judged as any grid, so a new context or code meets everything in the run it lands, with no table entry (excluded, with reasons: a thrown code a promise swallows, pairs halting before the driver); alone it caught none of the store misses, which are in listed pairs. **The covering samples** (`cover.mjs`, owner decision): a greedy cover of every pair of axis values over the whole enumeration of misuse, legal and pair grids (`side` is an axis), computed from the shapes, so a new value enters the next sample. Tiers: per pull request, the census and all pairs at a fixed seed (`fuzz-pr.yml`, `make fuzz-smoke`), so a failure belongs to it; nightly or before a merge, pairs within each code, nightly on a seed of the day (`make fuzz-premerge`); weekly or before a release, the full grids. **The ledger's programs are required** in every sample. **A capped oracle** (`--runtime-diff-cap`): 25 a grid, the census's stratified by code and outcome (a census took 878 s, 870 in the oracle). **The judge reads all it saw**: a claiming violation on a `not-fired` site that ran, another rule's violation at a site, a second code on a `not-fired` program. **`store-link`** read variants for SC1001 and SC1002.
A greedy cover cut the owner's random-order sample of mx rows from about 8,800 to 7,350 programs with no loss. With the ledger's 1,508 programs the pull-request sample is 13,099. Not yet run on a runner: 16,300 programs at 6.4-11.4 a second (cold, cached) is 24-42 minutes before the build. The one category the top tier ever misses, 24 programs of `pos-flush-fn` × `dynamic`, is a four-way interaction; `context × mount × layer` caught it on half the seeds.
Not built: test selection from role arms (only `execution_role` and owner arms are tagged, so ADR 0614's member path would select nothing); a premise ledger (no fact marks prose as a premise: review's job); `context × mount × layer` in the cover (29% more programs for a category caught on half the seeds).

A hint that the runtime does not warn excused its finding on its own word; `window.mjs` now measures it: a control run reads one signal per counter, and rc.17 warns there exactly when a strict-read window is open (24 `pos-flush-fn` × `dynamic` rows: none; mounted plainly: all). No evidence excuses nothing. The pair grids join the audit. The sample found three older false positives, `delete store.key` past an await; the per-code tier, a pending read through a helper in a handler's `onSettled` proven SC5001 (332 of 1,284 programs on main), which now takes the owner fixed point's `onSettled` gate.

<a id="adr-0616"></a>
## ADR 0616 — The runtime oracle runs its cases in `feedback batch` processes
Each oracle worker is one `feedback batch` process (packages/cli README), not a `feedback run` per case. A fresh run spent about 1.1 of a case's 3.2 s on case-independent work: Node and tooling imports (0.3 s), two Chromium binary hashes (0.13 s), Chromium's launch (0.23 s), parsing TypeScript lib declarations (0.25 s), process exit (0.3 s); the page's own pauses (1 s) are semantic and stay. A batch shares loaded tooling, one Chromium process (a context per case), the browser's pin (hashed at open and close, file stamp checked after every run, ADR 0600's trust) and the execution cache's harness and tooling identities; each run keeps its own snapshot, native analysis, Vite server, context and input pins, and starts from a fresh run's environment and working directory (Vite sets NODE_ENV). A process retires after a failed run, an application Vite config (application code in the process), a changed working directory, a lost browser or a context left open; one refusing its close takes its cases with it. Declaration files are parsed once per process, keyed by path, options and bytes. 741 fixture cases (c8, cache off): identical rows, 324 s against 840 s; 20 lg programs serially: 39.7 s against 64.9 s, and 10.6 s against 27.0 s on execution-cache hits, with the same cache keys in both modes. `--session per-case` keeps the old shape.

<a id="adr-0617"></a>
## ADR 0617 — The oracle samples are split over the run's runners by size
`audit.mjs pick` gives each grid's oracle sample one job, then adds parts one at a time to the grid with the largest part while the run has fewer than 20 jobs (the account's concurrent runners) and that part exceeds 200 programs (every N-th id, as before), replacing the by-name map (mx-recall 4, lg-recall 2). A program took 1.0 to 2.3 s of a job's differential, the same code 1.6x apart between runners, so a part costs its size, not its grid. That run never exceeded 20 concurrent jobs and started shards in no index order (shard 0 was 29th), so ordering large parts first would not keep them out of the queue; the budget does. Its samples give 20 jobs of at most 439 programs (census 6, both recall grids 3), about 17 minutes on the slowest runner before ADR 0616; the floor leaves a lean night's runners unused rather than add jobs whose setup and warm-state artifact cost more than they save. A changed part count makes that job's warm state cold once.

<a id="adr-0618"></a>
## ADR 0618 — Main's CI builds, and does not retest, a tree a pull request passed on
A pull request's passing CI run uploads `ci-tested-tree-<tree>`, the tree of the merge ref it tested. A push to main looks it up by its commit's tree and accepts it only from a fully successful `ci.yml` `pull_request` run in this repository. Then `rust-engine` and `wasm-package` only build (clippy, `cargo test --no-run`, the debug checker, the WASM package), writing main's caches; `wasm-parity` and `scripts-and-cli` are skipped; `rust-package` is unchanged. A missing, expired or unaccepted record runs everything, as does a merge after main moved (a different tree). CI cancels a pull request's superseded run, never main's. Owner: `.github/workflows/ci.yml`.

<a id="adr-0619"></a>
## ADR 0619 — An unlabelled `untrack` keeps the pending throw for the body's own source
rc.21 `untrack` sets `strictPending` in an active strict window, and `read()` throws `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ` on `strictPending && owner._parent === context` (#4630), so a pending memo, derived store or projection the strict body made throws through `untrack`, nested too. SC5001 reads that as the body's own read: a callback chain of exact unlabelled `untrack`s, the outermost in the strict window, and the source bound in the same function. A source made elsewhere, or a read under a child root, has another `context`. Answered by `Dialect::untrack_keeps_own_pending_throw` on the rc.21 triple. A hook's memo: ADR 0628. Owners: `execution_role/read_context.rs` (`untrack_throws_own_pending_read`), `local_access.rs`.

<a id="adr-0620"></a>
## ADR 0620 — `deep(store)` is a read of the whole store
Measured on rc.21: `deep(s)` warns `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED` in a strict window on any store, empty or shallow too, and throws for a pending derive; `snapshot` reads nothing. Rules-mining RS-R37. A proven SC1001 row at the call, uncertain over a pending derive. Owner: `local_access.rs` (`discover_whole_store_reads`).

<a id="adr-0621"></a>
## ADR 0621 — A whole-store read needs what it reads
Rules-mining CS-R15; every form measured on rc.21 in a strict window. `JSON.stringify` (`toJSON`), `Reflect.get`, `Array.from` and a template substitution read a key on any store; iteration (`for…of`, array and call spread, array destructuring) reads `length`, so needs an array literal; `{...s}`, `Object.values`, `entries` and `assign` read a key the store holds, so need an object literal with one. `{...s}` on `{}` is silent, so the old proven `s spread` row there was a false positive; a store of unknown shape is now uncertifiable. `Object.keys`, `in`, `hasOwn`, `for…in`, `!!s` and `snapshot` read none. Owner: `local_access.rs` (`store_initial_shape`). Correction (rules audit, reproduced in Chrome): `for…of` was certified because Type Facts keeps no entity at its operand, so `whole_store_source` asked nothing; it now takes the binder too. "Read none" holds only for a settled store: an uninitialized derive's traps throw (ADR 0635).

<a id="adr-0622"></a>
## ADR 0622 — `onFlush` and `from` are modelled on the rc.21 triple
`onFlush` ends in `queueMicrotask(callback)` on every path: deferred, no owner, no window. `from` calls nothing itself; the iterator a computation asks for runs the subscribe function in that pass, tracked and owned; with no consumer it never runs. Whether one consumes it is unknown at the call, so `from`'s callback has a conditional owner and a write in it is unproven. A mixed install keeps them in a scoped gap, so a namespace import of `solid-js` on a whole triple costs nothing. `from`, a 1.x name, left `V2_REMOVED_EXPORTS`. Owner: rust/crates/solid-dialect/src/solid_2.rs.

<a id="adr-0623"></a>
## ADR 0623 — A tracked read in a compute is no obligation
`async_read_role` maps a tracked read in a computation's callback to `Unknown`, so it is no render for SC5003; that `Unknown` also set `invocation_context_unproven`, making `createMemo(() => m())` over an async memo an SC5001 obligation in every project since the flag was added. A pending source suspends such a read: `tracked_computation_read` separates it from a read no role places. Owner: `owners/async_context.rs`.

<a id="adr-0624"></a>
## ADR 0624 — SC5007: `<Loading on>` handed a function
rc.21 `LOADING_ON_ACCESSOR`. `on` is typed `any`, so `tsc` is silent. Reported on the exact `Loading` for a function literal or any value Type Facts call a function; a union that may be one is an obligation; any other value states nothing. Owner: `loading_hold.rs`.

<a id="adr-0625"></a>
## ADR 0625 — SC3002: `onFlush` in an owned scope
rc.21 `ON_FLUSH_IN_OWNED_SCOPE` tests the owned-scope write guard's own condition, so the write role places the call. A warning, as Solid's is; an unplaced call is uncertifiable. Owner: `static_api.rs`.

<a id="adr-0626"></a>
## ADR 0626 — A write below a shallow store's root is the raw write
Rules-mining CS-R38, R39, measured: `createStore(v, { shallow: true })` serves values raw, so `state.list[0].a = 2` lands silently and `state.list = []` is dropped with `STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER`. SC2003, formerly uncertifiable, is silent past the root of a store a literal option proves shallow. Owner: `upstream_compat/shared_reactivity.rs` (`shallow_store_root`). Projections, derived stores and optimistic stores take the option too, after a seed where they have one (rules-mining CS-R39, CS-R46, reproduced in Chrome).

<a id="adr-0627"></a>
## ADR 0627 — An owned optimistic write before load is an obligation
rc.21 throws `OPTIMISTIC_WRITE_BEFORE_LOAD` from an async source's optimistic setter before its first answer, ahead of the owned-scope guard: SC2001 named the wrong code for the store form, and the signal form, unguarded since rc.17, was silent. No rule claims the code yet, so an owned write there is uncertifiable. Owner: `local_access.rs`; `Dialect::refuses_optimistic_write_before_load`. Correction (rules audit, reproduced in Chrome): this branch never ran, since it tested the setter's symbol against `async_sources`, which held accessors, so SC2001 stayed on the store form and the signal form stayed silent. Superseded by ADR 0636.

<a id="adr-0628"></a>
## ADR 0628 — A hook's memo under `untrack`, and a labelled `untrack`'s window
rc.21, in Chrome. A hook creates under its caller's owner, so `owner._parent === context` holds for the calling body. A hook returning the creation itself (`return createMemo(...)`, an arrow's body) is a returned source like a bound one: its plain read is SC5001, not SC1001 (the run throws); summaries carry it (ADR 0486). `untrack_pending_read` answers per read: `Throws` for a source a layer declares or a hook called directly in a layer creates (whole, or an object slot via `const h = useM()` or a pattern); `OtherOwner` for a module-scope source (it suspends); otherwise `Unproven`, an obligation, including a root's value and two creations in one hook. Layers are exact `untrack`s and what runs in place on their stack (an IIFE, a closure, a helper's callback); `untrack(m)` and `untrack(read)` read as `untrack(() => m())`. A helper reading a parameter under `untrack`, and a hook result no transport resolves, are obligations at the hand-off and the read, unless nothing the value reaches can be pending and this body's own: no call in the hook makes a computation (a signal or store of a data literal, a sync memo reading nothing), and a reached prop has plain signals from every call site (its getter runs here; an inline memo would be this body's). A module `const` holding a hook's result, read directly or via local `const`s, belongs to no body's owner. An `untrack` in a body with no proven window (one `dynamic` mounts) stays an obligation for a source that body makes: the read re-runs the body onto a fresh pending memo, never settling or reporting a code (also `a1-hook-root`, a prop's memo): no grid measures it; reading "no window" as "not under `untrack`" had certified it. A memo over an async source is pending with it, which no row states: under `untrack` it throws (it was certified) and a plain read was a proven SC1001 where the run throws; both are obligations. `untrack(fn, "label")` opens a window wherever it runs (`strictRead = label`), reporting reads and throwing on any pending source, in a handler too; `false`, `undefined` and `""` open none; any other expression is unplaced. Owners: `execution_role/read_context.rs` (`untrack_layers`, `untrack_pending_read`, `untrack_label`), `pending_sources.rs`, `source_discovery/returned_sources.rs`, `Dialect::reports_untracked_reads_at`.

A view's whole-value use has its sources' shape, not the store's: open. A write through a multi-source view meets the view's trap or the last source and is silent in Chrome: SC2003 is open there. <a id="adr-0639"></a>
## ADR 0639 — The strict window and the DOM event, measured in Chrome
The rc.21 window as read from source (`strictReadEvent`, `dispatchingEvent`) ran in real Chrome through the fuzzer's host, 30 programs, against the checker. Silent and clean: a sync handler (delegated, or a listener a `ref` adds), a handler past an await, one dispatched from another handler or an effect's apply, a timer or microtask scheduled from a handler or apply, a focus handler an apply fires. Warns, SC1001: an apply's read when a handler's `flush()` runs the apply, and after the apply dispatched something. Warns, uncertifiable: a listener dispatched from a body or a render effect's apply (`untrack(fn, label)` sets `ANY_EVENT`, so the body's window takes the dispatch); an owned write there throws, also uncertifiable. A delegated handler dispatched from a body does not run (the element is not attached yet). The hand-written programs showed no disagreement; the event axis now generating them (`evt-*`, `timer-from-*`, `micro-from-*`, misuse.mjs) found one: `flush()`'s guard, unlike the strict window and the primitive guard, takes no event, so a flush in a handler an apply dispatches warns `FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK`, and was certified. A flush in a non-component function is an SC2008 obligation while any dispatch in the program has no placed caller (`DispatchSite::withholds_flush_proofs`: callers excluding a strict-read window; not the receiver, as a listener on `window` flushes too). So a dispatch in a helper or body keeps it; a harness dispatching after an await, a dispatching handler, and a `flush()` no delivery reaches (a timer, past an await, a reviewed test runner) cost nothing. The first version took any visible dispatch, moving 8,732 legal lg-recall programs from clean to uncertifiable. `element.click()` was no hazard (an argumentless call had no resolved-call demand); now it is, and a dispatch directly in a `createEffect`'s apply is its own caller class (`DispatchCaller::EffectApply`): no window, as measured, so a handler's read keeps its proof while owner and flush proofs stay withheld. Any other unplaced `click()`, in a body or helper, withholds handler read proofs program-wide, as `dispatchEvent` always did.

Review of #173's added obligations found them wider than their evidence; each became a proof. Caller classes now apply to every receiver, as the flush veto reads a `window` dispatch's caller. The test-only check, and for a dispatch's own function the event-only check, hold in an open program: an export refuses, and unseen code cannot name what a module keeps. A `flush()` on the stack of a call no delivery reaches, via `createRoot` or a helper's inline callback, is no obligation. rc.21 emits the untracked-read diagnostic before throwing, so a `catch` keeps SC5001; SC5003 and SC5005 keep their demotion, unmeasured (`AsyncRead::caught`). A store read in layers that run only in place takes the role they land on. SC2009's reach is a call path, not a mention: a hook's closed-result slot (an expression-body arrow's too), callees, identifiers handed on, a `let`'s assignments. An unwritten `let`'s value, a pattern or member of a fresh literal (a spread of one, a quoted computed key) or a local hook's returned slot is that source (`closed_source`), under an `untrack` that keeps the throw. A catch that is one `throw` leaves the pass on every path; a setter is no read. `s.c.m()` is a proven SC2003 when `c` is an instance of a class in the store's literal, the setter is never named, the store is local, and the class has no `extends` or computed member. ## ADR 0641 — What the fuzzer's judge left unjudged
Reviewing the 898 findings the judge left unsettled found a false positive and a wrong rule every grid passed. SC5003 proved `<C a={m()} />`, but rc.21 warns from a render effect hitting the pending value (`dist/dev.js:2680-2705`), and a component prop is a getter, silent unless read (Chrome). It is now uncertifiable at any component tag's prop or children, with the blocker in its message; the proof (following the prop to the child's consumer) is in `docs/open-work.md`. A control-flow tag reads in a render and stays proven. `resolve` and `until` under `createTrackedEffect` were `leaf-owner-forbidden-call`, but rc.21 throws the observer guard's "Cannot call resolve inside a reactive scope", so on rc.21 they are SC2004 and SC2005 (`Dialect::leaf_call_throws_observer_guard`). Only `createTrackedEffect` has an observer: under `onSettled`, whose callback runs under `untrack`, the call stays `leaf-owner-forbidden-call`, reporting `PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE`; rc.17 measures otherwise (ADR 0614). The judge: an `agree` row judges every other violation on its lines, so no wrong rule hides behind a claiming one (of 39 rows surfaced, 34 are masked: Chrome reports only the row's code and each twin without the first throw reports the second rule's, so they go in `besideCode`; 5 are `primitive-in-directive-application` beside a sync memo in a `ref`, where no run shows the claim and only its documented silence excuses it); a finding with no counter on its line is judged by its component's entry, a placeless code by file; `gate` fails on a rise per (rule, verdict, reason), as a growing blind spot is a regression; every row with an unsettled entry runs in Chrome; `source` and `mount` are back on the differential's axes (their omission is why 7 of those 898 rows had been compared). `runtime-oracle.mjs` no longer waits forever on a batch process that printed its closing line but did not exit. Unexecuted violations stay reported and are not false positives by themselves: a rule claims "whenever this runs" (ADR 0458), the twin (`effect-apply-guard-open`, `cbp-apply`) shows the claim true once it runs, and the gate holds their count down. `deadsite.mjs`'s "real false positive" means code the program builds and can never run: a `--confirm-with-chrome` question, not a grid verdict.

<a id="adr-0642"></a>
## ADR 0642 — What delivers a listener on its caller's stack
Supersedes ADR 0329's "tree mutation stays trusted" for `remove`, and ADR 0639's `click`/`dispatchEvent` scope. With only those two as hazards, the program-wide veto missed everything else that runs a listener synchronously, certifying a handler's read, write and pending read clean from a body, a compute, `untrack` in a compute and `onSettled`, where Chrome warns or throws. Measured: `focus`, `blur`, `select` (`focus`, `focusin`; only the `select` event is queued), `remove` of a focused element and `removeChild` (`blur`), `checkValidity`, `reportValidity`, `requestSubmit`, `HTMLFormElement.reset`, `HTMLDialogElement.show`, `showModal`, `close`, `requestClose`, `showPopover`, `hidePopover`, `togglePopover`, `Document.execCommand` (`input` on a focused editable), `ElementInternals.checkValidity` and `reportValidity`, `HTMLSelectElement.remove`. `other.focus()` also delivers to the element losing focus, so the handler at risk need not be on the receiver. `delivers_synchronously` names exact declarations by the type facts' own names (`HTMLOrSVGOrMathMLElement.focus`, `ChildNode.remove`), so a project method called `focus` is no hazard; a `click` or `dispatchEvent` on `any`, an interface or a type literal's signature names no declaration and is an `Unknown` site (the runtime receiver may be an element), placed by the existing caller classes. Whether focus moves is a runtime fact, so each is taken to deliver, an unfocused `remove` included, and ADR 0640's created element is no exclusion here: `autofocus`, a selection or a tab key can focus an element no use names. The list cannot be complete, so a handler keeps the host-delivery proof only if its event is on a measured list (`event_keeps_delivery_proof`), read at the delivering element, not a component's prop; one denied delivery denies the function. State changes deliver `focus`, `blur`, `focusin`, `focusout`, `change`, `compositionend`, `beforetoggle`, `load`, `formdata` with no call to name (`t.disabled = true`, `replaceWith`, an `append` that moves, `new FormData`, a `popover` write, `Selection.collapse`, Solid's own unmount) on the stack of `flush()` in a body or `createRoot`, `render()`'s disposer in a body, memo, compute or `untrack`, and `insert()`: those handlers are uncertifiable in any program, `flush()` in one is SC2008's obligation, a hazard in one has no handler's stack, and an unmeasured name is denied too. Not hazards: `form.submit` (it delivers `formdata`, which the family holds), `setSelectionRange`, `setRangeText`, `scrollIntoView`, `details.open` (nothing synchronous). What the veto now withholds in the corpus is in `docs/open-work.md`. An apply's delivery keeps its read proof only while no flush drains under an owner; under one it has that owner and the handler's write throws (`g10`). Owners: `runtime_semantics.rs`, `base/contributions.rs`, `indexes/event_delivery.rs`, `cleanup.rs`, `solid-facts-backend/src/demand_plan.rs`.

## ADR 0643 — What installs a member no rule follows
No rule saw a plain local's Get or write as reactive, yet a store prototype makes each the store's, and an installed signal accessor makes `o.a = 2` the setter's call. Chrome rc.21, all certified before: `({ __proto__: store }).v`, the quoted key, `merge({ __proto__: store }, …).v`, `Object.create(store)`, `Object.setPrototypeOf(o, store)` and `Reflect`'s, `o.__proto__ = store`, `F.prototype = store`, a method that reparents `this`, `set("__proto__", store)` reaching `o[k] = v`, the receiver of `Reflect.set(o, k, v, store)` (`STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED`, `STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER`), and `Object.defineProperty`, `defineProperties`, `Reflect.defineProperty`, `Object.create`'s descriptors, aliased, destructured, by `.call`, by a literal key or via `globalThis` (`REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE`, a strict read through `get`). `installed_members.rs` answers at the install, flow insensitively. Clear: a function with its own body (judged there) or a plain prototype: `null`, a closed literal of functions and primitives, a `.prototype`, `Object.getPrototypeOf(x)`, `Object.create` of one. Anything else is SC9012 at the install, blocker `installed-member`. `Reflect.defineProperty` on a store is proved SC2003. A computed `["__proto__"]` key or a shorthand installs nothing. A data descriptor is `o.k = value`: the store-in-a-container gap. Owner: `installed_members.rs`.

Amendment (R5, 2026-11-08): each Get or write this file writes of an installed object (the literal, its binding, or a call handed it whole, placed at that call: `merge(o, …).v`) is its own `installed-member` U; a handed `__proto__` key marks every object a non-literal key writes. 42 flow store-proto false cleans fixed.

A function that is possibly a component is uncertain as before, so handed callbacks such as a table's `render` move from SC1001 to SC1004 at the same site, uncertifiable both ways. The rule read a one-parameter pattern and `const {a} = root`. A hook whose returns are all closed literals is proved plain. A helper stays a site: unlike the exact-root arm, this claims nothing about its run, and the fuzzer found 56 false cleans behind that gate. `o12`, a helper's parameter, waits for the census. The block's reads were its calls of proven sources, and a handed callee was skipped, so `try { props.v() }`, `try { props.v }` and a literal's getter certified while rc.21 warned `CAUGHT_REACTIVE_READ`. A call of a handed value stays a site, and a Get of a handed root or of a literal with an accessor becomes one; both are uncertifiable, `caught-read-unproven`. `member_get_may_read` is shared with SC5006. <a id="adr-0659"></a>
## ADR 0659 — Which build runs a site, and `--rendering hydrated`
No selection could describe a hydrated app: `csr` is false for it, node hid client rules, and SSR with browser is refused. `--rendering hydrated` selects both: no server build for client rules, proven server rendering for server rules. Packages resolve as the client, so `browser` joins its conditions and node is refused. `ExecutionPaths` holds two facts. `client_root_present` is any `render` or `hydrate` import, by name, since only its absence proves anything: under a server selection a client claim is silent with no root and stands with one; only server entries and no selection make it uncertifiable, `client-root-unproven`. A build arm is a region under a proven `isServer` (silent for a client claim) or under a constant nothing decides (uncertifiable, `build-constant-arm`). Both apply once over final findings, for the rules `claims_client_runtime` names, so no rule's code changes. Owners: `execution_paths.rs`, the rules catalog. Limits are in `docs/open-work.md`.

V needs `read_in_root_pass` (an unconditional position, then a chain to a module-scope mount; else SC5003 is uncertifiable, `root-mount-pass-unproven`) and `read_in_creation_pass` (the source is pending). SC5001 has no render gate: a later own memo still throws. The chain takes any synchronous holder under `render`, not just a bare tag; `<Show when={1}>` is a literal. Supersedes ADR 0642's "Not hazards: `form.submit`". `computation_provenance` answers `Async`, `Sync` or `Open` for a source's first argument. `Sync` needs a data value, a non-callable type, or a function whose closed return names no `NotReadyError`, directly or via a call. `Open` joins `async_sources` with `opaque` and `provenance_open`: SC5001, SC5003 and the read's SC1001 are uncertifiable, blocker `async-provenance-unproven`. `computation_is_async` stays the static rules' Promise proof. A parameter compute (`createMemo(options)`) is open even if its type would close it: no fact carries a value's signature. `SC8015`'s gate reads a getter's open source as a reactive read (`pending_open` is not `uncertain` about the backing). <a id="adr-0673"></a>
## ADR 0673 — A read only a hidden call runs is placed nowhere

A hidden call (getter, `toString`, iterator, tag, field, `Map` slot) has no call node, so a plain helper it runs had no row or obligation: certified, Chrome warns. `placed_nowhere` joins the old pair with ADR 0708's `runs_unplaced_through_callers` at the two read arms and in `discover_member`, role `Unknown`, blocker by the existing read wording. This changes the helper for all its readers. It also asks the hand-off census (`SiteReach` never entered a method only a coercion runs) and reads this file's calls of the binding (no edge leaves such a method). Each helper is visited once, as the walk fans out over every caller (without the set `solid-groove` went from 5 s to the 900 s kill), so a diamond past the depth bound may answer from its first path; ADR 0674 removes the bound. <a id="adr-0674"></a>
## ADR 0674 — The caller walk answers only by finishing

`runs_unplaced_through_callers` answered "placed" wherever it stopped: its depth, a helper visited on a longer path, a parameter default, an await on some paths, a generator, a nameless function. Each stop was a silent clean; four were live (`hidden-generator`, `hidden-generator-held`, `hidden-may-await`, `hidden-param-default`: certified, Chrome warns). It is now a depth-free worklist, each function once so order cannot change the answer, answering "unplaced" past a 4,096-site budget. Only an await on every path ends a branch as placed. `visited` terminates it; the budget only guards a pathological graph.

The walk read calls, so a helper in a member only a coercion runs, reached by `h.call(null)`, `[1].map(h)`, a copy or a tag, certified, in one file or two. It now reads every reference of the binding. In another file a hand-off is unplaced (no role reads a carrier there); a proven component stops the question (its render enters it however its value travels); an intrinsic element's attribute is the handler roles' to place. A type query is `HandOff::Value`: erased, nothing runs. That removes 208 SC6002 sites, 5 SC1001 and 1 SC2006 resting only on `ReturnType<typeof useThing>`.

A named function no edge enters, handed to a carrier in a body, had no read row: `[1].map(h)` certified. A carrier that provably runs it now proves SC1001 (`handed_async_read_role`); one that may not leaves it uncertifiable (ADR 0531's zero-run trade does not extend here). W's nets take the same walk.

<a id="adr-0675"></a>

`inheriting_call_role`'s reporting tail trusted the role at the call. Climbing the in-place layers from it: a scheduler's callback has no window, proved silent (`n06`, and the 21 rows `sc1001.md` counts); a function nothing places proves no role; a component's body keeps it. `reporting_role_where_the_call_runs`, reads only. `:817` is untouched: direct reads rely on it. A read in a window-inheriting callback in a named handler also called in place (`h(); <button onClick={h} />`) certified because the handler role answered first; a reporting in-place call now wins. `n13 n22 n23` hold, so `sc1001.md`'s step 5 is unneeded.
