`setSt((d) => …)`, `setN((p) => …)` and a `reconcile` key that is argument 0 run on the setter's stack, so the setter's call places a read there, in discovery rather than `layer_run` (which W's write walk reads). In an apply: proved V, where it certified (`r11 r18
r19 z01`). A key applied to the draft (`reconcile(v, key)(d)`) counts too. Where the write guard throws first the callback never runs:
no read claim, SC2001 stands (`z04 r16 r17`). A setter callback in a tracked effect runs quietly (`t1 t2`). A key on a `reconcile` held anywhere else, or anything else built for the setter (`storePath`), is unplaced: `r13`, `r14` lose a false V. A store's own Gets in its setter's callback are silent in any window: `dev.js` checks `!inDraft`, measured (`z05`, `z08` through a `const`). The test is identity of the tuple binding, so another store warns (`z09`), as does a signal's own read (`z10`). `CloudStorageContext:238` moves V to U: read in `setState(storePath(…))`. rc.21 exports no `storePath`, so the file does not load and has no Chrome truth; `sc1001.md` predicted this move. Tell the owner.

<a id="adr-0677"></a>
## ADR 0677 — A function only an arm reaches is in the arm; `NoHydration` is one
`ExecutionPaths` arms were lexical, so a helper only `if (isServer)` calls, or a tag only `isServer ? <X /> : null` renders, kept every client claim: `SC2003` and `SC2001` violations on code a client build never runs (ADR 0659's limit), likewise for a helper a `clientOnly` body calls (ADR 0664's). `arm_only_functions` runs to a fixpoint from the functions an arm names: a function whose references all lie in arms joins the weakest. A deferred arm passes only a call, since a tag's body has its window back. A server-only answer for a function other files may import is open, `build-constant-arm`, not silent. A function with no symbol stays out and keeps its finding. `<NoHydration>` is a new arm where a hydrating pass may run (the selection, or a `hydrate` call): it returns `undefined` while hydrating, and a plain mount renders its children, so claims there are uncertifiable, blocker `no-hydration-subtree`; with no hydrating pass they stand. An `export function f` holds all of `f`, so only export specifiers count as naming a binding.

<a id="adr-0678"></a>
## ADR 0678 — `render(Local, el)` is a root, not a component body
`render` calls its first argument as `code()` under `createRoot`, so a function it names has no strict window, like `render(() => ..., el)`; `<Local />` goes through `createComponent` and has one. The named form took the component's role: SC1001 on a read Chrome is quiet about, a pending read only uncertifiable. A read in a function whose every reference is such a call now takes the call's role, proving SC1001 silent and SC5001 true at module level. Reads only: a write under the root keeps SC2001. A `<Local />` anywhere, an alias, an export or a use in another file keeps the old answer, so `render(App, el)` with an imported `App` stays uncertifiable (D15, Chrome: an uncaught `NotReadyError`): the query and the recorded walk are per file.

<a id="adr-0679"></a>
## ADR 0679 — A window-inheriting call in a function not placed proves no role
ADR 0675 climbed the in-place layers around `createRoot` and `runWithOwner`, then kept the role at the call, which in a component is lexical: `const go = () => createRoot(...); setTimeout(go, 0)` was a violation, as was a helper called only from two handlers; Chrome is quiet on both. A function written in a component now answers from where it runs: a compiler or primitive callback keeps its role; a named one takes the roles of its direct calls and in-place hand-offs, one reporting position proving the claim and none proving it silent; one only handed to fresh-stack schedulers is silent; a nameless holder, or one handed anywhere else, is unplaced (`function-run-unplaced`). A direct call in a body (`h(); h()`) and `[1].forEach(h)` keep the true warning.

<a id="adr-0680"></a>
## ADR 0680 — Every uncertifiable row names its blocker
The flip's rule asks for a clear uncertifiable with a named blocker, but only rows the flip added had one: 9,216 of the corpus's 18,502 carried a function's name or nothing in `analysisContext`, the reason only in wording (SC1001 4,792, SC2001 3,220, SC9012 558, SC2008 237). `project_finding` now derives the blocker from the row's own holes, in the order the proof meets them; each backend escalation (`instance-field-run-unplaced`, `component-identity-unproven`, the four site-reach answers, `binding-before-initialization`, `compiler-refused-file`) names its own, and a final pass names a row from its rule. `proof-step-unnamed` is the last resort, not a blocker: `check.mjs` fails on it or any unnamed row, `scripts/flip/blockers.mjs` likewise over a sweep. The old context stays as detail, `blocker:context`. No finding moves.

<a id="adr-0681"></a>
## ADR 0681 — A cleanup registered with no owner never runs; a `ref` under `<Dynamic>` has no window
`createEffect`'s apply has no owner on any run (rc.21, measured: `getOwner()` null on the first run and a rerun, `NO_OWNER_CLEANUP` on both), so `onCleanup` there registers nothing, and a read reached only through that callback claimed what never happens. `refused_entry` now treats the callback as a refused entry: uncertifiable, `refused-creation-unrun`, like a creation refused in an apply. `createRenderEffect`'s apply is owned on its first run and keeps its answer, via the dialect's `apply_never_owned`. An intrinsic's `ref` runs during the body creating the element, so `dynamic_component_entry` steps out of it and the `ref` path answers via `dynamic_read_role`: under `<Dynamic>` its read is no claimed warning but uncertifiable, `callee-callback-timing`.

<a id="adr-0682"></a>
## ADR 0682 — A read attributed at a call has the window of the body it is in
`dynamic_read_role` ran only for direct reads, so a helper's pending read in a body `<Dynamic>` mounts kept the lexical `UntrackedRendering` and SC5001's violation under `<Loading>`, where Chrome retries and renders. The post-pass over `async_reads` now gives an attributed row a direct read's role and boundary cover. The helper's own site stays uncertifiable (unplaced, `function-run-unplaced`). With no boundary (Chrome: `ASYNC_OUTSIDE_LOADING_BOUNDARY`) or a mixed mount (Chrome: throws) the program stays uncertifiable.

<a id="adr-0683"></a>
## ADR 0683 — A method a project subclass may replace is not the standard one
TypeFacts resolves `held.forEach` to `Array.forEach` when `held: number[]`, and every in-place, fresh-stack and carrier proof trusts that, so `class Late extends Array` overriding `forEach` with a microtask left a violation Chrome contradicts (`d05 d06 g24`). A class extending `Array`, `Map`, `Set`, `WeakMap`, `WeakSet` or `Promise` (global, `globalThis.` member, or `const` alias) raises a recorded hazard per declared member, or for every member if a key is computed. While raised, `resolved_callee_call` answers nothing for that container's method, in the lookup and the memo context, so those proofs fail closed (uncertifiable). Per member, not container: the corpus's `ogl` vectors extend `Array`, and a container-wide veto moved 3 true SC8014 violations and 279 rows on methods no subclass replaces. An array-literal receiver is exempt, locally proven no subclass instance. Limits: a mixin-made class or a patched prototype raises nothing; these are premises, like `Object.prototype` writes. Cost: +68 uncertifiable in `app-game`, all but one SC9012, where classes overriding `Map` and `Set` members leave plain maps unproven; a receiver bound to `new Map()` could be proved like an array literal.

<a id="adr-0684"></a>
## ADR 0684 — A component's references are all uses
`props_reactivity` lists a component's references to prove its props closed. Two exemptions skipped references by position: anything inside the component's own function, and any same-named reference inside one of its elements. So `saved = C`, `me={C}`, `<C>{C({ run: setN })}</C>` and `<Dynamic component={C} run={setN} />` inside `<C>` handed the component on and stayed certified; Chrome throws (H-D5). Now only the name and the element's own opening and closing tag are exempt; any other reference escapes, and the reads are uncertifiable. The census's JSX row rests on this.

<a id="adr-0685"></a>
## ADR 0685 — `valueOf` and `__defineGetter__` are no slot calls
The closure census took any named member call as handing the receiver nowhere. `x.valueOf()` hands `x` out, and `__defineGetter__` and `__defineSetter__` write it, so a closed object's slot could be replaced while the read stayed proved: V where Chrome is quiet, certified where it warns (H-D4). Those three no longer count. They matter because `Object.prototype`'s members are the only names `tsc` accepts beyond a closed literal's own slots.

<a id="adr-0686"></a>
## ADR 0686 — A resolved call drops its obligation only if the target's summary is complete
ADR 0509 let an exactly resolved call transport drop its handed obligation. But the summary misses what the target reads through a captured parameter: `useT(c)` then `x.read()` certified; Chrome warns or throws. `interproc/completeness.rs` answers whether a target's summary is complete: default open; a captured parameter (any shape), `arguments`, `this`, a rebound binding, or a `const` walked to one is incomplete, through calls; a cycle adds nothing, a bound answers open. A parameter annotated only with primitive types is followed: no accessor, store or props object fits one (`id as any` is the program's own cast). Setters, actions and non-async accessors stay exact. Only `reactive_analysis.rs` reads it, so no result chunk holds a cross-file answer. Left to F0's later steps: `handed_value` on a call expression (`f01 f12`), `ArrayMembers` at a reporting role (`f02`).

<a id="adr-0687"></a>
## ADR 0687 — A helper whose callers differ is not a nullable owner
`conditional_owner` meant "owned and unowned contexts both reach this", and every such row said `runWithOwner` received a nullable owner. Track W's mixed hooks (ADR 0714) are another case: a function reached by an owned caller and an unproven unowned one, with no `runWithOwner` anywhere. A site in a callback whose owner the dialect calls `Conditional` keeps that wording; others get `owner_varies_by_caller`, with its own wording and blocker `owner-varies-by-caller`. No kind moves.

<a id="adr-0688"></a>
## ADR 0688 — A reactive value handed to `Function.call` with no target is an obligation
`Array.prototype.sort.call(store)` resolves to `Function.call`; the member's own declaration is no recorded query, so no write rule sees `sort`, and a reactive value silently reached code nothing reads. Now an invoking `.call` or `.apply` resolving to the standard declaration, with no function or source row for its receiver and a store, accessor, setter or action (or a member of one) among its arguments, is SC9012, `function-call-target-unresolved`. A plain value and a resolved project function keep their answers. It sits in `local_access.rs`, outside the write rules, and closes `values/d1n-array-proto-call`.

<a id="adr-0689"></a>
## ADR 0689 — A hook's result is handed where no binding holds it
`handed_value` followed a `const` holding a call and no other call, so `useT(p.n).read()`, `useT(n)()()` and `useT(n).forEach((f) => f())` opened no obligation and certified; Chrome warns on all three (`v08`, `f01`, `f12`, `f02`). Now a call is handed wherever it stands, a call of a call answers as its inner call, and a callback's parameter holds an element of its standard-library call's receiver. A transported array's member call (`x.forEach((e) => e.m())`) whose target's summary is incomplete raises the obligation no std call had to keep. All of it is now uncertifiable.

<a id="adr-0690"></a>
## ADR 0690 — What `children`, `mapArray` and `repeat` return is a memo's accessor
None was a source, so `const r = children(fn); r()` in a body certified; Chrome warns (T-D2, `w07`, `w08`, `k10`, `k11`). They are sources now, over-async like a memo, and `createMemo(fn)()` is a read at the call: a violation, or `created-accessor-read-unproven` when the compute may suspend. `.toArray()` is a handed call: uncertifiable. Fixture `created-accessors`.

<a id="adr-0691"></a>
## ADR 0691 — A destructure takes the read's role
`may_snapshot_at_setup` asked the semantic role, which answers a `ref`'s layers and rows with a deferred callback before any `ref` arm, so a store destructure there certified; Chrome warns: 152 false cleans. It now asks the read's role. All uncertifiable.

<a id="adr-0692"></a>
## ADR 0692 — The value-use census
What a program does with a reference of a proven source was decided nowhere: an unlisted form was a silent clean (`try { throw c; } catch (e) { (e as () => number)(); }` certified; Chrome warns). `value_use.rs` walks every reference of an accessor, setter, action, store root or props root, and every unbound creating call, to a position the analysis follows, else answers an SC9012 uncertifiable naming that position. A form no table holds defaults to the obligation, `value-use-unclassified`.

End-stage item 1c: an export's span is its whole statement, so the draft answered "followed" anywhere in an `export function` (`throw c` certified there; Chrome warned). `exported_directly` takes only `export { c }` and `export default c`; the fixture holds each form in each placement.

A `Followed` answer needs a proof; probes found five without one: a method's or slot's returned value, a call through a pattern over a holder (`const { c: h } = o; h()`), a JSX tag, a helper reading `arguments`, a helper destructuring a root. A walked call now needs a read, write or action row, or for an accessor a tracked position, so the census runs after rows merge. Exported holders are walked in other files; a context handed on (`useCtx(Ctx)`) is open. `createRoot` answers its callback's value (`returned_callback_value`), so a memo a module root returns is followed.

Facts schema 90: `inert_operands` (a statement's value, operands of `void`, `typeof`, `===`, `!==`). A plain assignment's target holds no value. Only a position met while being answered is a cycle.

Slots are kept by static key: another slot's Get or pattern key holds nothing of the source; a computed, escaped or non-canonical numeric key (`0x1`), a slot past a spread and a rest element hold every slot. `{ __proto__: v }` sets `v` as the prototype, so it is open; shorthand `{ __proto__ }` is an own slot. `{ ...holder }` in an object literal keeps the slots, one member step away. Past 8 returns, props or contexts or 512 positions a walk is open. A walked call is followed when a row or obligation stands at its callee or the call itself (not inside or around it), or when its role clears what the walked value does there: an accessor's read, a setter's or action's write, both for a tuple or root. A function's callers are followed only when the call graph resolves every call reference to it, an exported one's only in a closed program no namespace hands it out of (ADR 0203); else `value-to-unresolved-callee` or `value-forwarded`. No callers alone proves nothing. `mapArray`'s source goes where its result goes (`callback_requires_return_invocation`). One SC9012 per span: a census row at upstream's `package-callee` row is dropped.

W's review found five more unproven `Followed` answers (Chrome warns on each): `export default` of a literal or creating call (`value-exported-unnamed`); a pattern inside a parameter hop (only the hop's own reference is exempt from a row); a pending accessor at a tracked position (a tracked role excuses only a source not in `async_sources`, or a created one ADR 0665 proves Sync); a store or props root's member past the walk's first step (it needs a row on its chain, unless the alias is a source walked on its own, ADR 0463); a bare JSX child (an intrinsic parent inserts it, a component reads `props.children`, else open). The holder, reference and row maps are ordered: walks follow source order.

Proved: nothing new is a violation. Uncertifiable: rc.21 corpus 27,229 to 28,428 U, 377 V unchanged, 0 sites cleared, +946 sites (app-game 491, donegeon-client 104; W's fixes 162 of them); two rows moved from an alias's declaration to its use. An audit traced the 106 census rows the slot, hop and row steps removed from the fixtures: 23 returned to U via the guards above, 83 each end at a named proof (a row at the reached call, another slot, an inert position, a role, a parameter hop, …). Open patterns 90 to 8 (7 with ADR 0820).

<a id="adr-0693"></a>
## ADR 0693 — A `new Map()` is no subclass instance
ADR 0683 withheld a method a project subclass replaces unless the receiver is an array literal. A `new` of the global container itself, inline or in a binding nothing rebinds, is as local a fact, and an array literal counts through such a binding too. A subclass, its alias, a shadowing name and a rebound `let` keep the veto. Fixture `plain-container-receiver`.

<a id="adr-0694"></a>
## ADR 0694 — A store Get that only leads to a write is not a proven read in a dynamic-only body
Owner's ruling on SC1001 in `flush(fn)` in a body only `dynamic()` mounts, a violation under ADRs 0530 and 0542. A real read there (`const x = s.a`) keeps its violation though Chrome is quiet: a violation means wrong if it runs. A Get serving only a write's path (`s.a` in `s.a.v = 7`, `s.items.push(1)`, `delete s.a.o`, `Object.assign(s.a, x)`) is uncertifiable, `write-path-read-unproven`, like the write itself in that body; its path claims no more. `+=` reads its value and keeps the violation; a chain through a call is a read. `serves_write_path` asks SC2003's own tables by exact declaration; `via` carries the mark, and a row at a helper's call asks the Get it stands for. Fixture `flush-write-path`. `mx-recall`, `pos-flush-fn`: 72 of 2,514 programs, 84 SC1001 rows V to U, 0 outcomes. The judge rates both answers fine, so no old build fails the grid.

<a id="adr-0695"></a>
## ADR 0695 — What no primitive placed proves no silence, and walks finish
`allowed-unplaced-nested` answered silent for any function in an allowed region. It now answers `Unknown` unless a primitive's own arm places the function, a scheduler runs it on a fresh stack, only a host keeps it in a project dispatching nothing itself (ADR 0535), or it is a compute's returned closure the apply only `void`s; so `untrack(fn)` and `onSettled(fn)` stay silent, and an action's step keeps its proved SC5001. A cleanup stays silent only while nothing in the project can dispose an owner on a chosen stack (a `getOwner` or `createOwner`, a `createRoot` taking `dispose`, a kept `render`, `hydrate` or `onCleanup` result). The test is project-wide, part of `Section::ProjectPrimitives`' value: the draft asked one file, where an unrelated `getOwner` answered (`b1`, `b4`). A cleanup whose handle may be called is `Unknown`.

Bounds W's ADRs 0750-0752 left: `dynamic_component_entry` past 32 climbs names no entry, `Unknown` (33 nested `flush(fn)` promised a warning Chrome does not give); `may_throw_not_ready` walks each function once; a forwarded callback is an effect-phase layer; a sync helper hands on its chain's flight; `derived_objects` stands for what it did not read. The `chain-N` shapes found `report_helper_reads` stopping at 3 helpers: 70 false cleans, now uncertifiable; it follows the chain, each helper once. The rest are in `bounds-audit.md`.

<a id="adr-0696"></a>
## ADR 0696 — Three rulings: a read Chrome names otherwise, a guard no caller passes, a branch no fact decides
Owner's rulings, not to reopen. (1) `c16`: a destructure over a pending prop keeps `no-destructure`; the read is true, Chrome warns `PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ`, and the judge says so (`besideCode`). (2) `h17`: code behind `p.on` is proved silent only when every caller is in view and none passes `on`; a caller passing it, a spread, an exported component in an open program and a default keep their answers. Fixture `absent-guard-prop`. (3) A branch whose condition the checker cannot evaluate is reachable, directly and via a helper, for every rule: a violation means the code is wrong if it runs. `if (Math.random() > 2) s.a = 1` keeps its violation though the run shows nothing; `scripts/fuzz/expected-silent.json` holds it so nobody lists it as a false positive.

<a id="adr-0712"></a>
## ADR 0712 — SC2009 does not certify a setter handed as a prop
`<K s={setO} />` with `props.s(2)` in K's handler certified; Chrome threw (witness SC2009 a04). Each component element's prop and spread values are now asked `callee_reaches` of an async optimistic setter; a hit is uncertain at the value, `optimistic-reach-unresolved`. A function written in the prop is skipped (its call is placed where it runs); a provider's `value` is a prop too. A transported call of one is uncertain too (a backstop), and the transported pass's SC2009 results, formerly dropped, reach the report. Not built: prop value flow. Corpus +22 uncertifiable. Owners: `local_access.rs`, `reactive_analysis.rs`.

<a id="adr-0713"></a>
## ADR 0713 — SC4002 does not certify a read of a context provided somewhere
The census asked "is there a provider anywhere", so a read under a sibling's, hook's, conditional or member's provider certified and threw (witnesses g02-g06, a07). `context_census.rs` gives each context `Free`, `Open` (provided, escaped, exported, or not one unwritten `const`) or `Silent`. An `Open`, unknown or member read under an owner is an uncertain SC4002 static violation, `provider-not-proven-above`, unless `provided_above` proves every render of its function under `<Ctx value>`: children with no function layer between (attributes are the parent's); intrinsics; a hook or component up to 8 callers; a wrapper whose one `return` is the provider with `props.children` a direct child, no `?:` or logical between. Each such function: named, non-async, every reference a call or render site, not exported in an open program; no `eval`, no `value={undefined}`, the context a `const`. Chrome, 12 probes: 6 proven quiet, 3 open that throw, 3 open and quiet (a `For` callback, a `let`, the exported reader). Corpus +48 uncertifiable. Owners: `context_census.rs`, `owner_emission.rs`, `owner_discovery.rs`, `owners.rs`.

<a id="adr-0714"></a>

The same held path takes a local helper not called only from events, and a member call of a parameter in a function written in a memo, which `SiteExclusions::at` excluded on its lexical role (the generator found it, not the report). A read a helper makes through a parameter gets the async row it lacked at a tracked position, without a boundary, held likewise in a local function (`a13`, `net-peek`). Writes through a parameter stay an obligation; a row for an exact `run(setS)` would prove them. This reverses `TrackedDispatch.tsx`'s pin of a tracked caller's `pageSlice(props.items)` as clean: `<K items={{ slice: setS } as unknown as readonly number[]} />` passes `tsc`, the old build certified it, Chrome throws (patterns `net/prop-std-method-tracked`). Step 2: a call that receives the global object, `Function` or an intrinsic is recorded per file (`IntrinsicHandedAtCall`) and decided over the project (`handed_to_unseen_code`): it counts unless the callee is seen code that neither calls nor keeps it: a standard-library declaration on a closed list (`SEEN_STANDARD`: read-only reflection and tests, index 0 only but `Object.is`), `call`/`bind` followed to their target with the arguments shifted, or a project or dependency function whose parameter, followed three levels, hands nothing on. The global object or an intrinsic stored or returned counts. a window method runs with it as `this`; seen only on a closed list (`THIS_SEEN`: `getComputedStyle`, `matchMedia`, the scrolls, `postMessage`, `removeEventListener`) or as `addEventListener` with a listener that can reach neither its `this` nor its event (every parameter unread; a non-arrow names no `this`, `super`, `arguments`; nothing runs a string). An `on*` handler written on the window passes the same test or is `null`; a timer's function callback counts unless an arrow or never mentioning `this`. `top`, `parent`, `frames`, `opener` name the window, and a value typed `Window` (`defaultView`, `contentWindow`, `open()`, …) is the global object. An optional-chain `.bind` with no declaration is unseen. **Premise**, as ADR 0335's: a program that neither writes visibly toward iteration nor obtains an intrinsic or code from a string leaves iteration intact. Outside the model: a script element, a frame, code not analyzed. Fixture `iteration-written-slots`; flip-read.mjs `iter-*`, `wd-*`.

Step 2. Seen code is the called value, not the declaration a resolution names: `project_callee_at_parameter` with its exact answer (ADRs 0221, 0278, 0279), or `function_value_is_current` on a name for a listener, a timer's callback and a `call`/`bind` target. A value a call made, a written binding, a written or overridden method, and a conditional are unseen (`c ? noop : patch` with equal signatures has no target set). Every list needs the member unwritten: by name on any receiver, and by `patch_veto`, which now counts a bare global write (`scrollTo = f`). `patch_veto` of a window member also asks a global write under an open key. `Object.keys(x)` is recorded, not dropped, in its own slot: the census has no project to ask, and a key argument is an ordinary one, since it is coerced through program code (`Object.hasOwn({}, window)`). Step 2, recovered proofs. A listener or timer callback is read at the call that hands the window on, not over every caller: another call with another target hands no window, so an exported helper needs no closed world. Followed through a parameter by what that call gave it (plain name, unwritten, no default, no `arguments`), a `const` by what its initializing call returns on every path, a `let` whose every write is a plain literal, and a function entered only by direct calls in its file, never exported and with no name of its own. ## ADR 0815 — A finding covers a runtime code only at the position Chrome recorded
One SC9012 anywhere covered every code Chrome reported, and a claiming row covered its code anywhere in the file; the census put about 1,200 SC9012 rows in the corpus. Now (codes.mjs `coversCodeAt`, `withheldAtStack`): for a code with a place, a withholding or claiming finding covers only when its start (line and column) is a recorded position: a stack's top frame (the read, write or throw), the exact call position of a deeper frame on a line that mounts nothing (`render(`, `hydrate(`, a JSX component tag), or, for a claiming or declining row and never a withholding, a callback's registering argument (`cb1` in `onSettled(cb1)`). V8 puts a member call's frame at the property, so a frame in a callee also stands for the call's and its callee object's start, and a store or props trap on a member access likewise: a frame on the property, on a `delete`, on a call's `)` before an access (no source mapping past it), on a member key, on a `for...of` binding, on a spread's operand, on a destructuring's binding or on a template stands for the expression's start or the substitution's (call-starts.mjs). A destructuring element's frame stands for the pattern's start (reported once) and no other element. A frame on a JSX attribute's name stands for its one read (call, member, `in`, spread), with two for neither: no mapping there. A top authored frame in a callee has no authored frame inside that call, so native or dependency code did the read on what it was handed: a withholding at an argument covers a handed-value code there (`handedCodes`), for a callee that is no Solid primitive or setter, and at no deeper frame; a store write's target is a member call's receiver or a function's first argument, so only a project or standard function's first argument counts, never a dependency's: a judge does not assume what an unread body writes. Known limit: which argument a read went through (`Object.assign(out, st, holder.v)`). `evaluatedDead` keeps an opaque value (`JSON.parse(`) at `unexecuted` under the owner's ruling; a spelling that test misses makes the judge stricter, a visible false positive, not quieter, so it stays one spelling. Depth is the whole stack's: another module's frame stays, blank for an App.tsx row (`appStack`; ADR 0921). A mount is a position (a component tag, a `render(` callee), not a line. No line ranges. Chrome runs the instrumented twin (lines kept, columns not); positions go back to the clean text where the record is made (hits.mjs `cleanPositions`, on both record paths, `RECORD_SOURCES`). A placeless code: a claiming finding anywhere, never a withholding; `SETTLED_CLEANUP_UNOWNED` joins them, measured. New outcomes: `misplaced` (the claiming rule's violation off Chrome's position: a defect), `unplaced` (a code with a place and no position with a column: unjudged, never a pass). The pattern lane records 41-frame stacks; the oracle path keeps columns; the witness judge (no stacks recorded) keeps claiming rows anywhere and places withholdings at a hand-read `closedAt` `{ file, line, blocker }`. Reversed: ADR 0504's review scored a run-claiming violation at a site whose counter is 0 a false positive; it is `unexecuted` (owner: a violation means wrong if it runs; an unevaluable branch counts as reachable). Owner's ruling on the triage: a false clean of a generator-only shape (`proto-*`, `store-proto-*`, `installed-*`; CAUGHT in `evt-*`, `flw-*`) with a named uncertifiable on the site's own line, at the value and not the read, is at the right site and stops there. The judge is not widened: the outcome stays as measured, and the row is listed by exact id under `R-stop-named-U` with its blocker, once open-rows.mjs has checked that finding; CAUGHT needs a read rule's row at the exact position Chrome reported, the generator-only variants a named row in the statement holding the read, by Chrome's frame on the site's lines; a row with no such frame stays out. It covers no shape real code has and no other group. An uncaught `NotReadyError` (a pending read at a mount, `render(f1, el)`: no code, an empty message) is the program's result, not the harness's: outcome `pending-uncaught` with a row at the read's site, a false clean with none; its stack is the memo's creation, so the site's lines stand for the read.

<a id="adr-0820"></a>
## ADR 0820 — A generator a computation steps runs on its flight
`for await (const v of gen())` in an async compute with a read past the generator's first suspension: Chrome warns, certified (`f02`, P9). The operand sits before its own suspension, so no region reached it, and helpers stopped at a generator. A member now follows every generator it calls, on `for await` as an `async` helper: past an `await` the read is proved, past a `yield` it is an obligation, blocker `generator-stepped`. Any other stepping (a wrapper, `next()`, `yield*`, an inline generator) and a sync generator stepped across an await are obligations too. A generator's straight-line reads are a window only until its first `yield`. Stay clean: the first segment under `for await`, a sync generator a loop drains before any suspension. Measured, kept: in `createMemo(async function* …)` the runtime watches no flight (`watchAsyncTail` takes promises only), so a read after an `await` gives no diagnostic, and a later write does not rerun the compute; SC1002 V there states a true claim.

ADR 0781 withheld a standard call only where an assertion sits on its callee, its receiver or a binding holding one, so `run(t as any)` into a `Promise<void>` parameter, a field or a return holding the cast (`p3-cast-*`), `const t: any`, an array's covariance, a type guard, `coerce<T>`, a cast literal's member and `Object.assign(p, { then })` on a real promise certified where Chrome warns. Option (d) of the lying-casts research, stage 1: each file contributes `Hazard::StandardImpostor` for every site that may put a function at a census member of an object (an object literal member that is no data literal, a class element, an export, a member write, `Reflect.set`, `defineProperty`, a descriptor map), and for `*` where no fact names the key (a computed key, `Object.fromEntries`, a `Proxy` with a `get` trap, `Reflect` held as a value). While the program holds such a site for the member or for `*`, a standard call of it is withheld unless its receiver is a primitive or a value the standard library made, by a fact of its file: an array literal, a `new` of a global container, `Array.from`/`of`, `Object.entries`/`keys`/`values`, `Promise.resolve` of nothing or of a non-promise, `reject`/`all`/`race`/…, `fetch`, a new value a standard method makes from one, a call of a local function every return of which is one (an `async` one's promise), `window`, `document.body`, on the spot or in a binding nothing rebinds or exports and uses only as a method receiver; each factory unpatched (ADR 0781). A withheld call is an SC9012 `standard-member-impostor` row (the impostor may be a setter, `{ slice: setS }`); it stands for a downstream rule's lost row (6 corpus rows). Stage 1 is the members with a callback timing (`then`, `catch`, `finally`, the array iteration methods, `addEventListener`, `addListener`, `setActionHandler`, `replace`, `replaceAll`) and `slice`; the others stay ADR 0781's (docs/open-work.md). Premise: a dependency value typed `any` or `unknown` that holds an impostor, or a dependency object a project assertion calls a container, is not one; dependency source the program includes is censused. Proved: nothing new. ## ADR 0876 — A write whose own chain reaches a prototype patches any owner
ADR 0781's census read `G.prototype`, a bare global and a global object: `const gp = Object.getPrototypeOf(p); gp.then = f`, `(globalThis as any)[k].prototype[m] = f` and their aliases raised no patch, and the standard resolution stood: false cleans, and false positives where a standard `then` proved a fresh stack the patched one did not. `prototype_reach` walks the written object through assertions, members and bindings, a `let` by each assignment, to a `prototype` or `__proto__` member or a call that reads a prototype, the callee through its `const`, `.call`, `.apply`, `.bind`, a bracket or a comma. Such a write is `standard_patch("*", key)`, and `patch_veto` asks `("*", member)` and `("*", "*")` of every owner but a bare global, which no prototype write replaces. `delete` is keyed the same, and `setPrototypeOf` is `*`. The exemption: `Foo.prototype` as the written member's immediate object, `Foo` a function or class of this file nothing rebinds, counts only once the program writes a `prototype` key (`patch_vetoed`), by member, computed key or definer, `Object.assign(Foo, { prototype })` and an opaque source included (reviews D22, R45). An import, a parameter or a type alone is never exempt. A value that leaves the expression is ADR 0876's boundary, closed for a named key by `named_written`: a made receiver stays standard only while no site writes that member of an object it did not make. An unknown key through a value no chain reaches stays open (docs/open-work.md). `promise_intrinsics_intact` and `array_iteration_intact` do not read `patch_veto`, so `gp[k] = f` leaves both true: read in `after_await`, `settlement`, `pending_promise` and the aggregates answer at a call the veto has decided, and a tuple pattern's read is ADR 0803's census; `pre_await` and `pending_sources` were not traced. A veto that only keeps a proof from standing must not move rows or lift another veto, so the readers below ask `placing_standard_declaration`, never for proof: `mutation_builtin` and `tuple_mutation_method` (a withheld `Object.assign` that reads as no mutator lifts the wildcard on transported members, and transport claims an exact target: a false proof in the corpus, `impostor-withheld-mutation`), `handed_call`'s `f.bind(x)()` and the composite dispatch. The value walk goes on past a call `reached_patch_withheld` answers, after every other walk, with a memo and budget of its own: rows only. `open_compute` and chain links stay on the proof. Owners: `base/contributions.rs` (`prototype_reach`, `push_written_patch`, `patch_vetoed`), `value_use.rs` (`run_past_withheld`).

Now uncertifiable: the census program and fixture `pending-read-caught-module`'s `Caught` and `Inline`. Kept: `uncaught.tsx` and a caught read in a body, violations. ## ADR 0857 — `resolve` and `until` answer where the call that runs them runs
Census `handed-flush` (open-defects, restored by b7f560940): `const Row = () => { flush(() => { resolve(() => 1); }); return <span />; }` run by `[1].map(Row)` in a tracked JSX expression was certified; Chrome throws "Cannot call resolve inside a reactive scope" (and `until`'s). Two gaps. `flush(fn)` runs `fn` before it drains, with the caller's observer (probed, rc.21: tracked JSX and a memo compute throw; a handler, `untrack` and `render`'s code are silent), but its callback ended the placement: `resolve_scope_unplaced` took the dialect's `Inline` as a verdict. And a function's body was placed only by the in-place climb, never by the calls that run it. Now a dialect question, `inline_callback_keeps_observer` (`flush`'s argument 0, `dispatchAsInteraction`'s 1; not `render`), places a call in such a callback at the container call; `resolve_scope_of_entries` places a call directly in a function at each direct call of its binding and each standard-library call proven to run it (`carrier_provably_runs`), each asked as the call is (depth 4), and one tracked entry is a violation whatever the function's other entries are. The tracked-region test asks where the climb lands, not the original call. For the obligation, an `Inline` callback that does not clear the listener (`callback_inherits_listener`) is placed at its call, and an entry that may run the function (an empty or unknown receiver) under an observer leaves it open. A function the component census takes for an escaped component (a direct JSX return handed on, ADR 0331) keeps its violations as obligations: the census pair itself is uncertifiable, `component-identity-unproven`. Counting a carrier's run as an entry there (tried as ADR 0856) exposed a false positive in the read placement and was withdrawn (open-work). Proved (Chrome, rc.21): flush in tracked JSX and in a memo, a helper called directly or through a namespace helper's callback, fixtures `resolve-inline-entries` (`FlushInJsx`, `FlushInMemo`, `Called`), `rc21-resolve-leaf`'s `helper()` (its tracked entry; a handler calls it too) and `resolve-named-closure-unplaced`'s `NamedForEach` and `AliasForEachUntil`, obligations before. Now uncertifiable: the census pairs, `Mapped`, `Empty` (were certified), and 26 empty-receiver legal twins (clean before). Fuzz, 75c33dbbc against this build by id, Chrome confirming: recall contexts `handed-flush-each`, `-call`, `named-flush-call`, `flush-iife` (472 ids) 226 false cleans to 0, 0 false positives; legal twins (354) 0 false positives. Owners: `static_api.rs`, `solid-dialect` (`dialect.rs`, `solid_2.rs`), `execution_role.rs`.

Amended 2026-11-07 (reviews R13, R16): a walk that stops proves nothing about the entries it did not read, so the row no longer rests on the role at the call: at `ENTRY_DEPTH` the call is uncertifiable, `search-depth-limit`; at a generator or a function the binding may no longer hold, `function-run-unplaced`. An `async` function is walked: its prefix runs on the call's stack, and a site past its await already returned. Neither end was a silent clean on the shapes tried: other rows held them (uncertifiable, `function-run-unplaced`, or the sole-call climb's violation); the amendment makes the end explicit and names it.

## ADR 0858 — A coded uncaught throw is the program's result, not the harness's
Five mx-census rows for ASYNC_OUTSIDE_LOADING_BOUNDARY were `harness` (Part 13 on 75c33dbbc's build): `judgeSite` took any `runtime.failure` with no event of the row's own code for a failed run. In `patch-global`, `-window`, `-define` the body's pending read throws PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ, uncaught; in `cleanup-mount-mixed` that throw halts the page and the clicked button never exists. Both are what the program does. `codeOfUncaught` (codes.mjs) names the Solid code a throw's message carries (a `[CODE]` tag of the policy table, or a message `thrownCodes` knows); `judgeSite` now takes such a failure, and a `halted` failure whose page a coded error stopped, as a thrown event where Chrome placed it, scored like any report (the row's own code, or a secondary). Where the site's hit counter is 0 the row is `unexecuted`: nothing was measured there, neither a pass nor a problem (`OUTCOMES`, `UNJUDGED`, `u` in the matrix table). Any other failure stays `harness`. The known `harness` rows that stay: the `mount-named` pairs of PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ, PENDING_ASYNC_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE, ASYNC_OUTSIDE_LOADING_BOUNDARY and LOADING_ON_OUTSIDE_HOLD, where an uncoded `NotReadyError` thrown at module scope ends the module (ADR 0855), and `flw-settled-report-error`, with no runtime record. Frozen twins: judge-frozen.json `siteRows` (coded throw scored, uncoded throw harness, a later site unexecuted). Owners: `scripts/fuzz/matrix.mjs`, `scripts/fuzz/codes.mjs`.

## ADR 0859 — A layer that runs in place inside `flush(fn)` is placed where the `flush` call is
`inline_callback_execution_role` placed a read only when it was written directly in an inline callback that keeps the listener. A read in an IIFE inside `flush(fn)` fell through to the lexical arm, `inside-component`: `UntrackedRendering`, so a helper in tracked JSX (`[1].forEach(k)`, `k()`) that runs `flush(() => { (() => { s(); })(); })` was SC1001 V where Chrome (rc.21) is silent (54 of 136 legal ids Chrome-confirmed on the old build; also on 75c33dbbc). The arm now asks at the end of the site's in-place climb, so such a read answers as one written in `fn`: the `flush` call's role, or `Unknown` where the call is written in a helper not proven to run during the body. A site past an await, or an exhausted climb, keeps the old path. A helper that also keeps the literal (`twice`, `among_others`) proves a violation by its in-place run and nothing else: a non-reporting role becomes `Unknown`. Proved: a read in a memo's `flush` has no row. Now uncertifiable (no false positive left, no proof of silence yet): the read in a helper tracked JSX runs (Chrome silent), and the same read in a helper called from the body (54 mx ids, Chrome warns), which the old lexical arm reported by accident: with `flush` between the IIFE and the body, the fall-through was no proof. A read written in the body stays V. The climb stops at a named function, whose reads its calls place: past one, `render(() => { writeCount(); …})` lost an SC2001 V. ADR 0856's route (letting `flush`'s callback keep the helper in `inside_non_component_function`) stays withdrawn.

Correction (Part 15's smoke on the census judge, which needs a row at the site): the 70 → 0 holds for the direct binding; `flw-guard-yield` stays a false clean there until F1 (its U is SC9012 at the generator), and a binding whose U sits at its own line (`extracted`, `ns-*`) is a false clean in every context, not by this ADR. Reviewer R60: the same holds at a call's edge: an owner edge whose call follows a `yield` of a plain generator around it carries a possible unowned run (`MayResumeUnowned`), so a helper written outside the generator inherits it, also beside an owned edge from the body (`flw-gen-step-helper-body`: 7 ids with no row at the site before, SC4001 U after).

## ADR 0868 — An owned edge proves only the entries the graph holds
`f(); el.onclick = f` gave `f` one owned edge from the body and nothing for the click, which rc.21 runs with no owner (NO_OWNER_CLEANUP, Chrome). The emission read that context as every entry, and the site was certified. Now a silent site whose context is owned, or that a providing region answers, is uncertifiable (`owner-callers-unenumerated`) when a function between it and its component may be entered with no edge: a method; a function value no binding names (a slot, an array element, a class field) unless invoked in place or a whole call argument; a named function with an open entry census, a reference handed on (here or in another file, a value-only argument included: `keep.call(f)`, an `object` parameter, an `EventTarget` subclass's `removeEventListener`), or a call or inline run from a function no edge reaches; and any layer when the program calls direct `eval`. A whole argument of a primitive's call is the graph's own edge. The walk ends at a proven component and at the innermost owner-creating region (`CallbackOwner::Creates`). Reviewer D13 shaped all of it. Proved: nothing. Now uncertifiable: `flw-owned-*` 210 of 210 (140 certified false cleans on rmg7), D13's 147 shapes 35 certified false cleans on bbd9bfbf3, 0 now (126 U, 21 agree); legal twins 0 false positives (64 U). Corpus +73 SC4001 U, all apps. Fixture `owned-edge-escape`.

R29: a primitive's whole argument closes a layer only where the dialect gives that argument an owner (`callback_owner_at`). `action(q as any)` keeps `q` and `createContext(q)` holds it, so neither is a placement. `flw-owned-action` (35 ids, Chrome confirmed): 35 false cleans on the build before, 0 after, now uncertifiable.

Callers: a closed named layer is entered only by its callers, and each is entered as it is. `wrapUse` called once from an `f` that escapes stayed certified: the walk ended at `wrapUse`. It now follows each call and inline run out, through other files too, up to 8 calls, and past that the layer is open. It stops at a callback whose primitive creates the owner. Every `flw-owned` wrapper id (7,152, Chrome confirmed): 2,737 false cleans before, 0 after, uncertifiable. A caller in another file is asked the same question, by that file's graph path.

## ADR 0869 — A JSX boundary is no owner site
The scout expected NO_OWNER_BOUNDARY from a JSX `<Loading>` past an await or in a timer. The candidate is removed, with `allow_uncertain`, which only it set to false. The premise covers a component tag's own synchronous body only: an intrinsic's insert is an unrooted render effect, `CONTEXT_NOT_FOUND` can still fire, and closures the body keeps run off that root's stack. 5 false positives in `jsx-loading`/`jsx-errored` before, 0 after.

## ADR 0870 — A read after an await a branch may skip has a row
Scout map B1 (`research/scout-bind-afterawait.md`): the producer's flow scan sets `awaited` to false at an `if`, `?:`, `&&`/`||`/`??`, a logical assignment, a `switch` case, a label, an optional chain or a destructuring default, so no fact lists a read after `if (flag) await p; return n();`. `run` skipped a function with none listed: a silent clean (fuzz contexts `aw-if`, `-and`, `-or`, `-nullish`, `-ternary`, `-switch`, `-label`, `-optional`, `-dstr`). A function with an own `await` is now walked too: `after_conditional_await` takes every call, tagged template and store or props member read that `may_follow_own_await` (not in the other arm of the await's `if` or `?:`), or whose in-place layer (closure, IIFE, helper) is called at such a point, and reports each as `SuspensionUnplaced`. Position cannot tell an arm that leaves from one that falls through, and two conditions may correlate, so no row here is a violation. A read before the await, or in the arm without it, has no row. Proved: nothing. Now uncertifiable (Chrome warns, matrix --recall by id): the 9 contexts' 216 ids, false clean on f1a8da6a4, 54 of them store reads in a closure, helper or IIFE; legal twins `aw-else`, `-else-flip`, `-early` 0 false positives. Owners: `static_rules/after_await.rs`.

<a id="adr-0871"></a>
## ADR 0871 — A helper shared by computations is walked once per window set
B3: `done` was shared by every computation, so a helper two computations await was walked once, under the first one's windows. A read the first computation had already tracked was silent for the second as well (`aw-shared-after`, `aw-shared-before`). The walk key is now the function, its set of windows, and whether it is conditional. Each walk reports on its own rows, merged at the end: a row proved by one walk strengthens another walk's uncertain row at the same site (`push_defect_or_strengthen`), and a duplicate is dropped. A function walked under 16 window sets gets a last walk with no window, `search-depth-limit`, so recursion ends and nothing is lost. Proved (Chrome warns): `aw-shared-after` 24 agree (f1a8da6a4: 4 false clean, 20 recall gap); `aw-shared-before` stays 24 agree. Owners: `static_rules/after_await.rs`, `static_rules.rs`.

<a id="adr-0872"></a>
## ADR 0872 — A helper called past the computation's first suspension adds no tracking window
B4: `enqueue` pushed a helper's own window whenever its call was not untracked, so `await p; await late();`, with `late` reading `n()` before its own await, counted that read as a dependency. It runs after the computation's suspension, with no observer. A call that follows a dominating or unconditional await of the member (or any suspension) now marks the walk `past_suspension`, and the helper queued there gets no window of its own. Past a conditional await, it is an obligation (`past_on_some_path`). Proved (Chrome warns): `aw-helper-late` 24 agree (f1a8da6a4: 10 agree, 14 recall gap). Owners: `static_rules/after_await.rs`.

<a id="adr-0873"></a>
## ADR 0873 — An async compute a project wrapper hands to a tracked primitive is walked
B2: `tracked_async_computation_context` names an async function only at the primitive itself, so `const useQ = (fn) => createMemo(fn); useQ(async () => { await p; return n(); })` had no row (`aw-wrapper`, `aw-wrapper-2`). `handed_compute` follows each call that takes the function as a whole argument into the callee's parameter: a whole argument of a tracked primitive's compute slot, written in the wrapper's straight-line body, is `Tracked` and gets that primitive's context; a further project wrapper is followed (6 deep); a standard-library callee is the carrier rules'. Any other use is `Unplaced`, an obligation with `function-run-unplaced` naming the call: a non-exact callee, a replaced binding, a defaulted or written parameter, `arguments`, a stored, returned or deferred parameter, and a dependency's function (a test runner's `it`). A reference that calls the parameter as a statement in a synchronous wrapper's own body (`now(f) { f(); }`) is an in-place run, as an IIFE there is, and gives no answer: the in-place walk places it (Chrome silent on four probes). A used result (`(f) => f()`, `async (f) => await f()`) may be the compute's value, and an async wrapper may run it past its own await: both stay `Unplaced`. Proved (Chrome warns): `aw-wrapper`, `-wrapper-2` 48 agree (f1a8da6a4: 48 false clean). Now uncertifiable: `aw-inplace-return`, `-await` 32 (false clean on f1a8da6a4), `-late` 16 (recall gap before); twin `aw-inplace-stmt` 0 false positives. Corpus: measured by the sweep after this commit. Owners: `static_rules/handed_compute.rs`, `static_rules/after_await.rs`.

<a id="adr-0851"></a>
## ADR 0851 — Generator steps carry the owner and execution role

Calling a generator creates an iterator and runs none of its body. Owner edges and role classification follow exact step sites: `next`/`return`/`throw`, iterating operands, spread, destructuring and resolved standard `Array.from`. A held immutable iterator whose references are all steps uses those sites; exports, escapes, mixed or missing placements keep obligations. No creation and no export proves no run. A later segment of an async caller's `for await`, or sync iteration whose body begins with its await, is detached; standard in-place layers inside it are climbed first. W1/W2 fixtures pin exports and callbacks. A write takes the write walk at the step sites only where the step runs it on its own stack: the first segment, or a sync generator every creation drains in place; elsewhere it stays an obligation. Proved: `missing-owner` past a yield the stepping proves detached. Now uncertifiable: steps that differ or escape. Owners: `owners.rs`, `owners/owner_discovery.rs`, `execution_role/{body_roles,callee_roles,write_roles}.rs`.

<a id="adr-0852"></a>
## ADR 0852 — A rejected stepper flight is not proof of an observed error

Six uncached Chrome disagreements (`gen-yield-each` and `gen-sync-each`, NO_OWNER_CONTEXT, CONTEXT_NOT_FOUND, DYNAMIC_STATIC_PROMISE) distinguish the operation's throw from its observation. rc.21 signals `dev-shared.js:1107` (`watchAsyncTail`) catches rejection and invokes `handleAsync`'s rejection continuation (`:4150`); its `handleError` (`:3846`) stores/stages error status. Neither proves a later read or boundary surfaces that error. The earlier observation was recorded in `docs/open-work.md` and `scripts/flip/notes/gen-wip-handoff.md` at 0980aa424; retaining V there was insufficient. Context-read owner rows and static-dynamic rows reached through an async generator stepper now retain `async-flight-error-unobserved`, including dependency attribution. No judge or runtime classification changes. Owners: `owners/owner_emission.rs`, `static_api.rs`, `blockers.rs`.

Snapshot review found `shared` in `generator-step-owner`: a generator read as a value as well as called under an async memo bypassed `generator_creations` and the write walk resumed following creation calls, producing SC2001 V after yield. Named generators with unplaced references now terminate that walk with an obligation; their unplaced references also retain possible resumption in the owner analysis instead of assuming the creation owner persists. ## ADR 0879 — A call past a suspension whose callee names no symbol is an obligation
B5 (scout map): `report_call_read` and the dominated-call loop asked only for an accessor symbol, a typed-unknown symbol, a handed value or a parameter. A callee with no exact symbol (`fns[0]()`, `get()()`, `(c ? a : b)()`, a member of an untyped value) ended with no row. Such a call is now `CalleeInexact` (U) when it resolves to no declaration and no project function; an exact standard or package declaration (`res.json()`) and a `new` are not. Owners: `static_rules/after_await.rs`.

B6: on a native flight, a `then`/`catch`/`finally` argument that is neither a literal nor a current named project function (`then(api.fmt)`, a call's result, a parameter) was skipped. It is now `ReactiveCallbackUnresolved` (U) at the argument; a plain `null` or `undefined` (no declaration) is no handler. Twin `aw-catch-member` 0 false positives. Owners: `static_rules/after_await.rs`.

<a id="adr-0881"></a>
## ADR 0881 — An async or generator handler, and an async function a handler calls, are obligations
B7: an `async` or generator handler on the flight was skipped. Its prefix runs in the handler's microtask; a generator handler's iterator is the chain's value, which a later handler may step on the flight. Each is now an obligation region, `HandOffNotFollowed` ("an async handler", "a generator handler"). An async project function a handler calls joins the flight too, and no queue reached it: it is walked as an obligation with no window of its own (`enqueue_handler_calls`, review on D-R3d-2). Now uncertifiable: `aw-then-async` 16 (false clean on 0140ec6fb). The reviewer's `aw-then-calls-async` (a sync handler calling an async function, the read past its own await): Chrome is silent on 16 of 16 ids, kept as a legal shape. ## ADR 0882 — `.then` with no exact standard declaration reads its handlers as obligations
B8: a chain call whose callee resolves to no standard declaration (an `any` receiver, a thenable) returned with its handlers unread. A non-computed `then`/`catch`/`finally` now reads a readable handler as an obligation region (`HandlerMayNotRun`: it may run on the flight, inline, or never), any other handler as unresolved, and continues down the receiver's chain. Now uncertifiable: `aw-then-any` 16 (false clean on 0140ec6fb). `aw-then-cast` (`as unknown as Promise<number>`) was already exact: 16 agree before and after. Owners: `static_rules/after_await.rs`.

*Amended (batch 11, the `then` arm of the walk).* `expand_standard`'s `Promise.then` arm returned with no row, and without walking the receiver's own chain, when the receiver was not proven native, the call not withheld, and no premise claimed it native or undecided (a call's result: `q(promise).then(…).then(f)` with `q` an identity function). Chrome (rc.21) warns UNTRACKED_READ_AFTER_AWAIT at a handler's read there; the program was certified with no SC1002 row, so the arm is the one place a `then` handler vanished without a row (the fuzz program `then-withheld-chain` is masked: its computed write withholds `then`, and a withheld call rows its handlers, ADR 0874). The claim and undecided branches reported literal handlers only, dropping a handler handed by name, a member and a spread. Every handler is now a proof obligation (`HandlerMayNotRun`, or `IntrinsicMayBeReplaced` under a claim a visible write withdraws), resolved by `readable_handler`: a literal, a project function handed by name, a member of a closed object; any other value an unfollowed obligation; the receiver's inner call is walked. Cells `then-unplaced-chain{,-named,-member,-depth3,-identity}` (flip-read.mjs, 706 shapes each): 2,367 false cleans of 3,530 on main 743ce7c89, none now. Never a violation.

<a id="adr-0887"></a>
## ADR 0887 — A violation in a halted run is judged; a stacked one needs a Chrome twin

The grid judge left every off-site violation in a run that threw `unjudged(disturbed)`: 185 on the pinned smoke, none counted. Now a disturbed run is judged like any other, with three changes. A violation whose lines ran while Solid threw another code there first is `stacked`: "wrong if it runs" holds, but the halt is no evidence, so it scores `stacked-claim` (listed by id with its pair, exact ratchet per pair, not a #173 blocker) only for a (first code, rule) pair `scripts/fuzz/stacked-pairs.json` proves with a pattern that removes the first throw and makes Chrome report a code of the rule; the patterns run checks every proof. An unproved pair, lines that ran with nothing of the rule anywhere, and the rule's code placed only off the lines are false positives. A line that never ran stays unexecuted; a rule code Chrome could not place stays unjudged. `besideCode` is asked for each code the run recorded on the violation's own lines, not only the row's (`standsBeside`, matrix.mjs): a run can record two codes at one line, and the row's is only the first.

<a id="adr-0838"></a>
## ADR 0838 — Every pending read is accounted for, per rule and per path
ADRs 0835-0837 and R3's handed-flush contexts were one defect: an async walk ended with no row, and nothing noticed. `pending_accounting.rs` runs once in `reactive_analysis.rs` after every producer and before projection, and only adds rows. A pending source is one `async_sources` holds (`computation_provenance` not `Sync`, transitive over memos) or whose compute ADR 0802 leaves open (`source_compute_open`): a source not proved synchronous. Sites: each direct call of such an accessor, each member Get of such a store root, each call transport resolves to one, each read row of one. Rules, each accounted on its own: SC5001 (`PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ`, `PENDING_ASYNC_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE`), SC5003, SC5008 (claimed only inside a `try` whose handler does not only throw). A rule is accounted by a row at the read that reports it or whose decided role rules it out, ADR 0509's projected open, an SC5008 row at the read (SC5008), an SC9012 at the read or its reference, or a site reason; else an uncertifiable row, blocker `async-read-unaccounted` (never a V; it opens no strict read). Reasons and what each rules out: Discarded, PendingProbe, DeclaredServed (ADR 0632), FreshStack (a reviewed scheduler's callback, ADR 0435; an event delivery under no synchronous-dispatch veto, ADRs 0356, 0437): all three; SuspendsInComputation (tracked JSX, `computation_read_suspends`), OutsideStrictWindow (ADR 0443): SC5001; NotRenderEffect (a body, module evaluation, an apply (ADR 0614), a non-render computation; the warning is raised from `EFFECT_RENDER` only), UnderLoading, DeclaredFirstPaint: SC5003; NoComputationPass (module evaluation, an apply, a vetoless handler, a body only ever mounted as a tag): SC5008. `UntrackedCallback`, `Unreported`, `DirectiveApply`, `DeferredCallback` with no deferral fact and `Unknown` rule nothing out. Per path: ADR 0462's walk carries `decided` per site; a path that ends with no place (the bound, a visited function, a generator, a component, no binding, a written binding, a site no local call runs, a call another read's row holds) is a cut unless its role's reasons rule out all three rules. A cut walk leaves the read its own unplaced pair (`walk_cut`), dropped per rule only by a row at the read reporting that rule; a row the walk placed at a call speaks for the read only when no cut stands. ADR 0835's "only if no row" condition is gone. Not sites: `.call`/`.bind`/destructure hand-offs (the census's SC9012 at the reference). Not covered: SC5005 (a client-only source, not a pending claim), SC5006 (per boundary, ADR 0517), and SC1002's walks: `after_await.rs` `report_helper_reads`, `visit_call`, `report_call_read`, `expand`/`expand_mapped`, `follow_generators` end without rows (open-work, ADRs 0850-0854). R2h, on R3's handed-flush ids: a `try` around the read swallows its throw wherever the carrier that runs it stands (`caught` looked at the carrier only: SC5003 proved at `[1].forEach(hf)`, `hf.call()`, `flush(fn)` where Chrome reports CAUGHT only; on mg2 already); a body role of an escaped or uncertain component (`[1].forEach(K1)`, `K1` returns JSX) is no `NotRenderEffect` reason; a pending store root handed on whole (`Object.keys(store)`) is a site; a read in a method or getter keeps SC5008 open in a file with a swallowing `try` (`held.v` in the `try`). Proved: nothing new (R3's flush contexts' 524 V, Chrome agrees, come from `tracked_computation_read` keeping an `Inline` callback a render read). Now uncertifiable: every closed false clean. Fuzz, Chrome confirmed, mg2 (3aa2b4e3f) → pa11: R1/R6 1,316 ids 70 → 0 false cleans; R17 10 → 0; R18 36 → 0; R3's handed-flush 4,232 ids 1,641 → 0 (pa6: 510 and 64 `wrong-rule`); CAUGHT `jsx` `caught-handed-*` (new) 54 ids: pa6 24 `wrong-rule` → 0; legal twins 0 false positives; ao4, deep, fp, lgfp, lgflow unchanged at 0. Corpus (rc.21 frozen, mg2 → pa11): +2,941 U (SC5003 1,510 and SC5001 1,312 `async-read-unaccounted`, SC5008 28, 91 other async blockers), 0 removed, 0 V moved (1,241 from one absent module, ADR 0891). Owners: `pending_accounting.rs`, `local_access.rs` (`push_call_attributed_async_reads`, carrier `caught`); fixtures `pending-read-accounted`, `caught-handed-read`.

Fable's U review: 233 of ADR 0838's SC5001 rows sit in `<For each>`, `<Show when>`, `<Match when>`. rc.21 reads these in a computation of the component's own (`createMemo(() => props.when)`, `mapArray(() => props.each, ...)`, `Switch`'s memo over `mp.when`), so a pending value suspends it: Chrome reports ASYNC_OUTSIDE_LOADING_BOUNDARY with no `Loading` and no pending-read code. Dialect question `flow_prop_read_in_own_computation`, table `when`/`each`, rc.21 only (read from rc.21). `read_in_flow_prop` gives the site `SuspendsInComputation` alone, which rules out SC5001; `NotRenderEffect` would mask SC5003. Refused: a comment in the attribute (`/*@static*/` drops the getter), an element or function between the read and the attribute, a read that is an argument of a call in the attribute (`untrack(p, "x")` sets a strict window in the memo; this also refuses `Array.from({ length: a() })`, whose read is in fact held, a cost), a spread on the tag, a `Match` that is no direct child of an exact `Switch`. <a id="adr-0895"></a>
## ADR 0895 — A hand-on past an await is a run only on the wrapper's stack

Part 16's `aw-then-*-forward-*`: a literal or a named function handed through wrappers (`hc1(() => read())` over `const hc1 = (g) => { hc0(g); }`) had no row of the rule. `forward_runs` reads it as a run only when the wrapper resolves exactly, its parameter is a single unwritten name, and each hand-on is a whole argument written directly in the wrapper's synchronous prefix, on every completion, ending in a direct call of the parameter on every completion. Anything else is SC1002 U `handed-read-after-await` at the reads, never a name match: a timer, a handler, a returned function, a suspension, or `if (on) g()` under `run(hf, false)`, which the first build proved and the `after-await-handoff` fixture refused. Each parameter is asked once per query, since a doubling or recursive wrapper is 2^32 paths. The helper walk also follows a closed member whose slot holds a name (`{ m: hf }`), so a read behind `ho.m()` in a helper past the await is V where it was silent.

<a id="adr-0893"></a>
## ADR 0893 — A plain throw needs a report, not a run
Part 16 FP `flush-read-stored` and the class behind it: rc.21 `resolve`, `until`, a static `dynamic` source and `useContext` throw with no diagnostic, and a memo or other storing compute keeps the error until a read rethrows it (Chrome: unread silent, read logged; coded diagnostics fire at the call and do not move). `stored_throw::throw_end` walks each entry. A user effect compute with no `error` arm logs; a render or tracked effect's compute reports unless a boundary above takes it; any other tracked compute reports only through a read; JSX under `Errored` with a parameter fallback, `createErrorBoundary`, `isPending` and a `try` above the site, a read or a caller take it. A timer's, a frame's or an observer's throw reaches the host, and so does a promise handler's when its chain has no catch and its result goes nowhere (an unhandled rejection Chrome reports); a held or caught chain does not, and no other scheduler is read. One entry that reports keeps the violation; none, or a bound, is `computation-error-unobserved`. A function only referenced ends there too, unless the holder's body is followed to a direct call of that parameter or prop that reports. A read counts when it is a direct call of the accessor, reached, not past a suspension, and not in a component's prop or child (a boundary's children run, Show's need not). `resolve` and `until` throw only under an observer, so a body entry neither throws nor keeps the row. A lazy compute no read runs stays ADR 0576's. A caught throw fires no diagnostic, so it is no violation by default (coordinator's ruling); witness `SC2007/n01-ctl` moves from `keep` to `warn`, its recorded run having no diagnostic. Dialect questions `plain_throw`, `compute_throw_reports`, `compute_throw_logged`, `callback_swallows_throw`. Proved: nothing new. Now uncertifiable: the `st-*` false positives, 0 after. True V lost to U: `st-read-await`, `-getter`, `-show-true`, and a function kept in a slot or a rebound name, measured in the commits. Owner: `stored_throw.rs`.

<a id="adr-0894"></a>
## ADR 0894 — A setter or accessor a carrier provably runs is its call there
ADR 0815 left four open rows: `[1].forEach(setC)` and `untrack(c, "x")` had a census row at the argument and none at the run. `inline_carried_target` hands such a call to `discover_call_as`, as ADR 0516 does for `f.call()`, so the row takes the carrier's role and place. Index 0 only. A list carrier needs `HandOff::Inline` and `carrier_provably_runs` (a non-empty array literal); it takes a setter or an accessor, since the strict window stays and an accessor ignores `(v, i, arr)`. `untrack`, a primitive position `hand_off_at` leaves Unfollowed, has its own arm: an accessor with a non-empty string-literal label. An unlabelled `untrack(c)` sets no strict window and stays silent; a variable label stays U. Chrome rc.21 confirms the V in a body, a root, a memo and an effect apply, where a labelled `untrack` warns too. Open: the same call inside a named helper keeps its named U at the argument, no row at the run (open-work).

<a id="adr-0883"></a>
## ADR 0883 — JSX is an owner site where it creates something
Written JSX has no call node, so no owner rule saw it (F4). Chrome on rc.21: an intrinsic's dynamic child, attribute or spread is a render effect, so it raises NO_OWNER_EFFECT with no owner, module scope included, PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK in an apply and PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE in a leaf. A fragment's child (a memo) and a component element (its own transparent root, ADR 0869) are quiet with no owner and raise the same two in an apply or a leaf. A spread is a site whatever it holds. A `ComponentGetter` region creates nothing. `owners/jsx_sites.rs` lists the sites from `tracked_regions`, spreads and component elements; `jsx-effect` candidates are emitted as `effect`, anchored at the element's name, since a tracked region is its own ownership region. SC6002 and SC3001 reach JSX through their own walks. A site SC6002 proves in the apply's own text drops its SC4001 row: `assertNotInEffectCallback` throws when the node is set up, before the NO_OWNER_EFFECT check (`dist/dev.js:2636`), so that code is never reached. JSX does not differ from a creating call here (ADR 0483). Anywhere else the row stays, as an obligation: a helper may run from a timer with no window, and an uncertain throw may pass the guard (reviewer's R50). With no single attested `solid-js` copy the walk still places the site, as an obligation; so does a build with no client root in sight.

Reviewer D17 limits it. A static child is a site only if it may hold a function, `insert` makes that an effect (`{[fn]}`, an `any`): uncertifiable, excluded by a NonCallable type (a callability-only demand). `{label}` holding a function outright is a tsc error, so no site. JSX under a component element's getter runs where the component reads it (Chrome quiet when the body reads it): never a V.

Control flow: the owner's rule is that U stands only where no compile-time fact decides, and an exact tag identity is such a fact. Read per tag on rc.21 with Chrome probes: `For` and `Repeat` run the mapper as `runWithOwner(createOwner(), mapper)` at any arity, a reference too. `Show`, and `Match` as a direct child of `Switch`, call the child in their memo only when `child.length > 0`; at length 0 (a default or rest first parameter) it is a value, a later read raises NO_OWNER_*, and it stays U. `fallback` and a `children=` prop are not read: U. A dialect question names the tags (`control_flow_child_run`), identity is the exact primitive, the child is the element's only child and an expression written there as a function, and the owner edge carries no source because the tag's owner exists whoever owns the tag. A name gets no edge: it keeps its other entries (at most 33 written names in the corpus). Premise: `.length` is read at compile time, and only a bundler can differ from it: a downlevelled default raises it, a minifier dropping an unused parameter lowers it; that is a production build, with no diagnostic. User components are untouched.

Owner's direction: no render-prop placement proof for the flip. An unplaced site is a named U (open-work). F4 ids (306, Chrome confirmed): 276 false cleans before, 0 after, 140 proved, 166 uncertifiable; legal 996: 0 false positives, as before. The `flow-*` legal ids (168): 150 uncertifiable before the control-flow edge, 0 after, 0 false positives. Corpus: +181/-1 SC4001 U rows on 170 distinct sites (164 `function-run-unplaced`, 3 `owner-callers-unenumerated`, 3 others), 0 V added. Without the edge it was +5,015 rows on 3,379 sites.

<a id="adr-0896"></a>
## ADR 0896 — listener-reach stands at a withheld dispatch in any analyzed file
ADR 0645 follow-up (ADR 0815 limit closed): listener-reach also stands at a withheld dispatch in another analyzed file. The existing project-code registration predicate, including open reads of addEventListener, is an analyzed-project existential; it changes no registration/read U. A per-build boolean records ListenerProjectReach on every answer, false too; its section fingerprint recomputes the full predicate against the next build’s files and symbol/index answers. No event/target relation, V or clean proof is added. Fixture rc21-listener-cross-file pins direct, deferred, no-registration and open method-read forms.

<a id="adr-0877"></a>
## ADR 0877 — A const-held symbol key names no member
`symbol_key` follows an immutable same-file identifier binding through assertions to `symbol_made_at` at its initializer. No destructuring, mutable binding, `Symbol.replace` or `Symbol.species`; reflective definers keep `*`. The existing `symbol_open` census reopens sites when the global `Symbol` or `Symbol.for` may be replaced. The first replacement executes while Symbol is genuine, so this cycle cannot hide that replacement. Proved: a const holding a genuine symbol cannot name a standard member. Now uncertifiable: patched-Symbol twins remain held. Final m36 corpus: 67,019 to 66,905 U, 291 V unchanged; -114 U in inferay only. Solid-groove retains real open global writes. The previous zero-effect probe did not follow the binding correctly. Owners: `base/contributions.rs` (`const_symbol_key`, `symbol_key`).

## ADR 0897 — A composite callee is read through its operands, never its first token
A comma, conditional, logical or assignment callee names no symbol. `call_accessor_symbol` answered with the entity at the callee's span, which is its first token's: `(n.length > 5 ? n : plain)()` in a late helper's prefix read as `n` (SC1002 V; Chrome quiet, the call takes `plain`), `(slot = r)()` as `slot`, `(c ? r : r)()` as `c` (SC9012 only; Chrome reports at the read). In `report_call_read` a composite callee now goes through ADR 0517's operand reading: V when total and every operand is a reference to one untracked accessor (`read_obligation` unchanged; the region marks it as the symbol path's row), U `CalleeInexact` for any other mix with an accessor operand, no row only when every operand is that one tracked accessor (the same in 0517's member arm, whose tracked first operand ended the arm). An exact row sits at the accessor's reference, where V8's frame names the call. Fixture `after-await-rowless-ends` B9. Owner: `static_rules/after_await.rs`.

## ADR 0878 — A value walk the budget cuts owes a row at its root
The value census (`value_use.rs`) stops a walk at `MAX_STEPS` (512, unchanged) with `Open(value-use-unclassified)`, the one Open `position()` returns before recording a use: a caller that discards the answer (a parameter hop's `name_uses`, an escape or past-withheld walk) drops it, and a kept row could still fall to `used`, push_defect's per-start dedup, or `covered`. Measured on the pushed census tip 26468b004: compass-ui `apps/ui` cuts at `store.test.ts` 22890 (no row there or over it) and in `@kobalte/core` source, with one `value-use-unclassified` row in the project. Now a `cut` flag, set at any cut of the current reference's walks, files `value-use-unclassified:budget` at the walk's root (a creating call's span for P13), straight into the static defects, past every dedup and cover; it replaces the plain row the cut left at that same span, so the ledger counts it once. A root in dependency source puts it at each project reference of the symbol, else at each project import that lands in that package, else at the project's loads of the packages whose dependency files load it, climbed to a fixpoint; a load is an import, a re-export (`export … from`) or a module load (`import()`, `require`), and a module load with no literal specifier, or none that lands in a selected package's source, may land in any; with none at all, at every project load of a selected dependency package, and failing that at the start of the project's first file: never internal only (attested resolution, else a relative path). Fixtures `value-walk-budget-reexport`, `value-walk-budget-dynamic`. Fixture `value-walk-budget-transitive`: a cut in `dep-b`, which the project reaches only through `dep-a`, was certified and is now the budget row at the project's import of `dep-a`. It proves nothing and adds only U. Sites past the cut stay unvisited: the root's row is their obligation, not a row at each. Fixture `value-walk-budget`.

<a id="adr-0898"></a>
## ADR 0898 — A composite callee names no symbol; only an exact one proves a read
ADR 0897: `call_accessor_symbol` gave a composite callee its first token's symbol in every caller. `tracked_before_first_suspension` then credited `(n.length > 5 ? n : plain)()` before the await as a proven dependency, so `n()` past it had no row where Chrome reports (a certified false clean). It now answers no symbol for a composite callee; `read_accessor_symbol` applies ADR 0517's exact rule (total, every operand a reference to one accessor) where a dependency is credited, and any other composite falls to the possible-read scan (`Tracking::Possible`, U). The other callers (the producer-call skip, `number_signal_call`, pre_await's own-call skip and `call_reads`, `visit_call`) become conservative. Cost: `(n || plain)()` before the await proves no tracking (clean to U). Fixture `after-await-rowless-ends` B10. Owner: `static_rules.rs`.

Named `Show`/`Match` children follow the inline child's runtime `length` rule: length 0 supplies a value and proves no invocation. Their reads stay unknown unless another entry proves a role. Reviewer R57: the inline arm's "runs in the insert" holds only where the element is inserted, proven when it is written in another element's child or a fragment, or returned, with no function between; a held value (`shown()()`) is unknown. Census twin `flw-flow-show-inline-body`: 2 certified false cleans before, 2 U after. Reviewer R59: insertion is identity, not containment: the element is a direct child, the value of a child expression, or a branch of a conditional or logical one; a return counts only from a proven component, whose return the runtime renders. A plain function's returned `<Show>` and a part of a returned value are Unknown (`flw-flow-show-returned-plain`). Reviewer R62: the parent must insert too: an intrinsic element, or a fragment that is itself inserted (recursively); a component element's children are a prop it may call, and a held fragment is a value.

## ADR 0884 — A call the compiler resolves to a primitive is never unseen
The primitive rules read names, so `const held = box.m; held(f)` or `const { Errored: E = S.Errored } = S; E(p)` left the operation with one SC9012 `primitive-value` at the binding and nothing at the call. Where the compiler's resolved declaration is a dialect primitive in a package the dialect declares primitives in, the call now gets SC9012 `primitive-alias-call` at its callee. A code with no site line is covered only by its own rule's row, so the call also gets a U of each rule that primitive answers to: SC2006 and SC2008 for `flush`, SC6002 for creating calls, SC7002 for computations, and an `onSettled` alias goes through the owner pass, which owns SC4001. A U, not a proof: the declaration comes from the types, and a cast can say anything (ADR 0781).
