Coordinator's ruling (Part 17) on the census twin `flw-flow-for-async`: an async `For` child hands `insert` a Promise, `insertBefore` throws, Solid reports REACTIVITY_HALTED, and the V's site runs afterwards with the engine stopped, so its code cannot fire. The halt counts whatever triggered it (a DOM error from the program is the program's own failure) when it is recorded and the hit order places it before the claimed effect could fire. The grid host now records each diagnostic's place in the hit-order log (`orderAt`, chrome-host/runner.tsx; records made before are stale). `haltPlace` (classify.mjs): the first halt precedes every hit of the V's counters, each hit in the log, is `halted-claim`, counted exactly (`haltedClaim`), listed by id (`R-halted`), not a false positive and no #173 blocker. ## ADR 0901 — A binding that exactly holds a primitive names it
`const r = S.refresh`, `const { refresh: r = S.refresh } = S`, `const r = refresh` and `const box = { m: refresh }; const r = box.m` named no primitive, so `r(m)` carried another rule's SC9012 and no row of `refresh`'s rule: R-call-arg, uncertified false cleans. Such a binding names the primitive for every rule, as the import does. A namespace member read joins ADR 0459's slots (a non-optional static or string-literal member; a default only when it is the same member); a `const` alias of a named import, or the member of a closed holder (non-exported `const` literal, every reference the whole initializer of a non-exported `const`), is a name of the import (`symbols::import_alias_source`). An exported namespace slot stays exact in its file (ADR 0516) and keeps its value-use SC9012, since an importer's mention resolves nothing. A direct `eval` in the file withdraws the holder form and ADR 0481's member aliases, as does a `with` statement for 0481 (facts schema 92). ADR 0339's TDZ guard and ADR 0884 compose: an exact alias is a known name, so 0884's arm no longer fires there; the residual forms (open-work) keep it. ADR 0711's binder test is unchanged: an alias stays an attribution it does not confirm. Fixture `primitive-namespace-aliases`.

<a id="adr-0904"></a>
## ADR 0904 — A classic script's top-level declaration patches the global
A classic script's (no import or export) top-level `var` or function declaration becomes the global object's property, and its `let`, `const` or class becomes the global lexical binding every module resolves through, so `var Math = …`, `function setTimeout(f) { f(); }` or `let Promise = …` replaces the standard global with no write. The patch census (`standard_patches`) saw assignments and loop heads only, so every `patch_veto` user (`TimerCallback`, `PathFind`, ADR 0875's `symbol_open`, the receiver proofs) trusted the replaced global. Now each top-level declared name of a non-module file (functions with a body, named classes, bindings, runtime namespaces; not in a declaration file or a `declare global` block) raises `("", name)`. A name a block or a class expression scopes is counted too: a patch more, never one less. Proved: nothing (the module twin stays as it was). Now uncertifiable or violated: a call of the replaced global (fixture `classic-script-global-function`: the program's own `setTimeout` runs the callback now, SC2001 violation; fixture `classic-script-global-let`: `then` is the script's, uncertifiable). Fixture `classic-script-global-module`: the same declarations in a module stay local. Owner: `classic_script_globals` in `base/contributions.rs`.

## ADR 0907 — A logged error is placed by its own frames
A user effect logs the throw of its compute or of a computation it read (`NoOwnerError: …`, `ContextNotFoundError: …`) and an `<Errored>` boundary logs what it caught: Solid reported the code, but no error reached the window. Both runners kept only `String(arg)` of a `console.error`, so the judges gave a logged code no position: `unplaced`, 80 aw-* census rows unjudged. The chrome-host and fast runners now keep, index-aligned with `consoleErrors`, the logged error's own stack and its cause's (`consoleErrorPositions`, mapped like `errors`); `classify.mjs` `loggedEvents` places a logged code as a thrown one, in `judge` and `offSiteVerdicts` alike. A logged value that is no error has none and stays unplaced; no classification changed. Review: the cause's frames place a logged code only where it has no frame of its own in the program under test (App.tsx or any other file under the case's directory, any depth or extension, never its `node_modules`; R66 and its follow-up), or where the cause carries the same code; a cause that is another code thrown elsewhere would put the outer code at that site (frozen rows `ADR0907/logged-cause-other-code`, `ADR0907/logged-own-frame-helper`). The oracle-confirm path keeps no stack (Playwright's console text): a logged code there stays unplaced, an open candidate. Frozen row `ADR0907/logged-code-placed`. Owner: the judge (scripts/fuzz).

The value census walks its sources in symbol-id order, and a symbol id hashes the file's absolute path (`symbol:h:…`). ## ADR 0909 — A whole-program walk's starts are units
Edit latency (owner, 2026-11-09: no slower than 207b13142). A value-census start (a source's reference, or P13's creating call) is a unit: its answer (obligation, ADR 0878 cut, uses, escaped uses) is a function of the build and the start alone, so the starts run on the workers (`pipeline::parallel_balanced_with_state`, one census each, caches that answer from the build alone) and the unchanged driver files them in start order. `Census::begin_start` resets each start's state; `hop_starts` was the one leak (a hop another start made answered a later start's binding call, quieter, in symbol-id order: ADR 0905's dependence), now per start. ADR 0878's budget stays inside the start. after_await's members are units too: the start members are decided and built on the workers, and walked ahead of the unchanged driver on drafts of their own; ADR 0871's bound (the one read of the walks' state) moved to the driver (`Reach::bounded`), and a walk done ahead is filed only for exactly its member and only where every exact span it found unseen is unseen in the real draft (`ProgramDraft::spans_agree`), else walked again in place. Kept answers on the edit path: a proof-check pass asks each memo once until the store marks (`reads::PassAnswers`, `Store::marks`); the memo begin keeps the hazards index, updated with the changed files, stamped with its lookup's ticket, refold-checked in shadow and verify-reused modes.

Positions are `reads::file_slots` (unrecorded): a hand-out that notes its one file, a count, a stage driver assembling per-file chunks, or a lookup cache whose readers record what they read of it. ir-read-lint counts `file_slots` callers; `facts.files` is left only in the two accessors. Owner discovery's find-by-path is a hand-out of the one file (`first_file_by_path`).

The compiler drops the operand's `()`, `as`, `satisfies` and `!`; V8 gives a delete no position, so Chrome's frame is the operand's last positioned expression (a call, a link) or the statement. SC2003 reports a delete from its chain's root, through them (`delete_anchor`). An assignment, update or mutator call keeps the member's start (its frame is the property). Fuzz `sw-delete-link`, fixture `store-delete-anchor`.

A `throw` operand had no holder, so SC9012 sat on the thrown value, never at the `catch`'s call Chrome reports; a default export of a literal or call stopped at the export. The census holds the operand (`value-thrown`, its row kept) and walks, as past an escape, the parameter of the innermost `try` around it in the same function, out of an anonymous synchronous IIFE through its one call; elsewhere the row stays at the `throw`. A default export is followed into the references of the compiler symbol declared exactly at that statement, reached from an import's alias target; its row stays. 414 to recall-gap, and REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE's 36 `use-write-throw`; nothing else moved (base, src, combo, pairs, legal).

ADR 0933's universe (`proof_universe.rs`: the calls of accessors, setters, actions and `onFlush`, and the first Get and each write through a store or props root) is checked on every build (`site_accounting.rs`). A site ends in a row, an SC9012 obligation, a defect or violation, the compiler's discard, or a `SiteSilence` (a closed list, one variant per producer return, on a premise that names code), recorded beside the producer's rows. A site left with none gets an uncertifiable row of its rule's code, blocker `strict-read-`, `write-`, `action-`, `store-write-` or `on-flush-unaccounted`, never a violation. A read no strict-read window can hold ends on module scope, an `await` its function reaches, or a function nothing enters (a closed program's export or private function referenced nowhere; a returned or stored closure is not: U). `SOLID_CHECKER_IR_ABLATE_SILENCE=<id>|all` drops silences.

<a id="adr-0944"></a>
## ADR 0944 — One answer for how a callback slot runs, matched by every walk
The rule walks ended at a primitive's callback and each decided alone what that meant: `untrack(() => onCleanup(..))` in `createTrackedEffect` is still the leaf owner's child (`untrack` clears tracking, never the owner), `latest` and `isPending` keep the observer, `flush(fn)` runs `fn` first. 80 Chrome-confirmed false cleans on 3b9688ae3 (the `pl-*` cells of ADR 0940: SC3001 52, SC6002 15, SC1002 6, SC2006 5, SC2008 2) and the same shapes beside them. `Dialect::slot_run(primitive, argument, count)` is now the one answer: `InPlace { owner: Inherits | Supplied { argument } | Creates | Detached, observer: Keeps | Clears | Unstated, throws: Escapes | Swallows }`, `Computation`, `Deferred` or `Unknown`, derived from the callback questions and from nothing else (`the_slot_run_is_derived_from_the_callback_questions`; the pinned table beside ADR 0941's). Every walk matches it with no wildcard:
- **SC3001** (`cleanup.rs`): `through_slots` crosses in-place callbacks (and IIFEs between them) in the leaf's own text, `slot_callback_operations` the ones inside a helper's body and a callback named by value (`untrack(cb)`). What the slots make of an operation: tracking off turns `resolve`/`until` into the creation the leaf refuses (f3); an owner that is `null`, `undefined` or a component's `getOwner()` const (`SuppliedOwner`, ADR 0206) is no leaf's (silent), the owner `getOwner()` returns in the call's own non-suspending function is the call's (`Ambient`), any other value is uncertifiable; a call that takes what its callback throws (`isPending`) reports no plain throw (`flush()`, the observer guard) but still a cleanup or a creation, whose diagnostic comes first. `createRoot`, a computation and a deferred callback are not crossed.
- **SC6002, SC2008** (`effect_phase.rs`): the `Untrack` name match is `crosses`: with no owner the callback must keep `context === null`, so `Inherits`, `runWithOwner(null | getOwner())` cross and `undefined` (the guard tests `=== null`), a component's owner and unproven values do not; `flush()` crosses every slot whose observer the dialect states. `getOwner()` is no earlier call (`Dialect::runs_no_user_code`).
- **SC2006** (`action_step_body_at`): `climb_through_slots` crosses an in-place callback that lets a throw out, not `isPending` and not `flush(fn)`, whose own call throws first. **SC1002** (`visit_call`): `latest`, `isPending` and `flush(fn)` run on the continuation; `untrack` clears tracking on purpose, a root's callback has an owner (silent); `runWithOwner` as before. `owner_set_around` and `resolve_tracked_scope_within` ask the same.
Chrome (rc.21 dev, 79 probes beside the cells): every positive is a finding at its site, `runWithOwner(null | component owner)` and `isPending(() => flush() | resolve())` are quiet, no static-only finding. Fixtures `slot-run-leaf-owner`, `-effect-phase`, `-action-step`, `-after-await`. Not done: the other `layer_run` and climb readers (G3, G4's lint), tagged templates and a slot called in a JSX expression inside a leaf, a callback with a parameter, an async or generator callback, and `expand`'s `untrack` match (the promise-flight walk, `after_await.rs`).

<a id="adr-0945"></a>
## ADR 0945 — Two more shapes the proof lint ratchets: a primitive's name, an unmatched slot that ends silent
ADR 0933's lint (`scripts/proof-lint.mjs`) counts the code shapes that decide a silence without a fact the proof log can name. Two shapes were uncounted. `primitive-name-match` (G3): a primitive compared or matched by its enum name in the analysis and rules crates (`== Some(Primitive::Untrack)`, `matches!(kind, Primitive::OnCleanup | ..)`). The call is resolved exactly, but which primitives behave alike is the dialect's answer (AGENTS.md invariant 4); the dialect crate, which owns the vocabulary, is not asked. 90 hits in 29 files: 3 with a stated reason (`server_rules.rs` only picks the word `status` or `header` for a message), 87 pending review. `silent-unmatched-slot` (G4): a `_` or `None` arm that does nothing, `continue`s, `break`s or returns none, so a walk over slots, arguments or facts ends silently for the case no arm names; whether a hit-less walk is clean or unknown is its caller's. 74 hits in 46 files, all pending review. Both baselines only shrink (a new hit fails, `--update` lowers a count); a hit leaves by moving behind a `Dialect` question or an uncertifiable result, or by stating why it may stay. <a id="adr-0947"></a>
## ADR 0947 — An uncertifiable finding names its blocker as typed facts
The +37,672 U of batch 8 (fix 9's veto, ADR 0939) was seen only after the sweep: an uncertifiable row named its blocker as text (`analysisContext`) and no location. The internal JSON (uncertifiable stays internal, ADR 0328; no text, ESLint or exit-code change) now gives each uncertifiable finding `blockedBy { kind, scope?, formula?, fired?, missing? }` and the report a top-level `hazards: [{ id, sites: [{ path, start, end }] }]`, sorted by path then span, which the rows name by id (`solid-reactive-ir/src/blocked_by.rs`; `scripts/u-blockers.mjs` reads it). The review's four points (`/Users/thomas/dev/sc-scratch.noindex/fable-u-review/review.md` §1) decide the shape:
- `kind` is the blocker constant of `analysisContext` (`blockers.rs`), no second enum. The field is `blockedBy`, not `blocker`: the coverage snapshot's `blocker` is a string the one-way count keys on and stays produced by `scripts/coverage.mjs` from `analysisContext` alone (`u-blockers.test.mjs` scans for a script that reads `blockedBy` into it).
- A veto is a formula over whole-program hazards, so a row names its `formula` (`handed-root-patch`, `impostor-veto` for `standard-member-impostor`; `array-slots-withdrawn` for `tuple-iteration-written`) and `fired`, the hazards present that the formula can read, sorted, in any evaluation order (`handed_root_atoms`; `every_hazard_a_formula_reads_is_one_of_its_atoms`), never one chosen "decisive" fact. `scope` is `program`, not a guess at reaching code. The script derives from `fired` the alternatives that lift a veto (minimal sets of present hazards; for `handed-root-patch`: every open write, or the handed side) and ranks only hazards on an alternative: one present but on none decides nothing and ranks no site.
- A hazard has N sites, shared by thousands of rows: the table holds them once. Sites exist where the census keeps one (`OpenMemberWrite(key)`, `StandardImpostor(member)`); `StandardRootHanded`, a patch and the rest keep none, so they list none, never a guess, and the script reports each kind's share with no location.
- `missing` is the typed fact that would decide a row whose predicate is one test, "the first undecided fact" (counts are upper bounds: proving it may expose the next hole). It is in the schema and the script; no emitter fills it yet.
Every other uncertifiable finding with a named context carries `{ kind }`. Measured on the rc.21 corpus's 11 kui projects (release build, 27,314 uncertifiable rows, every one with a `kind`): 7,602 are `standard-member-impostor`; the three open writes `pageList[activePageNow] = …` at `feature-messages/src/session.ts:379,604,613` are the one hazard (`OpenMemberWrite(*)`) that alone lifts 3,957 of them, 3,370 more rest on `ReflectiveDefinitionSpelling` (no site enumerator yet), the rest on a handed root with no site record. Only the added field moves: coverage, the ownership gate and the guard count read the same.
Not done: `blockedBy` formulas for the rows that hold `standard-member-impostor` outside the census (`hold_withheld_layer_rows`), the typed premises step that gives the rest of the contexts locations, handed-site enumeration (so no `(handed site, open-write site)` pairs yet), P1 (`--assume`) and P2 (a ratchet on U per `missing`), review §3.

`scripts/doc-budget.mjs` (byte limits on docs, fixture READMEs and agent-facing files, the `docs/` allow-list, the ratchet on the limits) is removed with its calls in `make verify` and `verify-delta`: owner, 2026-11-11, "it is annoying to slow down because of that". Docs stay short by the guidance in AGENTS.md; nothing condenses them to fit a limit.

<a id="adr-0949"></a>
## ADR 0949 — A package's read is reported at every project site that enters it
**Item 1, the verdict.** The SC4002 row at `node_modules/dep-wrap/index.jsx` 135-148 of WASM parity case `w3-visible-js` (ADR 0943's two added rows) is right: neither a duplicate nor misplaced. `dep-wrap` is visible to the program (`allowJs`, `maxNodeModuleJsDepth` 1) but not selected (`--dependency-source dep-router` only), so `dependency_source_root` is `None` and it is analyzed as the program's own code; its call to the router's `useLocation()` is a project call (ADR 0290), reported where it is written, as its pinned SC1001 at `props.children` is. Selected as well (`context-package-selected-wrap`), its findings are package findings (ADR 0284) and the requirement must stand at the project site that runs it: `<StaticWrap>`. It did not: that site got no row (the hole below), so dropping the `dep-wrap` row would have lost a U with no attributed replacement.

**The hole.** ADR 0290 reports a requirement in package source at a project *call*. A package function is entered by project code in other ways, and each got no row: a JSX tag renders a component under a root of its own (the owner context is a seed, ADR 0869, no edge), a function is handed on as a value, a method or getter is reached through its holder, a dynamic `import()` leaves the package off source. 18 of 31 probe programs of an entry matrix (a package whose exports read a context made with no default, one `App` per entry; Chrome rc.21 development build through the runtime oracle) threw `ContextNotFoundError` and were certified clean at 29b3f9312 (every positive of the tag forms, `<Dynamic component={Panel}>`, `<Dynamic {...{ component: Panel }}>`, a prop's component, a function child, `For`'s named child, `createEffect(useUi, …)`, `run(useUi)`, `new Reader().read()`, `registry.current`, `lazy(() => import("pkg"))`, `<Ui.Panel>`, `<ui.Panel>`, an import alias, an exact local alias, a render in `Show` / `For` / module scope / a nested component, a spread). The ones that did not drop, with the row they got: `prop-call` (`props.comp({…})`, SC9012 `closed-parameter-member-dispatch`), `call-hook-memo` (a hook whose memo reads the context: SC5003), `async-call` (SC4001). Chrome reports nothing for `timer-cb` (`setTimeout(useUi)`), `handler-cb` (`onClick={useUi}`), `cb-memo` (a memo nothing reads) and `async-jsx` (an async component's read past its `await`); a certified result was right there, and ADR 0949 gives the first three a U (a legal clean → U; the last is not a seed, below).

**Entries.** Per read in package source of a context with no default that nothing proves provided above in its file (ADR 0943's open reads, now recorded whatever owner context their function holds: `Emission::package_reads`, `PackageRead`), `owners/package_entries.rs` adds the project sites, each an SC4002 obligation (uncertifiable, `provider-not-proven-above`, never a violation), with `OwnerRequirement::through_dependency_entry` (`Call`, `Render`, `Reference`) worded for the site:
1. *Renders.* A walk back from the read's function through every owner edge, the exact call and tag sites the call graph names (`function_call_sites`, the resolution that proves component identity; also an exact local alias, ADR 0331, `alias_rendered_tag_function`) and a nested function's enclosing function, through package functions only. A project element that renders a function it reached is a site at its opening tag. Calls into a function the call walk did not visit (it stops at an edge a package function creates and at a callback edge naming no call) are sites when the caller's context may carry an owner.
2. *References.* Any other project reference to a symbol the package declares, when the package holds such a read, is a site at the reference: a value handed on, a holder (a class whose method reads, an object's getter or member), a namespace. It is not one when it is the callee of a call or the tag of an element that resolves exactly to a function (1 decides), an import or export specifier, a type position or query, a context binding (the project's own read is its own row), or written in a function proven never to run. Which function a value runs is not followed, so precision is traded for the zero false clean.
3. *Dynamic loads.* `discover_boundary_defects` read only import declarations: a literal `import()` or `require()` of a package that stays off source (ADR 0300) had no SC9005. It has one at the load, named for the exports its consumer reads (`lazy` reads `default`; a destructuring its members; an escaped result `*`).
A project function no owner edge reaches runs its entries unless `SiteReach` proves it never runs (as `caller_unplaced`). Seeds leave out a read on every path past an `await` (it runs with no owner: SC4001's `NoOwnerError`, no `ContextNotFoundError`) and one in a `try` whose `catch` has no `throw`, call or tagged template (the throw is swallowed where it happens: a router's `isMounted()` probe, which would have made every `<Router>` an obligation).

Fixtures `context-package-component` (the tag forms; `Quiet`, `Plain` and a type use silent), `-entries` (the references; a package with no context read silent), `-lazy`, `-selected-wrap` (item 1's pair with `dependency-source-integrity-r2-visible-js`); 19 new rows, and 9 in `uncertifiable-repro/sc4001` (the five `Dialog` tags of `pkg-kobalte-dialog-setters` and four more: kobalte's tags are references to the `Dialog` object). Fuzz: `package-component`, `package-handed` and `package-holder` beside `package-export` and `package-hook` (`direct` binding, the seven CONTEXT_NOT_FOUND contexts each, 42 programs a source): release builds, mx-recall CONTEXT_NOT_FOUND, 3,705 programs: 29b3f9312 has 126 false cleans (all three sources), the new build none (2,646 agree, 1,059 recall-gap; 77 compared in Chrome, 0 behaviour differences); three entries in `rediscovery.json`. Corpus (rc.21 frozen, 34 projects, release builds 48b28302… before, 115b6db1… after): the standard command (host inference) moves nothing (U 124,473, V 209: most projects run without dependency source); with `--runtime-target browser --runtime-build development` (every Solid package as source) U 134,096 → 134,344 (+248) in 7 projects (error-menu-web +169, pekochan-blog +34, sensor-sim +14, compass-ui +10, skyjtx-website +10, probus-hk +6, ai-memory-ui +5; kobalte's `Tooltip`, `Dialog`, `Tabs`, `DropdownMenu` and `Select` tags are most of them), V 209 → 209. A reference is reported at the call it is an argument or callee of, or the opening tag it is an attribute of (Solid's stack frame for the run sits there), else at itself; the judge places a diagnostic at those positions.
Not done: a provider the project renders around the entry (`<Dialog>` around `<Dialog.Content>`) is not proven across the package boundary; SC4001's own requirements entered by a render are not attributed; an `any`-typed receiver names no symbol; a function reached through a data structure the package builds that the project reaches with no symbol of the package; package module scope. `docs/open-work.md`, the rule page.

**Addendum 1 (2026-11-11): every package finding that leaves the report stands at the project site that enters it.** The same hole as above, for every rule and every entry. research-callonly (Opus, read-only, 64 cases, Chrome rc.21 development build) found 25 false cleans at 29b3f9312 in package code: SC2001 ×5 (`createMemo(() => bump())`, a package getter in a memo, a body call, `<Bump/>`, `<SetBy set={setN}/>`), SC1001 ×6 (`<Panel value={n()}/>`, `<Own/>`, `<Panel read={n}/>`, `count.call(null)`, ``count`x` ``, `new Counter()`), SC4001 ×4 (a handler's getter, `.call`, tagged template and `new` of a package hook that creates an effect), SC4002 ×10 (`createComponent(Inner, …)` inside the package, `Panel()`'s hop; the nine slice 1 already covered). Two causes. (1) ADR 0284's drop (`diagnostics.rs`, `findings.retain(in_project)`) discarded every finding located in a package, uncertifiable ones too: wherever no summary or attribution had carried the execution to a project site, a package `U` became silence. (2) The re-reporting follows exact calls only (reads: calls and getters; writes: never; owner requirements: `enters`).

*One place.* `solid-facts-backend/src/package_drop.rs`, `hand_off`, is the only code that removes a package finding (and the withheld ones: `withheld_coverage`): each dropped finding, a violation or an obligation of any rule, stands again as an **uncertifiable** finding of its own rule (`analysisContext` `package-finding-at-entry:<code>`, `BlockedBy::dropped` the code) at every project site that may run the package function holding it, found over the final rows by `owners::package_entry_table` (`Program::package_entries`: per package function that holds a row, the calls, tags and references that enter it, by the walk back through the package's own callers that slice 1 built; `Program::package_references`: per package, the project references no exact call or tag decides, which may run any of its functions). A site that already holds a finding of the rule keeps its own row; a finding with no proven site stands at the project's imports of its package, else its first file, else its `tsconfig`: never silence, never a violation. `diagnostics.rs` asserts that every drop left a project row (`DropReport::every_drop_attributed`); the unit tests count drops against replacements.

*A false proof under the silence.* The two cases that stayed silent after the hand-off (`count.call(null)`, ``count`x` ``) had no package row to hand over: the read ended in `end_read_no_window` as "a function nothing enters" (`NothingEnters`, ADR 0936). A project's use of a package function names the declaration's symbol (its `.d.ts`), never the runtime function the package's source declares, so `symbol_references` of the runtime symbol is empty and `foreign_reference_hand_offs` found no reference: a package function used only as a tag, a `new`, a `.call` or a holder was concluded never to run. `SemanticLookup::symbol_references_through_redirects` asks every symbol a runtime redirect ties to it, and `foreign_reference_hand_offs` uses it. Moving every consumer of `symbol_references` (about forty) moved two fixtures (four SC1001 obligations fewer: `context-package-component`, `package-write-entries`) and has no corpus delta: not done, `docs/open-work.md`.

*The claiming rule, not any rule.* The runtime oracle credits an SC9012 withholding at a site for every code (`withholdingRules`); the coordinator's ruling (2026-11-11) is that a Chrome code is covered only by a row of the rule that claims it (`scripts/fuzz/codes.mjs` `codePolicy`). Three of the 25 had only SC9012 or SC6002 rows. (a) `own-pkg-tagged-handler`, `own-pkg-new-handler` (`NO_OWNER_EFFECT`, SC4001): a package function that no owner edge reaches (a tag, `new`, a getter's holder, a value handed on) holds context zero, the emission skipped its effect as code that never runs from syntax, and no requirement existed for any walk to attribute. `Emission::package_requirements` records each owner-needing operation (effect, cleanup, boundary, settled cleanup, context read) of such a package function, and `attribute_package_entries` stands it as an SC4001 obligation (`function-run-unplaced`, never a violation) at each call and reference that may run it: never at a render (the component runs under an owner of its own) and never at a call, `new`, tag or `f.call` written in a project function that holds an owner on every run (`runs_owned_here`: `hand_off_at` is `Call` or `Inline`, and the function's context has no unowned bit); a value handed on stands wherever it is written. (b) `wr-jsx-pkg-prop-setter` (`REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE`, SC2001): the package calls a member of its own parameter (`props.set(1)`) and the render hands in a signal's setter: the call may be that setter. Addendum 4 below replaces the first version of this claim (gated on the package's own dispatch obligation, bare identifiers only) with `package_handed_value_claims`.

*Chrome, the same 64 cases on the new build* (`runtime-oracle.mjs` for the page, the checker run here with every Solid package as source, judged as the oracle judges: a finding of a rule that claims the code, a declining uncertifiable one, or a withholding, at the diagnostic's line or an application frame): 29b3f9312 leaves exactly the 25 ids unproven-clean; the new build leaves one, `pk-hook-via-project-helper` (`run(useUi)`): its SC4002 obligation stands at the call `run(useUi)`, the oracle's diagnostic sits at `f()` in the helper (line 2) with no stack frame to reach line 4 (the matrix cells of this shape, `package-handed` through `module-helper`, were clean in slice 1's fuzz run). 24 of the 25 are covered by a row of the claiming rule (addendum 4 below closes the last, and the two not among the 25 that SC9012 alone covered). The two silent cases: `rd-pkg-dotcall` and `rd-pkg-tagged` are SC1001 obligations at the `.call(null)` and the tag.

*Fixtures.* `package-write-entries`, `package-read-entries`, `package-owner-entries`, `context-package-hop` (each with a silent twin where one exists: a package function entered only from a handler or a tracking scope). Moved: 68 existing fixture projects, +644 uncertifiable rows and no row removed or changed in kind: none a violation, none located in a package, no status moved; 633 are `package-finding-at-entry` (SC9012 and SC5003 most, then SC1001, SC2003, SC4001, SC5001, SC4002, SC5008, SC6002, SC1003, SC7002; 9 SC8003, a style rule: duplicate props in a package's JSX), 11 the SC4001 requirement's own at tags, `new` and getters. The four new fixtures hold 31 rows.

*Corpus* (rc.21 frozen, 34 projects, release builds, 48b28302… before, 74484e24… after): the standard command moves nothing (U 124,473, V 209, per project); with `--runtime-target browser --runtime-build development` U 134,096 → 145,994 (+11,898), V 209 → 209, no project changes status. Of the +11,898: 248 are slice 1's SC4002; 11,341 are `package-finding-at-entry` (5,707 at a proven project entry, 5,634 at the project's import, first file or `tsconfig`: **no entry is proven**); the rest the SC4001 requirements. The import-level rows are one row per import and rule: viviana-ui-web 2,432, app-game 722, donegeon-client 400, probus-hk 288, skyjtx-website 170, kui/feature-messages 156 (per project and rule: the report). The owner's ruling keeps them for now (fail closed; no "proven not entered" premise until fuzz evidence backs one). Summed wall time at 10 jobs: 259,832 ms before, 341,052 ms after (`package_entry_table` and the entries walks, debug-free release builds, one run each: not a timing gate).

Not done: `createComponent(Inner, props)` inside a package is no call edge of `Inner` (`pk-createcomponent-hop`: the project call `Panel({})` gets an SC9012 and the SC4001 obligation stands at the import); a provider the package renders above its own hop (`Provided`, certified quiet before, uncertifiable now).

**Addendum 4 (2026-11-11, coordinator rulings on addendum 1).**

*Style rules are not handed off.* A style rule's finding in a package (SC80xx: `jsx-no-duplicate-props`, `prefer-for`, `prefer-show`) says how the package's own JSX is written, not what the project's code does at run time, so no project site has anything to answer for. `hand_off` drops it under the named premise `package-style-not-project` (`DropPass::PackageStyleNotProject`, `PremiseKind::PackageStyleNotProject`, typed and written to the proof log with the finding's package site); every runtime-code rule keeps the hand-off. Fixture `package-style-rule` (the project's own SC8003 is a violation; the package's, in `Competing`, leaves no row). The nine SC8003 rows of addendum 1's fixtures are gone with it, and 815 of the 11,341 `package-finding-at-entry` rows of addendum 1's corpus run (every one SC8003, SC8014 or SC8015).

*One row per package and rule at the first project import.* A finding with no proven entry stands once per rule at the project's first import of its package (importing file's path, then offset), where there is no import at the project's first file, and where there is no file at the configuration; it used to stand at every import. In the corpus, with every Solid package as source, the rows at an import or first file (no entry proven) are 541, where addendum 1 had 5,634.

*The helper's call is an entry.* A package function the project hands whole to a project helper that calls its parameter runs at that call: `run(useUi)` with `function run(f) { return f(); }` runs `useUi` at `f()`, where Solid's stack frame stands (Chrome: `CONTEXT_NOT_FOUND` at line 2, not at `run(useUi)`; the research case `pk-hook-via-project-helper` read as unproven clean because only line 4 held a row). `WalkCtx::helper_calls` follows the reference, a whole argument of a call that resolves exactly (`project_callee_at_parameter`) to a project function whose parameter is a plain identifier nothing assigns, to each call of that parameter written directly in the helper (`owned_by_body`), and on through the parameter handed whole to another helper, three deep (`HELPER_DEPTH`). Each is an exact entry (`DependencyEntry::Call`) of `references_to`, so the SC4002 requirement, the SC4001 requirement and the dropped package findings stand there beside the reference's own row. A helper that stores, returns or passes the parameter on in a shape this does not read adds none (`HelperKeeps`: Chrome is quiet there; the reference's row stands). Fixture `context-package-entries` (`Helper`, `HelperVia`, `HelperKeeps`, `PureHelper`).

*A value handed in a shape no walk reads gets the claiming rule's row (superseded by addendum 5: any entry shape, every capability).* `package_handed_value_claims` (`owners/package_entries.rs`) replaces the dispatch-gated SC2001 claim of addendum 1. At every project call or render whose callee resolves exactly to a function in a package analyzed as source, an exact setter (`source_discovery.setters`) or reactive accessor (`accessors`) that rides in any argument or JSX attribute shape but a bare identifier argument (the call walk reads that one) is an uncertifiable row of the rule that claims the runtime code: SC2001 `setter-handed-to-package` for `REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE`, SC1001 `accessor-handed-to-package` for `STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED`. It does not ask what the package holds (`rd-pkg-createcomponent` holds no row at all: `Inner(props) { props.read() }` is reached through `createComponent`, and the project call `Panel({ read: n })` gets only SC9012 `value-to-unresolved-callee`, a withholding the oracle credits for every code and the coordinator's ruling does not count). A reference that is a callee, or sits in a function written in the value, is no handed value; a reference the facts demanded no entity for (`[setN]`, `c ? setN : off`) is resolved through its declaration's entity. Children (`<Pkg>{setN}</Pkg>`) are not read, and a callee that resolves to no function (a package namespace's member as a tag: `<Dialog.Content onOpenChange={setOpen}>`) hands no claim: both stay open (docs/open-work.md). Chrome, rc.21 development build, the shapes of the fixtures: `Bump({ set: setN })`, `Bump({ set: [setN][0] })`, `<Bump set={setN} />`, `<Reader read={n} />`, `Reader({ read: n })` fire the code and are covered by a row of the claiming rule (uncertifiable); `<Bump set={(v) => setN(v)} />` and `<Reader read={() => n()} />` fire it and are violations (the package calls the closure in the component body); `<Label />`, `<Reader read={() => 1} />`, `keep(useUi)` and `run(pureHook)` are quiet.

*Chrome, the 64 research cases on this build:* 0 unproven clean and 0 covered by a withholding alone (`tools/judge.mjs`, the checker run with every Solid package as source). The row moves of the 28 changed fixture projects were each read: 101 rows removed (the first-import collapse and the style drop), 62 added (the claims, the helper calls, the new fixture components; one violation for each closure).

*`repro-causes`.* The three held cases (`write-after-await`, `write-in-package-component-handler`, `write-object-handed-to-package`) list their expected rows beside the mechanism they reproduce; each gains the `package-finding-at-entry` rows its files now hold (5, 35 and 8: SC1001, SC1003, SC2003, SC4001, SC5001, SC5003, SC9012, SC6002, SC7002 at the project sites that enter the package code). A moved-findings record like a snapshot update, read row by row; the mechanism each case reproduces is unchanged.

*Corpus* (rc.21 frozen, 34 projects, release builds, 48b28302… before, 4b8dc961… after): the standard command moves nothing (U 124,473, V 209, per project); with `--runtime-target browser --runtime-build development` U 134,096 → 140,526 (+6,430; addendum 1: +11,898), V 209 → 209, no project changes status. `package-finding-at-entry`: 5,332 at a proven entry, 541 at an import or first file (5,707 and 5,634 in addendum 1). The new claims stand at no corpus site (0 `accessor-handed-to-package`, 0 `setter-handed-to-package`: no corpus app hands one to a function that resolves exactly into package source); SC4001 `function-run-unplaced` 215 → 524 and SC4002 192 → 440 are the requirements at entries and at helper calls.

**Addendum 5 (2026-11-11): a capability handed in any entry shape, a carrier's tag an entry.** W's package-site cells (ADR 0957: every code with a template, each site moved into a package, thirteen entries, 4,229 programs, Chrome host and the real oracle) left 476 false cleans on 833a785f2 (0 false positives, 0 wrong rule), 409 of them a capability handed to package code by an entry the call walk does not follow: `new Pkg(setN)`, `Reflect.apply(pkgFn, undefined, [setN])`, `createComponent(Pkg, { setN })`, a tag of `dynamic(() => Pkg)`, a getter of an object a package call returns, a JSX prop, a shorthand property. Four mechanisms, all uncertifiable and never a violation:

*Mentions and carriers.* `PackageMentions` (`owners/package_entries.rs`, built once with the owner stage and kept in `OwnerView`) holds the project's references to the symbols a package declares (from the TypeScript reference table) and the immutable bindings that carry one as a value (`const Lazy = dynamic(() => Panel)`: the initializer holds `Panel` where it does not call it; a call's result, `const location = useLocation()`, carries nothing; `const B = A` follows). A carrier's use as a JSX tag is an entry: a package reference for the hand-off (every finding of the package stands at the element's opening tag, where a render's frame stands), and for the SC4002 requirement (not SC4001: a render runs under its own owner).

*Claims by capability.* `package_handed_value_claims` asks every call, `new`, JSX element and tagged template that mentions a package (its callee, tag or tag function, or any argument, names a package symbol or a carrier) for the exact capabilities it hands in any argument, attribute, spread or substitution (`Capabilities`: `source_discovery`'s setters, accessors, stores, actions; a shorthand property resolves through `ObjectPropertyFact::shorthand_binding`, since the facts answer the property's symbol at its span). Each is an uncertifiable row of every rule claiming the runtime code its use throws: a setter, SC2001 (`REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE`); an accessor, SC1001 (`STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED`); a store, SC2003 (`STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER`) and SC1001; an action, SC2002 (`ACTION_CALLED_IN_OWNED_SCOPE`); blockers `*-handed-to-package`. A reference that is a callee, or sits in a function written in the value, is not handed (that function's own site). A bare argument of an exact call (not `new`, not `.call`) whose parameter in the callee is a plain identifier no nested function (a closure, a getter, a method) captures is the call walk's: its summary of the callee says what the callee does (`wr-memo-pkg-handed-setter` is a violation; `access(store)` is proven clean and stays clean); a captured parameter may run later, where no walk follows (`make(setN).value`). `supersede_claims` (`package_drop.rs`) drops a claim where a violation of the same rule stands inside its span (`DropPass::SupersededByViolation`, proof-logged).

*Owner after an await.* `runs_owned_here` is false inside an async function or a generator: code after an `await` runs with no owner whatever the function was entered under (`NO_OWNER_*` through `new` and tagged templates in an async handler).

*Not claimed (addendum 6 claims it through the async-read channel).* SC5001 for an async accessor: the dialect emits it only through the async-read channel and `solve` panics on it as a static violation (the first version did, and every program that handed an async accessor to a package crashed: the PENDING_ASYNC cells read `harness could not judge`); children (`<Pkg>{setN}</Pkg>`); a callee that resolves to no function and names no package symbol.

*Measured.* The 4,229 cells on the final release build (0d472d4e4): 49 false cleans, 0 false positives, 0 wrong rule, every one an id W listed (no new false clean). 30 of the 38 `pending` entries of `scripts/fuzz/rediscovery.json` (2,060 of 2,109 ids) are closed on this build and say so (`fixed` names the fix and the build); the 8 others stay listed, expected-failing, with the mechanism: PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ 13 and PENDING_ASYNC_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE 4 (the SC5001 channel above); UNTRACKED_READ_AFTER_AWAIT 10 (a read after an await in package code reached through `new`, a getter or a tagged template); PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE 8, CLEANUP_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE 4 and FLUSH_IN_TRACKED_QUEUE_CALLBACK 4 (a package constructor or getter run in a leaf scope: the call walk does not follow it and the package holds no row); PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK 4 and FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK 2 at `call` and `call-inner` (a package operation run from an effect callback: the static walks hold no package-side row for it, though the project twin gets a violation). The other 427 of the 476 are closed (every one of the 49 was among the 476). Fixture `package-handed-values` (each shape, the call walk's own `Walked`, silent `Plain`); 5 rows moved in existing fixtures (read one by one: an SC4001 at the carrier tag `<LocalAlias>` and four SC1001 `accessor-handed-to-package` at calls whose callee captures the parameter).

*Corpus* (rc.21 frozen, 34 projects, release, 48b28302… before, a4-5 build of 0d472d4e4 after): the standard command moves nothing (U 124,473, V 209, per project); with every Solid package as source U 134,096 → 141,320 (+7,224; addendum 4: +6,430), V 209 → 209, no status change; `package-finding-at-entry` 6,118 at a proven entry, 444 at an import or first file. The claims stand at 50 corpus sites (accessor 30, action 10, store 6, setter 4). Summed per-project wall time at 10 jobs 273,227 ms (270,300 ms for addendum 4's build).

*Checks and ratchets.* `make verify-delta`: green through proof-gate and ir-edit-fuzz; the fuzz lane 1,525 of 1,526: `api-fire.test.mjs` exceeds its 300 s limit alone on this Mac and hands the judge a synthetic checker result, so it measures only the fast runtime's growing cell count (W's package sites), not this change (reported, the limit not changed). Expectation changes: guard counts (241 keys), `ir-read-lint` `typescript-table` 1 → 2 for `package_entries.rs` (`PackageMentions::build` reads the reference table once per build, like `references_to`), `KIND_GAPS.PackageStyleNotProject` (a style rule has no runtime code for a cell to judge), the `package-component` source exempt from the package-site twin (it is the package program itself).

**Addendum 6 (2026-11-11): a package operation a project site runs in a context where the runtime refuses it is the claiming rule's row at the site.** Addendum 5 left 49 of W's 4,229 package-site cells false clean on 0d472d4e4 (0 false positives, 0 wrong rule), four mechanisms, none of them a finding the package holds: (1) an async accessor handed in (PENDING_ASYNC_UNTRACKED_READ 13, PENDING_ASYNC_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE 4; SC5001, the async-read channel); (2) a package constructor or a returned object's getter run in a leaf scope (PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE 8, CLEANUP_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE 4, FLUSH_IN_TRACKED_QUEUE_CALLBACK 4; SC3001, whose call walk reads exact calls only); (3) a read after an await in package code reached through `new`, a getter or a tag (UNTRACKED_READ_AFTER_AWAIT 10; SC1002); (4) a package function that flushes or creates a computation, called from an effect's apply callback (PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK 4, FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK 2; SC6002, SC2008, whose origin walk goes from the operation up and gives up at an exported function). A package finding is dropped and handed to the sites that run it (ADR 0958), so a rule that holds no row for the operation hands nothing. A read-only diagnosis (Astra, `astra-pkg52/diagnosis.md`) located each gap in its walk.

*One census, one table.* `owners/package_entries/operations.rs`. `OperationRegions` finds, per project file that mentions a package, the lexical regions in which an operation is a claim: the callback arguments a leaf owner runs (`callback_owner_at_call` is `Leaf`: `onSettled`, `createTrackedEffect`), the callback arguments an effect's apply phase runs (`callback_runs_in_effect_phase`, `callback_reruns_in_effect_phase`), and the async function literals that are the tracked compute of a computation (`callback_semantics_at(.., 0, ..).tracks_reads`; a site there counts after an await when `may_follow_suspension` says so). `package_operations` lists, for the packages a site in a region mentions, each `onCleanup`, each creating call the leaf owner refuses (`creates_forbidden_primitive`), each `flush`, and each read the program holds in them. Their locations join the rows of `package_entry_table`, so the table that places a package finding also places an operation: the project sites that may run the innermost function holding it (calls, renders, helper calls) and, for any reference to a symbol of the package no exact call or tag decides, every operation of the package (the reference may run any of it). `package_operation_claims` then asks each site its context and writes one uncertifiable row per rule: SC3001 (`LeafOwnerOperation`, `uncertain`, `possible`, blocker `leaf-owner-unproven`) at a site in a leaf owner's callback; SC2008 for a `flush` and SC6002 for a creating call the dialect says throws there (`throws_creating_in_effect_callback`) at a site in an apply callback (`package-operation-in-effect-apply`); SC1002 (`ReactiveReadAfterAwaitThroughCall`, `package-operation-after-await`) for a read at a site after an await. Never a violation: whether the site reaches the operation (a branch, a guard in the package, a later entry) is not proven. The row stands at the entry's own anchor (the call or `new` expression, the opening tag, the tag of a template), where Chrome's frame stands.

*What a walk already reads is not claimed again.* The leaf walk and the after-await walk read a plain function an exact call enters (its body minus the functions, getters and class members written in it); a site that is such a call adds nothing for an operation in that extent. A constructor, a method, a getter, a closure written in the entered function, a reference, a render and a tag are not read, so the claim stands there. The effect phase has no walk from the apply down, so every entry stands there, an exact call included. A leaf row, an apply row and an after-await row are each written once per site, whatever the number of operations behind it.

*Rare entries are coarse (coordinator, addendum 7).* A getter holder (`make(...).value`), a tagged template and a reference are entries nothing models: their rows come from the census of the package (an operation anywhere in it, for a reference) and the lexical context of the site, never from a proof of which function the entry runs. This is the coarse fail-closed rule for those shapes, an uncertifiable row of every rule whose code the site's context could carry, typed (`leaf-owner-unproven`, `package-operation-in-effect-apply`, `package-operation-after-await`). It is a later proof target: the exact function a getter or a tag runs would turn the row into the walk's own V or a silence with a named premise.

*Async accessor.* `package_handed_value_claims` also writes SC5001 where it hands an async accessor to a package (the SC1001 claim stands beside it): an `AsyncRead` row, the one channel the dialect emits SC5001 through (`solve` panics on a static violation of it, the addendum 5 crash), with the invocation unproven (`invocation-context-unproven`, an obligation: SC5001 is claimed for a leaf as well as an untracked read, and neither is proven here). A reactive source (an accessor or a store) handed in at a site after an await of an async tracked computation is SC1002's row too (`package-operation-after-await`).

*Measured.* The 4,229 cells on the release build of this slice (`package-cells.mjs`, Chrome host and the real oracle, 0 behaviour differences): 0 false cleans, 0 false positives, 0 wrong rule (recall-gap 2,351 → 2,400: the 49 became rows of the claiming rule; agree 212, clean 342, uncertifiable 808, unplaced 307, not-fired 160 unchanged). Corpus (rc.21 frozen, 34 projects, release): the standard command moves nothing (U 124,473, V 209); with every Solid package as source U 141,320 → 141,326 (+6, all `app-game` SC5001 `invocation-context-unproven`: async accessors handed to package code), V 209 → 209, no status change; the operation claims stand at no corpus site. Fixture `package-operations` (each context, the exact call the walk reads, the silent shapes); the other 1,137 snapshots did not move (`make coverage`). Not covered (docs/open-work.md): a callback named by value (`onSettled(cb)`), a site in a project helper that a region calls, an object a region receives from outside it and reads a getter of, a nested function the entered function returns and the project calls later; the project twin of an exported helper called from an apply (`runtime-oracle/flush-in-effect-callback` lists it as `open`).

<a id="adr-0950"></a>
## ADR 0950 — What-if runs: `--assume <hazard>` predicts what proving a hazard fact away would lift
ADR 0947 says which hazard facts veto a row and ADR 0954 which of those rows are legal code, but neither says what a proof of a hazard would do to a program. The what-if build answers that before the proof is built: `solid-checker-what-if --assume OpenMemberWrite(*),RootPatch ...` analyzes one project with the named hazard facts (the ids of `blockedBy.fired` and the `hazards` table) treated as absent. It is a prediction, never a fact about the shipped checker, so it is built so that it cannot be mistaken for one.
Gates (each pinned by a test). The cargo feature `what-if` is off by default and in no `default`, workspace or CI list; `make build-what-if` alone builds it (`make test-what-if` its tests), into its own target directory (`rust/target-what-if`) as `solid-checker-what-if` (`scripts/what-if-gates.test.mjs`: the manifests, workflows, scripts and make targets that could take it; the one seam and the module are behind it). There is one seam, `hazard_present` (`indexes.rs`), which answers absent for an assumed hazard the program has and records that it did; hazard facts only (the array-slots formula's atoms and the ordinal hazards' whole-index readers are not touched), and per hazard id, not per site. A default build has none of it: it answers `--assume` as an unknown argument and holds none of the what-if strings (`what_if_default_process.rs`). The what-if binary is one-shot (no daemon, `--certify`, `--serve`, stdin request, text or ESLint format, analysis log) and tests pin each refusal. Its JSON is tainted: a top-level `assumed` (`hazards`, `flipped`) always, and a typed `assumed` premise on every finding once an answer flipped; the seam cannot say which finding read a memoized whole-program answer, so the taint is the whole run's (a superset of what the assumption touched). Every consumer of the checker's JSON refuses a tainted report at its intake (`scripts/lib/assumed.mjs`: coverage, the fuzz judge and its scans, the sweeps and gates, the ESLint adapter, the owned WASM client, the feedback CLI; 20 intakes and three inline copies in the published packages, each with a test that fails without its guard). `scripts/what-if.mjs` is the one reader, with a reader of its own that wants the taint exactly as written; it is built on the proof-ablation harness (`transitions`, `summarize`), groups a row as ADR 0954 does (`kind formula [fired]`, ways to lift from `liftingSets`), runs a control that assumes nothing on every project and program (its report must equal the shipped checker's, key order aside, or the run stops and names it) and compares the programs that moved with Chrome's record directly (line and policy based, not the fuzz judge, which refuses a tainted report).
Predictions (`scripts/what-if.mjs`; the rc.21 frozen corpus and ADR 0954's 10% sample; the shipped checker and the what-if build are one tree). Corpus: 34 projects (133,839 uncertifiable rows and 209 violations in the shipped reports) under 56 sets (the 34 of the sample and 22 the corpus's own groups ask for); sample: the 34,925 programs with a veto-formula row, under 34 sets. A control that assumes nothing ran on every project and program: identical to the shipped checker's report on all 34 projects and all 34,925 programs, 0 errors. Counts, not safety. `OpenMemberWrite(*)` alone lifts no corpus row: kui feature-messages has nine sites (three in `session.ts`, lines 379, 604 and 613, six in `@solidjs/router`'s dist), and assuming all nine away changes only the blockers, because the rows also fire `OpenMemberWrite(<symbol>)`, `ReflectiveDefinitionSpelling` and `RootPatch`: the seam is per hazard id. With `ReflectiveDefinitionSpelling` it lifts 26,816 rows in 31 projects (3 become violations, 116 clean sites obligations); `RootPatch` + `StandardRootHanded` 2,178 in 3 projects (3 violations, all `prefer-show` rows of kui's kernel); `ReflectiveDefinitionSpelling` alone 2,122 in 3; `StandardRootHanded` alone 38; `StandardImpostor(*)` 4; `RootPatch`, `StandardRootReceived`, `StandardPatch(.Symbol)` and the `then` and `forEach` impostors lift nothing in the corpus; the biggest sets lift 34,000 to 37,000 rows in 32 or 33 projects. No set made a clean site a violation or lost or demoted a violation (`cleanToV`, `vLost`, `vToU` are 0 in every set of both runs). On the sample: `RootPatch` alone lifts 16,083 rows in 6,521 programs; with `StandardRootHanded` 34,180 in 14,402 (21 become violations, 261 clean sites obligations); `OpenMemberWrite(*)` alone 23,140 in 9,979 (18 and 104); `StandardImpostor(*)` with `(then)` 9,951 rows and 234 violations.
Chrome's record of the 25,257 programs that moved (the shipped checker's runs with `--runtime-diff all`, each program under each set it moved in; the comparison's reading of "covered" agrees with the oracle's on 13,048 of 13,048 diagnostics). The shipped checker's own outcomes on those programs are 12,007 recall gaps, 10,808 uncertifiable, 1,393 typed away, 789 not fired, 154 clean, 105 agreeing and one `unplaced` (`mx-dynamic-static-promise-patch-global-x-direct-none`, ADR 0815's open defect): no false clean, false positive or wrong rule. The verdicts on the assumptions, which are never verdicts on the shipped checker (a violation the shipped checker also reports at the place, 39 rows on 4 programs whose own outcome is `not-fired`, is left out): no unsafe verdict for `RootPatch` alone (16,083 rows to clean, 5,219 of them still covered elsewhere and 9,912 where Chrome is silent), `StandardRootReceived`, `ReflectiveDefinitionSpelling` alone, `StandardPatch(.Symbol)` and the other `OpenMemberWrite(<name>)` ids; unsafe: `StandardImpostor(then)` (1,742 false cleans in 908 programs: a thenable impostor really present loses its obligation; 9 false positives), `StandardImpostor(forEach)` (460 false positives in 421 legal programs), `StandardImpostor(*)` (45 false cleans), `StandardRootHanded` (11 false cleans in 3 programs, 18 false positives in 12) and `OpenMemberWrite(*)` (8 in 2, 16 in 11). On this sample `RootPatch` is the assumption with the largest lift and no unsafe verdict; assuming `StandardImpostor`, `StandardRootHanded` or `OpenMemberWrite(*)` away wholesale would lose a real obligation or add a violation Chrome does not report.
Limits. A prediction is counts, not safety: the Chrome comparison is per program and per assumption, a program tsc rejects (`typed-away`) has no record, and the corpus has no runtime. The taint is the whole run's, the seam is per hazard id, the array-slots atoms and the ordinal hazards' whole-index readers are not assumable, and a lifted veto changes the blockers of rows that other atoms still veto (kui feature-messages: assuming all nine `OpenMemberWrite(*)` sites away changes only the blockers). Owners: `solid-reactive-ir/src/what_if.rs`, `indexes.rs` (`hazard_present`), `solid-facts-backend/src/main.rs`, `scripts/what-if.mjs`, `scripts/lib/assumed.mjs`; `make build-what-if`, `make test-what-if`.

## ADR 0951 — A reflective definer put to a literal key withholds that key only (the fix-9 narrowing)
ADR 0939's `handed_root_patch` withholds a standard call once a standard root goes on as a value and some write may have put a function at the call's member. Its `written` read three things: an open write of the member or of any key (`OpenMemberWrite`), a symbol-keyed write beside a replaceable `Symbol`, and `ReflectiveDefinitionSpelling`, the presence of *any* spelling of `defineProperty`, `defineProperties` or `__defineGetter__` in the program, as a member, a destructured property or a callee, whatever it defines. A project's own tests spell them (kui: `Object.defineProperty(navigator, "clipboard", …)`, `(window, "matchMedia", …)`, `(HTMLElement.prototype, "clientHeight", …)`), and so does dependency source, so the blanket fact made every standard call of a program that hands a root on a row of its own: kui's 7,602 `standard-member-impostor` rows are 7,382 of `handed-root-patch` (3,958 with an open write under a key no fact names, 3,424 with this spelling and no such write) and 220 of the impostor census.

*The proof.* The definer census (`definers`, ADR 0314) already says what each spelling may define and `impostor_veto` already reads it: a call of a member-spelled definer with a string-literal key defines that key and no other (`DefinerKey::Sole`); a spelling that names no literal key (`defineProperties`, a variable key, a definer held as a value, destructured or called under another name) may define any (`DefinerKey::Any`). The per-file hazards now carry both (`ReflectiveDefinitionAny`, value 0, and `ReflectiveDefinitionSole(key)`, the key's hash), and `handed_root_patch`'s `written` reads `ReflectiveDefinition(*)` or `ReflectiveDefinition(<the call's member>)` in place of the blanket spelling. `ReflectiveDefinitionSpelling` stays for its other readers (the class-prototype and memo reads of ADR 0314). A row's `fired` names the new atoms (`ReflectiveDefinition(*)`, `ReflectiveDefinition(isFinite)`), `--assume` reads them back (`hazard_of_id`), `scripts/u-blockers.mjs` and `scripts/what-if.mjs` know them. No other formula changed; the seam in `indexes.rs` and the what-if wiring are untouched.

*Fuzz.* The seam shapes (ADR 0935; `bseam-<member>-<form>` and its twin `-other`) gain the forms `definelit` (`Object.defineProperty(target, "<key>", …)`) and `reflectlit` (`Reflect.defineProperty`): the helper writes the literal, the member's own in the misuse context and a sibling's in the twin, for `Number.isFinite`, `Promise.prototype.then` and `setTimeout`, under the three table codes. Cells (release, Chrome host and the real oracle, `--runtime-diff all`, no cache): 108 misuse programs, all recall-gap on the build before (63d6d2c42, release f9ce9b5ff27a…) and on this one (0 false clean, 0 false positive, 0 wrong rule); 108 legal twins, all uncertifiable before, now 94 uncertifiable and 14 clean (0 false positive, 0 behaviour differences). The programs of the grids that spell a definer (35,400: 26,969 recall misuse programs and 8,431 legal ones, 463 and 42 cells of five codes, among them the 62 `patch-*` and `recv-*` contexts of `flip-read.mjs`), checker alone on both builds: 10,914 have fewer uncertifiable rows and none more, no program's violations at its judged site change, no misuse program's judged site stands in no row now (0 of 26,969) and none is free of uncertifiable rows; 12 legal programs (the `then` twins of the new forms) are, which the seam run's Chrome host found quiet. The rest differ only in the atoms `fired` names.

*Fixtures.* `handed-root-patch-definer-key-same` (the helper defines `isFinite`: the call stays withheld, SC9012 `standard-member-impostor`, `Object.defineProperty` itself is not), `-other` (it defines `isNaN`: certified, no row), `-any` (a variable key: the helper's definer and the call are withheld); Chrome (rc.21): the first and third fire STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED and are covered, the second is quiet. Three fixtures moved, each row read (`make coverage`: 1,153 projects, 15,540 findings): impostor-receivers 24 → 16 `standard-member-impostor` rows (the program's one definer defines `then` on `Object.prototype`: 8 calls of other members are no longer withheld), read-after-await-premise-tripwire-define −6 +1 (the Promise premise is still withdrawn by its own tripwire: the SC1002 `handed-read-after-await` row stands where the veto's rows had masked it) and standard-patches −6 +1 (the fixture's own claim, ADR 0781, a write of another name keeps the proof, now holds for a definer). The guard's drops rows say so (`scripts/guard/counts.json`, ADR 0951, proof `handed_root_patch`).

*Measured.* Corpus (34 projects, release, the build before the narrowing against this one, standard and the browser development build). Standard: uncertifiable rows 124,496 → 119,001 (−5,495), violations 209 → 218 (+9), in four projects: kui 18 (−873), kui 22 (−1,733, violations 1 → 7), kui 23 (−2,495, 0 → 3, status uncertifiable → violation) and skyjtx-website (−394). 5,768 rows left (SC9012 `standard-member-impostor` 3,241, `tuple-iteration-written` 891, SC5003 `pending-read-unresolved` 792, `handed-member-dispatch` 242, `parameter-use-unmodeled` 138, …) and 282 stand: 148 SC2001 `function-run-unplaced`, 80 SC9012 `value-forwarded`, 26 `value-in-carrier`, 9 violations (the next hole behind the lifted veto). Development build: 141,349 → 138,854 (−2,495), violations 209 → 212 (+3), kui 23 alone (status uncertifiable → violation); 2,634 rows left (1,406 `standard-member-impostor`), 142 stand (65 SC2001, 49 `value-forwarded`, 3 violations). The seven kui sites the batch-10 brief names are violations on the standard build: TopicListPage.tsx 298 (`props.query` read in the component body, SC1001) and 314 (`typed()` in a `createEffect` apply callback, SC1001), TopicsRoute.tsx 522 (two `query()` reads in an apply callback, SC1001) in project 22, and Drawer.stories.tsx 127, BarChart.tsx 214 and LineChart.tsx 240 (SC8015 `prefer-show`, a violation in kui's other five projects already) in project 23, BarChart and LineChart in project 22 too; in the development build the three of project 23 only (the other projects still hold an open write of an unnamed key). Each new violation is the rule's own proof with no change to it: no new V came from a rule's proof changing, and none stands at a judged site of the grids.

*The prediction.* ADR 0950's what-if run assumed the hazards away wholesale and counted what lifts: `ReflectiveDefinitionSpelling` alone 2,122 rows in 3 projects, with `OpenMemberWrite(*)` 26,816 in 31 (3 violations, 116 clean sites obligations), on a corpus of 133,839 uncertifiable rows (a mode of its own: neither of the two above). The proof lifts the fact only where every definer names a literal other than the call's member, so it can lift less, and it lifted more rows than the prediction in the standard run: 3,241 `standard-member-impostor` rows in four projects, against 2,122 in three. The gap is that the fired-set model counts the veto's own rows; a withheld call also shadows the rows downstream of it (tuple iteration 891, pending reads 792, handed-member dispatch 242: 1,925 of the rows that left), and the same lift exposes 282 rows behind it. What the wholesale assumption cannot say, the real run does: the nine new violations, each a rule's ordinary proof that stood behind the veto; the prediction's three were the kui kernel's `prefer-show` rows. What is left of kui's rows is `OpenMemberWrite(*)` (three writes in `session.ts`, six in `@solidjs/router`'s dist) and the impostor census's `*` (52 sites in kui feature-messages: constant string keys read across files, `Object.fromEntries`, keys that are parameters), each its own proof (docs/open-work.md).

## ADR 0952 — A runtime-oracle case lists no site the checker now covers
`scripts/runtime-oracle.mjs` fails a case whose `open` entry (a known unproven-clean site with its reason) no longer fires as one (`stale-open`), but nothing runs `fixtures/runtime-oracle`: not `make verify`, not CI (`fuzz-pr.yml` watches the script's path and never runs the cases). Entries outlived their defects as the checker moved from silence to uncertifiable results (the flip series, ADRs 0661, 0665). Batch 9 saw two (`r4c-leak`, `r9-b3-childrenasync`, ADR 0946); the full run on 29b3f9312 (742 cases, 4 workers, 4.5 minutes) has 12, and the same 12 are stale with 3b9688ae3's binaries, so nothing in batch 9 caused them. In each, Chrome still reports the code at the listed site and a finding covers it: SC5003 uncertifiable at `r4c-leak` :43 (`async-provenance-unproven`: the memo's compute calls a handed callback, so no fact proves it sync) and at `r9-b3-childrenasync` :15 (`root-mount-pass-unproven`); SC1001 uncertifiable at `c2d-method-tracked` :19 and :32 and, at the helper's call :12 (a frame, which the oracle accepts), for `r9-d1-mixmemoslotreadinbody`; an SC1001 violation at `r9-b3-childrenreadinbody` :14; SC4001 uncertifiable on the owner cases' lines (`review-r1-owner-mix` :5 and :13, `review-r3-owner-double-call`, `review-r5-owner-class-control`, `review-r6-owner-default-control`, `review-r6-owner-jsx-getter-control`, `review-r7-event-array`, `review-opus5-t6-rest-timer-owner`). No false clean (the run has no `unproven-clean` row) and no false positive (`staticOnly` is empty for all 12). The fix is at the cases: the `open` entries are removed and each note says what covers the site. That tightens the oracle (a site that falls silent again is `unproven-clean`, exit 1); no judge, assertion, limit or classification moved. On 29b3f9312: `stale-open` 12 to 0, `covered-uncertifiable` 195 to 206, `covered-violation` 112 to 113, every other case and verdict as before.
Not done: the comment at `fileJudgedCodes` in `scripts/runtime-oracle.mjs` still names `r4c-leak` and `r9-b3-childrenasync` (the file is in the fuzz runtime cache key, `oracleRecordHash`: an edit cold-starts every grid's runtime records, so reword it with the next change that needs the file); `oracle-behaviour-detached-owner-layers` is `type-error` (TS2305: rc.21's `solid-js` index does not export `createOwner`, its `solid-js/internal` does, rc.17's index did when the case was written), so the run still exits 1: rewrite it against rc.21; `c4-seen-origin` branches on `Math.random()`, so its diagnostics differ between runs, its verdict does not; a gate that runs the fixtures would have caught the rot.

## ADR 0953 — The row ledger names 36 of the aw-helper-late cell's 359 stacked claims; none is a false clean
Evidence only: no ledger row, classification or ratchet moved. The cell is mx-recall `UNTRACKED_READ_AFTER_AWAIT` × `aw-helper-late`, 706 programs. On 29b3f9312 (checker 7f5a78f05b36; real Chrome on all 706: 706 same as the fast host) 131 are `agree`, 216 `recall-gap` and 359 `stacked-claim`; no false clean, false positive, wrong rule, `not-fired` or `typed-away`. The 359 are one shape: each row's own site is `agree` (`detail.primary`), and the pair `UNTRACKED_READ_AFTER_AWAIT|reactive-read-after-await` (kind `repeat`) stacks exactly one more claim, SC1002 at the second read of the same source, which Solid does not report again. A twin at scale (the program with its first read blanked, the line kept; real Chrome and the checker): 359 of 359 report the code at the remaining read, a violation covers it, nothing else is reported and no finding is static-only (the 6 `prop-value-cast` programs report at the parent's attribute line, ADR 0938). So no second claim is a false positive and no row hides a false clean.
Why the ledger (`open-defects-rows.json`, `complete: false`, listing the problem rows earlier smokes drew by id) names 36 (30, and the 6 `prop-value-cast` ids added at 75a37bc83 when ADR 0938's judge turned them from false positives into stacked claims): the covering sample no longer draws the cell. Since ADR 0916 the pull request's tier asks a recall program only for its pairs with its code: 1 program of the cell with no required ids, 44 with the ledger's (36 stacked, all held: CI's smoke on main ran mx-recall `R-stacked 36`); an every-pair cover, as before, drew 66 of the 706 at any seed. `open-defects.json` has no family entry with outcome `stacked-claim`, so `gridStanding` gives the other 323 no standing in a strict or a non-strict run: each is a problem row where a run reaches it, the full grids (the audit's 323) or a rotated cover seed (15 of 30 seeds draw at least one). Reproduce: `focus.mjs --codes UNTRACKED_READ_AFTER_AWAIT --grids mx-recall --runtime-diff all -- --contexts aw-helper-late`.
Options, each a classification or ratchet change, so not made: (a) ledger the 323 ids (`stackedClaim` 202 to 525, the pair 46 to 369); (b) one family entry (`idPrefix mx-untracked-read-after-await-aw-helper-late-`, the code, outcome `stacked-claim`), so an unlisted row prints as `unlisted` in a non-strict run, no ratchet moves, strict runs still need ids; (c) the judge scores a `repeat` claim as the agreement it is where the pair's twin holds (the pair's count 46 to 0 in the ratchet); (d) one SC1002 per source per computation, which changes what a user sees. Recommended: (c), since Chrome's silence there is Solid's dedup and the twin proves each claim, with (b) until it lands.
Owner ruling (2026-11-11, relayed by the coordinator): (b) now, (c) in batch 10. (b) is made, and it is a classification change: `open-defects.json` holds one family entry for the cell (`idPrefix mx-untracked-read-after-await-aw-helper-late-`, the code, context `aw-helper-late`, outcome `stacked-claim`, item `R-stacked`, so a regenerated ledger keeps its cause and no ratchet count moves). A stacked claim of the cell the ledger does not list is `unlisted` in a run off the ledger's seed (printed, not a failure); a strict run (the ledger's seed, a manifest id) still wants the id listed; another outcome, context or code of the cell has no standing. Measured on 29b3f9312: the cell run, which needed a look for 323 rows, prints `known open mx-recall: 36 programs still fail (R-stacked 36)` and `323 failing rows outside the smoke manifest and off the ledger, not judged`, nothing needs a look, exit 0; `open-defects.test.mjs` has three tests more, and the cover, smoke, focus and patterns tests pass (65). The entry goes when (c) covers all 359.

**Option (c), landed (batch 10; the owner's ruling, a judge change).** `matrix.mjs` `judge` takes `twins` (finding label to `{ holds, why }`); a repeat claim (classify.mjs `offSiteVerdicts`, why `repeat`, of a pair `stacked-pairs.json` marks `repeat`) whose twin holds is the agreement it is, so the row keeps its own outcome and `detail.twins` records each twin's verdict (`held`, or why not). The twin (`repeat-twin.mjs`) is the program with the first read, the site that holds the first report's frame (`firstAt`, new on a repeat row), replaced by an inert `1;`, judged as a program of its own at the remaining read for the first code: it holds when that judge says `agree` and the violation that covers the read is the claim's own rule's. The grid runs the twins of a chunk's stacked-claim rows in one `evaluate` (`settleRepeats`, cases under `<out>/twins/`, the runtime cache as for any program) and judges those rows again; `--runtime-diff` compares each twin with Chrome as a program of the stratum `outcome=twin`, and `judgeChrome` re-judges with the row's recorded twins. A claim with no twin, or one that failed, is scored by its pair exactly as before: a twin only ever removes a claim, never adds an outcome, and the twin's judge is the same judge, so a twin where no violation covers the read is a `false-clean`, where the runtime reports nothing at it a `false-positive`, with the wrong rule a `wrong-rule`: none holds, and the row stays a stacked claim. Without `twins` `judge` answers as it did (judge-frozen's 498 rows, unchanged), and `legal.mjs` is untouched. Not twinned: a repeat in a halted run (`disturbed-repeat`) and the first-throw pairs, which stay pair-scored.
Measured on 29b3f9312 (checker 7f5a78f05b36), old judge and new on the same tree otherwise, joined by program id. The cell (706 programs): old agree 131, recall-gap 216, stacked-claim 359; new agree 490, recall-gap 216, stacked-claim 0: exactly the 359 rows moved, each stacked-claim to agree (its own outcome), the other 347 unchanged in outcome and detail; 359 twins held, none failed. Chrome, `--runtime-diff all` on the cell with its twins: 1,065 programs compared, 0 behaviour, 0 attribution, 0 inconclusive. The smoke's cover (every pair, seed 1: 54,098 programs of the 11 grids, the census whole): 54 rows moved and nothing else (no other outcome or detail changed in any grid): mx-recall 36 stacked-claim to agree; mx-census 18, of them 4 to agree (STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER `aw-helper-late-{assign,delete,pick-assign,pick-delete}`) and 14 to `not-fired` (their own code is not reported at the primary site, which the claim had made a problem row: LOADING_ON_ACCESSOR 6 and STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED 4 `aw-helper-late-*`, STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER `aw-shared-{after,before}-{assign,delete}` 4); 54 twins held, none failed. The census's 148 stacked claims left are of the seven first-throw pairs, none a repeat; the audit at 63725c6bf lists 353 stacked claims in mx-recall, all in this cell, and none in the other nine grids but the census, so every repeat claim of the grids has been judged. The second repeat pair, `STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER|no-store-mutation-outside-setter` (8 ledger rows), moves with the first: its table kind is `repeat` too and the same twin proves it; restricting (c) to one pair would need a table flag no reason supports.
The ledger and ratchet move with it, the 54 rows by id (they are exactly the ledger's rows of the two pairs): `open-defects-rows.json` loses them, `stackedClaim` 202 to 148, `causes.R-stacked` 202 to 148, the pair `UNTRACKED_READ_AFTER_AWAIT|reactive-read-after-await` 46 to 0 and `STORE_WRITE_OUTSIDE_SETTER|no-store-mutation-outside-setter` 8 to 0; the family entry of (b) is removed, as the ruling said (359 of 359 covered). `open-rows.mjs --update` was not used: the PR tier's cover is a different selection than the ledger's seed, and an update from it would add the unlisted rows. A cover smoke after the change needs a look at the same 22 items as before it (package-site false cleans, ADR 0957, and census manifest rows that were worse before). Tests: `repeat-twin.test.mjs` (15: the split with twins, the twin's construction and refusals, `twinHolds`, and the whole path on the fast runtime's records, the twin-fails cases included); `offsite.test.mjs`'s repeat row carries `firstAt`; the three tests of the (b) entry give way to two that the cell has no standing. Not done: the smoke manifests (`scripts/fuzz/smoke/mx-census.json`) still record the 18 census rows as stacked claims (they read better, not worse); the reselect is the deferred one.

<a id="adr-0954"></a>
## ADR 0954 — Which uncertifiable rows are legal code and which are misuse, per veto formula
The blocker report (ADR 0947) says what lifts a veto, not whether lifting is safe. `scripts/fuzz/blocker-scan.mjs` regenerates the grids' programs from their rows and runs the checker alone on each (no runtime; `--format json --feedback-facts`, as the harness does), writing per program its uncertifiable rows by blocker kind (in the judged site region, or away) and the rows whose `blockedBy` names a formula or a missing fact. `scripts/u-verdicts.mjs` joins them with the verdicts a grid run already holds (Chrome's, per program: the audit at 63725c6bf's `per-program/ids-<grid>.tsv.gz`, 11 grids; a program it lacks is `unjudged`, never guessed) and ranks the groups `(kind, formula, fired set)` and `(kind, missing fact)`, every list biggest first and ties by key, so two runs print the same bytes. A row is `legal` (a legal-grid program Chrome ran: `clean` or `uncertifiable`), `away` (elsewhere in a confirmed misuse program: Chrome reported nothing there), `blocking` (it stands in the judged site of a misuse program whose outcome is `recall-gap`: Chrome confirmed the defect and the checker only declined, so lifting it must give a violation, never a clean), `shadowed` (at the site beside a violation) or `unjudged`; `misuse%` is blocking plus shadowed over the judged rows. The site region is the harness's (`regionOf`); a row at a frame only Chrome recorded reads `away`. Tests: `u-verdicts.test.mjs`, `blocker-scan.test.mjs`.
Measured on 29b3f9312 (release checker 7f5a78f05b36), a 10% sample of every grid (`--sample 0.1`, seed 1: 216,060 programs, 186 programs/s; one, mx-census `mx-caught-reactive-read-prop-unread-cap-async-direct-none`, Oxc cannot parse and the checker exits 2); counts are the sample's, the grids' are about ten times these, and 3,886 programs (1.8%) are newer than the audit's table:

Reading it. `standard-member-impostor` is 326,951 of the 684,124 rows (48%) and 600 of them (0.2% of the judged) stand at a confirmed defect: each of the 15 `handed-root-patch` groups above has at most 158 blocking rows (misuse% 0.7 or less), the 3 `impostor-veto` groups none; the veto's cost on the grids is legal and off-site code (legal plus away is 99.8% of its judged rows). `array-slots-withdrawn` (`tuple-iteration-written`, 33,651 rows) is the other way: its two groups carry 2,983 and 2,963 blocking rows (16.6% and 22.4%), so a narrowing there must keep a violation.
