Not a veto but the same table's lesson: `function-run-unplaced` has 82,097 rows at 56.5% misuse%, `value-in-carrier` 53.1%, `owner-varies-by-caller` 96.2%, `store-write-unproven` 93.1%: lifting those blindly would certify defects. Not done: `missing` has no emitter (0 groups); the exact scan of every program (about 3 hours here) and the blocking rows themselves, which are not read here.

The full-grid scan (2,155,003 programs, `scan-full.sh`) was started on 29b3f9312 and stopped at about 850,000 programs (coordinator, 2026-11-11): batch 10 makes that build stale (the package hand-off of ADR 0949, the slot climbs of ADR 0959), the sample already gives the ranking, and the run slowed the cells' measurements. Its partial rows are kept unread in the VM (`b10/runs/scan-full.jsonl`, 705 MB). The counts above are the 10% sample's; the exact full-grid counts come once, after batch 10, in the run that also holds the full audit and the smoke reselect.

<a id="adr-0955"></a>
## ADR 0955 — A veto's corpus delta runs only where its hazards are; its fixture rises are named, never blocked
A veto reads whole-program hazards (ADR 0947), so a change to one moves rows only in the projects where those hazards are present, and two sweeps of all 34 corpus projects answer what the old build's `hazards` table already scopes. `scripts/corpus-delta.mjs` (`make corpus-delta OLD=<old solid-checker-rust> DELTA_HAZARDS=...`): the old results are `--baseline` (a `corpus-sweep.mjs` report, which keeps each project's `hazards` table from now on) or a run of the old checker over every project; a project is affected when its table holds a hazard the change names (an id, or its head) or a row of a named blocker kind; the new build runs on those and on `--controls` unaffected projects (most uncertifiable rows first), which must not move. The report is deterministic: violations and uncertifiable rows old to new per project, uncertifiable rows per blocker kind, the moves by rule, kind and blocker, and `needs a look`, which fails the run (a violation the old build did not have, a control that moved because the scope missed a hazard, a project the checker did not answer). Limits, said in the script: a table lists only the hazards a row names, so a NEW veto, or a fail-closed result that reads no hazard, names `--all`; a project with no table is in when the old build records no hazards at all (a build before ADR 0947) and has none when it does (a project with no hazard omits the table). Demonstrations on three corpus apps (sensor-sim, pekochan-blog, expenses-app; release builds, 46 s): e771f6a3d against main, scope `OpenMemberWrite,StandardRootHanded`: 3 affected, 0 rows moved; 3b9688ae3 (no table, so all in) against main: +22 uncertifiable, every one an SC4002 `provider-not-proven-above` (expenses-app +6, sensor-sim +16, pekochan-blog 0), which is ADR 0943's dependency context, and nothing else moved.
The per-fact rises (review P2) are a separate append-only list, `scripts/guard/rises.json`, read by `scripts/guard/rises.mjs`: per existing fixture (a snapshot the base also has: the coverage snapshots and the reproduction set's), each `code blocker` key whose uncertifiable rows rose against `count.mjs`'s base, named by a row `{ fixture, key, from, to, adr, fact }`. An unnamed rise is printed and never fails (a fail-closed change is meant to raise the count, and a guard that blocked it would push the change toward silence); a malformed row, an ADR not in decisions.md, or a base row gone or changed fails. `--append "ADR NNNN" --fact "..."` names every unnamed rise. `counts.json` keeps its `code blocker` keys and is not rekeyed. Both run in `make verify` (verify.sh; verify-delta's `guard-count`) and are in CONTRIBUTING.md's paragraph on semantic changes. Tests: `corpus-delta.test.mjs` (9), `guard-rises.test.mjs` (5).

<a id="adr-0956"></a>
## ADR 0956 — The reduction keeps the blocker the checker reports, not the wording
`scripts/repro-reduce.mjs` held a result by labels read from wording: the rule, the message family and the analysis context kind (`scripts/uncertifiable-causes.mjs`), on a line holding a marker; its guard against new `tsc` errors catches one drift to another cause behind the same words, not the rest. With `--blocker <kind>` it holds the typed blocker of ADR 0947: `blockedBy.kind`; for a veto `--formula` and `--fired`, the exact set of hazards that fired, in any order (one hazard more or fewer is another veto, never held); for a row one test decides `--missing <fact>[:<member>]`; `--rule` and `--marker` narrow it, and the kind alone holds any row of that kind. The text key is as it was. Refused: `--family` or `--context` beside `--blocker`, `--formula` without `--fired` (or the other way round), `--formula` with `--missing`, and the typed flags without `--blocker`. The key is `scripts/lib/repro-key.mjs` (`parseKey`, `holdsKey`, `describeKey`), tested in `repro-key.test.mjs` (9 tests). End to end in the VM with the release build: a 23-line grid program (`mx-strict-read-untracked-body-use-self-apart-wrapper-helper`, an SC9012 row `tuple-iteration-written array-slots-withdrawn [VisibleIterationWrite]`) reduces to 13 lines in 100 checker runs and 6.5 s, holding that blocker; the same program with `--fired TupleSlotIterationMayBeMutated` stops at "the result does not hold in the copy before any edit"; `--formula` alone is refused. `scripts/u-blockers.mjs` and `scripts/u-verdicts.mjs` print the formulas and fired sets the flags take.

<a id="adr-0957"></a>
## ADR 0957 — The package site is a default twin: any site can sit in a dependency, reached through thirteen entries
A finding in package source is dropped (ADR 0284) and reported only at the project's call that runs it (ADRs 0290, 0943), and the fuzzer put every site in the project: a package was only a helper's callback (`fz-lib`) or SC4002's context (`fz-ctx`). A researcher's 25 Chrome false cleans on 29b3f9312 were all in package code (SC2001 writes, SC1001 reads, SC4001 after a click, SC4002 through a `createComponent` hop) and no cell held them. `scripts/fuzz/package-site.mjs`: `describeUse` prints a site as the project would (binding, prelude, module lines), and `packageSite` moves that text into an export of `fz-site`; its parameters are the free names the text uses (a TypeScript parse; Solid's names are imported there, types are stripped with `module.stripTypeScriptTypes`, so the package is plain JS with a `.d.ts` of `any`), and App.tsx writes an entry instead: `call`, `call-inner`, `dotcall`, `reflect`, `tagged`, `new`, `getter` (`make1(..).value`), `callback` (handed by name to a project helper), `jsx`, `jsx-inner` (a `createComponent` hop inside the package), `hop` (`createComponent(Site1, ..)`), `dynamic`, `lazy` (the last two mounted with `render`: an element a statement discards never runs the component). Two value modes: handed (the entry passes the project's signals; the checker reads a handed parameter as unresolved and may decline) and `-own` (the package creates the signal, store or memo, so only the attribution through the entry is in question). The runtime places a diagnostic at the entry (package frames are dropped, as for `fz-lib`), so the site is the entry's line. A shape is `pk: <entry>` (`-pk-<entry>` in the id) on the plain shape of a cell in the recall grids (direct binding, no layer: the other axes change how the project reaches the site, and a package is reached only by its entry), for the four codes first (`PACKAGE_SITE_CODES` in matrix.mjs); a component entry runs the site in the component's own body, so it is a misuse cell where the code's `body` cell is one and a legal cell where that is (`componentSide`: `not-legal` rows on the first pass were that). `scripts/fuzz/package-cells.mjs` runs just these programs (`--list`, or `--out` with the binaries): one `--ids` manifest and one focus run per (grid, code).
The invariant (`package-site.test.mjs`, `package-site-coverage.mjs`): every source in `SOURCES` and every context of every recall cell builds a program whose site is in the package, or is in `PACKAGE_SITE_EXEMPT_SOURCES` / `PACKAGE_SITE_EXEMPT_CONTEXTS` with a reason (both empty: `package-export` and `package-hook`, which looked like candidates, take the twin by handing `fz-ctx`'s context or hook to `fz-site`; a context that repeats its site, the stacked `aw-*` pairs, holds two package sites); an exemption for something that has a variant fails too.
Measured on 29b3f9312 (release; checker 7f5a78f05b36, producer 998ad2d92a37; every program through the Chrome host and the real oracle: 1,701 programs, 0 behaviour differences, 0 attribution differences): mx-recall SC2001 (780) agree 40, false-clean 608, recall-gap 72, unplaced 60; SC1001 (208) agree 40, recall-gap 40, false-clean 84, not-fired 32, unplaced 12; SC4001 (96) agree 24, false-clean 72; SC4002 (91) recall-gap 14, false-clean 70, unplaced 7. lg-recall (526 legal programs): SC2001 clean 166, uncertifiable 122; SC1001 clean 100, uncertifiable 60; SC4001 clean 68, uncertifiable 10: no false positive, no wrong rule, no `not-legal` anywhere. The false cleans are the class: package code reached through anything but an exact call of a project-resolved callee (every entry but `call` and `call-inner` with handed values; with `-own` values even those). Named apart, not in the researcher's list: SC2001's `refresh` variant at an exact call, handed (20 programs: the package-side `refresh(m1)` is an uncertifiable `reactive-dispatch-unresolved` no rule of the code declines). `lazy` is `unplaced` in every cell (the component renders after the import resolves, with no authored frame and an `async-outside-loading-boundary` of its own); `dynamic` is `not-fired` for SC1001 (Solid runs a dynamic component untracked, so no strict read warns). rediscovery.json lists the 834 false-clean ids in five entries as expected-failing until the Mac's ADR 0949 (`fixed: pending`).
Part 2 (coordinator's ruling, 2026-11-11): the package variant is crossed with every code that has a template, not the four. A stratum sample of the other codes first (464 programs of 4,229, four per stratum of entry class and value mode, main 29b3f9312, 0 behaviour differences) found 30 false cleans at handed exact calls (`call`, `call-inner`) in nine codes: ADR 0290's attribution re-reports owner requirements at the project's call, and no rule re-reports the other codes' findings from package source. The ruling: widen, and list them: the same package class, not a new blocker. `PACKAGE_SITE_CODES` (matrix.mjs) is every code with a template (30; the four of the first cells lead, and 25 of the 30 have a cell for the plain shape) and `package-cells.mjs` returns each unit's `rows`. Measured on 29b3f9312 (release, checker 7f5a78f05b36, producer 998ad2d92a37; every program through the Chrome host and the real oracle, 0 behaviour and 0 attribution differences) on the 21 codes beyond the first four that the cells reach, 2,528 programs in 35 units: mx-recall 1,904 (false-clean 1,156, recall-gap 284, unplaced 228, agree 108, not-fired 128), lg-recall 624 (clean 473, uncertifiable 151): no false positive, no wrong rule, no `not-legal`. rediscovery.json lists the 1,156 false-clean ids in 27 more entries (1,006 at an entry that is not an exact call, 150 at a handed exact call), `fixed: pending` until the Mac's ADR 0949 hand-off; they are expected-failing, listed and not hidden. Not done: no fixture (the shapes are generated; the Mac's fix adds its own, with published bytes where it claims a real package); the recall contexts beyond each code's base cells take the package variant by construction (the invariant test builds one per context) but are not crossed in the grids.

<a id="adr-0958"></a>
## ADR 0958 — A package finding leaves the report only by being handed to the project; the proof gate fails a drop with no attribution
ADR 0949 makes `package_drop::hand_off` the one place a finding located in a package leaves the report: it stands again as an uncertifiable one at each project site that may run its function (or once per package and rule at the project's first import, or under the named premise `package-style-not-project` for a style rule). The owner's ruling (2026-11-11): enforce it in one place, and test it by counting drops against attributions.

*The count.* With the proof log on, `hand_off` writes an `attributed` line beside each `DependencySource` `drop` line (the same file and span; its `to` lists the project sites the finding stands at, `proof_log::record_attributed`). The proof gate (`scripts/lib/proof-log.mjs`, check C2(c) `package-drop-unattributed`) fails a `DependencySource` drop whose `attributed` line is missing, lists no site, or is at another span: a package finding that left the report with no project row is the false clean ADR 0949 closes. A C2 hit is never accepted as a gap. A style rule's finding is dropped under its own named pass (`PackageStyleNotProject`) and needs none. The check runs over every fixture project in `make proof-gate` (the process test; `scripts/proof-gate.test.mjs` holds the unit tests): the gate saw 2,274 `DependencySource` drops across the fixtures and 4 `PackageStyleNotProject`, every drop attributed. The runtime assertion in `diagnostics.rs` (`every_drop_attributed`) and the unit tests of `package_drop` stay beside it.

*The lint half (2026-11-11).* The proof-lint kind `finding-filter` (`scripts/proof-lint.mjs`) counts a `retain`, `retain_mut` or `extract_if`, a `dedup*` of a collection named for findings, rows or sites (or comparing a location, id, rule or kind), and a rows-collection rebuilt from a `filter` of itself, in the non-test sources of the IR, the dialect and the rules crate and, for this kind only, the two backend files where findings leave the report (`diagnostics.rs`, `package_drop.rs`: `FINDING_FILTER_ONLY`). 69 hits in 39 files, each read; a stated reason covers a file's hits (`scripts/proof-lint.allowlist.json`), a new hit fails, a fallen count fails until `--update` lowers it, so the baseline only shrinks. What the 69 are: (1) **the seam and typed drops (10)**: `package_drop.rs` (the style drop `PackageStyleNotProject`, `supersede_claims`' `SupersededByViolation`), `diagnostics.rs` (`SiteUnreached`, ADR 0696), `projection.rs` (`RuntimeConfiguration`, `ExecutionPaths`, the unfollowed-dependency value, `OwnedByEnabledRule`), `attribution.rs` (`ClosedProgramExportDischarge`), `pipeline.rs` `gate_rows!` (`CompilerDiscardedRegion`), `effect_execution.rs` (`ApplyExecution::NotProven`, `SiteSilence::ApplyNeverRuns`): each a `DropPass` or `RowStage` written to the proof log with its premise; (2) **rows replaced by a named obligation or kept once (8)**: `reactive_analysis.rs` (a write or an async read whose declaration the producer withdrew becomes an obligation; a transported write replaces its own; an SC9012 `value-*` row yields to the uncaptured-source one at the span), `context_census.rs` (an exported context becomes the verdict Open), `resolver_dependencies.rs` (the walk bound stands as the site itself), `site_accounting.rs` (a premise with no span records no silence, so the site stays unaccounted), `pending_accounting.rs` (a reason removed: fewer ways to account a read); (3) **drops of rows a gate decides (3), no proof-log line yet**: `owner_graph.rs` `apply_settled_gates` (an out-of-band `onSettled` sheds its leaf operations: the owner fixed point's `OutOfBand` decision) and the cleanup requirement inside a leaf callback whose gate is Owned (SC3001 owns the claim, ADR 0942), `pipeline.rs` (the SC4001 effect requirement of a JSX site SC6002 proves throws on every run, ADR 0883); (4) **caches, memo tables, work lists and dedups of values (48)**: pruned to the current paths, compared by equal location, no finding held. One drop is named but its premise is not a proof: `effect_execution.rs` drops an SC6001 row (`directive_creations`) when the apply is not proven, an unknown apply included (`ApplyExecution::NotProven`); SC6001 is a partly silent rule (`partlySilentRules`), and whether an unknown apply should be an obligation is a review item, not changed here. Open (the rest of the lint): a lint cannot see an early return on a finding collection; the three unlogged drops of (3) should become `DropPass` stages so the proof gate's C-checks read them.

<a id="adr-0959"></a>
## ADR 0959 — A callback the slot climb cannot cross is an obligation of the rule that asked, never a silence; a climb ends with a typed stop
The slot climbs of the leaf-owner, action-step and observer-guard walks (ADR 0944) stopped at a callback that takes a parameter, is async or is a generator (`slot_layers.rs` `climb_through_slots`, cleanup.rs `through_slots`), and the callers read the stop as "not this rule's site" with no blocker: the silence census (research-silence, 2026-11-11) named it the critical hole, five Chrome false cleans on 29b3f9312 (`untrack((_?: number) => { flush(); })` in an action step and in a tracked queue callback, the `async` twins, `latest((_?) => flush())`) and four more shapes that ended only in an SC4001 uncertifiable of the wrong rule (`onCleanup` behind such a slot). W's ADR 0960 cells generalized them: 119 false cleans on 29b3f9312 (CLEANUP_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE 16, PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE 72, FLUSH_IN_ACTION 11, FLUSH_IN_TRACKED_QUEUE_CALLBACK 18, RESOLVE_IN_REACTIVE_SCOPE 1, UNTIL_IN_REACTIVE_SCOPE 1, every `pl-<layer>-<param|async>-<host>` cell).

*The coarse rule (coordinator, batch-10 addendum 7).* A callback with a parameter the primitive may never pass, or an async callback whose code before its first await runs in place, is a rare shape; the precise continuation of the climb (a parameter the dialect proves is never passed, the pre-await prefix) is a later proof target. Now the stop is a typed obligation of the asking rule at the exact site, never a violation, never silence: (1) **SC3001** (cleanup.rs): the call written under such a callback of a primitive that runs it in place (`slot_layer(..).run` is `InPlace`), before it reaches the leaf callback, is a `LeafOwnerOperationKind::UnmodelledSlot` row (rules wording: SC3001, `uncertain`, `possible`, blocker `leaf-owner-unproven`) when it may operate (a primitive the leaf owner refuses, or any call that is no primitive: a helper, a member; not a primitive the owner never refuses, not an accessor read, not a setter), so SC4001's `Delegate::LeafOwnerForbiddenCall` finds its row inside the call and the census's four wrong-rule shapes end in the right rule; (2) **SC2006**: `climb_through_slots` crosses the callback and sets `InPlaceClimb.unmodelled`, `action_step_body_at` returns it, and the step it reaches is an obligation (`function-run-unplaced`), not a violation; (3) **SC2004/SC2005**: `isPending` catches what its callback throws (ADR 0941), but an async callback's synchronous throw is the function's rejection, which it does not catch: `in_async_swallowing_slot` makes the call an obligation (`async-slot-unmodelled`).

*The typed stop (batch-10 addendum 3).* Each climb ends with a `ClimbStop` (`execution_role/slot_layers.rs`): `NoFunction`, `NotSlot`, `NoSlot`, `UnmodelledInPlace`, `UnmodelledElsewhere`, `JsxPlaced`, `RunsElsewhere`, `Declined`, `Bound`. Every stop is the dialect's or the compiler's proof that the code runs elsewhere than the walk's context, or an obligation of the rule that asked, and a caller decides each one with no wildcard: `climb_through_slots` returns `Option<SlotClimb { climb, stop }>`, `action_step_body_at` matches the stop exhaustively (`#[deny(clippy::wildcard_enum_match_arm)]`), the leaf walk's `through_slots` returns `Result<Crossed, ClimbStop>` and `ClimbStop::owes_obligation` (no wildcard) names the two stops that owe one (`UnmodelledInPlace`, `Bound`). The refactor moved no finding on any fixture (1,147 projects, 15,503 findings); two edges no fixture reaches changed: a leaf path of more than sixteen layers that never reaches the leaf callback is an obligation (it ended silent), and a slot call written in a JSX expression ends the obligation question at that call (the compiler places it).

*Measured.* Ten-code cells (`pl-<layer>-<form>-<host>` and W's ADR 0962 `ae-*`, `tw-*`: 2,529 programs; release, Chrome host and the real oracle, no cache, `--runtime-diff all`, 0 behaviour differences): 553 false cleans on 588affbf3 (agree 702, recall-gap 1,274; ADR 0960's 119 and ADR 0962's 434); 0 false clean, 0 false positive, 0 wrong rule on the final build (rel-17, HEAD 05e4e80ad, checker sha256 85a62f7cc595…: recall-gap 1,539, agree 990, every recall gap the claiming rule's own uncertifiable row). The first final run found one false clean of its own, `pl-ispending-async-action` (an `isPending(async …)` in an action step): the generator narrowing of `action_step_body_at` had stopped crossing the slot of an async callback `isPending` catches no synchronous throw of; fixed in 05e4e80ad and every cell rerun. Package cells (4,229): 0 false clean, 0 false positive, 0 wrong rule (recall-gap 2,400, agree 212, clean 342, uncertifiable 808, not-fired 160, unplaced 307, the same 307 ids as ADR 0949's run). Corpus (34 projects, release, 588affbf3 against the final build, standard and the browser development build): this ADR's rows (`leaf-owner-unproven`, `async-slot-unmodelled`, an SC2006 obligation at an unmodelled slot) add none; the slice as a whole adds 18 uncertifiable rows in each build, all ADR 0961's (below); V 209 and every project's status unchanged. The typed stop moved no fixture finding (1,147 projects, 15,503 findings).

*Amended (batch 11, slice 5's lead).* A callback NAMED at module scope reached a slot that takes what its callback throws (`isPending`) through another reader than the literal: `slot_callback_operations` (cleanup.rs) took the slot's swallowing for every callback and dropped the `Flush` it found, so `const cb = async () => { flush(); }; createTrackedEffect(() => { n(); isPending(cb); })` was certified clean for SC3001 while Chrome (rc.21) throws `[FLUSH_IN_TRACKED_QUEUE_CALLBACK]` at the flush: an async function's synchronous throw is the rejection of the promise it returns, which `isPending` does not catch (ADR 0960 measured the literal form). The helper path (`isPending(() => cb())`, the swallow at `push_crossed`) was the same. Decision: a slot that takes a throw takes only one a callback proves synchronous end to end. `LeafScopeResolution::entered_async` is set when a walk enters an `async` function; the walk of a callback (named, or the helper a literal calls) saves the flag, asks, and reads it: where it was entered the `Flush` is kept as `UnmodelledSlot`, SC3001's uncertifiable row (`leaf-owner-unproven`) at the slot call, as the literal arm answers, never a violation (the coarse rule above) and never silence; a synchronous callback keeps its silence, and a flush after an `await` is no part of the callback's stack. The flag is conservative: an async function entered anywhere under the slot's callback keeps the flush an obligation. `nm-shapes.mjs` (`nm-<form>-<host>`, 12 cells: `async`, `wrap-async`, and the twins `async-after`, `sync`, `wrap-sync`, `gen`, on `createTrackedEffect` and `onSettled`) measured it: main 3b22442d4, Chrome host and the real oracle: the 4 misuse programs false clean, the 8 twins 0 false positive; the fix: recall-gap 4, 0 false clean, the twins unchanged; fixture `flush-in-tracked-named-async`. The action host's twin (SC2006 and SC2008 rows) and the `resolve` twin (SC2004) were covered already, and the `pl-*` cells of ADR 0960 hold the literal forms.

<a id="adr-0960"></a>
## ADR 0960 — The primitive-layer axis takes a callback with an unused parameter and an async callback
The slot climbs of the leaf-owner, flush and cleanup walks (ADR 0944) stop at a callback that has a parameter or is async: `untrack((x) => flush())` and `untrack(async () => { flush(); })` in an action step or a tracked queue callback, `latest((x) => flush())`, and `onCleanup` behind such a slot were certified clean, and Chrome reports the code at the site as it does for the plain callback. A researcher confirmed five on main; no cell held them. `PL_CALLBACK_FORMS` (primitive-layer-shapes.mjs) gives every layer two more values, `<layer>-param` (`untrack((p0?: number) => …)`; `createRoot`'s callback already takes its dispose, so its form names that, `(p0: () => void) =>`; `model.mjs bareParam`, printed by print.mjs) and `<layer>-async` (the site runs before the callback's first await), so `PL_LAYERS` has 21 values and `pl-<layer>-<form>-<host>` 126 contexts, each crossed with the ten PL codes and every host. The table of what Chrome reports (`PL_MEASURED`) was re-measured by the probe (`primitive-layer-scan.mjs`: every pl-* context a cell of every PL code, both grids, 4,284 programs, real Chrome on all, 0 behaviour differences): the old layers' rows came out equal to the module's, and the table has the new rows (694 (code, host, layer) cells). What it shows: a parameter or an async callback changes nothing for `untrack`, `latest`, `owner`, `owner-null`, `root` and `flush`, except that `isPending(fn)` catches a synchronous `fn`'s throw and not an async one's (`ispending-param` is legal for FLUSH_IN_ACTION and the tracked queue's code, `ispending-async` is misuse).
Measured on 29b3f9312 (release, checker 7f5a78f05b36): the new cells of the ten codes, mx-recall 249 programs: 119 false-clean (CLEANUP_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE 16, PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE 72, FLUSH_IN_ACTION 11, FLUSH_IN_TRACKED_QUEUE_CALLBACK 18, RESOLVE_IN_REACTIVE_SCOPE 1, UNTIL_IN_REACTIVE_SCOPE 1), 130 agree or recall-gap (NO_OWNER_CLEANUP, UNTRACKED_READ_AFTER_AWAIT, FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK, PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK all agree: their slot climbs do not stop); lg-recall 531 programs, all clean or uncertifiable: no false positive, no wrong rule. The 119 are one cause (the climb), five of them the researcher's shapes. rediscovery.json lists them in six entries as expected-failing until the Mac's ADR 0959 (`fixed: pending`). Not done: a callback with a used parameter, a generator callback, and an async site past its first await (a different question: the promise flight).

<a id="adr-0961"></a>
## ADR 0961 — A function entered twice is walked twice; code no walk places is an obligation of the rule that asked: generators, parameter initializers
W's ADR 0962 cells measured two mechanisms on 29b3f9312, 434 false cleans (FLUSH_IN_ACTION 90, FLUSH_IN_TRACKED_QUEUE_CALLBACK 112, PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE 232), five of them first found by research-b11 (`b11-d1-visited-args`, `b11-d3-visited-swallow`, `b11-gen-project-wrapper`, `b11-628-dep-action`, `b11-step-gen-in-gen`). The generator and parameter-initializer extensions below were each a Chrome false clean found while probing the slice (research-fc553 F2, R1; the truncated-climb probe; research-outside-body groups B and D); the owner ruled each into batch 10 and no new ADR number.

*A function entered twice.* cleanup.rs kept one `visited` list per walk of a leaf call, keyed by the file and the span of a function body: the second entry answered "complete, nothing here" whatever it was entered with. `make(true); make(false)` skipped the second call (ADR 0280's argument standings are per call), and `isPending(g); g();` in a helper took the direct call for the one `isPending` already walked, whose flush it catches. The key is now the entry too (`Visited`, `entry_key`: the kind of entry, whether it is guaranteed, the argument standings the call proves, the class `this` is exact for), so a body entered a second time under another context is walked again, and a cycle still ends where a body is entered again the same way; a callback a slot runs is walked on a copy of the list. The rows are the walk's own: `runTwice()` and `pendingThenDirect()` are SC3001 violations (Chrome throws at the second entry).

*A generator no walk places (SC2006).* Every SC2006 path dropped a site whose owning function is a generator, so a generator handed to anything but `action` itself was silent: a generator literal handed to a project helper that makes the action, to a dependency's (source analyzed), `yield*` into another generator, a helper such a generator calls. `action_step_body_through` answers an obligation (`function-run-unplaced`, uncertifiable, never a violation) for a site whose owning generator is no action's own argument and may be stepped, when the project or a package analyzed as source names `action` (`in_generator_step`); a generator that is the action's own argument keeps the walk's proof (a violation). **R1:** `generator_may_be_stepped` counted a named generator's call only when it is an iterated operand; it now counts every call of it whose result a statement does not discard (`Array.from(g())`, `new Set(g())`, `g().next()`, `const it = g(); it.next()`, a loop over a stored iterator: Chrome throws FLUSH_IN_ACTION at each and the checker was clean). A call a statement discards runs none of the body.

*A generator stepped from an effect's apply (F2).* The effect-phase origin walk (SC2008, SC6002) followed no stepping: a generator a `for…of`, a spread, `.next()` or a `yield*` chain runs in an effect's apply was dropped, and Chrome fires FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK and PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK at its flush and creation. The walk now continues through a generator literal handed to a call and through a named generator whose exact project call stepping it sits in the apply, marking the origin `mixed`: the rows built on it answer `generator-step-unplaced` where the stepping is not followed, and a violation where the body is visible and stepped in place; `runs_unplaced_once` and `action_step_body_unplaced` ask `generator_may_be_stepped` of a generator owner instead of dropping it.

*A leaf generator (group B).* The leaf walk (SC3001) took a call of a generator for complete with nothing in it: a generator the leaf callback steps (`.next()`, `for…of`, a spread, `Array.from`, a stored loop, `yield*` through another generator, an async generator's `.next()`) was clean while Chrome throws PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE at a creation in its body. A call of a generator whose result is not discarded and whose body holds a call is a `GeneratorStepped` operation (`note_generator_stepped`, every resolved-function arm): an uncertifiable SC3001 row, blocker `generator-stepped` (SC1002's name for the same shape, ADR 0820), at the call and at each place that steps the iterator (V8's frame for the creation is the stepper's).

*A parameter initializer (PD).* Code written in a parameter's default, a destructuring default, an IIFE or a closure in one, a helper the default calls, or a constructor's default runs when a call of the function omits the argument. Every net asked about code in a function body (`containing_body_function`, `body.contains`), a site in a parameter list has no owning body, and the climb's `in_list` read "runs only when its call omits the argument" as a never-run: Chrome fires FLUSH_IN_ACTION at `function h(x = flush())` an action's step calls (and at an arrow, an IIFE, a closure and a helper in the default), and the effect phase's codes in an apply, while the checker was clean. The shared net (`unplaced_blocker`: SC2006, SC2008, SC6002) answers `parameter-initializer-unplaced` (blockers.rs) for a site in a parameter initializer, or in a layer that runs in place in one (the climb's recorded layer calls), of a function called or referenced anywhere: coarse, never V, never silent. Not an obligation: the default of an action's own step generator (evaluated when the action creates the generator, before the first step, ADR 0454) and a function nothing calls or names. In the callers: SC2008's unplaced branch takes the function that owns the call, its parameter list included; SC6002's role veto (`runs_off_every_apply`) cannot clear a default (the role reads no initializer's placement); the ADR 0710 ownerless exemption asks the function that owns the site, not the component that merely declares the helper. The read-placement net (`unplaced_step`, shared with SC1001's summaries) is untouched. **After an await (group D):** the SC1002 walk read an accessor call written directly in a default only; a call in the async computation's own body after its await that runs an initializer holding a call or a member access of the function it calls (or the same-file class it constructs) is an uncertifiable row (`parameter-initializer-unplaced`, or the blocker of the member the walk started from when that is a handed async function) at the call expression's start, argument-aware in one direction only (an argument keeps the default from running only when syntax proves it defined: `null`, a function expression, a primitive, object or array literal, the standard of `argument_values.rs`, where a type says nothing about runtime nullishness; no argument, `undefined`, a spread, an identifier and any other expression run it, so `h(maybe)` and `h(c ? 1 : undefined)` are rows; a destructuring pattern's defaults run whatever the argument is). A `new` call's SC3001 and SC9012 rows stand at its `new` token, where V8 and the judge place a `new` frame. The leaf hosts are covered by SC9012 withholding rows at the helper's call (`leaf-callback-opaque`, `value-as-parameter-default`), as before; the judge classes them `withholding`, not `claiming`, and SC3001 has no row of its own there.

*Measured.* Cells (release, Chrome host and the real oracle, no cache, `--runtime-diff all`; 588affbf3 against the final build rel-17, HEAD 05e4e80ad, checker sha256 85a62f7cc595…). The generator axis (`ge-<step>-<host>`: for…of, spread, `.next()`, `yield*`, `Array.from`, `new Set`, a stored iterator's loop, an async generator's `.next()`, stepped in an action's step, an effect's apply or compute, a memo, a tracked effect or an `onSettled`): 3,588 false cleans on 588affbf3 in the 5,724 programs the grid held then (F2 and R1: FLUSH_IN_ACTION 240, FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK 180, PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK 540; group B: FLUSH_IN_TRACKED_QUEUE_CALLBACK 522, PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE 1,584, CLEANUP_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE 522); 0 false clean, 0 false positive, 0 wrong rule in the final grid's 6,102 programs (recall-gap 4,582, agree 1,520; the grid gained the async generator's `.next()` since). The parameter-initializer axis (`pd-<form>-<host>`: value, IIFE, closure, helper, destructured, constructor, in an action's step, an effect's apply, a tracked effect, an `onSettled` and an async memo after its await; 2,952 programs): 1,225 false cleans → 0/0/0 (recall-gap 2,924, agree 28). The legal twins (`ge-idle-*`, `pd-idle-*`, `pd-passed-*`: 504 and 924 programs): 0 false positive, 0 wrong rule (uncertifiable 1,146, clean 282). The leaf-host `pd-*` cells are covered by the SC9012 withholding row at the helper's call, which the judge classes `withholding`, not `claiming`: no SC3001 row of its own stands there (open-work). Decorators and `accessor` fields are untested: the pinned toolchain leaves the syntax in place and the pinned Chrome rejects it. Corpus (34 projects, release, standard and the browser development build, 588affbf3 against the final build): +18 uncertifiable rows in each (124,473 → 124,491; 141,326 → 141,344), every other row unchanged, V 209, no project's status moved, wall time over the 34 projects 170.8 s → 166.8 s and 272.5 s → 269.1 s (one run each: noise). The 18: 17 SC1002 rows at calls after an await (app-game 8: `prepareCoverageTables.ts` 3, `prepareCurveDocument.ts` 4, `paintRuntime.ts` 1; dashiboard-ui 4: `GroupsEditor.test.tsx` 2, `processing.test.tsx`, `persist.test.ts`; solid-groove 5: `EditorView.test.tsx`), each a call that omits an argument whose default holds a call or a member access, a standard-library call or a property of an earlier parameter among them (`uploadBuffer(gpu, buffer, source)` against `sourceOffset = 0, byteLength = source.byteLength - sourceOffset`; `scheduleIdle()` against `previousTime = performance.now()`), in the walk of an async function handed to a call the walk does not follow (ADR 0873's handed compute), whose blocker `function-run-unplaced` every uncertain row of that walk takes while the message states the initializer's reason; and one SC3001 `generator-stepped` row (`solid-nest/src/createAnimations.ts:52`, `animate(prevTree, nextTree)`, a generator a tracked effect creates and stores). R2, a flush in any generator becoming an SC2006 obligation once `action` is named: 0 rows, no flush site stands in a generator body in either build (the SC2006 rows are all in function bodies or at module level). The 69 (standard) and 84 (development) SC9012 `leaf-callback-opaque` rows at `new` calls in leaf callbacks moved four bytes, to the `new` token (same file, line and context): nothing else moved. Fixtures: the new and extended fixtures (generator-stepped-in-apply, flush-in-action-generator, leaf-generator-stepped, parameter-initializer-unplaced, parameter-initializer-after-await, package-action-step, package-action-step-plain, leaf-visited-entry(-namespace), leaf-unmodelled-slot, flush-in-action-unmodelled-slot, rc21-latest-ispending-observer-guard) hold each positive, negative and variant, judged by Chrome (every diagnostic covered, the negatives silent; a stackless warning in a helper is judged by the cells).

*The argument (batch-10 addendum 13).* The first version of the after-await net took any argument that is not the literal `undefined` as one that keeps the default from running. Review found the hole after the final run and Chrome (rc.21) confirmed it: `h(maybe)` with `const maybe: number | undefined = Math.random() > 2 ? 1 : undefined`, and `h(c ? 1 : undefined)`, run the default after the await and warn UNTRACKED_READ_AFTER_AWAIT while 588affbf3 and 05e4e80ad were silent (the older walk read an argument the same way). The owner ruled it into batch 10. `proves_defined` (after_await.rs) is the standard above; the other hosts' nets (SC2006, SC2008, SC6002, the leaf scopes) are not argument-aware and need no change: a default of a function the program calls or references is an obligation whatever the call passes. Measured (release, 4f752fd9d, rel-18 checker sha256 f9ce9b5ff27a…): the argument axis `pd-<form>-<host>-undef|cond` (value, iife, closure, helper and ctor forms in the five hosts, 4,920 programs of the seven PD codes) is 2,080 false cleans on 588affbf3 (no PD net there), 336 on 05e4e80ad (all UNTRACKED_READ_AFTER_AWAIT) and 0 on rel-18; the whole pd grid on rel-18, 7,872 programs, Chrome host and the real oracle, `--runtime-diff all`, no cache: recall-gap 7,788, agree 84, 0 false clean, 0 false positive, 0 wrong rule, 0 behaviour, attribution or inconclusive differences; the legal twins, 924 programs: uncertifiable 796, clean 128. Corpus (rel-17 against rel-18, standard and the browser development build): +5 SC1002 rows in each (124,491 → 124,496; 141,344 → 141,349: app-game `prepareDocument.ts:51` and `paintRuntime.ts:235`, `319`, `636`; dashiboard-ui `persist.test.ts:34`), V 209, no status change; against 588affbf3 the slice is +23 uncertifiable rows (22 SC1002, 1 SC3001) in each. make coverage: only the extended fixture moved (+3 rows). The cost is the rows of calls that pass an identifier or an expression to a default-bearing parameter after an await; a type-proved definedness would remove most (open-work).

*Not done (docs/open-work.md).* The exact stepper (the helper's parameter flowing to `action`, the iterator's stepping position) and the exact omitted-argument proof for the effect and action hosts; a class constructed from another file for SC1002; SC3001 rows of its own for a parameter initializer a leaf callback's call runs. Decorators and `accessor` fields are untested: the pinned toolchain leaves the syntax in place and the pinned Chrome rejects it. Groups A (members: getters, setters, constructors, coercions, protocols, proxies, tags, class fields) and C (exported helpers called in an apply) of research-outside-body are batch 10b.

*The after-await walk's `visited` (batch 11, slice 9A).* The SC1002 walk (`after_await.rs`) kept one set of the calls and tagged templates it had entered, keyed by span alone, and a span is an offset within one file: a call in a second project file at the same offsets, with the same length, as one the walk had entered in the first was skipped, and the read it reaches lost its proof. The same program with the call one byte away is a violation. The set is keyed by file and span (`Walk::visited`, as `queued` and `placed` already were). Never silent on any shape probed (12 programs, memo and effect hosts: the colliding call a project helper, an immediately invoked arrow or function expression, a tagged template, `forEach` and `map` callbacks, a `.then` continuation), so there is no false clean for a rediscovery cell to fail on: the hand-off nets (`handed-read-after-await`, `function-run-unplaced`) rowed each, uncertifiable, and the change is a proof recovered (`.then` stays uncertifiable). The real oracle (Chrome rc.21, `fixtures/runtime-oracle/after-await-cross-file-same-span`) fires UNTRACKED_READ_AFTER_AWAIT at the four reads: `covered-uncertifiable` on the old build, `covered-violation` now, and the silent twin is quiet. The generator prints no two files' calls at equal offsets (the equality is forced by comment padding), so the pin is `fixtures/reactive-ir/after-await-cross-file-same-span`, the oracle case and `scripts/fixture-same-span.test.mjs`; an `aw-xfile-*` axis is open work (docs/open-work.md).

<a id="adr-0962"></a>
## ADR 0962 — The entry shapes: an action's step body reached indirectly, and a function entered twice under different contexts
A researcher confirmed five Chrome false cleans on main 29b3f9312 that no cell held. (1) SC2006, FLUSH_IN_ACTION: the action's step body is a generator reached indirectly, a generator literal handed to a project helper that makes the action (`const run = (g) => action(g); run(function* () { flush(); })`), the same through a dependency whose source the checker reads, and `yield* inner()` with `flush()` inside `inner`. (2) SC3001: a function entered twice under different contexts, the second entry the forbidden one: `make(true); make(false)` (PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE) and `isPending(cb); cb();` inside a tracked queue callback (FLUSH_IN_TRACKED_QUEUE_CALLBACK). `entry-shapes.mjs` makes them recall cells (base shapes only, `BASE_ONLY_CONTEXTS`; not crossed with other codes by the census, `CENSUS_SKIPS`, as the `pl-*` ones), each with legal twins. FLUSH_IN_ACTION: `ae-helper`, `ae-helper-named` (the generator handed by name), `ae-package` (a generated dependency the context ships, `fz-act`: `export function runAction(cb) { return action(cb); }`; print.mjs `programPackages` takes a component's `packages`, `prim action`'s `via` names the callee) and `ae-delegate`; their twins `ae-*-plain` differ only in what makes the generator an action's step (the helper or the package returns it and a handler steps it; a plain generator delegates and a handler steps it). PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE and FLUSH_IN_TRACKED_QUEUE_CALLBACK: `tw-<layer>-<spelling>-<order>-<host>[-<place>]`, the site the body of `g`, entered once under the code's tolerant layer (`owner-null`, `ispending`: primitive-layer-shapes.mjs `PL_MEASURED`) and once directly; spelling `call` (`isPending(g); g();`) or `flag` (a helper `make(p0)` picks the entry, `make(true); make(false)`); the tolerant entry `first` or `last`; host `tracked` or `settled`; place none (the entries in the host's own text), `in` (the body of a helper `both` declared in the host's callback) or `out` (declared in the component's body); the twin `both` enters it twice under the layer. The `owner-null` cells take no `effect` variant (no owner: NO_OWNER_EFFECT). The API-fire plan (`api-fire-plan.mjs`; once one test, `api-fire.test.mjs`, now two halves by the owner's ruling, scripts/fuzz/README.md) leaves the contexts out, as the arm and seam families (their twins are the Chrome host's to judge): the plan stays 1,883 programs (they would add 80); `entry-shapes.test.mjs` fires one program of each of the 80 cells on the fast runtime and pins the printed shapes.
Measured on 29b3f9312 (release, checker 7f5a78f05b36), every program through real Chrome (`--runtime-diff all`, no cache): the family's 3,480 programs, mx-recall 2,280 and lg-recall 1,200, 0 behaviour, 0 attribution and 0 inconclusive differences between the fast host and Chrome. mx-recall: agree 576, recall-gap 1,270, false-clean 434; lg-recall: clean 504, uncertifiable 696: no false positive, no wrong rule, no `not-fired`. The 434 are all the researcher's: FLUSH_IN_ACTION 90 (`ae-helper` 30, `ae-package` 30, `ae-delegate` 30 of 30 programs each); FLUSH_IN_TRACKED_QUEUE_CALLBACK 112 and PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE 232, exactly the cells with the tolerant entry first in a helper (`-in`, `-out`; both spellings, both hosts: 14 of 30 and 29 of 60 programs in each of eight cells). The controls hold: the generator handed by name (`ae-helper-named`) is declined (30 recall-gap), the entries in the host's own text and the forbidden entry first in a helper are read (agree or recall-gap), and the `both` twins and `ae-*-plain` are clean or uncertifiable. The pattern, a function entered a second time only inside a helper's walk and only after a tolerant entry, is what cleanup.rs's per-walk `visited` set (`function_forbidden_operations`) would give; the fix is the Mac's ADR 0961. `make fuzz-focus` on these cells exits 1 on main (434 false cleans), and rediscovery.json lists them in three entries, one per code, `fixed: pending` until that fix: expected-failing, listed and not hidden. A cover smoke with them needs a look at the 434 more, and nothing else moved (census, base, source, combination and pair grids as before).
Not done: no fixture (the shapes are generated; the Mac's fix adds its own); the action family is placed for FLUSH_IN_ACTION only (an owner operation in an action's indirect step body is another code's cell); the twice-entered family for the two codes the researcher named (SC3001's cleanup code has no tolerant layer in a leaf host, PL_MEASURED), with a boolean flag as the only argument and `g` declared in the host's callback (`g` declared in the component's body with the entries in the host's own text was measured once on the same build, 720 programs, agree or recall-gap, and not kept as cells); a first entry other than the tolerant layers (a handler or a timer for a flush, the body or a root for a primitive) is not crossed.


<a id="adr-0963"></a>
## ADR 0963 — The object-boundary shapes: a site run by a construct the host triggers, and an exported helper an apply calls
A researcher confirmed 64 Chrome false cleans on main 9e5a45080 that no cell held: SC1002 (a read after an `await`) and SC3001 (a leaf `onSettled`) silent when the site is run by something that is not spelled as a call, and SC6002 and SC2008 silent for an exported helper an effect's apply calls. `ob-shapes.mjs` makes them recall cells (base shapes, `BASE_ONLY_CONTEXTS`; not crossed with other codes by the census, `CENSUS_SKIPS`; left out of api-fire's plan), each with a twin `-never` that keeps the construct and never triggers it (the member never read, the class never constructed, the iterator never stepped, the coercion not made, the helper uncalled). Family A, `ob-<construct>-<host>`: 29 constructs on SC1002's `memo-async` and `effect-async` hosts (the construct is triggered after the compute's `await`) and on SC3001's `settled` and `tracked` leaves (its three codes): an object's `get` and `set`; a coercion (`toString`, `valueOf`, `[Symbol.toPrimitive]`, `toJSON`); a user `[Symbol.iterator]()` or the `next()` it returns, consumed by `for…of`, spread or `Array.from`; a Proxy trap (`get`, `set`, `has`, `deleteProperty`, `ownKeys`, `apply`, `construct`); a constructor (`new C`, a subclass's `super()` and implicit super, `super.m()`, a function only ever called with `new`); a class field's initializer (the site itself, an IIFE, a helper's call, the superclass's field); a tagged template. Family C, `ob-<form>-apply`: an exported helper, `export function` or `export const`, called in an effect's apply for SC6002 and SC2008: in the file, through a self-import under an alias, imported by the name of an `export { x as y }` specifier, through a second module that re-exports it (`lib.ts`); `local`, not exported, is the control. The construct is raw text around the program's marked statements, a `frame` container (model.mjs `FRAME_CONTAINER_KINDS`, printed by print.mjs; the body is a function body of its own, or one expression for a field initializer); a cell takes a few base shapes (`obShape`: the direct binding bare, in an `iife` or a `helper`, and `alias`; family C the direct binding, bare or `iife`): 250 contexts, 2,928 programs. Decorators and `accessor` fields are not generated: the pinned toolchain does not take them.
Measured on 29b3f9312 (release, checker 7f5a78f05b36), every program through the Chrome host and the real oracle (`--runtime-diff all`, no cache): 2,928 programs, 0 behaviour, 0 attribution and 0 inconclusive differences between the fast host and Chrome. mx-recall 1,464: false-clean 814, agree 92, recall-gap 528, misplaced 30, no wrong rule, no `not-fired`; lg-recall 1,464: clean 324, uncertifiable 1,102, unexecuted 38, no false positive. The 814: SC3001 730 (CLEANUP_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE 146, PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE 438, FLUSH_IN_TRACKED_QUEUE_CALLBACK 146; 365 on each leaf: every program of the accessors, `toString`, `valueOf`, `[Symbol.toPrimitive]`, `for…of` and spread over a user iterator or its `next()`, the Proxy `get`, `set`, `has`, `deleteProperty`, `ownKeys` and `construct` traps, and `new C` with `super()` or implicit super; `new` of a function in its helper shape); SC1002 28 (14 on each host: `[Symbol.toPrimitive]`, `for…of`, spread and `Array.from` over a user iterator in three of four shapes, an accessor with the read in an IIFE); SC6002 42 and SC2008 14 (the exported helper in seven of eight forms). The rest are read (agree: SC1002's plain accessors, constructors and field helper, every tagged template but ten on the leaves, the `export { x as y }` function and `local`) or declined (recall-gap, a fail-closed U: `toJSON`, `Array.from` over a user iterator or its `next()`, the Proxy `apply` trap, `super.m()`, the field initializers on the leaves). The 30 `misplaced` are `new` of a function on the leaf hosts: the checker claims `leaf-owner-forbidden-call` at the callee inside `new (F1 as …)()` (8:10 in the direct shape) and Chrome's frame is the site (6:7); not attributed (the checker's anchor for a `new` expression, or the judge's recorded call position for one). The 38 twin violations (owner ruling 2026-11-11: a violation means wrong if it runs, ADR 0458 `unexecuted`, ungated): SC1002's accessor, `new C`, `super()`, implicit-super and field-helper twins carry a `reactive-read-after-await` violation at a site that never runs; each has a live partner Chrome ran and confirmed, and none fires another code. `make fuzz-focus` on these cells exits 1 on main (814 false cleans, 30 misplaced), and rediscovery.json lists them in nine entries (six codes' false cleans, three codes' misplaced), `fixed: pending: batch 10b`: expected-failing, listed and not hidden.
Not done: the helper of family C stays in App.tsx (a site is judged where the printer marks it, in one file), so a helper that lives in a second file is reached through a self-import and a re-export, not through a module that holds its body; family A's constructs on the apply host and family C's forms on the other hosts; a construct defined before the `await` and triggered after it; class accessors (object literals only), static initializers and blocks, `Reflect` and `Object.defineProperty` accessors; decorators and `accessor` fields (the pinned toolchain).

*Decision (batch 10b, 6cc48e006).* The walks that follow calls get a net for the runs that no call shows, and an exported helper an apply calls is an obligation. (1) SC1002 (`after_await.rs` `reads_run_unplaced`): when the program holds a tracked async computation (the prepass's starts) and a suspension (`program_suspends`: an `await`, an implicit suspension, a `then`/`catch`/`finally` call, named as a premise only), a reactive read the role engine could not place (Unknown or Unreported) whose code a member route runs is an uncertifiable row (`function-run-unplaced`, `field-initializer-unplaced` directly in a class field's initializer, `search-depth-limit`); new `AfterAwaitObligation::RunUnplaced`. (2) SC3001 (`static_api.rs`): a call a leaf owner forbids (`cleanup::leaf_forbids`) written where a member route runs it, in a program that names a primitive opening a leaf scope (`project_opens_leaf_scope`), is the rule's own row through the static-violation channel, and adds none where the leaf walk's own operation stands (`LeafRowsAccount`: an operation reached through a helper is reported at the helper call, a frame of the stack it throws on; an opaque call's SC9012 row answers for no site inside its arguments: `new Proxy(target, { get() { onCleanup(f); } })` runs the trap where the proxy is read, a frame the row's line is not, and a first cut that let it answer reintroduced 200 false cleans in W's `ob-proxy-*` cells). (3) The route: `execution_role::runs_implicitly` (a class element, an object literal's accessor or method, a tag function, a function a `new` runs) and `Routes::{Every, Members}` on ADR 0708's unplaced walk, so the net of a rule whose sites are everywhere does not ask for every hand-off: `unplaced_member_blocker` climbs the exact calls above the site to depth 4 and, if the bound stopped it, asks again without the bound (`runs_unplaced_exhaustive_members`, each function once, a climb the layer bound stopped continues from where it stopped). A function handed where nothing follows it is run by a call, which the walks answer themselves (the leaf walk's SC9012 withholding row), and a parameter initializer is ADR 0961's call-site net (the legal twin `h(1)` for `function h(x = count())` stays quiet). (4) Group C (`effect_phase.rs` `named_layer`): an exported function keeps the walk and its origin is `mixed` (ADR 0516): the creation or flush in it is an uncertifiable row (SC6002 `effect-owner-unproven`, SC2008 `handler-dispatch-unproven`), the unexported twin stays a violation; the claim that the code only ever runs in an apply is not made for an export, the calls the walk sees are proven runs. A call the call graph withheld (ADR 0339: a `const` arrow is not hoisted, and a call through an import cycle, a self-import included, may run before the binding's declarator) is followed too, as a run the walk does not prove (`mixed`): the walk took it for no call, and the helper was certified clean for want of an edge. `runtime-oracle/flush-in-effect-callback` loses its `open` entry (the exported helper's flush: the checker has a row at the site, the verdict is covered-uncertifiable). Not a violation anywhere: nothing here proves the member is reached from the continuation, a leaf callback or an apply.

*Measured (batch 10b).* Chrome (rc.21, the real oracle): the 93 outside-body cases of the research on the batch-10 head: 53 unproven-clean (44 SC1002/SC3001 members, 9 exported helpers; the PD and leaf-generator cases had closed in batch 10) and on the fix 1 (`async-iife-L`, an attribution question: the checker's violation is at the async IIFE, Chrome's frame one line below). W's cells (release, `ecells.mjs --prefix ob-`): the batch-10 head (fast host) 644 false cleans (SC1002 28, SC3001 560, SC6002 42, SC2008 14), 0 misplaced (the `new` anchor moved to the `new` token in ADR 0961); the fix (Chrome host, `--runtime-diff all`, mx-recall, 2,928 programs): 0 false clean, 0 misplaced, 0 wrong rule, 0 behaviour, attribution and inconclusive differences, `make fuzz-focus` exits 0 on the code's ob cells; the twins (lg-recall, 2,928 programs, the Chrome host): 0 false positive, 0 not-legal, 0 disturbed, 0 behaviour differences (38 `unexecuted` violations at sites that never run, ADR 0458, as before). The first build of the fix left 12 false cleans, the `const` arrow forms of family C reached through a self-import (`ob-exp-const-{import,alias,reexport}-apply`, the generator's stand-in for a helper in a second file): the call graph withholds that call (ADR 0339) and the walk took a withheld call for none, so the helper was certified clean (a `function` has its edge: a violation); following it as `mixed` closed them, and the fixture `exported-helper-in-apply` holds the pair. Corpus (34 projects, release, the batch-10 head against the fix): standard U 119,001 -> 119,423 (+422: SC1002 `function-run-unplaced` +334, SC3001 +88), V 218 -> 218, no status moved; development build with package sources U 138,854 -> 139,766 (+912: the same plus package findings at project entries, SC3001 +194, SC6002 +156, SC1002 +140), V 212 -> 212. Wall time over the 34, back to back: 257,298 ms -> 251,504 ms (rel-22). Snapshots: 61 uncertifiable rows added over 21 existing snapshots, none moved or removed; five new fixtures (`member-routes-after-await`, `member-routes-leaf` and their `-none` twins, `exported-helper-in-apply`), each judged by Chrome. A first cut that asked every Unknown-role read cost +4,876 SC1002 rows in the corpus (event handlers handed to components): the member route is the narrowing.

*Open.* A function stored where nothing follows it and invoked through an unresolved call after the `await` (`callbacks.forEach((c) => c())`) was a Chrome false clean on main and on this branch (probe `handed-callback-R`: SC1001's row only); the broad net covered it at +4,876 U, and ADR 0964 closes it with an obligation at the unresolved invocation (a call-centric row). The net asks reads the role engine could not place: a member read with another role is not asked. Coarse: a read in a member is a row whatever the continuation touches and whatever the computation tracked before its first suspension (ADR 0195). Decorators and `accessor` fields stay untested (the pinned toolchain).


*Amended (batch 10c, B2).* The Fable review of batch 10b found 12 Chrome false cleans (9 SC1002, 3 SC3001) in this ADR's own shapes, written as a function VALUE instead of a method or an accessor: `{ toString: () => … }`, `{ valueOf: function () … }`, an arrow Proxy trap, `{ [Symbol.iterator]: function* … }`, `{ then: (res) => … }`, `{ toString: show }`, `o.toString = () => …`, `K.prototype.toString = function …`, a `defineProperty` getter; `runs_implicitly` took a property only when it was a method or an accessor (`!property.data`), and the cells (`ob-shapes.mjs`) wrote every member as a method. Decision: the key decides, not the spelling. `stored_in_host_called_slot` (`execution_role/site_reach.rs`, read by `runs_implicitly`, so by SC1002's and SC3001's nets alike): a function stored under a key the host reads by itself runs without a call however it is written (a function literal, a name that is its binding, an assigned function): the keys of a coercion (`toString`, `toLocaleString`, `valueOf`, `toJSON`), of the promise protocol (`then`), of the iterator protocol (`next`, `return`, `throw`), `handleEvent`, a computed key no literal decides (`[Symbol.iterator]`), the trap names of a `new Proxy` handler, and the `get` or `set` of a descriptor handed to `Object.defineProperty`, `Reflect.defineProperty`, `Object.defineProperties` or `Object.create`. A function stored under any other key runs by a call of its slot, which ADR 0964's call-site row answers; the review's broad form (every function stored in a property) is the cut that cost +4,876. Same rows, same blockers (`function-run-unplaced`), never a violation. `rv-shapes.mjs` adds the route written in the host's callback (`rm-*`, 14 forms on the two async hosts, 13 on the two leaf hosts) and at module scope (`rh-*`, the review's own spelling: ADR 0565's hand-off already answers a function written in the member's own body), each with its `-never` twin. *Measured, Chrome host and the real oracle (`--runtime-diff all`):* main ec68d519d: the SC3001 codes 120 false cleans in the callback and 120 at module scope (of 130 each; 10 recall-gap), SC1002 28 at module scope (28 recall-gap in the callback); the fix: recall-gap on all 158 + 158 programs, 0 false clean, the 158 + 158 twins 0 false positive (78 of the SC3001 twins, 39 in the callback and 39 at module scope, were certified clean on main and are uncertifiable now: nothing proves the route is never triggered). Cost with ADR 0964's, below. Not done, in `docs/open-work.md`: a handler object assigned to a name and handed to `new Proxy` later (`handler.get = fn`), a function named across files (`{ toString: imported }`: the binding is read in its own file), a computed key built from a value.

<a id="adr-0964"></a>
## ADR 0964 — The stored-function shapes: a call to a function the walk cannot resolve, after the `await`, is an obligation
A function the program stored where nothing follows it (an array, a map, an object's list, a variable) and invoked through the name it was stored under, after an async computation's `await`, runs on the continuation. The call resolves to no function the walks know, so a source read in the stored function is untracked and Chrome (rc.21) warns `UNTRACKED_READ_AFTER_AWAIT` at it while the program was certified clean for SC1002, beside only SC1001's uncertifiable row at the read (probe `handed-callback-R` and six more, batch 10b; ADR 0963 listed it as open). `sf-shapes.mjs` makes them recall cells (one base shape, the direct binding; `BASE_ONLY_CONTEXTS`; skipped by the census; out of api-fire's plan), `sf-<form>-<host>`, with two legal twins, `-never` (the names are used and nothing calls) and `-before` (invoked before the `await`: a dependency of the computation). Forms: `foreach` (`list.forEach((c) => c())`), `index` (`list[0]()`), `const` (`const f = list[0]; f()`), `mapget` (`registry.get("k")!()`), `bus` (`bus.emit()` over the listeners the program pushed, an object literal's method iterating `this.listeners`), `promise` (`await Promise.all(tasks.map((t) => t()))`), `param` (`run(stored)`, a project helper that calls its parameter: the control the walk already proved); hosts `memo-async` and `effect-async`: 42 contexts.

*Decision.* An invocation the walk cannot resolve is a proof obligation of SC1002 where the continuation reaches it, never a violation (nothing here proves what was stored or that it reads a source) and never silence. (1) `after_await.rs` `unresolved_invocation`: the call is no construct, no primitive, no accessor, setter or action, and its callee is an identifier declared as a parameter or as a variable, or the result of a call that is no primitive (`get(k)!()`, `f()()`); a `function` declaration and a `const` bound to a function literal (`same_file_function`: that function, or a throw before its declarator) are not stored values. It reads the call's own syntax and bindings: a resolved-call fact is demanded for few calls, so its absence says nothing (a first predicate on it cost +5,602 rows). (2) The row is `reactive-read-after-await` uncertifiable at the callee, blocker `unresolved-invocation` (`AfterAwaitObligation::UnresolvedInvocation`), asked where the walk has the call: in `report_call_read` after the hand-off (ADR 0669) answered nothing, and for the calls the member's own body writes behind a suspension. (3) The route a method takes: a class method or an object's method the continuation calls holds the invocation inside it, and no walk of the continuation reaches inside, so `reads_run_unplaced` asks the same predicate of every call in code a member route runs (`unplaced_member_blocker`), under ADR 0963's region premise (a tracked async computation and a suspension). A handed async function's rows carry `function-run-unplaced`, as its reads' do. The `param` form stays a violation where the walk follows the argument (ADR 0669); the row at the parameter's call stands beside it. A callee that is a typed candidate (ADR 0294: a parameter typed `Accessor<T>` whose implementation is not visible) keeps its SC9012 `type-only-source` row and gets none of this (the migration matrix pins `conditional-await-accessor.tsx`'s SC1002 silent, and the type says what the callee may be, which a stored function's does not).

*Measured.* Chrome (the real oracle, `--runtime-diff all`, no cache), release: the sf cells on the batch-10 head (647b0ccce, fast host): 10 false cleans of 14 (`foreach`, `const`, `mapget`, `bus`, `promise` on both hosts; `index` is SC9012's computed-call-target row already, `param` a violation), `make fuzz-focus` exits 1; on the fix: 0 false clean (recall-gap 12: a fail-closed U where Chrome fires; agree 2), and the 28 twins 0 false positive (SC1001's row at the stored function stands in all, as before). The 14 probes (`handed-callback-R`, the six more, a closed literal bus without `this`, a function-valued bus, an object method and a class method): 11 covered uncertifiable, 2 covered violation (the `param` and object-method controls), the event-handler control quiet. The fixture `stored-function-after-await` holds eight shapes beside the quiet `Before`, `Handler` and `Resolved`: every Chrome diagnostic covered, the twin `stored-function-after-await-none` quiet. Corpus (34 projects, release, ADR 0963's build against this one): standard U 119,423 -> 120,416 (+993: SC1002 `unresolved-invocation` +465 and `function-run-unplaced` +533 on handed async functions, 40% of all in test files, mostly test callbacks handed to `it`; one SC9012 `handed-member-read` more, 6 rows less where the claiming row stands at the same span), V 218 -> 218, no status moved; development build with package sources U 139,766 -> 140,823 (+1,057: the same plus 70 package findings at project entries), V 212 -> 212. By what the called name is (a rough count over the added rows): a parameter 40%, a variable destructured from a call (`const { getByText } = render(...)`, `await setUp()`) 31%, another variable 23%, a call's result 3%. The broad net that asks every Unknown-role read costs +4,876. Snapshots: 28 rows of this ADR over existing fixtures (21 `unresolved-invocation`, 7 `package-finding-at-entry`), and the rows of the two fixtures whose construct-only function `Legacy` moves from `member-routes-after-await` to `member-routes-leaf`; no other row moved; the sf programs and the fixtures are in `scripts/fuzz/sf-shapes.test.mjs` and `fixtures/reactive-ir/stored-function-after-await`.

*Open.* The cost is every unresolved invocation behind an `await` in a tracked or handed async function, whatever it holds: a clock or `random` parameter, a testing library's destructured query, a DOM function taken from `window`. Two lines would drop most of it and need a fact this slice does not demand: a resolved declaration for an identifier callee (a standard-library or package function is exact: `getComputedStyle`), and the argument a closed set of callers hands a parameter (a literal arrow the walk already places, as the row beside the `param` violation shows). The `dispose` a `createRoot` callback is handed is a row too (one fixture row, three in the corpus): exempting it is a match on a primitive's name, which proof-lint counts, and wants a dialect fact. Hosts other than SC1002's two async computes, and the same gap for SC3001 or SC6002, were not measured.

*Amended (batch 10c, B1).* The review found 8 Chrome false cleans in this ADR's own shape: a stored function invoked through a form the predicate did not match: `bus.cb()` and `holder.cb?.()` over a member the program assigned (a member callee that types resolve to a property is no `inexact_callee`, and `unresolved_invocation` took only an identifier or a call's result), `fn.call(undefined)` and `cbs[0].apply(undefined, [])` (the callee is the member `call`), a `let` the program writes again (`function_called_at` takes the initializer for the value), a tagged template of a stored tag (a tag is no `CallFact`), `new` of a stored function (`call.construct` returned false at once). Decision: each is the same fact, a function the walk does not enter runs here, and `stored_function_value` answers it for the callee of a call or a `new`, the receiver of `.call` and `.apply` (`local_access::dispatch_receiver`: exactly `Function.prototype`'s), and a tagged template's tag: (1) an identifier declared as a parameter or a variable that is no function the walk resolves: a `let` the program writes again is no longer its initializer (`function_value_is_current`), a `new` of a class expression is exact; (2) a call's result that is no primitive; (3) a member the project declares as a slot (`SemanticLookup::project_slot_without_body`): every declaration in project source (not the bundled library, no installed package), no function indexed at the symbol or at the declaration's own name (a class method, an object method and a function declaration are read where they are written, ADR 0963; the declaration kind a resolved call gives says it too), not a closed object literal's member (the walk enters it, ADR 0351); a receiver or a tag is an element access, or a member whose property the project declares, or whose chain the project holds (a local or a parameter; not a global, an import, or `this`). The row stands at the invocation, blocker `unresolved-invocation` (at the `new` keyword for a construction, where V8's frame stands: the judge matches a row to a frame's position). The slot rule is asked in the member-route net too: a method that calls `this.cb()` runs the invocation inside it, which no walk of the continuation reaches. Additive by construction: the rows the net filed before are the same predicate on the same paths (`unresolved_invocation_known`), and the new ones (`unresolved_invocation_widened`) are filed after them, in a set of their own (`Walk::invoked`) that claims no callee another row stands at, never where the hand-off (ADR 0669) or the inexact callee (ADR 0879) has a row, and the census row index leaves them out (`reactive_analysis.rs`): a first build claimed the callee and dropped 29 SC9012 and SC5003 rows (`value-held-in-binding`, `pending-read-unresolved`), which the corpus diff showed (rows removed: 0 now). *Measured, Chrome host and the real oracle:* the 28 stored-call programs of `rs-*` (14 forms on the two async hosts) on main ec68d519d: 22 false clean, 6 recall-gap (SC9012's handed row covers `bind`, `field` and `thismeth`); the fix: recall-gap 28, 0 false clean, 56 twins 0 false positive; the sf and ob cells unchanged (0 false clean, 0 misplaced, 0 false positive). Corpus (34 projects, release, main against this build): standard U 120,416 -> 121,994 (+1,578: SC1002 `unresolved-invocation` +1,087, `function-run-unplaced` +490 in handed async functions and member-route code, SC9012 +1; no row removed; V 218 -> 218, no status moved); development build with package sources 140,823 -> 142,463 (+1,640: the same plus 108 package findings at project entries); a rough split of the member slots asked, by the declaration kind of the resolved call on an intermediate build (1,218 rows): a function-typed property 454, a method signature 233, an arrow-valued property 54, no resolved declaration 477 (the first build asked every method too: +2,617). The cost is the price of the review's shapes, tracked as expensive: the rows stand whatever the slot holds (an options bag's callbacks, a store's accessors). Not done, in `docs/open-work.md`: a receiver `this.cb.call(…)`, a project function named across files assigned to a slot, plain-named methods and parameter calls followed instead of asked (the review's narrowings, for the proof batch).

<a id="adr-0965"></a>
## ADR 0965 — Every typed cause of an uncertifiable row is an assumable premise (batch 11, slice 1)
ADR 0950's what-if run assumes the hazard *facts* a veto formula reads away. Two of the ~160 blockers in use (`standard-member-impostor`, `tuple-iteration-written`) are formulas over them; every other uncertifiable row stands on a typed cause no census holds (`function-run-unplaced`, `unresolved-invocation`, `generator-stepped`, `parameter-initializer-unplaced`, `field-initializer-unplaced`, `package-source-unavailable`, `function-call-target-unresolved`, `import-unresolved`, ...). The owner's plan (2026-11-11 16:12) is to make each cause measurable before its proof is built, so the proof batch is ranked by payoff and by what Chrome says.

*Decision.* A cause is a hazard of its own kind: `--assume Premise(<kind>)`, `<kind>` a blocker kind (`blockedBy.kind`: the head of the finding's `analysisContext`, kebab-case, no detail). The id is checked for its shape and the set is open (a constant of `blockers.rs`, or a context a rule names where it emits); a kind no row carries lifts nothing, and `scripts/what-if.mjs corpus --premise-sets N` derives the ids from the corpus's own rows. Assuming one takes every uncertifiable finding of that kind out of the report, all-or-nothing as per hazard, as if a proof had discharged the premise for all of them. Nothing else moves: a violation, a finding of another cause, and the other rules' rows at the site stay as the run made them.
The seam is still `hazard_present` alone (`what-if-gates.test.mjs` pins it): `Hazard::Premise(&'static str)` is a variant only the feature has, no census holds it, and the seam answers it present wherever it is asked, so only an assumption makes it absent and records the flip. Once the rows are final the pipeline asks the seam for each premise the run assumed (`SemanticLookup::assumed_premises`) and keeps the answers in `Program::assumed_premises` (a field only the feature has, never serialized). The backend's `what_if_filter.rs`, one module behind the feature and the only place the what-if build takes findings out of the report, drops the uncertifiable findings of those kinds before the verdicts and the status, so `status` is the assumption's. A cause needs no emission site of its own: the blocker of every row is known at one point, the finding's `analysisContext`, whichever of ~120 sites or projection rules (`blockers::for_read`, `for_rule`, ...) assigned it; and a finding leaves only there, in a build whose report is tainted, so the proof-lint kind `finding-filter` (ADR 0958: a finding leaves the report through the attribution seam) has nothing to account for in a default build.
The taint is ADR 0950's: `assumed.hazards` the ids as given; `assumed.flipped` every assumed premise (a premise is asked present wherever the seam is, so a program with no row of that kind is tainted and unchanged, which `what-if.mjs` compares as the same report); an `assumed` premise on every finding that stays. A default build has none of it: no variant, no field, no module, no change to any report (a premise row names no `fired` and the hazard table is as ADR 0947 left it; the script derives the id from `kind`), and `what_if_default_process` still finds none of the what-if code in the default binary.

*What it measures and what it cannot.* The payoff is exact for the rows that leave: they leave as clean. A proof may instead make them violations (a placement premise proved to run here), or only move the hole (the next blocker stands behind the row), and a row another rule withheld at the site because of the dropped row does not come back: the count is an upper bound on the win of the proof that is built, the direction ADR 0950 states. The safety is Chrome's, on the grids: a lifted row at a site Chrome fires at is a false clean the proof must not make; the corpus has no runtime. The grid half is `what-if.mjs grid`, which runs a `Premise(<kind>)` set on the blocker scan's programs that carry a row of that cause (the scan's `u.kinds`, not only the ones with a formula row), then `movers` and `judge` set the moved rows against Chrome's record as for a hazard (measured below, on a sample). Two causes the plan names as hazards of their own were not at slice 1: the two climb stops that owe an obligation (ADR 0959: `UnmodelledInPlace`, `Bound`) and the search limits. This sentence first said the stops end in `leaf-callback-opaque` and share it with the unresolved callbacks; that was wrong (they are `leaf-owner-unproven` rows), and slice 2 makes each stop a premise of its own, in the what-if build only (the amendment below); the search limits are ordinary premises (`search-depth-limit`, `search-budget-exhausted`).

*Measured (corpus payoff).* 34 projects, the manifest's configuration (browser, development build), release, the shipped checker (rel-33) against the what-if build of the same tree, the 56 causes with at least 50 uncertifiable rows (`corpus --premise-sets 50 --jobs 10`; 61 minutes on the Mac in Low Power, app-game's 56 runs alone 28): the control, the what-if build assuming nothing, is identical to the shipped checker's report on all 34 projects, 0 errors, and no set made a violation (`uToV` 0 in all 56). Rows that leave the report as clean, with their share of the corpus's 140,793 uncertifiable rows (a cause's other rows stand at a site another row still holds, so fewer leave than the cause has: `function-run-unplaced` has 11,369 rows, 5,242 leave): `standard-member-impostor` 32,730 (23.2%; the veto family ADR 0950 and 0954 already rank by hazard), `tuple-iteration-written` 12,573 (8.9%), `import-unresolved` 7,109 (5.0%, in 11 projects: modules the corpus does not resolve, an input question rather than a proof), `pending-read-unresolved` 6,386 (4.5%), `function-run-unplaced` 5,242 (3.7%), `handed-member-dispatch` 4,331 (3.1%), `package-finding-at-entry` 2,934 (2.1%), `unproven-container-root` 2,851 (2.0%), `parameter-use-unmodeled` 2,608 (1.9%), `handed-member-read` 2,584 (1.8%), `parameter-member-target-unresolved` 2,216 (1.6%), `value-to-unresolved-callee` 1,683 (1.2%), `async-read-unaccounted` 1,394 (1.0%); the next 40 causes are each under 1% (`unresolved-invocation` of ADR 0964: 334, 0.2%). `node scripts/what-if.mjs rank` prints it; the report is the measurement's, not a result about safety.

*Measured (Chrome safety, grids).* A 1% sample of the grids by id hash (`blocker-scan.mjs`, 21,761 programs, 186 s) lists the causes each program carries (`u.kinds`); 15,821 programs carry a cause of one of the 65 premise sets that have at least 15 programs (31,836 pairs); `what-if.mjs grid` ran them under the shipped checker and the what-if build (7 minutes; control 101 of 101 identical, 0 errors), and 14,887 moved (a premise lifts a row in every program that has one). Chrome (`--runtime-diff all`, the real oracle) ran 9,015 of them (61%): every program of the first 43 (grid, code) groups in the grids' order, a 25% sample (by id hash) of the 40 largest of the rest, and every program left that moved under one of the twelve causes that looked safe (the full run would have passed its estimate: about 3 programs a second in Low Power); the sample is biased to the base grids and the large groups for the other causes. `judge` set each lifted row against Chrome's record (6,559 of 6,615 diagnostics read as the oracle covers them). No counterexample in the judged programs (judged, false clean 0): `standard-member-impostor` 3,287, `pending-read-unresolved` 557, `installed-member` 528, `parameter-call-argument-unproven` 266, `listener-reach` 126, `summary-read-unplaced` 93, `value-use-unclassified` 85, `handler-member-unresolved` 17, `package-finding-at-entry` 16, `async-read-unaccounted` 16, `root-mount-pass-unproven` 13, `optimistic-reach-unresolved` 7, `caught-read-unproven` 6.
