Taken away wholesale, a cause that costs false cleans (false clean / judged): `function-run-unplaced` 365/3,449, `handed-member-dispatch` 145/1,128, `handed-member-read` 95/452, `value-forwarded` 95/456, `owner-callers-unenumerated` 90/185, `owner-varies-by-caller` 82/181, `unproven-container-root` 54/588, `closed-parameter-member-dispatch` 39/151, `tuple-iteration-written` 30/1,586, `callee-callback-timing` 13/68, `value-in-carrier` 12/1,248, `handler-dispatch-unproven` 9/21, `value-held-in-binding` 6/414, `store-operation-may-not-write` 6/30, `destructure-root-unproven` 6/138, `leaf-callback-opaque` 5/173, `value-to-unresolved-callee` 4/121, and one each of `async-provenance-unproven` (52), `read-backing-unproven` (466), `withheld-object-member` (309) and `unresolved-invocation` (630: the row of ADR 0964 is the only cover of a stored function's read there, as intended). The grids hold no program with a row of `import-unresolved`, `parameter-use-unmodeled`, `parameter-member-target-unresolved`, `exported-parameter-member-dispatch`, `parameter-member-path-unresolved`, `value-root-in-helper`, `preference-read-unproven`, `spread-content-unproven`, `handler-spread-unresolved` or ten rarer causes: their safety is unmeasured (corpus shapes the grids do not build). A first judgement on 5,667 programs had `unresolved-invocation` at 0 of 193 and `tuple-iteration-written` at 9 of 531: a cause with no counterexample moves with the programs run, so none here is safety.
*Reading.* Counts, and no counterexample in a sample, never safety (ADR 0950's limit): a cause is assumed away wholesale where a proof lifts it narrowly, so a false clean says the proof must be narrower than the cause, and none says only that the rows of that cause stand at sites Chrome is silent at in the programs run. For the proof batch (payoff high, Chrome-safe first): `standard-member-impostor` (23.2% of the corpus's rows, 0 of 3,287: its proofs stay per hazard, ADR 0947 and 0951; the cause as a whole is no proof), `pending-read-unresolved` (4.5%, 0 of 557), `installed-member` (0.7%, 0 of 528), `listener-reach` (0.5%, 0 of 126), then the causes that need a narrow proof: `tuple-iteration-written` (8.9%, 30 of 1,586: the research designs' Symbol-key proof is a narrow one) and `unproven-container-root` (2.0%, 54 of 588). `function-run-unplaced` (3.7%) and `handed-member-dispatch` (3.1%) are the big causes whose wholesale lift is unsafe, each of their proofs a placement or dispatch fact for one shape; `import-unresolved` (5.0%) is an input question (modules the corpus does not resolve), not a proof. The full table, with each cause's corpus rows and its Chrome tally: `node scripts/what-if.mjs rank --report corpus.json --judged judged.json`.

*Amended (batch 11, slice 2): the climb stops are premises of their own, and the sentence above about them was wrong.* The two stops of the slot climb that owe an obligation (ADR 0959: `UnmodelledInPlace`, `Bound`) do not end in `leaf-callback-opaque`: they are `LeafOwnerOperationKind::UnmodelledSlot` operations, SC3001's uncertifiable `leaf-owner-forbidden-call` rows with the blocker `leaf-owner-unproven`, which every uncertain leaf operation shares (the entered-async rows of ADR 0959's amendment, helper rows that run on some completions only, identity and call-site-gate rows). `leaf-callback-opaque` is SC9012's unresolved callback only. (The first version of this slice keyed the variants to `leaf-callback-opaque` and never fired; a probe of the what-if binary on `leaf-unmodelled-slot` showed it, and the process tests now pin it.) Each stop is a premise of its own, in the what-if build only: `LeafOwnerOperation.stop` (`#[serde(skip)]`, in no serialized form and no report) is set from the `ClimbStop` at the one literal that owes the obligation, a gated `LeafOwnerOperation::what_if_cause` names the premise kind a row is owed for (`leaf-owner-climb-unmodelled-in-place`, `leaf-owner-climb-bound`, or `leaf-owner-unproven` for any other reason), and `what_if_filter.rs`, still the only place the what-if build takes findings out, lets a `leaf-owner-unproven` finding leave when every cause the leaf operations at its site name was assumed (a row owed for several reasons stands while one stands); every other finding leaves by its blocker kind as before. `Premise(leaf-owner-unproven)` therefore means the rest only. The seam's tests (`what-if-gates.test.mjs`) are unchanged: no new reach of the module, one `retain`. Fixtures: `leaf-unmodelled-slot` (the in-place stop), `leaf-slot-climb-bound` (new: seventeen `untrack` layers around a `flush()`: one row; sixteen: the walk's violation), `flush-in-tracked-named-async` (the rest).

*Measured (slice 2: every moved program, at f584863f2).* The head is main e199fdd4e (slices 0 to 5) with this slice's part 1; it holds the three `unaccounted` kinds of ADR 0966, which the first run of this slice (on 4c744964c) did not, so that run was dropped and everything redone. Main has since taken ADR 0967's flip (#216 and #217: a value counts as handed to a package the build does not read unless a fact of the program proves where it goes), which adds `unaccounted` rows where such a package may run a function; the table is a measurement of f584863f2 and does not hold those rows. **The 1% sample** (`blocker-scan.mjs --sample 0.01 --sample-seed 1`: 21,814 programs, 69,849 uncertifiable rows, 105 causes, 142 s) has 16,108 programs that carry a cause of one of the 68 premise sets of at least 15 programs; `what-if.mjs grid` ran them under the shipped checker and the what-if build, and the **control (the what-if build assuming nothing) on every program**: 16,108 of 16,108 identical to the shipped checker's report, 0 errors (slice 1 ran it on 101). 15,082 programs moved and **Chrome (`--runtime-diff all`, the real oracle) ran every one of them** (slice 1: 9,015 of 14,887): 14,751 have a record, the other 331 have none (the oracle types the program away, or its `tsc` run overlaps the checker's rows); the judge's reading of "covered" agrees with the oracle's on 10,627 of 10,757 diagnostics (98.8%). **The causes the sample holds too few of** (fewer than 15 programs: 55 causes, among them `leaf-owner-unproven`, the stops' parent) ran on **all** their programs of the audit's scan of the whole grids (22,760 programs, rescanned at this head): control 22,760 of 22,760 identical, 15,511 moved, 15,436 with a record in Chrome (75 typed away), 10,624 of 10,658 diagnostics read as the oracle's. Six sets move nothing, the grids holding no row of the cause that would leave: `async-component-prop`, `mount-form-window`, `carrier-run-unproven`, `value-pending-in-jsx`, `package-operation-after-await` and `leaf-owner-climb-bound`. **The three `unaccounted` kinds of ADR 0966 stand together at a flush site** (SC2006, SC2008 and SC3001, a row each), so none of them alone empties a site: `flush-in-action-unaccounted` moves no program, `flush-in-effect-unaccounted` 243 and `leaf-operation-unaccounted` 93. The combined set `flush-universe-unaccounted` (the three at once: what a proof that a flush site is accounted for lifts) moves 594 programs of the sample, every program that carries one of them (498 + 336 + 226 rows, none to a violation, none left standing), and Chrome has a record of 591: **11 have a false clean** (one row each: FLUSH_IN_TRACKED_QUEUE_CALLBACK 9, FLUSH_IN_ACTION 1, FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK 1) and every other row of the 591 is silent in Chrome (1,026) or covered by another row (19).

A judged cause with a counterexample is counted by programs here; the judge counts rows (`what-if.mjs rank` prints both against the corpus rows: for instance `function-run-unplaced` 719 false-clean rows in 618 of 6,046 programs).

*Reading (slice 2).* The counterexamples are findings about premises, not about the shipped checker: `Premise(<kind>)` takes every uncertifiable row of that cause out of the report, wholesale, and a Chrome diagnostic at a lifted site says only that the proof that lifts the cause must be narrower than the cause, as slice 1 said; nothing here changed a finding, a snapshot or a serialized form of the default build. The first full judgement of the sample moved three causes slice 1 had without a counterexample to one with: `summary-read-unplaced` (1 program of 165), `optimistic-reach-unresolved` (5 of 38) and `caught-read-unproven` (8 of 39), so **no counterexample is not safety**: it says that the rows of that cause stood at sites Chrome is silent at in the programs run, a 1% sample of the grids and the whole-grid populations of the rare causes, and the corpus has no runtime to say more. The stops of the slot climb are the clearest case of a cause that earns its rows: `leaf-owner-climb-unmodelled-in-place` has a false clean in 88 of its 154 programs (the rows ADR 0959 added because Chrome fires at them), `leaf-owner-unproven`, now the rest, in 242 of 346; the bound has no program in the grids, so its verdict is the construction's (a flush under seventeen layers is an obligation, and nothing here says what Chrome does there). Unmeasured, and why: `import-unresolved` (the grids hold no program that imports a module nothing resolves), `exported-parameter-member-dispatch` and `spread-content-unproven` (no grid program holds a row of either), `leaf-owner-climb-bound` and the five other sets above that move nothing, and `value-escaped-from-untrack` (6 programs moved, no Chrome record).

*Tests.* `what_if_premise_id_names_a_blocker_kind` (the id and its shape); `what_if_process`: a premise takes the rows of its blocker out and taints the rest (fixture `stored-function-after-await`), every cause of a program assumed away certifies it, a premise no row carries moves no finding, a malformed id is an error; `what-if-gates.test.mjs`: the variant, the assumed list, the field and the filter module are compiled only with the feature and the filter takes findings out in one place; `what-if.test.mjs`: `premiseSetsFromBaselines`. Owners: `solid-reactive-ir/src/what_if.rs` (`premises`), `base/contributions.rs` (`Hazard::Premise`), `base/contributions/blocked_by.rs` (`hazard_of_id`), `indexes.rs` (`hazard_present`, `assumed_premises`), `pipeline.rs`, `solid-facts-backend/src/what_if_filter.rs`, `diagnostics.rs`, `scripts/what-if.mjs`. Slice 2: `what_if_process`: a callback the climb cannot read is a cause of its own (`leaf-unmodelled-slot`: the stop lifts its five rows, the rest and the bound none), the bound of the climb (`leaf-slot-climb-bound`: its row, and the violation at sixteen layers untouched), a leaf row owed for another reason is the rest's (`flush-in-tracked-named-async`), every reason assumed lifts every row and assuming none changes none; `what_if_filter`'s unit tests (a stop lifts its own rows and the rest the others; a row owed for several reasons stands while one stands; every other finding leaves by its kind); `what-if.test.mjs`: the stops are premise sets of their own, asked beside the kind they share. Owners: `solid-reactive-ir/src/lib.rs` (`LeafStop`, `LeafOwnerOperation::stop` and `what_if_cause`), `cleanup.rs` (the stop at the one literal that owes the obligation), `solid-facts-backend/src/what_if_filter.rs`, `scripts/what-if.mjs` (`PREMISE_PARENT`).

<a id="adr-0966"></a>
## ADR 0966 — Every flush site is accounted for, per code (batch 11, slice 5)
A flush no producer rowed was a clean nobody accounted for: SC2006 returns from its arm with no row when the program steps no action callback, SC2008 when the program defers none or the walks answer "placed off", SC3001's leaf walk when the flush is not in a leaf callback, and every one of those exits read the same whether a fact ruled the code out or the walk gave up (`unplaced_blocker` answers "placed off" for a function no call edge reaches, a body that may be a component and an owner set around the call as well as for a proof). `flush_accounting.rs` (its own typed endings and its own universe, like ADR 0838's and ADR 0886's passes; run once after the site accounting, over the final rows) lists the flush census's references (`render` and `hydrate` are listed for its veto and answer none of the three questions: each code asks its own producer's question of the primitive, SC2006 the dialect's `throws_inside_action_step`, SC2008 its `warns_inside_effect_phase`, SC3001 the leaf walk's own operation kind, `cleanup::files_as_flush_operation`, one mapping with `forbidden_operation_kind`) and asks, per code it applies to, whether the site ends in a row of that code's rule (a violation, an uncertifiable row, a leaf operation), an SC9012, the compiler's discard, or a typed reason (`FlushSafe`); else the code's own uncertifiable row, never a violation: SC2006 `flush-in-action-unaccounted`, SC2008 `flush-in-effect-unaccounted`, SC3001 `leaf-operation-unaccounted` (blockers.rs).

The reasons are the producers' own silent exits written down, each a fact of the program or of the site's census class and nothing else: the three program censuses (`NoSteppedAction`: no primitive the project names runs a callback as an action's steps; `NoDeferringPrimitive`: none defers one to a later flush; `NoLeafScope`: none runs one under a leaf owner), the census class of the caller that puts the flush on an empty stack (`FreshStack`, `AwaitContinuation`, `ModuleInitialization`, and `TestRunner`, assigned only to a flush written directly in a callback a reviewed runner schedules: `direct_callback_contains`), and `NeverRuns` (`SiteReach`). Owner ruling 5a (2026-11-12): a give-up is not a reason. None of the walk-derived exits is named (`unplaced_blocker`, the effect-phase origin, the climb that stopped), and neither are the census classes that may stand inside a step (`DetachedOwner` keeps the action depth, `TestHelper` may be called from a step inside a test, an `EventHandler`'s delivery may be dispatched from a step) nor `EffectApply`: those sites stay uncertifiable. Owner-approved (2026-11-12): the pass and its ids (`flush_accounting.account.<end>`) join the list of passes with their own typed endings (`site_accounting.rs` test), and the pass joins the proof lint's `PRODUCERS`; it names no primitive and ends no walk in a silent slot, so it needs no baseline entry.

*Measured* (34 projects, release, slice 0's candidate log and the pass): the census lists 1,799 references, 1,481 end in nothing today (1,452 in test files). Rows on: standard U 121,998 to 122,413 (+415: SC3001 310, SC2008 103, SC2006 2), development build +695 (SC3001 316, SC2006 276, SC2008 103: the packages analyzed as source name `action`, so `NoSteppedAction` no longer holds), 0 removed, V unchanged. Ablation (`SOLID_CHECKER_IR_ABLATE_SILENCE=all` or a reason's id): each reason is witnessed by a fixture (`flush-universe`, `-no-action`, `-bare`, `-test-runner`). Not done: the `SiteSilence` per-code endings of slice 4 will fold this pass in; `FlushServerBuildSelectsNone` (a server build) is not named.

*Review of #213 (2026-11-12), B1 and B2, fixed here.* A census class says where the function around the flush runs; it ends the three codes only when it is a statement about the flush's own stack. (B2) The flush is written in the body of its function, not in a parameter list: a default value runs wherever its function is called (`late = (x = flush()) => x` in a timer's callback, `late()` in an effect's apply: FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK in Chrome), which the class of the code around the nested function does not say; so no class reason for a site in a parameter list (`owners::in_parameter_list`), `ModuleInitialization` excepted. (B1) For `FreshStack` and `TestRunner`, no function around the flush is a named function expression whose name is used (`setTimeout(function tick() { flush(); … tick(true) })`: the name is bound inside, so the code using it enters `tick` again on the stack of its own host, an action's step or an effect's apply: FLUSH_IN_ACTION in Chrome); `AwaitContinuation` needs only the first. A site that fails either has no reason, so its uncertifiable row. Both were certified on main (no accounting) and signed here as `FlushSafe::FreshStack`; the fuzz cells `fc-*` (`scripts/fuzz/fc-shapes.mjs`) found four false cleans on the PR head (the old build fails `make fuzz-focus`), none on the fix; `scripts/fuzz/patterns/flushclass` and the fixtures `flush-universe-class` and `flush-universe-test-runner` hold the shapes.

*Re-review of #213 (same day), N1 to N3, fixed here.* The same cause in three more shapes: code written in the scheduler's callback that does not run when the callback runs. `callback_runs_it` (one function for `FreshStack` and `TestRunner`) asks, of what is written inside the call that hands the callback over: no parameter list holds the flush, no function is a generator (N2: `[1].map(function* () { flush() })` only creates the generator object, `.next()` runs the body on whatever stack calls it) or a named function expression its own code uses (B1), and no class field initializer (N1: an instance's construction runs it), JSX element or compiler tracked region (N3: a prop written in JSX is a getter that runs where the component reads it) holds it, the last three by `runs_with_its_function`, the predicate `continuation_function` already asks of the code after an await. A function or region written around the call is not asked: the callback still runs from the scheduler. Chrome confirmed three false cleans in an effect's apply on the fix of B1 and B2, and the cells `fc-field-*`, `fc-gencarrier-*`, `fc-jsxprop-*` five (an action's step for the prop, a leaf callback for the generator); none on this fix.

*Second re-review of #213 (same day), N4 and N5, fixed here.* Two more places where the class says less than the proof needs. (N4) `ModuleInitialization` was given unasked: the census gives a flush written directly in the suite body a reviewed runner runs inside its registration (`node:test`'s `describe`, jest-circus, mocha, playwright; ADR 0438) the class of the registration's position, and the class, parameter-list, JSX and field questions had been asked of the registration call, not of the flush. `Late = class { x = flush(); }`, a default value, `<Child v={flush()} />` or a named suite body its own code uses ended all three codes while an instance's construction, a call, a read of `props.v` or a second call of the name ran them elsewhere (Node 24 with the real `node:test` and rc.21 ran the first in an effect's apply and raised FLUSH_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK; Chrome cannot load `node:test`, so the generator cannot express the shape and the fixture `flush-universe-inline-suite` holds it). `suite_body_runs_it` asks, of everything written inside the outermost registration of the chain, the questions `callback_runs_it` asks (`runs_when_the_call_runs`, one predicate for the three classes); a flush directly in the body, in a nested body or in a named body nothing uses keeps the class. (N5) `callback_runs_it` dropped the resolved call of `fresh_stack_callback_call`, whose documentation says a consumer that relies on the stack must refuse `catch` and `finally`: they run their handler through the receiver's `then` (ECMAScript 27.2.5.1), and a Promise subclass whose `then` runs it at once calls it on the stack of the caller, an effect's apply, an action's step or a leaf callback. A call that `dispatches_through_receiver_then` gives no reason; a plain `Promise`'s `catch` is refused with it (precision lost, three rows a site, none in the corpus). Chrome on the previous head: three false cleans, one per host (`patterns/flushclass/recvthen-*`; cells `fc-recvthen-*`, 42 contexts with their twins); none on this fix. Corpus delta 0 (standard 122,211, development build 142,933): no site is ended by `ModuleInitialization` or `FreshStack` there (all 945 are `TestRunner`). Open, measured only (owner ruling pending, review finding F1): fake timers (`vi.useFakeTimers`, `jest.useFakeTimers`) run a timer's callback on the caller's stack (`vi.runAllTimers()` in an apply), the host premise of every `FreshStack` use and not of this fix.

<a id="adr-0967"></a>
## ADR 0967 — A flush in a function a package nothing here reads may run gets no census reason (batch 11, B3 of the review of #213)
The Fable review of #213 (B3) found a flush the accounting certified on a program census while a package ran it under a step: an installed package whose source the build does not read (a standard build reads none; a development build reads the packages whose manifest names a Solid package) can call `action`, `createEffect` or `createTrackedEffect` itself on a function the project hands it (`runStep(() => { flush(); })`, `action(function* () { cb(); })()` in the package), and nothing in the build's own table shows it. The three program censuses (`NoSteppedAction`, `NoDeferringPrimitive`, `NoLeafScope`) read that table only; Chrome (rc.21) reports FLUSH_IN_ACTION at the flush, while the program names no action, effect or leaf primitive (`probes/p21-dependency-step`, certified on the PR head and on main). Owner ruling, option b, fail closed, no premise (a premise like ADR 0613's dependency-flush premise would say what the package does, which nothing here can know): **a flush in a function a package nothing here reads may run gets no census reason**, so it keeps its uncertifiable row of each code (`flush-in-*-unaccounted`), never silence and never a violation. The class reasons (`FreshStack`, `AwaitContinuation`, `ModuleInitialization`, `TestRunner`, `NeverRuns`) are unchanged: each is a statement about the flush's own stack.

`package_hand_off.rs` answers, per function that owns the flush (the function written around it, each enclosing function too), whether a package nothing here reads may run it. A package runs a function it is handed and a function that function calls, so the question follows the routes a function reaches a package, each by the facts of the program (references and containment by the binder's declarations, never a spelling): the function written as an argument of a call, a `new` or a tagged template's substitution, or inside one (a member of an object literal, a method, an element of an array literal, a branch of a conditional, a spread); a reference to its binding (a declaration, a `const` arrow, a named function expression) written as such an argument, through every `const` copy, assignment and holder, in the file or, through an import, in another; a JSX prop, a spread attribute, a child expression or a `use:` directive of a component the program does not read; a project function's parameter that goes on to any of these (`function wrap(f) { pkg(f) }`, followed with the call graph's exact mapping); a function a project function returns, whose calls evaluate to it (`pkg(make())`, an immediately invoked function); a reflective call that forwards its arguments to its target (`pkg.run.call(null, cb)`, `.apply`, `.bind`, `Reflect.apply`, `Reflect.construct`: the target is known only when it is a project function); and a caller: a function whose exact call edge reaches the function (`pkg(() => helper())`), asked the same way. A function or a JSX element written inside the argument is its own business (`crosses`).

What receives the value decides (owner-approved list, declaration-resolved, no spelling): (1) a **dialect primitive**: the call resolves through `primitive_at_call` to a declaration in a Solid package whose symbol root is in the dialect catalog (ADR 0826); a JSX tag the dialect names is the same; (2) a **reviewed fresh-stack scheduler**, standard-library declarations only: `queueMicrotask`, `setTimeout`, `setInterval`, `requestAnimationFrame`, `requestIdleCallback` and their `WindowOrWorkerGlobalScope`, `AnimationFrameProvider` and `Window` member forms, `Promise.then`/`catch`/`finally`, `Scheduler.postTask`, `MediaSession.setActionHandler`, the `IntersectionObserver`, `ResizeObserver`, `MutationObserver`, `PerformanceObserver` and `ReportingObserver` constructors; (3) a **reviewed test runner's registration**, by the callee's identity (`reviewed_test_runner_registration`: `test_runner_callback` without the suite-body timing it also answers, which is `Unproven` for `bun:test` and where a registration has declarations in two runners): the callee's root identifier resolves only to declarations in an installed declaration file of `vitest`, `@vitest/runner`, `bun-types/test.d.ts`, `@types/node/test.d.ts`, `@playwright/test`, `playwright/types/test.d.ts`, `@jest/globals`, `@types/jest` or `@types/mocha`, is one of `test it describe suite beforeEach afterEach beforeAll afterAll before after`, possibly through `only skip todo concurrent sequential parallel serial fails fail fixme slow describe each for skipIf runIf`; nothing else of those packages is exempt (`expect`, `vi.*` and `waitFor` are not); (4) the **standard library**: the resolved declaration is a default-library declaration (`Array.prototype.*`, the `Promise` executor, `JSON`, `Map`); (5) a **project function**: a function with a body the call graph resolves exactly (`project_callee_at_parameter`), whose parameter is followed, and a DOM element (an intrinsic JSX tag: its handlers run on the browser's own stack); a project declaration no function body defines (a function-typed parameter or field: it may hold a package's function) is not one; (6) a **package the build reads as source**: the declaration's file is among the analyzed files (`in_dependency_source`), as in the development build with the Solid plugin. A declaration in a package the build does not read (a `.d.ts` under `node_modules`) and a callee nothing resolves get no census reason.

Not followed, named in `docs/open-work.md`: a value kept in a collection or a property and read elsewhere (`handlers.add(cb)`, `handlers.forEach(pkg.run)`), a method of a class whose instance is handed, an assignment into an object a package exports, a project component's props (the component's body is analyzed, the props object is not followed), a class constructor of the project that stores a callback, and an export a framework enters from outside. *Measured* (34 projects, release): standard U 122,211 to **122,236 (+25**: SC2006 24, SC3001 1; 0 removed, V unchanged), development build 142,933 to **142,943 (+10)**; corpus time unchanged. The arguments, references, members, JSX, parameters and callers cost **+12 standard, +0 development build** (the packages that name a Solid package are read as source, so their receivers are known): a callback handed to `expect(() => { …; flush() }).not.toThrow()` (compass-ui, 2), to a function-typed parameter (`body(store, settled)`, 1), to `Sortable.create` (app-game `createFlip.ts`, 4), to a UI package's `<Button onClick>` (1), to `createEventListener` and `access` (folder-tabs, kui, probus-hk). The route of a returned function costs **+13 standard, +10 development build**, all test doubles in kui (`fakeStream()`-style factories that return an object literal whose methods call `flush()`, handed to `expect(…)` or to a project function that passes it on) and two helpers in solid-groove: the object is the value of the call, and handing any member of it hands the object. They are precision lost, not defects the corpus reports (a test double's `connect()` is called by the test), and the one route the owner's list did not name; dropping it gives back 13 and 10 rows. A first build that took `test_runner_callback`'s answer for the runner's identity gave +169 on the first routes: 147 of them were flushes written in the `describe` bodies of compass-ui's `store.test.ts`, because `describe` has two declarations there (vitest's and `@types/jest`'s) whose suite-body timings differ, which `SuiteBodyRun::Unproven` answers with nothing; the registration is a reviewed runner's by its declaration all the same (`reviewed_test_runner_registration`, the callee's identity without the timing; the fixture's `Bun.test.tsx` pins it with `bun:test`, whose suite bodies are `Unproven` by design). Chrome (rc.21, real oracle): the twelve patterns `patterns/pkghand/*` (a package `fz-step` that names no Solid package) are false cleans on the previous head's build and fine on this one, and the cells `ph-*` (`scripts/fuzz/ph-shapes.mjs`: thirteen routes by three hosts, 78 contexts with their twins) give 39 false cleans on the previous head's build (13 per code) and 0 on this one (39 recall gaps; the 39 twins uncertifiable, never silent). Fixtures: `flush-universe-package-hand-off` (72 rows: 24 handed sites by every route, the package named through a namespace and an alias, a returned function and a reflective call, and the silent twins: the standard library, a project function, a DOM element, a shadowing local, a reviewed runner's registration); `impostor-withheld-no-run` (+2: its program-wide veto withholds every `forEach`, so their callee is a callee nothing resolves) and `rc21-anchor-listener-forms` (+2: a `.call` on a variable that holds the DOM method forwards to a target nothing resolves).

*F1 of the review of #213 (owner ruling 2026-11-12), added here.* `FreshStack` says the host runs the callback from an empty stack. A fake clock replaces the host's timers (`setTimeout`, `setInterval`, `requestAnimationFrame`, and with jest's modern timers `queueMicrotask`) with ones the test advances, so the callback runs on the stack of whoever advances the clock, which may be an action's step or an effect's apply. Confirmed in vitest 4.1.7 on solid-js 2.0.0-rc.21 (development build, jsdom): a real `setTimeout` callback's `flush()` runs on an empty stack and raises nothing; the same callback run by `vi.advanceTimersByTime(10)` inside an `action(function* () { … })` step throws `[FLUSH_IN_ACTION]`. **A program that installs fake timers gets no `FreshStack` reason**: every flush a scheduler's callback holds keeps its uncertifiable row of each code (`flush-in-action-unaccounted`, `flush-in-effect-unaccounted`, `leaf-operation-unaccounted`), never a violation. Only `FreshStack` is withheld; `TestRunner` (the runner calls the callback), `AwaitContinuation` (the promise job queue no fake clock replaces) and `ModuleInitialization` stand. The ruling named the file; the census is the program's, because the clock is global to the run: a test reaches it through a helper another file declares (`useClock()`), and the flush is written in the code under test, not the test file. It is read once per build and only when a scheduler's callback would end a flush (`flush_accounting.rs`, `FakeTimers`). The installer is recognized by the compiler's resolution, never the spelling alone: a member's or a destructured key's property that resolves to a declaration named `useFakeTimers` in an installed declaration file of `vitest`, `@types/jest`, `@jest/environment`, `@jest/globals` or `bun-types/test.d.ts` (path components, as ADR 0438's runner list is; `vi.useFakeTimers()`, `jest.useFakeTimers()`, `bun:test`'s `jest`, a call through any typed handle, `vi["useFakeTimers"]()`, `const { useFakeTimers: install } = vi`); or the same name written as a member of, or destructured from, an object such a library declares (the entity of a property that is not called is not in the table, and the one a shorthand key names is the local binding: `const install = vi.useFakeTimers`, `vi.useFakeTimers.call(vi)`, `(vi as any).useFakeTimers()`, `const { useFakeTimers } = vi`), the object resolved through its import aliases to a reviewed library's declaration. The unit test `a_fake_timer_installer_is_a_reviewed_library_declaration` pins the declaration list; the fixtures `flush-universe-fake-timers` (vitest through a helper file: nine rows; its `real` twin, `vi.useRealTimers()` and `vi.advanceTimersByTime`, none; the `jest`, `bun` and `destructured` variants: three each) pin the rule, and nine probes of spellings (alias, `.call`, string key, renamed import, pattern key, namespace import, `vitest`, a cast, a typed handle) agree. *Measured* (34 projects, release, against #216's head before this change): standard 122,236 to 122,236, development build 142,943 to 142,943, **0 rows move**: the corpus's 945 sites that end on a class reason (every one on `TestRunner`, 152 of them in five test files that call `vi.useFakeTimers`) end on no `FreshStack` at all, and the programs that install fake timers (compass-ui, dashiboard-ui, kui) hold none either. The fuzz grid cannot express it (the oracle's page runs in Chrome, where vitest and jest do not load): an `outside` entry in `scripts/fuzz/rediscovery.json` says so. Named misses, each a decision not built (`docs/open-work.md`): another library's installer (sinon's `useFakeTimers`, `@sinonjs/fake-timers`' `install`, jasmine's `clock().install()`, `node:test`'s `mock.timers.enable()`), a configuration that enables fake timers without a call (jest's `fakeTimers: { enableGlobally: true }`), a value read through a handle no reviewed library declares (`const clock = vi; const install = clock.useFakeTimers`), a destructured parameter, and a receiver the compiler types `any` that is not written as the reviewed object (`const loose = vi as any; loose.useFakeTimers()`).

*The flip (the Fable review of #216, owner ruling 2026-11-12), added here.* A review of #216 (head a0c17af80, BLOCKED) probed fourteen programs that stay silent where a package runs the flush as an action's step; each is a false clean on `main` too, five of them routes the PR did not list. The cause was one default: `position` answered "not handed" for every context it did not recognize (an iterable, a receiver, a `yield`, a class field, `this.x =`), and a value that is called asked nothing of the function that owns the call. The ruling: **a value counts as handed unless a fact of the program proves where it goes**, so the flush keeps its uncertifiable row of each code. `package_hand_off.rs` reads each reference of a function, and of each holder it is copied into, at its position:

- *a call* (`f()`, `new C()`, a tag, a JSX tag, `f.call(…)`, `holder.run()` of a member the project declares) runs it on the stack of the function the call is written in, so the owner is asked (the review's N-B: `[1].forEach(cb)`, `untrack(cb)`, `call(cb)` with `call(f) { f() }` inside a step a package runs);
- *an argument* is judged by its receiver's declaration: a dialect primitive and the standard library's inline callbacks run it where the call is written (the owner is asked); a reviewed fresh-stack scheduler, a DOM listener's registration and a reviewed runner's registration run it elsewhere and keep nothing; an exact project callee goes on in its parameter (a component's props are parameter 0, a `use:` directive's accessor is parameter 1, a reflective call's forwarded arguments are shifted by the thisArg, `Reflect.apply(f, …)` runs `f` inline: the review's E1); a package the build reads as source is known; the rest is handed: a collection's insertion, a standard call the argument-behavior table does not list (`Promise.resolve`, `Object.assign`, `Array.of`: they may return or copy the value), a package nothing reads, a spread, a callee nothing resolves;
- *a JSX prop, spread, child or directive* by what the element is: an intrinsic element is the DOM's, `Dynamic` is the component its `component` prop names (a string is an intrinsic tag, a project function is that component, anything else is handed: E2), a project component's props are its first parameter (E3), a package read as source is known, any other is handed;
- *the initializer of a binding, the value of an assignment, a `return`* goes on in every read of the holder (the file's and its importers'); an assignment into an import, `this` or a call's result is handed; a function that returns the value is itself asked, since a package that runs it may call what it returns (N-A: `pkg(() => cb)`);
- *a member* `holder.slot` is the member's own value, `holder.run()` runs a member whose property symbol the project declares where it is called, any other method of a holder (`list.forEach(pkg.run)`) may pass its members on and is handed, and a write through the holder takes nothing out;
- *an operand nothing takes* (the whole operand of `typeof`, `===` or an expression statement) is not handed; **anything else is**.

A function is also handed when written in a class (an instance, a subclass or `this` may hand it on from where nothing here follows: r2a), when exported from a program whose boundary is open (code this build cannot see may enter it; a binding it exports is read by such code), and when its module's namespace escapes with no name the analysis can read (`runNs(NS)`: the namespace census of ADR 0347, N-C). A closed boundary is the owner's declared premise (ADR 0193), so an export nothing in a closed program enters keeps the reasons (it never runs).

*Measured* (34 projects, release, against #216's head): standard U 122,236 to **122,294 (+58**, all SC2006 `flush-in-action-unaccounted`), development build 142,943 to **142,943 (+0)**. The open-boundary export rule is **1** of the 58 (kui `feature-alerts/src/fixtures.tsx`; switched off, the other 57 stand). The 57 are one kui test file, `feature-messages/src/messages.test.tsx`: the helper `mount(component)` puts `component` in the holder `dispose` (`const dispose = render(component, container)`: a call's result may hold its argument), the closure it returns calls `dispose()`, `screen()` spreads that object into `{ ...mounted, fake, session }`, and a test does `const { fake, dispose } = screen(); expect(fake.urls)` with vitest's `expect` (a package nothing reads, not exempt by the owner's list). The holder-following is field-insensitive, so every callback handed to `mount(…)` is judged handed; no package step runs there at run time, so these are precision lost (named in `docs/open-work.md`; the cut is a field-sensitive destructuring of a returned object literal). `make coverage`: 36 existing fixture snapshots add 362 uncertifiable rows (SC3001 161, SC2006 126, SC2008 75; none removed): the flushes written in exported functions of fixtures that carry no `.solid-checker/runtime.json`, so an open boundary; each README says so.

*Evidence.* The review's fourteen probes were silent on #216's build and keep their three rows now (`rv-r1a`, `r1b`, `r1c`, `r2a`, `r3`, `r4`, `n1`, `n2`, `n3a`, `n3b`, `n3c`, `n4` with the real `@solidjs/web` `Dynamic`, `n5`, `n6`); the owner-approved twins (the standard library's, a project function's, a DOM element's, a shadowing local, a reviewed runner's) stay silent; the 49 earlier probes are unchanged but `x-collection` (a named miss of #216, now handed). Chrome (rc.21, the real oracle): the thirteen new `ph-*` routes by three hosts (`set`, `forof`, `recv`, `resolve`, `class`, `assign`, `props`, `getter`, `reflect`, `inline`, `param`, `untrack`, `dynamic`) are **39 false cleans on #216's build (13 per code), 0 on the flip's** (their 39 legal twins: 39 clean, now uncertifiable); `patterns/pkghand/namespace-step`: a false clean on #216's build, fine on the flip's. Three rediscovery entries (13 ids per code) and two `outside` entries (the namespace route: the cells cannot place a second module's export; an open-boundary export: Chrome cannot enter an export from outside). Fixtures: `flush-universe-package-hand-off-flip` (45 rows over fifteen handed sites, eight silent twins; `variants/open`: three rows against the closed parent's silent `Unreferenced`). Named misses, each a decision not built: a field-insensitive holder (above); a class's functions are handed whole (a flush in a method keeps its row whether or not its instance reaches a package); an export of a closed program that only a framework enters; a standard call the table does not list hands its function arguments; a destructured parameter is read through its pattern's names.
<a id="adr-0968"></a>
## ADR 0968 — A site ends for a Chrome code only by an ending that answers that code (batch 11, slice 4)
Any row or finding at a site's span used to end the site: `Endings` kept, per `(path, start, end)`, only the best class of any row, defect or violation, whatever its rule or table, and a named silence ended every table's site at its span. A read that only the async-read table's row stood at, or a write that only another rule's finding stood at, was accounted for, although neither row answers the Chrome code the site can raise (STRICT_READ_UNTRACKED, REACTIVE_WRITE_IN_OWNED_SCOPE).

**Each site now carries the codes it may raise (`SiteCode`: `StrictRead`, `ReactiveWrite`, `OptimisticWrite`, `ActionCall`, `StoreWrite`, `OnFlush`, each with its `RuntimeCode` and home rule), and each (site, code) ends only by an ending that answers it.** `credits(policy, code, by)` is one `match` over the ending's kind with no wildcard: a row of the site's own table answers the table's codes, a census obligation answers a code that has a place, a finding answers the code when the dialect's policy (`Dialect::code_policy`, ADR 0955) says the rule claims it (a rule that declines it answers it only as an obligation), the compiler's discard answers every code; no policy for a code answers nothing (fail closed). A named silence ends the codes its `SiteSilence::codes()` lists (an exhaustive `match` per variant): the read silences STRICT_READ, `SetterOptimistic*Unguarded` REACTIVE_WRITE only (an optimistic write before the first answer still fires), `UnreachedWriteCannotRun` REACTIVE_WRITE and the action call (module-scope optimistic writes can still fire), the shared-reactivity silences STORE_WRITE, `static_api.on-flush.role-allows` ON_FLUSH; the four removal silences (`ApplyNeverRuns`, `ClosedProgramExportDischarge`, `ArgumentPrimitiveDischarge`, `ArgumentMemberPrimitiveDischarge`) answer what the removed row's ending answered (`RemovedBy`). A (site, code) with no ending and no silence gets the **code's own** home rule's uncertifiable row (`unaccounted_row(file, site, code)`): SC1001 `strict-read-unaccounted`, SC2001 `write-unaccounted`, and, new, SC2009 `optimistic-write-unaccounted` for the OPTIMISTIC code. Never a violation.

A write site carries OPTIMISTIC_WRITE_BEFORE_LOAD beside REACTIVE_WRITE when the setter's source is optimistic and may be async, or when no fact names the primitive that made it (`optimistic_async_setter`, one definition shared with SC2009's producers, `local_access.rs`): an unresolved primitive is fail closed, and a sync source (`createOptimistic(0)`), which the existing proof of synchronous names, has no such code. `StaticDefectFamily::rule_name()` maps a defect family to its rule exhaustively, and the rules crate pins that it equals `static_defect_wording`'s (a test fails when the two drift); the IR still matches no rule by a name prefix.

**The proof log and the gate.** One `site` line per (site, code) (`code`, `by`: `table:<t>`, `census`, `rule:<name>[:u]`, `discarded`, `credited`: whether the strongest ending at the span answers the code), and the `silent` lines of the named silences carry `codes`. `scripts/lib/proof-log.mjs` folds C1, C2(b) and C3 per (site, code): only a named end (`rule: "end"`) ends a code, and only the codes its `codes` list (a line with no `codes`, a line of another pass at the span (`PendingSafe`, `FlushSafe`, a `row:*` stage, SC4001) and a site line with no `code` end none, fail closed: the writer omits an empty list, and the checker then files the code's row; the review of #218's F1, **owner-approved** 2026-11-12), and the classifier's decisions are about the table's first code (`SITE_CODE`), so SC2009's line at a setter call has no decision to be explained by; `scripts/lib/proof-credit.mjs` reads `site.code`. This is a judge change, **owner-approved** (2026-11-12) with no C2 exemption for a site whose only ending answers another code: that is the case the slice exists to expose. The 22 sites the gate now reports (the 17 the corpus fixtures show, 12 reads, 4 writes and 1 setter whose primitive no fact resolves, and 5 in the slice's own two fixtures) are listed in `fixtures/proof-log/candidates.json`, owner "batch 11 slice 4: masked site, the code's U row stands; the proof is its producer's own"; `gaps.json` stays `{}`.

*Measured* (34 projects, release, against #216's head, which is main): standard U 122,236 to **122,240 (+4**, all SC1001 `strict-read-unaccounted`, app-game: `createTreeView.ts:172`, `solid-three/core/store.ts:383`, `:384`, `:391`), development build 142,943 to **142,948 (+5**, the same four and jar-hell-web `theme.ts:34`); **0 `optimistic-write-unaccounted` rows** on the corpus (the brief's estimate was under 50); nothing removed, V unchanged. `make coverage`: nine existing fixtures move by **22 uncertifiable rows, none removed** (SC1001 14: `caught-handed-read`, `handed-carrier-tracked`, `handed-net`, `handed-net-guards`, `async-carried-leaf-owner`, `async-unsuspendable-call-attributed`; SC2001 7: `rc21-optimistic-before-load`, `rc21-optimistic-handed-prop`; SC2009 1: `local-alias-literal-slots`), each a site that only another table's row or another rule's finding stood at. Fixtures: `ending-by-code-other-rule` (a read ended only by the async-read table's row: SC1001's row beside SC5003 and SC5008; a write ended only by SC2009's violation: SC2001's row beside it) and its `-crediting` twin (the own rule's finding stands: no row beside it), `optimistic-write-site-code` (async, aliased, sync, and an object-slot alias whose primitive no fact resolves). Chrome (rc.21): the read raises CAUGHT_REACTIVE_READ only and the write OPTIMISTIC_WRITE_BEFORE_LOAD ahead of any owned-scope guard (`caught-handed-read`, `rc21-optimistic-before-load`), so the new rows are U where Chrome fires one other code, the cost of not having a proof that the second code cannot fire; no defect is found (nothing was a false clean), so there is no rediscovery entry. Named in `docs/open-work.md`: an OPTIMISTIC row on a setter whose primitive is unresolved is a fail-closed trade until slice 8 names SC2009's silences (`NoOptimisticAsyncSource`, `OptimisticSourceSynchronous`).

## ADR 0969 — Every owner call ends, per code, in a row, an SC9012, the compiler's discard or a named silence (batch 11, slice 6)

**The defect.** SC3001 (CLEANUP_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE, PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE) and SC6002 (PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK) had no site universe: a call of `onCleanup`, or of a creation (`createMemo`, `createEffect`, `createRoot`, `render`, ...), that no walk rowed was silent by omission, and nothing said whether it was silent because no leaf owner or effect apply can reach it or because a walk gave up (`unplaced_blocker`'s `None` answers both "placed, nothing reaches" and an anonymous function, a component by convention, a function with no call sites, a parameter list). No confirmed defect: a ratchet, so no rediscovery entry.

**The universe.** `owner_call_sites` lists every call whose resolved primitive is one of these, outside dependency source, whatever its callee is spelled, with the codes `owner_call_codes` gives it (one definition, asked again by the silences): `onCleanup` raises CLEANUP_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE, a creation a leaf owner refuses (`cleanup::creates_forbidden_primitive`) PRIMITIVE_IN_FORBIDDEN_SCOPE, and on a client build a creation the dialect says throws on an apply (`throws_creating_in_effect_callback`, the argument test is `static_api::first_argument_is_function`, shared with both SC6002 arms) PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK (`SiteCode::{CleanupForbidden, PrimitiveForbidden, EffectCreation}`, ADR 0968's per-code endings). A leaf operation ends the code its kind answers (`leaf_operation_row`: a cleanup, a creation, a call the walk could not place; the observer guard, a flush, a callback no walk followed answer none of these), an SC9012 ends every code, and a finding of the claiming rule ends its code. **SC4001 does not decline** (ruling of the coordinator, owner delegated: `codes.mjs` unchanged): an SC4001 obligation at an `onCleanup` ends nothing for SC3001's code. A site no ending and no silence holds gets the code's own row, **never a violation**: SC3001 `leaf-operation-unaccounted` (the blocker flush accounting shares) and SC6002 `effect-creation-unaccounted`. A removed leaf operation (an effect whose apply never runs) names what it answered.

**The silences (seven, each a typed `SiteSilence` with a premise registered in `boundary.mjs`; coordinator-approved, owner delegated).** Every silence that rests on the owner graph or on a census is gated by **B3** (policy A, owner-ruled): no package the build does not read may run the function that holds the site (`UnreadPackageHandOff`, ADR 0967). A package a build reads as source is known; a component only an unread package renders (the router's) is not trusted to be rendered under an owner: that waits for a proof from the package's source (proof batch). In `owners/leaf_universe.rs` (the owner stage, where the graph is in scope): **`OwnerCallNeverRuns`** (ADR 0458's query proves no execution runs the site, premise `SiteReach`, every code); **`OwnerCallModuleScope`** (no function owns the site and no instance field's initializer holds it: module evaluation runs it once on a stack of its own, premise `ModuleScope`, every code); **`LeafNoOpenerInProgram`** (the project names no primitive that opens a leaf scope, `project_opens_leaf_scope`, which includes a package read as source, premise `ProgramCensus`, SC3001's two codes); **`LeafOwnerAbsent`** (the owning function's context has an edge, no leaf bit and is not the bare open-world seed of an export, `open_world_unowned_only`, premise `OwnerContextNoLeaf`, SC3001's two codes); **`OwnerCurrent`** (the context gives an owner on every entry, owned, a leaf owner or both: no unowned bit, so `inEffectCallback && context === null` is never met, premise `OwnerContextCurrent`, SC6002); **`EffectCreationRunsOffApply`** (the role engine places the site off every apply: rendering, a deferred callback, a directive apply, module code, discarded code, an event handler where no synchronous dispatch is present, premise `RoleDoesNotReport`, the exit the second SC6002 arm took silently) and **`EffectCreationOwnerSetAround`** (an exact primitive whose callback creates an owner wraps the site, premise `OwnerSetAround`; `owner_created_around_call` answers only `SlotOwner::Creates`, since `owner_set_around`'s "sets or may set" also holds for `runWithOwner` with an owner that is a value or null, and only where every layer the climb crosses runs in the primitive's synchronous run: not a generator, a parameter list, or after a suspension of an async layer). The graph's two silences and the role silence answer only where the site is plain (written directly in a function that is no generator, not in a parameter list and not after a suspension of its async function), with no layer around it entered by a route the graph has no edge for (`layer_entry_open_by_reference`, ADR 0868's test without its component-render premise: a function that returns JSX is entered by renders alone only where every reference of its binding is a JSX tag; its "caller holds no context" question extracted as `caller_holds_no_context`), and for a function no package the build does not read may run (B3). The two effect-creation silences were `static_api`'s, asking the role or the wrapper and nothing else, until the review of #220 found them ending sites Chrome throws at (below).

**The proof gate and the decline (judge change, coordinator-approved, owner delegated).** A site no proof ends gets the code's home row, which under policy A is the ruled ending for a function a package the build does not read may run, a layer entered by reference and the like, so `make proof-gate` reported 2,705 sites that end in nothing. The checker now writes, for each group of codes (SC3001's pair, SC6002's) of an owner-call site that no silence of the owner stage ends, a typed `declined` line naming why (`Decline`: `UnreadPackageMayRun`, `InstanceFieldInitializer`, `ParameterList`, `GeneratorBody`, `AfterSuspension`, `NoOwnerNode`, `NoEdgeIntoFunction`, `OpenWorldExport`, `LeafBitPresent`, `UnownedEntry`, `EntryByReference`: a closed enum, no catch-all, the id function denies a wildcard arm). A decline is no proof and ends nothing: it goes to the proof log only and no ending or silence reads it (`an_owner_call_decline_ends_nothing` holds that a declined-only site keeps its uncertifiable row and that no silence lists its code). The gate (`scripts/lib/proof-log.mjs`, `ownerCallDeclined`) accepts an owner-call (site, code) that ends in nothing if and only if the code's home row stands at the span (the final report's `leaf-operation-unaccounted` or `effect-creation-unaccounted`, uncertifiable) AND a `declined` line at the span lists the code; either alone, a decline for another code, one with no code list, and any other table's site stay hits. Five unit tests, three of which fail on the old gate.

**Found while building the fixtures.** Where the callee's declarations sit under no installed package directory (a project's own ambient `declare module "solid-js"`, or declarations that disagree on the package) the effect-phase walk, which asks `callee_package_root`, files nothing, and nothing else accounted for the creation: `createMemo(() => 1)` written directly in a `createEffect` apply was **certified** although the runtime throws PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK there. The creation is now a site with no silence, so it gets `effect-creation-unaccounted` (fixture `owner-call-effect-creation-unresolved-package`; the same program with the declarations in an installed package is the walk's violation). The generator writes every program against the pinned real install, so the shape is an `outside` entry in `scripts/fuzz/rediscovery.json`.

**Chrome (rc.21) and the fuzzer.** The fixtures' programs ran in real Chrome (the runtime oracle): `owner-call-effect-creation`'s legal components (`RootInMemo`, `Body`, `Handler`, `Timer`, `Fielded`, `SharedBody`, `SharedTimer`, the module-scope memo, `dead`, `stored`) are quiet (the checker's row at `Handler`'s inline arrow is the cost named below); `InApply` and `RootInApply` fire PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK, each covered by SC6002's violation; `owner-call-leaf-universe` fires only at the leaf walk's two violations (`Opener`, `Both` through `useBoth`); the no-opener program, its `with-opener` twin and the `sync-dispatch` twin (the click's target cannot reach the handler, so Chrome is quiet and the U is the cost) are quiet; the unresolved-package program fires against the real packages (the walk gives its violation there), so its ambient form, certified before, was a false clean. `make fuzz-focus` on the three codes needs no look (0 false clean, 0 false positive): on the build with the review's fixes the misuse grids are unchanged against the build before (mx-base 1,320 programs: agree 1,128, recall-gap 192; mx-combo 1,320: agree 1,099, recall-gap 221; mx-census 8,262), and the legal grids are all uncertifiable (lg-base 1,680, lg-combo 1,680; before slice 6, 1,680 and 1,025 of 1,680 were clean): the cost of policy A, since the generator's programs export their components from an open program, where every function is handed. The review's cells (`er-shapes.mjs`, below) are in the recall grids.

**Measured** (34 projects, release, against the head with #218 and the flip, standard build; the figures after the review's fixes, the first ones in brackets): the raw universe, with endings and no silence, is 5,988 (site, code) lines of which 5,802 rows (unique sites 5,352: SC3001 creation 2,677, SC3001 cleanup 272, SC6002 2,403); with the silences **+4,372 uncertifiable rows (SC3001 `leaf-operation-unaccounted` 2,465, SC6002 `effect-creation-unaccounted` 1,907) [+2,876: 2,337 and 539]**, none a violation; development build +4,381 (SC3001 2,475, SC6002 1,906) and nine SC6002 `package-finding-at-entry` rows superseded at the same sites [+2,878, six]. The review's fixes add 1,496 rows to the first measurement (SC6002 1,368: B3 gating the role silence 1,248, a layer entered by a reference 51, a function no edge reaches 34 (an inline handler is one), after an `await` 29, an unowned entry 6; SC3001 128, a layer entered by a reference: the components F1 found), 0 removed, 0 violations moved. What the silences end (unique records, owner stage): `owner-context-no-leaf` 697 [827], `effect-creation-runs-off-apply` 666 [recorded wherever the role placed the site: 1,857], `owner-context-current` 22 [239], `no-opener-in-program` 19, `module-scope` 13, `effect-creation-owner-set-around` 4 [13], `never-runs` 1. What stays uncertifiable, by decline (unique (site, code), standard): SC6002 `unread-package-may-run` 1,714, `entry-by-reference` 99, `no-edge-into-function` 44, `after-suspension` 34, `unowned-entry` 16; SC3001 creation `unread-package-may-run` 1,845, `entry-by-reference` 162, `no-edge-into-function` 151, `after-suspension` 34, `open-world-export` 25; SC3001 cleanup `unread-package-may-run` 176, `entry-by-reference` 49, `open-world-export` 14, `no-edge-into-function` 8, `after-suspension` 1 (B3 is most of it: a component an exported route hands to the router). `make coverage`: **1,197 projects (the three review fixtures are new): 279 existing projects gain 1,380 rows and lose 5 (1,375 SC6002 `effect-creation-unaccounted` and one SC3001 are new; the five removed are the old offsets of four SC6002/SC9012 rows and one `effect-creation-unaccounted` that moved only because `owner-call-effect-creation`'s comments grew, counted again among the added); no row changes kind, no violation moves** (against main, in all: 452 existing projects move, 4,311 rows added and 7 removed, and 8 are new); the first measurement had 423 projects and 2,906 rows (the seven removed, which stay, are slice 5's SC3001 flush rows in `resolve-inline-entries` and `slot-run-effect-phase`, which yield to any row of their rule that overlaps the flush call, and the new row at the `resolve` or `until` inside it now does: both stay uncertifiable beside it, named in `docs/open-work.md`). Fixtures: `owner-call-leaf-universe`, `owner-call-no-leaf-opener` (variants `with-opener`, `package-opener` in the development build and `package-unread` in the standard one), `owner-call-effect-creation` (variant `sync-dispatch`), `owner-call-effect-creation-unresolved-package`, and the review's `owner-call-component-by-reference`, `owner-call-saved-callback` and `owner-call-root-generator`.

**The review of #220 (BLOCKED, and the fixes).** The Fable review of the head 280f766c1 (`slice6-review/review.md`, probed read-only) found three of the seven silences ending sites rc.21 throws at: certified on the head, and on main with no silence at all (so no output got worse, but #220 would have signed them with a named proof). Each is a hole in its silence's premise, not in the rule. **F1, `OwnerCurrent` and `LeafOwnerAbsent`:** `named_layer_entry_open` (the emission's test of what enters a function, ADR 0868) stops at `function_proven_component` (a function that returns JSX that no call enters, one that a reference also enters included: `Escaped`) before it looks at the references, and the graph gives such a function an owned context: the component-render premise the ruling excludes. `function view() { createMemo(…); return <p/> } … pick = view; createEffect(() => 0, () => { pick!(); })` runs `view` on the apply's stack with `context === null`. Fix: `layer_entry_open_by_reference`, for the universe's silences only: a component is entered by renders alone only where every reference of its binding is a JSX tag (the opening and the closing name are one reference); the emission keeps its own test and its premise (a change to SC4001's proofs is a slice of its own, with grid diffs). **F2, `EffectCreationRunsOffApply`:** it asked the lexical role only, which places a handler or a timer callback off every apply from where the function is registered and follows no other reference (`saved = h` then `saved!()` in an apply; an array's element; a generator body `untrack` hands back and an apply steps), and it carried none of the conditions the graph's silences have. Fix: the silence moved to the owner stage and rests on the role AND the same three facts (a plain site, `layer_entry_open_by_reference`, B3). **F3, `EffectCreationOwnerSetAround`:** `owner_created_around_call` never asked whether the callback runs in the primitive's synchronous run (`createRoot(function* () { createMemo(…) })`, stepped from an apply: the body runs after the root's owner is gone). Fix: every layer the climb crosses must be plain (no generator, no parameter list, no site after a suspension), the owner stage's `generator-body` decline being the same question. Each fix is Chrome-confirmed (oracle cases `owner-call-component-by-reference`, `owner-call-saved-callback`, `owner-call-root-generator`: `unproven-clean` on the head of #220, `covered-uncertifiable` on the fix), has a fixture (a positive, the negative and the twin; the README tables) and is a fuzz cell: `er-shapes.mjs`, 14 contexts of the recall grids, of which the head of #220 certifies 12 programs (`view`, `saved`, `timer` and `root` on an apply, three each) and the fix none (rediscovery.json lists the three entries; the `array` and `generator` routes and the leaf cells are covered by the SC9012 at the call that reaches the function, on both builds). The cost is the corpus rows above: B3 now gates the role silence as it gates the graph's (policy A), and an inline event handler (`onClick={() => …}`, a function value no binding names that is not a whole argument of a call) is no closed layer for the entry test, so a creation in one keeps its row (at most the 34 sites above; Chrome is quiet there). The proof batch's refinement: a literal the compiler binds as the element's event handler is entered only by the event.

**The re-review of #220 (BLOCKED once more: B1).** The F2 fix left one hole in the role silence: the test that a site is plain asked `function.generator` of the innermost function only (`leaf_universe.rs`), so a creation in an arrow, an IIFE or an `untrack` callback written inside a generator was a plain site, the role engine placed it where the iterator is made (rendering), and `EffectCreationRunsOffApply` ended it, though an apply that steps the iterator with a `for…of` or a spread runs the layer with no owner (`probes2/r6q-forof-certified`, `r6r-spread-untrack-certified`; Chrome rc.21 PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK). Fix: `generator_layer_around` asks every function layer around the site, innermost first, and stops at the layer whose callback runs under an owner its primitive creates (`creates_owner_for`, the emission's own notion of an owner layer): the owner is current there whoever steps what is around it, so a creation in a root's callback inside a generator stays silent (`effect-creation-owner-set-around`) and its `createRoot` keeps its row. The same predicate guards the graph's two silences (`OwnerCurrent`, `LeafOwnerAbsent`) and the effect pair, since all four follow `plain`. Measured on the re-review's 21 generator probes, the head of #220 against the fix (release, closed boundary): `r6q` and `r6r` certified → one SC6002 row each; ten others gained the SC6002 row their silence had hidden (the file already had other rows); nine controls unchanged; no row lost. The fixture `owner-call-generator-layer` pins the three false cleans, the owner-layer stop and the cost (`Idle`: a generator made and never stepped keeps its row, Chrome quiet). Chrome-confirmed through the oracle case (the entry hands every export to the real `@solidjs/web`, so B3 covers the sites on every build and the case cannot show an unproven clean: the closed run of the fixture can) and the fuzz cells `er-genlayer-apply` and `er-genuntrack-apply` (the generator and its iterator at module scope, for the same reason: no function is around the call that makes the iterator, so B3 asks nothing of it): the head of #220 certifies the three `genuntrack` programs, Chrome confirming (false clean), and the fix none (recall-gap, an uncertifiable row stands; the legal twins become uncertifiable, the named cost); the `genlayer` cells are covered on both builds by the ADR 0710 row at the creation. Corpus (34 projects, release): 0 rows added or removed against the head in the standard build and in the development build (one SC9012 `standard-member-impostor` row of `expenses-app` `src/lib/clerk.tsx:49` differs from an earlier run of the head: the head itself gives it when rerun alone, 948 rows on both builds).

**The check of B1 (BLOCKED once more: N1, N2).** `slice6-review/b1-check.md`: the lexical form of B1 held, and two stops of the entry walk (`layer_entry_open`, the emission's test of what enters a layer, which the universe shares) let a layer through. **N1:** B1's test asked the functions written *around* the site, so a function written outside a generator and called from its body (a helper, an arrow beside it, a plain function `yield*` delegates to) had no generator layer, and the caller walk, which follows a closed layer's calls out to their callers and asks each caller's layers the same question, took the generator's body for a placed run: `function helper() { createMemo(…) } function* gen() { helper(); yield 1 }`, `const it = gen()` in the component body, `for (const _ of it)` in an apply (`probes3/a4b`, `a4c`, `a4e`; Chrome rc.21 PRIMITIVE_IN_EFFECT_CALLBACK). **N2:** its stop at an owner-creating layer (and at a control-flow child) returned before it read the function's references: `createRoot(function f() { if (!saved) { saved = f; return; } createMemo(…) })`, then `saved()` in an apply, runs the callback on its own stack under no owner (`b5`); `owner_created_around_call` and `generator_layer_around` had the same stop. Fix, for the universe only (`Premises::Universe`; the emission keeps the premises of ADR 0868, a change to SC4001's proofs being a slice of its own with grid diffs): a layer that is a generator is an open entry, whether it is written around the site or is the caller at the end of a call (a root's generator callback is no owned run either, so the test precedes the owner-creating stop); the stop at an owner-creating layer or a control-flow child holds only where nothing references the function (`referenced_only_by_entry`: a callback with no binding cannot be referenced, a named function expression is asked for the references of its own name, the position that enters it being no reference); the same test guards the lexical climb and the climb of `EffectCreationOwnerSetAround`. Measured: the 115 probes of the three reviews, the head against the fix (release, closed boundary): exactly `a4b`, `a4c`, `a4e` and `b5` move (certified → one SC6002 uncertifiable row each), every other probe is unchanged, no row lost. Fixture `owner-call-layer-stops` (the head certifies all six components; the fix files four rows and leaves the two controls silent: a root's named callback nothing references, a helper called from a plain function). Chrome (oracle case `owner-call-layer-stops`, the shapes at module scope because the oracle's entry hands every export to the real `render`, a hand-off B3 answers with a row below an exported component): the self-saving root is `unproven-clean` on the head and covered on the fix; the three N1 shapes are covered on both builds (a helper at module scope has no rendering role: the graph gives it `unowned-entry` and the ADR 0710 row stands), so the closed run of the fixture and the probes are N1's evidence. Fuzz: `er-rootself-apply` (the root at module scope): the head certifies the three programs, Chrome confirming, the fix none; `er-genhelper-apply`: recall-gap on both builds, so rediscovery.json lists an `outside` entry for N1 saying why. Corpus (34 projects, release): 0 rows added or removed against the B1 head, standard (126,670) and development (147,321) builds.
